# The diagonal half, as a weighted-graph problem

Curvature has left this half entirely: the functional on the diagonal locus, the criticality equation it compresses to, the association-scheme rigidity, and the two-class case settled.

Part of [findings/INDEX.md](INDEX.md), the index that says which file to open.

Definitions and normalisations are quoted from [conventions.md](conventions.md), which sources $\mathcal D_n$ and $\Delta$ back to this file.

---

### $\theta$ on the diagonal locus is a weighted-graph functional

For $W=\mathrm{diag}(w)$ in $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$ the eigenvectors are the $\varphi_{ab}$, and the transported $c^2_{\alpha\beta\gamma}$ is $1$ exactly on **triangles** $\{a,b\},\{b,c\},\{c,a\}$ and $0$ elsewhere. So the whole functional collapses:

$$\theta(w)=\sum_{a<b}w_{ab}^3+3\!\!\sum_{a<b<c}\!\!w_{ab}w_{bc}w_{ca},\qquad \sum_{a<b}w_{ab}^2=1,\quad \sum_{b\ne a}w_{ab}=0\ \ \forall a,$$

the second constraint being Ricci-flatness. **PROVED at every $n$ and every weight as [Lemma L231](#lemma-l231) below**, where it stood on four test points until 2026-09-18.

#### So B1's residual is an extremal graph problem with no curvature in it

A symmetric weight on the edges of $K_n$, zero row sums, unit norm, scored by its cube sum plus three times its triangle sum; and $S=0$ says the **negative** edges carry no triangle. The balanced two-block sphere product is exactly the weight $1/(p(p-1))$ inside the blocks and $-1/(pq)$ across, whose negative graph is $K_{p,q}$, bipartite, hence triangle-free. What is owed reads:

> maximise $\theta(w)$ over weights whose negative graph is triangle-free; is the maximum $\theta_0(n)$, attained only at the balanced complete-bipartite weight?

#### And the triangle sum is a trace, which collapses it further

Writing $M$ for the $n\times n$ symmetric matrix of edge weights (zero diagonal), $\sum_{a<b<c}w_{ab}w_{bc}w_{ca}=\tfrac16\operatorname{tr}M^3$, so

$$\theta=\tfrac12\Big(\sum_{a,b}M_{ab}^3+\operatorname{tr}M^3\Big),\qquad M\mathbf1=0,\quad\operatorname{tr}M=0,\quad\operatorname{tr}M^2=2 .$$

**This is the form [Lemma L231](#lemma-l231) proves.** **The constraints are three linear-or-quadratic conditions on a single symmetric matrix**, and the objective is an entrywise cube sum plus a spectral one.

#### The balanced sphere product is exactly the spectral maximiser, and that is why it wins

$M\mathbf1=0$ forces an eigenvalue $0$, so the other $n-1$ satisfy $\sum\mu=0$, $\sum\mu^2=2$, where $\sum\mu^3$ is maximised at one large positive against $n-2$ equal negatives, with value $2\sqrt2(n-3)/\sqrt{(n-1)(n-2)}$. **The balanced product attains it to six digits**, ratio $1.000000$ at $n=6$ and $n=12$, with spectrum exactly $(\lambda,\,0,\,-\lambda/(n-2)\ \text{repeated})$: $(1.34840,\,0,\,-0.13484^{\times10})$ at $n=12$. Unbalanced products fall short monotonically toward balance, $0.9768$ at $[5,7]$, $0.8967$ at $[4,8]$, and the $C_5$ blow-up reaches $0.5111$.

#### No graph condition is used for this

The extremality follows from $M\mathbf1=0$, $\operatorname{tr}M=0$, $\operatorname{tr}M^2=2$ alone, not from $S=0$, not from triangle-freeness, not from criticality. So the balanced product's optimality on the diagonal locus is, to leading order, **a spectral fact about symmetric matrices annihilating $\mathbf1$**, and what B1 owes is only that the entrywise term cannot compensate a spectral shortfall.

#### The spectral half is almost the whole answer

$\operatorname{tr}M=0$ with $\operatorname{tr}M^2=2$ caps $\operatorname{tr}M^3$ at $2\sqrt2(n-2)/\sqrt{n(n-1)}$, so $\tfrac12\operatorname{tr}M^3\le1.2309$ at $n=12$ against $\theta_0(12)=1.236033$, **$99.6\%$ of the target from the spectrum alone**  *(**SUPERSEDED**: this cap ignores the zero eigenvalue that $M\mathbf1=0$ forces, which the next section's count uses. With it the cap is $1.213562$, a share of $98.18\%$, **exactly saturated** at $S^6\times S^6$, and the split is $1.236033=1.213562+0.022471$, [r389](../reviews/REVIEW_r389.md).)*. The balanced product sits at $98.6\%$ of that cap, and its entrywise term supplies the remaining $0.0449$. So the two halves are not independent: bounding them separately gives $\max|M_{ab}|+1.2309=1.3657$, over by $10\%$, and the coupling is what a proof has to use.

#### Both halves carry the same constant, which states the residual exactly

For $k$ reals with $\sum x=0$ and $\sum x^2=s$, $\max\sum x^3=s^{3/2}(k-2)/\sqrt{k(k-1)}$. Applied to the spectrum ($k=n-1$ after the forced zero) it gives $T\le2\sqrt2\,c_n$; applied row by row ($k=n-1$ entries per row) it gives $E\le c_n\sum_as_a^{3/2}$, with the **same** $c_n=(n-3)/\sqrt{(n-1)(n-2)}$ and $\sum_as_a=\operatorname{tr}M^2=2$. Writing $E_0=2\theta_0-T_{\max}$ for the balanced product's entrywise share, $0.0449$ at $n=12$, just $1.8\%$ of $2\theta_0$, the whole of B1 is

$$E-E_0\ \le\ T_{\max}-T\qquad\text{for every admissible }M ,$$

a single trade-off: **a competitor must gain on the entrywise term exactly what it loses on the spectral one.** Bounding the two separately cannot show it, since $E\le2c_n\sqrt{s_{\max}}$ with $s_{\max}\ge2/n$ already exceeds $E_0$ by an order of magnitude. What the balanced product has is $T=T_{\max}$ exactly and $E$ almost zero.

#### And that decomposes $\theta_0$ itself, in closed form

Since $(n-1)(n-2)-n(n-3)=2$ identically,

$$\theta_0(n)=\underbrace{\sqrt2\,c_n}_{\tfrac12T_{\max},\ \text{spectral}}+\underbrace{\frac{2\sqrt2}{n\sqrt{(n-1)(n-2)}}}_{\operatorname{tr}W^3,\ \text{entrywise}},\qquad c_n=\frac{n-3}{\sqrt{(n-1)(n-2)}} .$$

Both terms match to nine digits at $n=6,8,10,12,14,20$, and the second **is** $\operatorname{tr}W^3$ at the balanced sphere product, a closed form the record did not have, decaying like $2\sqrt2/n^2$. So $\theta_0$ is the spectral cap plus a vanishing correction, and the conjectured maximiser is the unique point that saturates the first while keeping the second positive.

#### The mechanism behind the trade-off is that entries and eigenvalues cannot both concentrate

$E$ and $T$ are third moments of two vectors, the entries $M_{ab}$ and the spectrum $\mu_i$, carrying the **same** second moment $2$, since $\sum_{a,b}M_{ab}^2=\operatorname{tr}M^2$. Each is maximised by concentration, and concentration in one forces spread in the other: a matrix with one dominant eigenvalue is near rank one, whose entries are all of size $\mu/n$; a matrix with one dominant entry has eigenvalues near $\pm|M_{ab}|$, whose cubes cancel and leave $T\approx0$. The balanced product sits at the eigenvalue-concentrated end, taking $T=T_{\max}$ and paying $E=2\sqrt2/(n\sqrt{(n-1)(n-2)})$, which is $1.8\%$ of $2\theta_0$ at $n=12$ and falls like $n^{-2}$. **That is the shape a proof of $E-E_0\le T_{\max}-T$ has to exploit**, and it is a statement about symmetric matrices rather than about curvature.

#### And criticality on the diagonal locus compresses to an $n\times n$ equation

The Euler–Lagrange condition for $\theta$ under $\operatorname{tr}M^2=2$, $M\mathbf1=0$, $\operatorname{diag}M=0$ is

$$\big(M^{\circ2}+M^2\big)_{ij}=\theta\,M_{ij}+v_i+v_j\qquad(i\ne j),$$

for some $v\in\mathbb R^n$, the multiplier of the Ricci-flatness constraint. Fitted jointly for the coefficient and $v$, it holds at **residual $10^{-16}$** with the coefficient coming out exactly $\theta$ at $S^3\times S^3$, $S^6\times S^6$, $S^4\times S^8$ and the $C_5$ blow-up. Contracting against $M$ recovers $2\theta=\langle M^{\circ2}+M^2,M\rangle$, since $M\mathbf1=0$ kills the multiplier term.

#### And the multiplier vanishes

Summing the off-diagonal equation over $j\ne i$ and using $M\mathbf1=0$, $(M^2)\mathbf1=0$ makes the left side $s_i-s_i=0$, leaving $0=(n-2)v_i+\mathbf1^{\mathsf T}v$ for every $i$, so all $v_i$ agree, and then $(2n-2)v=0$ gives $v=0$. Since $(M^{\circ2})_{ii}=0$ and $(M^2)_{ii}=s_i$, the diagonal is forced too:

> **A coordinate-diagonal $W=\mathrm{diag}(w)$ in $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$ of unit norm is a critical point of $P$ if and only if**
> $$M^{\circ2}+M^2=\theta\,M+\mathrm{diag}(s_1,\dots,s_n),\qquad s_i=\sum_jM_{ij}^2 .$$

Verified at $10^{-16}$ at $S^3\times S^3$, $S^6\times S^6$, $S^4\times S^8$, $S^5\times S^7$ and the $C_5$ blow-up, with the diagonal reading $2/n$ at the vertex-transitive ones. The "if" direction uses symmetric criticality: the diagonal locus is the fixed-point set of the coordinate sign-flip group acting on $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$.

#### So the whole diagonal critical-point equation lives in $n\times n$ matrices rather than $\binom n2\times\binom n2$ ones

, the recorded "vertex system" for blow-ups is this equation restricted to block-constant $M$, and here it is with no hypothesis at all.

#### Lemma L68, the symmetry defect on the diagonal locus, and the entry cap criticality forces

Write $M_a$ for row $a$ of $M$ and $s_a=\lVert M_a\rVert^2$, and let $g_\gamma:=\lVert[\mathrm{ad}(v_\gamma),W]\rVert^2$ be the [Petersen–Wink](../references/petersen-wink-2019-bochner-technique/notes.md) Prop 1.7 density whose trace over the strictly positive eigenspace is the symmetry defect $\Delta$ of [bounds.md](bounds.md). On the diagonal locus every eigenvector is a coordinate bivector $\varphi_{ab}=E_{ab}-E_{ba}$, the structure tensor is the single monomial $[\varphi_{ab},\varphi_{bd}]=\varphi_{ad}$ for distinct $a,b,d$, and everything becomes explicit.

**(i) The density, with no hypothesis at all.** $[\mathrm{ad}(\varphi_{ab}),W]$ moves only the $2(n-2)$ directions $\varphi_{ad},\varphi_{bd}$, sending $\varphi_{bd}\mapsto(M_{bd}-M_{ad})\varphi_{ad}$ **and $\varphi_{ad}\mapsto(M_{bd}-M_{ad})\varphi_{bd}$**, **two moved families, which is where the factor $2$ comes from**, each $2\times2$ block carrying two nonzero entries; the pairing throughout is [conventions.md](conventions.md)'s half-trace, in which $\lVert\varphi_{ab}\rVert^2=1$ and $\{\varphi_{ab}\}_{a<b}$ is orthonormal

$$g_{ab}=2\sum_{d\ne a,b}(M_{ad}-M_{bd})^2=2\lVert M_a-M_b\rVert^2-4M_{ab}^2=2(s_a+s_b)-4(M^2)_{ab}-4M_{ab}^2,$$

the middle step because $M$ is hollow, which makes both dropped coordinates equal to $M_{ab}^2$. **This is the diagonal specialisation of the decomposition in [bounds-relaxations.md](bounds-relaxations.md)** $g_\gamma=2\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha^2R_{\gamma\alpha}-2Q_\gamma$ of [bounds.md](bounds.md), not a new identity: on this locus $R_{\gamma\alpha}$ is the indicator that two pairs share exactly one index, so $\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha^2R_{(ab)\alpha}=s_a+s_b-2M_{ab}^2$, and $Q_{(ab)}=2(M^2)_{ab}$. What the specialisation buys is that both terms become elementary functions of one weighted graph.

**(ii) $\Delta=0$ is a blow-up condition.** Since $\Delta=\sum_{M_{ab}>0}g_{ab}$, it vanishes exactly when every **positive** edge joins **twins**, $M_{ad}=M_{bd}$ for every $d$ outside the pair. Linkage is then transitive, because $a\!-\!b$ and $b\!-\!c$ positive force $M_{ac}=M_{bc}>0$. So the components of $G^+=\{ab:M_{ab}>0\}$ are cliques of mutually twin vertices, $M$ is constant inside each and across each pair of them, and every entry between distinct components is $\le0$; conversely any such $M$ has $\Delta=0$. **Block-constancy alone is not the condition**, the $C_5$ blow-up is block-constant and carries $\Delta=12$, its non-adjacent block pairs being positive. *Corollary (Reviewer 292):* a singleton block forces an identically zero row, since all its entries are cross entries, hence $\le0$, and they sum to $0$. So $\Delta=0$ with $\ker W=0$ forces every block to have size $\ge2$, on this locus $\Delta=0$ is already the generalised sphere-product shape, which is the converse to the "$\ker W=0$ is load-bearing" note in [b_symmetry_defect.py](../scripts/lib/b_symmetry_defect.py).

**(iii) At a critical point the density is affine in the entry, and caps it.** The criticality equation above reads off-diagonally $M_{ab}^2+(M^2)_{ab}=\theta M_{ab}$, and substituting into (i) cancels both quadratic terms:

$$g_{ab}=2(s_a+s_b)-4\theta M_{ab}\qquad(a\ne b).$$

Since $g_{ab}\ge0$, **every entry of a diagonal critical point is capped by its own two row norms**, $2\theta M_{ab}\le s_a+s_b$, with equality exactly at twin pairs **in the off-pair sense of (ii)**, $M_{ad}=M_{bd}$ for every $d$ outside the pair, which is *not* equality of rows: identical rows would force $M_{ab}=0$ and so miss every positive edge the cap is used on; so for $\theta>0$

$$\theta\ \le\ \min_{M_{ab}>0}\frac{s_a+s_b}{2M_{ab}},\qquad\text{and exactly}\qquad \theta=\frac{s_a+s_b}{2M_{ab}}-\frac{g_{ab}}{4M_{ab}}\ \text{ at every positive edge.}$$

The cap needs criticality, symmetry, hollowness **and $M\mathbf1=0$**, the last kills the Ricci-flatness multiplier, without which the off-diagonal criticality equation is not the one written; scale enters through $\operatorname{tr}M^2=2$, which is what makes the coefficient $\theta$ rather than a scale-dependent multiplier. *No sign condition on $\theta$ is used: the cap holds at every sign, and the $\theta>0$ guard on the min form is needed only to divide.* Hollowness, hollowness is what makes the two dropped coordinates equal $M_{ab}^2$ in the very inequality that follows: $\lVert M_a-M_b\rVert^2\ge2M_{ab}^2$ by dropping all but two coordinates of the row difference, *which is $g_{ab}\ge0$ written out, not an independent argument*, and criticality converts the left side. **Criticality is load-bearing**: maximising the violation over the diagonal locus without it reaches $+0.075$, $+0.227$, $+0.334$, $+0.370$, $+0.368$ at $n=5,6,8,10,12$.

**(iv) Traced over $\mathfrak p$, and this part needs $M\mathbf1=0$ as well as criticality.** *Summing (iii) over positive edges lands on $2\theta\sum_{M_{ab}>0}M_{ab}$, and it is zero row sums that make that positive mass exactly half the $\ell^1$ mass: hollowness with $M\mathbf1=0$ gives $\sum_{a<b}M_{ab}=0$, hence $\lVert M\rVert_1=2\sum_{M_{ab}>0}M_{ab}$.* **Without $M\mathbf1=0$ the identity is false by a factor of two**, $M=c(J-I)$ is hollow, symmetric, unit-norm and satisfies the boxed criticality equation with $\theta=(n-1)c$, part (iii) holds at it exactly, and (iv) reads $3$ against $6$ at $n=4$. With $d_a^+=\#\{b:M_{ab}>0\}$ and $\lVert M\rVert_1=\sum_{a<b}\lvert M_{ab}\rvert$, and $\Delta$ summed over **unordered** positive pairs, against [conventions.md](conventions.md)'s ordered default, under which $\Delta$ doubles and the identity carries $\tfrac14\Delta$:

$$\theta\,\lVert M\rVert_1=\sum_a d_a^+s_a-\tfrac12\Delta .$$

**This is route R10's conversion of defect into $\theta$ deficit, exact and with no estimate anywhere, on the diagonal locus only.** Both (iii) and (iv) are **equalities** at $S^{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times S^{\lceil n/2\rceil}$, returning $\theta_0(n)$ at $n=6,\dots,20$, and at the $C_5$ blow-up, returning $0.670820$.

##### What it does not reach, and the fence that follows

**The cap is an equality at every balanced product**, so relaxing its right-hand side to a function of $n$ alone is either false at the maximiser or is the diagonal conjecture restated. An entry-free form needs an inequality tying $\max_+M_{ab}$ to the row norms, which is the coupling this file already names as the missing ingredient. *So (iii) restates that gap in sharper coordinates and closes none of it.* **It is also not uniformly the best bound on record**: at an exact $n=6$ critical point $M=\sqrt{2/21}\,Z$ with every $s_a=\tfrac72c^2$ and every positive edge at $c=\sqrt{2/21}$, it gives $1.080123$ against $\theta=0.308607$ while the sharpened $\lambda$-weighted bound of [target.md](target.md) gives $0.661300$, *rebuilt here from the reviewer's matrix, criticality residual $2.8\times10^{-17}$*. That point has $S_{\mathfrak{mmm}}=12$, so the comparison is off the target set and the two bounds have not been raced on $\{S=0\}$. Among contractions using only $g\ge0$, "min over positive edges" is LP-optimal; anything stronger must bound $g_{ab}$ **from below**.

**$\Delta$ is genuinely discontinuous, and the tolerance is a modelling choice rather than a numerical one.** At the $n=7$ Weyl point of Reviewer 292, two blocks of three at $\tfrac16$, cross $-\tfrac19$, then $M_{06}=+\varepsilon$, $M_{16}=-\varepsilon$, $M_{02}\mapsto\tfrac16-\varepsilon$, $M_{12}\mapsto\tfrac16+\varepsilon$ with $\varepsilon=5\times10^{-10}$, the deciding entry normalises to $9.487\times10^{-10}$, and $\Delta$ jumps from $2/3$ to $0$ as the positivity threshold crosses it. *The reviewer read this as the record's `defect()` disagreeing with the closed form; rebuilding it here shows both return $0$ at their shared $10^{-9}$, so the disagreement is between two tolerances and the statements are about the exact $\Delta$.*

**The mechanism does not lift through $\mathrm{Ric}(W^2)$.** For a diagonal $W$ the operator square has pair entries $M_{ab}^2$, so $s_a=\mathrm{Ric}(W^2)_{aa}$, and the coordinate-free statement (iii) suggests is $g_\gamma=2\langle\widehat{\mathrm{Ric}(W^2)}v_\gamma,v_\gamma\rangle-4\theta\lambda_\gamma$. That is exact at every diagonal critical point, the **ungraded** $C_5$ blow-up included, and fails by $2.00$, $2.22$, $2.33$ at $\mathbb{CP}^{2,3,4}$ against $\lvert g\rvert$ of order $6$, $3.3$, $2.3$ ([run_defect_identity_scope.py](../scripts/runs/run_defect_identity_scope.py)). The reason is (i): the general carrier of $s_a+s_b$ is $\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha^2R_{\gamma\alpha}$, and $\mathrm{Ric}(W^2)$ is only its diagonal-locus face. **[Lemma L199](bounds.md) gives that carrier in closed form and names the deficit exactly**: the general identity is $g_\gamma=4(\mathrm{Ric}(W^2)\wedge\mathrm{id})_{\gamma\gamma}+12\,\mathrm{Alt}(W^2)_{\gamma\gamma}-4\theta\lambda_\gamma$ at every critical Weyl tensor, so **the whole of the failure above is $12\,\mathrm{Alt}(W^2)_{\gamma\gamma}$**, which is $\tfrac{4(2m-1)}{3m}$ at $\mathbb{CP}^m$ and reproduces $2$, $\tfrac{20}9$, $\tfrac73$ against the measured $2.00$, $2.22$, $2.33$. *It vanishes identically on the diagonal locus, a coordinate-diagonal $W$ having no four-index-distinct entry and its square none either*, which is why the displayed form is exact there. **So the general form is an identity rather than a restatement**, and what still governs the transportation relaxation of $Q_\gamma$ is the fence rather than the absence of a carrier. *Gate 2 owed on the one step that is not recorded: $Q_\gamma=2(W^\#)_{\gamma\gamma}$ per row, which with criticality pins $Q_\gamma=2(\theta\lambda_\gamma-\lambda_\gamma^2)$ instead of relaxing it into that class.*

#### The SECOND-ORDER operator also lives in $n\times n$ matrices, and it is the criticality map's own derivative

The criticality equation above is $\Phi(M)=\theta M+\operatorname{diag}(s)$ with $\Phi(M)=M^{\circ2}+M^2$. Its derivative is immediate, $D(N^{\circ2})[H]=2M\circ H$ and $D(N^2)[H]=MH+HM$, and it *is* the second-order operator of [Lemma L24](second-order.md) restricted to this locus:

$$\mathcal L(N)\;=\;M\circ N+\tfrac12(MN+NM)\;=\;\tfrac12\,D\Phi(M)[N],\qquad L_e=\mathcal L/\theta .$$

Verified to $6\times10^{-17}$ at $S^3\times S^5$, $S^6\times S^6$ and the **ungraded** $C_5$ blow-up, on random hollow symmetric zero-row-sum $N$, **and $L_WH$ stays exactly diagonal**, off-diagonal mass $0$, so the diagonal locus is $L_W$-invariant and the whole second-order computation closes inside $n\times n$ matrices rather than on $\Lambda^2$.

**The restriction is STRICTLY LOSSY, and the claim that it loses nothing is WITHDRAWN.** *Reviewer 303 found the dent; verified independently here and it is wider than reported.*

| point | $\Delta$ | diagonal $\lambda_{\max}$ | full space | |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| $C_5$ blow-up | $12$ | $1.206011$ | $1.206011$ | equal |
| the unpadded $n=9$ witness | $12.07$ | $1.303584$ | $1.303584$ | equal |
| $S^2\times S^3$ | $0$ | $0.250000$ | $0.750000$ | **full violates, diagonal blind** |
| $S^2\times S^4$ | $0$ | $0.333333$ | $0.666667$ | **full violates, diagonal blind** |
| $S^2\times S^6$ | $0$ | $0.400000$ | $0.600000$ | **full violates, diagonal blind** |
| $S^3\times S^5$, $S^6\times S^6$ | $0$ | $0.375$, $0.400$ | $0.500$ | lossy |

**It is lossy at every sphere product**, and at three of them the full space exceeds $\tfrac12$ while the diagonal restriction sees nothing. The earlier claim rested on two blow-up-type violators where it happens to be tight, and generalised from them, *the exact failure this record warns about, a structural claim read off the points that were easy to compute.* **So proving the diagonal statement would NOT prove V3**: the $n\times n$ problem is a genuine sub-problem, not an equivalent one, and it is blind to violations carried by non-diagonal directions.

*What survives*: at a **diagonal** critical point the diagonal $\lambda_{\max}$ is a valid lower bound for the full one, so exceeding $\tfrac12$ there still certifies non-maximality, it just cannot be relied on to detect it. And $0.375$ and $0.400$ are $\tfrac{p-2}{2(p-1)}$ at $p=5,6$, so the derived block formula does give the *diagonal* value at a sphere product.

**So on the diagonal locus Lemma L24 is an explicit small spectral problem**: a diagonal local maximum needs $\lambda_{\max}(\mathcal L)\le\tfrac\theta2$ over hollow symmetric $N$ with zero row sums. *That is the diagonal half of [plan.md](../plan.md)'s V3*, reduced from a $\binom n2\times\binom n2$ operator to an $n\times n$ one, with no representation theory and no isotypic decomposition, the machinery whose isotypic form was refuted at Gate 2.

**The operator identity is off by a diagonal.** Diagonal invariance is a theorem, off-diagonal mass exactly $0$ at critical, non-critical and non-Ricci-flat diagonal $W$ alike, but the pair matrix of $L_WH$ is **hollow**, while $M\circ N+\tfrac12(MN+NM)$ carries a diagonal $\langle M_i,N_i\rangle$, measured up to $0.19$. **The correct object is the derivative of the whole criticality map, $\tfrac12D[\Phi-\operatorname{diag}(s)]$**, and the hollowing is exactly what makes the row sums cancel, i.e. it *is* the preservation of Ricci-flatness. The Rayleigh form $\sum_{ij}M_{ij}N_{ij}^2+\operatorname{tr}(MN^2)$ is exact and proved, in the $n\times n$ Frobenius normalisation; **the $\Lambda^2$ pairing is half of it**.

**Diagonal invariance has a combinatorial proof.** Two distinct index pairs meet in exactly one vertex, so $(R\#S)_{\alpha\beta}\ne0$ forces $\alpha=\beta$, invariance is forced by the pair combinatorics rather than measured, and holds at non-critical $W$ and for $H$ outside $\mathcal W_n$.

**Why the reduction can never be sharp.** $L_W([A,W])=\tfrac\theta2[A,W]$ for **every** $A\in\mathfrak{so}(n)$, and those orbit directions are entirely off the diagonal locus, so the diagonal criterion carries at least a factor of two of slack at every maximizer.

**And the deformation exhibited here was not even admissible.** [Lemma L24](second-order.md) needs $N\perp W$, and only the row sums were checked: $\langle W,N\rangle/\lVert W\rVert\lVert N\rVert$ is $+0.221$, $-0.196$ and $+0.156$ at the three sums used to verify it, so it certified nothing. *The repair is $\sum_ca_c\theta_c^{-2}=0$, a single linear condition on $(a_c)$, orthogonality to the vector $(\theta_c^{-2})$, so the signs must differ; $a_c\propto\theta_c^2$ satisfies it only for $a\equiv0$*, which restores orthogonality to $10^{-16}$.

**Padded points break the consequence outright.** $S^4\times S^4$ plus an isolated vertex at $n=9$ is disconnected by the record's own criterion ($\dim\ker M=2=\#$components) and has $\theta>0$, yet $\lambda_{\max}(L_e)=0.5714$, and the indicator $N$ there has $\cos(N,M)=+1.000000$, i.e. it **is** the radial direction, so the "non-radial" clause fails as well. Same at padded $S^3\times S^3$ ($0.600$) and $S^3\times S^4$ ($0.583$). *The repair is "at least two components **carrying an edge**", not "disconnected".* And the admissibility condition is $\sum_ca_c\theta_c^{-2}=0$: $\langle N,M\rangle=\theta^2\sum_ca_c\theta_c^{-2}$ at the $\Lambda^2$ level, twice that in Frobenius, *the constant is $\lVert M^{(c)}\rVert^2$ in whichever pairing is used, since $\langle N,M\rangle=\sum_ca_c(\theta/\theta_c)^2\lVert M^{(c)}\rVert^2$; the $8$ once recorded here is wrong in both, r309 and r310 disagreed on it, and this derivation settles it. Only the exponent $-2$ enters admissibility, and it is normalisation-independent*, so $\sum a_c=0$ enforces nothing and the cosine reaches $0.99$ across $205$ sums.

**The brief's central worry does NOT bite, and that is worth keeping.** The diagonal locus is an $L_W$-invariant subspace of $\mathcal W_n$, verified at non-critical $W$ too, so the full $\lambda_{\max}$ is never below the diagonal one and **exceeding $\tfrac12$ on the locus does certify failure**. What the invariance costs is only sharpness, not validity.

**A trap that fabricates V3 counterexamples: $\Delta$ is not sign-symmetric.** Padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ has $\Delta=0$ on the $\theta>0$ branch and $\Delta=12$ on the $\theta<0$ branch, and sits at $\lambda_{\max}=\tfrac12$ exactly, so reading $\Delta$ off the wrong branch manufactures a point that looks like $\Delta>0$ with $\lambda_{\max}\le\tfrac12$. *The record's convention is $\theta>0$, and this is why.*

**"Disconnected" is not the operative hypothesis either.** Connected points fail the same way, $S^2\times S^2$ and the $K_3$, $K_4$ blow-ups all have $\dim E(1)\ge2$ and full-space $\lambda_{\max}=1$.

#### Lemma L99, a diagonal critical point with two components carrying an edge fails second-order maximality

**The construction, and it is a derivation rather than a check.** A direct sum of diagonal critical points is critical **once scaled as criticality forces**: $M=\bigoplus\lambda_iM_i$ with $\lambda_i\theta_i=\theta$. *Proof*: the blocks are $\circ$-orthogonal, so $Q(M)=\bigoplus\lambda_i^2Q(M_i)=\bigoplus\lambda_i^2\theta_iM_i$, and $Q(M)=\theta M$ forces $\lambda_i^2\theta_i=\theta\lambda_i$, hence $\lambda_i\theta_i=\theta$ at every $\lambda_i\ne0$. Unit norm is $\sum_i\lambda_i^2=1$, so $\theta^2\sum_i\theta_i^{-2}=1$ and

$$\theta=\Big(\textstyle\sum_i\theta_i^{-2}\Big)^{-1/2}.$$

**This is the harmonic-square law**, which [nondegeneracy.md](nondegeneracy.md) and [target.md](target.md) already carry; additivity closes the loop, $\theta(M)=\sum_i\lambda_i^3\theta_i=\theta^3\sum_i\theta_i^{-2}=\theta$. *The three numerical checks that stood here, at $10^{-17}$, and the $7\times10^{-3}$ residual for equal scaling, are corroboration of a two-line argument.* On such a point the **component-indicator** deformation $N_{ab}=a_{c(a)}M_{ab}$, with $a$ constant on each component, gives an admissible direction, **and the condition is $\sum_ca_c\theta_c^{-2}=0$ over the EDGE-CARRYING components**, since $\langle N,M\rangle=\theta^2\sum_ca_c\theta_c^{-2}$ and a singleton component contributes nothing whatever its $a_c$. *The condition $\sum a=0$ that stood here is the one this file withdraws ten lines above, and the row-sum check beside it has no content, row sums vanishing identically for this family.*

**$\dim\ker M$ is the NUMBER OF CONNECTED COMPONENTS, and it is a theorem.** Criticality on the diagonal locus reads $M^{\circ2}+M^2=\theta M+\operatorname{diag}(s)$ with $s_a=(M^2)_{aa}$, so $M^2-\theta M=\operatorname{diag}(s)-M^{\circ2}$, **the Laplacian of the weighted graph $w_{ab}=M_{ab}^2$**, its off-diagonal being $-M_{ab}^2$ and its diagonal the matching row sum, $M$ being hollow. Its kernel is spanned by the component indicators. Each indicator lies in $\ker M$ by the within-block Ricci-flatness, and $\ker M\subseteq\ker(M^2-\theta M)$, so the two spans coincide. *The census of some $400$ exact critical points that stood here, at $10^{-15}$, verified a three-line consequence of [Lemma L129](#lemma-l129-the-spectral-gap-is-half-open-lambda-is-never-an-eigenvalue) in this same file.*

**So the $A=\operatorname{diag}(a)$ family is live exactly where at least TWO components carry an edge**, and there it delivers a non-radial admissible $N$ with $\mathcal LN=\theta N$ exactly. Writing $k^+$ for the number of edge-carrying components: at $k^+\ge2$, taking $a_{c_1}=\theta_{c_1}^2$ and $a_{c_2}=-\theta_{c_2}^2$ gives $N\ne0$ with $N\perp M$, so $\lambda_{\max}(L_e\vert_{W^\perp})\ge1>\tfrac12$ and [Lemma L24](../results.md) fails; at $k^+=1$ the condition forces $N\propto M$, which is radial and certifies nothing; at $k^+=0$ the family is empty. **The admissible eigenspace has dimension $k^+-1$**, so three or more edge-carrying components give a violation of multiplicity at least two.

**The conclusion is on the record elsewhere, and the join is owed.** [ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md)'s direct-sum saddle lemma carries the same eigenvector $H=\lVert W_2\rVert^2W_1-\lVert W_1\rVert^2W_2$ with the stronger conclusion $\mathrm{Hess}=+3\theta\lVert H\rVert^2$, and that file already ruled this claim *\"on the record four times over\"* while leaving the numbered block standing. **That saddle lemma is unnumbered, and [Lemma L66](support-dichotomy.md)'s audit found it misattributed as well**, so three results now point at one unnumbered theorem, and the single numbered family they should form is recorded as owed in [LEDGER.md](LEDGER.md). *It also inherits the same padding defect: at $W_2=0$ the eigenvector vanishes, which is the $k^+=1$ case below.*

**Lemma L99, proved.** *Let $M\in\mathcal D_n$ be a diagonal critical point with $\theta>0$ whose support graph has at least two components carrying an edge. Then $\lambda_{\max}(L_e\vert_{W^\perp})\ge1$, so $W$ is not a maximiser.* **The hypothesis is sharp for this family**, the $k^+=1$ case being exhausted by the radial direction, *and the wider statement that stood here, for every disconnected point, is refuted by this file's own padded $S^4\times S^4$ row at $n=9$, where the extra components are singletons, $\lambda_{\max}(L_e)=0.5714<1$ and $\cos(N,M)=+1$.*

*Observation, at the dimensions run, and its subject occurs nowhere else on the record.* **The algebraic candidates: the outcome stands, the statement was wrong twice.** They do not *project* to zero, at the $C_5$ blow-up the projections are $0.211$, $0.211$ and $0.047$; it is the **Rayleigh quotient** that vanishes. And they are not three candidates but two: criticality forces $\mathrm{proj}(M^{\circ2})=-\mathrm{proj}(M^2)$ identically, verified here to $7\times10^{-17}$, which is why the $n=9$ witness returned $0.059$ twice. The real class is the $\mathrm{Aut}(M)$-invariant matrices, and the vanishing at sphere products is a dimension count, the invariant admissible subspace is $0$-dimensional there.

*Observation, at the one point named.* **And the representation-theoretic obstruction is a property of SYMMETRIC points only**, and the general form of the claim it corrects, that $L_e$ is scalar on each isotypic component, holds only where that component is multiplicity-free, as [second-order.md](second-order.md) now says. At an $n=8$ point with $\Delta=11.16$ and $\lvert\mathrm{Aut}(M)\rvert=2$ the top eigenspace is one-dimensional and its eigenvector **is** fixed pointwise, deviation $0$, with invariant directions reaching the full $\lambda_{\max}$. So equivariant construction is unobstructed there. *What survives is narrower and still useful*: a **uniform** equivariant recipe fails, because it must also fire at the symmetric points, where the maximum invariant Rayleigh quotient is $0.000$ against $\theta/2=0.335$.

*Observations, at the dimensions run; the block-graph statistic's subject occurs nowhere else on the record.* **Three further corrections from the same round.** The mixed piece's $\tfrac12$ is explained outright: $\Pi_{\mathcal W}H_A=c^\ast[D_X,W]$ **exactly** for mixed $A$, with $c^\ast=\frac{q(q-1)-p(p-1)}{(n-1)(n-2)}$, so that piece *is* the orbit tangent, forced everywhere, and **the $p=q$ annihilation is simply the case $c^\ast=0$**. The isotypic claim dies most cleanly on this record's own live-target point, where $\mathfrak k(W)=0$ exactly, $\mathrm{Sym}_0(\mathbb R^8)$ is a *single* isotype of multiplicity $35$, and $L_e$ takes **eight** distinct values on it. And the block-graph statistic fails at $n=12$ too, three $\Delta>0$ critical points with edge average $\le\tfrac12$ and $\lambda_{\max}$ up to $1.152$, with **all seven** readings of "block graph" at or below the bound at the four-block one.

*One correction to how the hunt was framed*: a $\Delta>0$ point with $\mathrm{Sym}_0$ maximum $\le\tfrac12$ would refute only the $\mathrm{Sym}_0$ **certificate**, not V3 itself, which is about $\lambda_{\max}$ on all of $e^\perp$.

#### Observation L98, the diagonal floor, measured at $1+\frac1{2(n-3)(n-4)}$

*Gate 2, [r677](../reviews/REVIEW_r677.md) and [r678](../reviews/REVIEW_r678.md) on identical briefs, **both GAP and agreeing: the mathematics is consistent everywhere it can be checked, and the status of the statement is not**.*

> **THE SOURCE WROTE THIS FORMULA WITH A QUESTION MARK OVER THE EQUALS SIGN, AND ASKED THAT IT BE QUOTED AS AN UPPER BOUND WITH ITS POPULATION.** The record dropped **the question mark, the population and the hedge**, the fold-in removed the hedge and **the numbering commit promoted the unhedged form into a heading.** *The same hardening then happened independently at four further sites.* **A census bounds an infimum from above only**, so the floor is measured, and the label `Observation` is right while the bare $=$ is not.

> **"$\Delta>0\Rightarrow\lambda_{\max}(L_e)\ge1$ SURVIVES" IS WRITTEN UNSCOPED. IN THE BLOCK WHOSE JOB IS THE SCOPE WARNING. AND ITS OWN TABLE REFUTES IT THREE LINES BELOW**, at $0.6089$, $0.5628$ and $0.5535$. *The warning itself is right and **understates**: the off-locus cushion is $1.1027\times$ today, not the $1.1070\times$ recorded, and every such figure on the record is an upper bound that has only fallen.*

> **THE NEIGHBOURS COLLAPSE EVEN THOUGH THIS FLOOR DOES NOT.** Rebuilding $\mathcal L(N)=M\circ N+\tfrac12(MN+NM)$ from the cubic and decomposing under $S_p\times S_q$ gives the **entire** diagonal spectrum at a sphere product, with $\lambda_{\max}=\frac{p_{\max}-2}{2(p_{\max}-1)}<\tfrac12$ and a padding term $\mu_{\max}/2\theta$, **reproducing nine measured numbers in this same file exactly**, settling a doubtful plan item, and turning *"every minimiser connected"* into a corollary. **But L98's own floor cannot be derived, because the minimising family was lost in the fold-in**, recovering the minimisers is a run, named and not opened.

> **THE POLES AT $n=3,4$ ARE EXCLUDED SILENTLY, AND BOTH ARE VACUOUS.** At $n=4$ every diagonal critical point with $\theta>0$ has twin positive edges, so $\Delta=0$, proved by hand. *$n=5$ is an untested prediction of $1.25$, and the source census reached $n=5$ without reporting it.* **The table's rows are all $n=8$ and the block never says so**, while the fitted dimensions are $n=10\dots16$, leaving $n=6,7,8,9$ untested inside the range the block names.

> **THE TABLE MEASURES ONE QUANTITY ON TWO DIFFERENT SUBSPACES**, which the source asked the record to state and which it did not. *Its second row **is** a critical point, both rows were polished to $2.1\times10^{-16}$ three days and twenty commits ago, and the "provisional until polished" note here is stale.* **Two figures dismissed as "artefacts of small $n$" are the formula's own values at $n=7$ and $n=8$**, unrecorded corroboration from populations the fit never saw.

**And the cushion is DIAGONAL-ONLY.** Both censuses report every $\Delta>0$ point clearing $\lambda_{\max}/\theta\ge1.04$, twice what V3 asks, and offer it as a factor-two margin for a proof. **Both censused the diagonal locus.** **The comparison must be made on $\lambda_{\max}(L_e)$ itself**, which is the quantity Lemma L24 bounds by $\tfrac12$ and the quantity the censuses report, *an earlier version here quoted $\lambda_{\max}/\theta$, dividing by $\theta$ a second time, and compared it against a census figure in the other normalisation.* On the right quantity: **the diagonal minimum over the census fits a closed form converging to $1$**, *[Reviewer 310](../reviews/REVIEW_r310.md), from a census of $571$ diagonal $\Delta>0$ critical points at $n=6\ldots16$, the formula checked here against every value it reports*: $\min\lambda_{\max}(L_e)=1+\frac1{2(n-3)(n-4)}$, exact across seven consecutive dimensions, $1.011905$ at $n=10$, $1.006944$ at $n=12$, $1.003205$ at $n=16$, with **every minimiser connected** and the $n=16$ witness verified in the full Weyl space at criticality residual $2.2\times10^{-16}$. **So the $1.025$ once recorded here was the minimum of a population that stopped at $n=8$, and the "factor-two cushion" is an artefact of small $n$.** [Reviewer 309](../reviews/INDEX.md) reproduces $1.025$ exactly and does not contradict this, its census also stops at $n=8$, so the two answer different questions. An independent solve here ([run_check_diagonal_floor.py](../scripts/runs/run_check_diagonal_floor.py), block-constant zero-row-sum $M$, every solution screened against $\mathcal W_n$) reaches $1.0089$ at $n=12$, above the formula's $1.006944$ and so consistent with it. **The stronger reading $\Delta>0\Rightarrow\lambda_{\max}(L_e)\ge1$ survives, and is now sharp, there is no slack left to spend on a proof.** Meanwhile the non-diagonal witness sits at $\lambda_{\max}(L_e)=0.608885$, **a factor $1.68$ below the diagonal floor** ($59.4\%$ of it; *an earlier version read "less than half", which is false, half of $1.025$ is $0.5125$*), and the margin has since fallen further. **[Reviewer 305](../reviews/REVIEW_r305.md) found two more genuine non-diagonal $\Delta>0$ points**, validated here frame-independently (trivial kernel, no rank-$2$ kernel bivector, inside $\mathcal W_n$ to $3\times10^{-16}$):

| point | $\theta$ | $\Delta$ | $\lambda_{\max}(L_e)$ | margin over $\tfrac12$ | criticality |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| the recorded witness | $1.088948053$ | $1.512231$ | $0.608885$ | $1.2178\times$ | $5\times10^{-16}$ |
| new | $1.081549248$ | $2.400930$ | $0.562755$ | $1.1255\times$ | $1.1\times10^{-11}$ |
| new | $1.079758866$ | $2.762850$ | $0.553481$ | **$1.1070\times$** | $1.2\times10^{-11}$ |

**So the off-locus cushion is $1.107\times$, not the factor of two the diagonal locus shows**, and the two new points are *approximate* critical points, residual $10^{-11}$ rather than machine precision, so their $\lambda_{\max}$ is provisional until polished. **A third point r305 reported as new is the known padding** and is excluded here as it was before: $\theta=1.079098984$, $\lambda_{\max}=0.555213$, $\dim\ker W=7$ with a rank-$2$ kernel bivector, i.e. an $n=7$ point in a rotated frame, *the coordinate test reports no padding and is wrong*, which is why the frame-independent rank certificate is the one that runs. *A cycle taking the cushion as general would be relying on a diagonal statement to prove a non-diagonal one.*

**Two things that make the target look easier than stated.** The margin is not thin: over $66$ distinct $\Delta>0$ points the minimum of $\lambda_{\max}(L_e)$ is $1.0417$, they clear not merely $\tfrac12$ but **$1$**, so the diagonal half of V3 appears to hold in the strictly stronger form $\Delta>0\Rightarrow\lambda_{\max}(L_e)\ge1$, and a counterexample would need $\theta>2\lambda_{\max}\gtrsim1.1$, at the level of $\theta_0(n)$ itself. And there is an **explicit hand-checkable family**: for $u=e_a-e_b$, $v=e_c-e_d$ and $N=uv^{\mathsf T}+vu^{\mathsf T}$,

$$\frac{\langle N,\mathcal LN\rangle}{\theta\lVert N\rVert^2}=\frac{M_{ac}+M_{ad}+M_{bc}+M_{bd}-2M_{ab}-2M_{cd}}{4\theta},$$

verified exact here at $S^3\times S^5$, $S^6\times S^6$ and the $C_5$ blow-up. *Its reach is partial*: it certifies failure at $10$ of $13$ $\Delta>0$ points by hand with no eigen-solve, and the blow-up is one of the three it misses, its best value being $\tfrac13$.

**No counterexample to V3 was found** across some $700$ random critical points at $n=6\ldots10$, $946$ block-constant blow-ups to $n\le16$, the sphere, padded, direct-sum and $C_5$ families, and a direct penalty search.

#### Contracting it against the top eigenvector turns B1 into a single missing factor

With $u$ the top eigenvector, $u^{\mathsf T}M^{\circ2}u=\theta\mu_1+\sum_is_iu_i^2-\mu_1^2$; and $M^{\circ2}$ is entrywise nonnegative with row sums $s_i$, so $u^{\mathsf T}M^{\circ2}u\le\max_is_i$. Where the row norms are equal, every vertex-transitive $M$, which includes the sphere products and the $C_5$ blow-ups, the two $s$ terms cancel and

$$\theta\le\mu_1\le\sqrt{\tfrac{2(n-2)}{n-1}},$$

the second bound following from $\sum\mu=0$, $\sum\mu^2=2$ and the forced zero, and **attained exactly by the balanced product**, $1.264911$ at $n=6$, $1.348400$ at $n=12$. Since

$$\theta_0(n)=\frac{n-1}{n}\sqrt{\frac{2(n-2)}{n-1}}$$

identically, **the whole of B1 on the vertex-transitive locus is the factor $(n-1)/n$**: what is owed is $\theta\le\frac{n-1}{n}\mu_1$ where the contraction currently gives $\theta\le\mu_1$.

#### And that factor is one inequality, stated exactly

The lossy step was bounding $u^{\mathsf T}M^{\circ2}u$ by the Perron eigenvalue $\max_is_i$. But $u\perp\mathbf1$, since $M\mathbf1=0$ and $\mu_1\ne0$, and on the vertex-transitive locus $\mathbf1$ **is** the Perron vector of $M^{\circ2}$, with eigenvalue $2/n$. So the second eigenvalue is what bounds it, and $\theta\le\frac{n-1}{n}\mu_1$ is *equivalent* to

$$u^{\mathsf T}M^{\circ2}u\ \le\ \frac1n\sum_{i\ge2}\mu_i^2\ =\ \frac{2-\mu_1^2}{n} .$$

#### It holds with equality at the sphere products

, $0.066666667$ at $S^3\times S^3$ and $0.015151515$ at $S^6\times S^6$, both sides agreeing to nine digits, with $u$ also being the second eigenvector of $M^{\circ2}$ there, and strictly at the $C_5$ blow-up, $0.023843$ against $0.123843$. So the equality case is the conjectured maximiser, exactly as it should be.

#### Lemma L97, the vertex Laplacian is PSD at a diagonal critical point, $K^2-\lambda K\succeq0$

#### The general test vector gives a MATRIX statement, strictly stronger than the scalar one

*Gate 2, [r637](../reviews/REVIEW_r637.md) and [r638](../reviews/REVIEW_r638.md) on identical briefs, **both CONFIRMED**. The identity, both display equalities and the factor $\tfrac12$ are correct; one word beside them is not.*

> **THE IDENTITY IS EXACT AND CONSUMES LESS THAN THE HEADING SUGGESTS.** $K^2-\lambda K=L(K_{ab}^2)$ **entrywise**, so the non-negative quantity is exactly $v^{\mathsf T}(K^2-\lambda K)v$ with nothing dropped. It uses diagonality, the pair-diagonal entries of criticality, and hollowness, **not Ricci-flatness, not maximality, not $\lambda>0$**, so it holds at every diagonal critical point, saddles included. *The $a=b$ row is a tautology, which is the whole role of $D$; hollowness is what makes $D$ simultaneously $\operatorname{diag}(K^2)$ and the graph's degree matrix.* **The factor $\tfrac12$ is exactly the double-count price** under the record's ordered-pair convention, which the block does not state. And **$\lambda=\theta$ is derived rather than assumed**: contracting the pair equation against $K$ reproduces this file's own cubic verbatim, which also pins the half-trace pairing.

> **THE WORD "EQUIVALENTLY" IS FALSE, AND `git` SHOWS THE SCAR.** PSD is equivalent to the **open** interval $(0,\lambda)$ being empty; the endpoint $\kappa=\lambda$ gives $\kappa^2-\lambda\kappa=0$, which PSD permits. **The half-open form is strictly stronger and comes from [Lemma L129](diagonal-half.md)**, which additionally consumes $K\mathbf1=0$ and $\lambda>0$, hypotheses L97 does not use. *An earlier commit had the open bracket; a later one changed it to closed and left the word standing.* **So anyone citing L97 alone for the endpoint is citing L129 without knowing it.** *L129 also uses L97, so the two are not independent confirmations.*

> **"STRICTLY STRONGER" IS TRUE, AND THE FILE CONTRADICTS IT SEVENTY LINES BELOW.** The claim holds against the scalar statement as this block defines it, the single top-eigenvector instance, and the gap is **load-bearing**, since the counting corollary $r\le2/\lambda^2$ needs *every* positive eigenvalue $\ge\lambda$. The matrix form also has wider reach: the scalar derivation above needs equal row norms, the $v=u$ instance does not. **But a bold sentence below asserts the matrix statement is *not* stronger**, correct only against the *family* of scalar instances over all eigenvectors, silently changing the referent. *Both are currently citable and they are opposites.*

> **ONE SENTENCE POINTS THE WRONG WAY.** *"The scalar Poincaré inequality below is the single instance $v=$ top eigenvector"*, what is below is an **open conjecture**, and the $v=u$ instance is an *identity*, making that box equivalent to the $\tfrac{n-1}n\mu_1$ factor which is precisely what is owed. Read as pointing *up*, the sentence is true.

*Provenance to [r125](../reviews/REVIEW_r125.md) is **true and checkable to the minute**, r125 landed four minutes before the block, and if anything the note understates it, since r125 stated the conclusion itself. "Gate 2 owed on this section" was accurate for the identity and stale for the surrounding paragraphs, which carry four verdicts. A dead pointer sends `post F29` to a file that does not contain it; it lives in [target.md](target.md).*

On the diagonal locus the criticality equation is $K^{\circ2}+K^2=\lambda K+D$ with $D=\operatorname{diag}(\operatorname{diag}K^2)$. Pairing with an **arbitrary** $v$ rather than an eigenvector,

$$v^{\mathsf T}Dv-v^{\mathsf T}K^{\circ2}v=\sum_{ab}K_{ab}^2\big(v_a^2-v_av_b\big)=\tfrac12\sum_{ab}K_{ab}^2(v_a-v_b)^2\ \ge0,$$

so $v^{\mathsf T}(K^2-\lambda K)v\ge0$ for every $v$, i.e.

$$\boxed{\ K^2-\lambda K\succeq0\ }\qquad\text{equivalently}\qquad\textbf{$K$ has no eigenvalue in }(0,\lambda].$$

> **THE INTERVAL IS HALF-OPEN, AND THE ENDPOINT IS THE HALF THAT MATTERS.** *Read as $(0,\lambda)$ the endpoint is addressed nowhere in [findings/](.) or [ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md), and a whole line of work turned on it.* **[Lemma L129](#lemma-l129-the-spectral-gap-is-half-open-lambda-is-never-an-eigenvalue) proves $\lambda\notin\operatorname{spec}(K)$ in four lines** from the same identity: $\ker L(w)$ is spanned by the indicators of the connected components of $\{w_{ab}>0\}$, each of which $K$ annihilates, so $Kx=\lambda x$ forces $L(w)x=0$, hence $Kx=0$, hence $x=0$.

**That quantity has a standard name, and naming it makes the proof one line.** $K^2-\lambda K$ is exactly the **weighted graph Laplacian** $L(w)$ on $n$ vertices with edge weights $w_{ab}=K_{ab}^2$: the diagonal reads $\sum_bK_{ab}^2$ because $K$ is hollow, and the off-diagonal entry is $-K_{ab}^2$ precisely because the vertex equation holds at $(ab)$. A weighted Laplacian with **nonnegative** weights is positive semidefinite for free, via $v^{\mathsf T}L(w)v=\tfrac12\sum w_{ab}(v_a-v_b)^2$, which is the display above. *Reached independently by a cold reviewer working from the definitions; the identity was already here, and two routes agreeing on it is what that is worth. Confirmed to $10^{-17}$ at $S^3{\times}S^3$, $S^4{\times}S^4$, $S^5{\times}S^5$, $S^3{\times}S^4$, $S^4{\times}S^5$.*

**This also says exactly why the statement dies off the locus.** Off the diagonal locus the vertex equation at $(ab)$ picks up the $W^{\#}$ and $C^{pq}$ terms, so the off-diagonal entry of $K^2-\lambda K$ is no longer $-K_{ab}^2$. The matrix stays symmetric with the same diagonal but its weights are no longer squares, and **nothing keeps them nonnegative**, so the free positivity argument is not weakened off the locus, it is simply unavailable. The table below is what that looks like.

**The scalar Poincaré inequality below is the single instance $v=$ top eigenvector.** The matrix form is strictly more: it constrains the *whole* spectrum of $K$, not one Rayleigh quotient.

**A counting corollary, immediate.** Positive eigenvalues of $K$ are each $\ge\lambda$, and $\sum\kappa^2=\lVert K\rVert_F^2=t\le2$, so their number is

$$r\ \le\ \frac{2}{\lambda^2}\,. $$

**This holds at every even $n\ge6$, not only at $12$**: $2/\theta_0(n)^2=n^2/((n-1)(n-2))<2$ throughout. *And the contrapositive is the useful half*, since $t=2$ exactly on the locus, $r\ge2$ forces $\theta\le1<\theta_0(n)$, an **unconditional reduction of diagonal branch 1 to the $r=1$ case**, needing no trade-off assumption. At $\lambda=\theta_0(12)$ this is $r\le1.309$, forcing **$r=1$: the vertex matrix of a diagonal critical point at the conjectured value has exactly one positive eigenvalue.** Checked across eight products, balanced and unbalanced, both parities, every one has $r=1$ and an empty $(0,\lambda)$ gap, with $2/\lambda^2$ running $1.31$–$1.80$.

#### It does NOT survive off the diagonal locus, and the failure is systematic

*The question was posed and answered in the same cycle, because the falsifier was named first.*

| | padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$, $n=6,8,12$ | $\mathbb{CP}^3$ | $\mathbb{CP}^4$ | $\mathbb{CP}^5$ | $\mathbb{CP}^6$ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| positive eigenvalues of $K$ | $1$ | $2$ | $3$ | $4$ | $5$ |
| how many lie in $(0,\lambda)$ | $1$ | $2$ | $3$ | $4$ | $5$ |

**REFUTED as a statement about non-diagonal criticality.** *Gate 2, reviewer 130.* A rotated sphere product is genuinely critical ($\lVert Q(W)-\theta W\rVert_\infty\le1.1\times10^{-16}$, $\theta$ invariant to nine digits) and off the locus, and the PSD statement **still holds** there, at $S^3\times S^3$ out to $\lVert F\rVert=0.44$ with an empty gap, likewise at $S^2\times S^3$ and $S^6\times S^6$. **The property survives on a neighbourhood of the locus inside the critical set.** What the catalogue tested was something weaker and criticality-free: "every positive eigenvalue in $(0,\lambda)$" is just $\mu_1(K)<\lambda$, and $\mu_1^2\le\operatorname{tr}K^2=t$, with $t(\mathbb{CP}^m)=3/(m+1)$ and $t=1$ at padded $\mathbb{CP}^2$ against $\lambda^2=3/2$, every tabulated point has $t<\lambda^2$, so the outcome was forced with no criticality in it. **The derived necessary condition is $K^2-\lambda K\succeq0\Rightarrow t\ge\lambda^2$. The question of whether it then holds at every critical point with $t\ge\lambda^2$ is ANSWERED, and the answer is NO.** *Gate 2, reviewer 128.* $S^4\times S^4\oplus\mathbb{HP}^2$ at $n=16$ is non-Kähler, critical, and has $t=1.281$ against $\lambda^2=0.639$, comfortably inside the condition, with $\lambda_{\min}(K^2-\lambda K)=-0.1467$. Diluting by $k$ copies of $S^6\times S^6$ drives $t\to2^-$ like $1/k$ with the statement failing throughout, so **no threshold on $t$ strictly below $2$ rescues it**.

*Two further corrections to the evidence base.* The failures are **frame-independent**, with $t^*(\mathbb{CP}^2)=1$ and $t^*(\mathbb{HP}^2)=0.6$; and the "$500$ of $500$ random frames" datum is **not evidence of a dichotomy**, it is decided by $\mu_1\le\sqrt t$ before criticality is consulted at all. Six irreducible non-Kähler witnesses now carry the negative, $\mathbb{HP}^2$, $\mathbb{HP}^3$, $\mathrm{SU}(3)$, $\mathrm{SO}(5)$, $\mathrm{SU}(4)$, $\mathrm{SO}(6)$ bi-invariant, all critical to $10^{-17}$, replacing a Kähler-only table. *Reviewers 127 and 128, independently.* **A direct sum satisfies PSD iff every summand does**, since $c_i=\lambda/\lambda_i$ makes $\mu/\lambda$ scale-invariant, so no direct-sum construction can ever repair a failing block. Below is the original table, which tests $t<\lambda^2$ rather than the statement:, the statement fails as badly as it can. The counting corollary fails with it: $\mathbb{CP}^6$ has $5$ positive eigenvalues against $2/\lambda^2=2.34$. And at padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ it fails in **500 of 500 random frames**, which is [post F29](INDEX.md)'s frame-dependence appearing again in a second guise.

**The failure is confined to one place, and that is an identity rather than an observation.** Write $E:=\big(K^2-\lambda K\big)-L(K_{ab}^2)$ for the deviation from the Laplacian. Then **$E$ is hollow at every $W$ whatsoever**, critical or not, diagonal or not: $K$ is hollow, so $(K^2)_{aa}=\sum_bK_{ab}^2$ and $\lambda K_{aa}=0$, which is the Laplacian diagonal exactly. *Confirmed to $10^{-17}$ at random Weyl tensors, $n=5,6,7$.* So the diagonal of $K^2-\lambda K$ is the same function of $K$ off the locus as on it, and **every bit of the off-locus failure is carried by off-diagonal entries**. *Hollowness needs no criticality and not even a distinguished $\lambda$: it holds for every scalar, since $K_{aa}=0$ kills the $\lambda K$ diagonal outright.*

**$E$ does better than hollow: its ROW SUMS VANISH, and that is forced at every critical point in every frame.** *Gate 2, reviewer 127.* Writing $U:=\lambda K-K^{\circ2}-K^2$ with the diagonal zeroed, $U\mathbf1=0$ is automatic, and

$$v^{\mathsf T}(K^2-\lambda K)v=\tfrac12\sum_{a,b}\big(K_{ab}^2+U_{ab}\big)(v_a-v_b)^2 .$$

**So PSD is exactly the nonnegativity of the effective weights $K_{ab}^2+U_{ab}$**, a statement about the off-diagonal mass of $W$ on $\Lambda^2$, and not about criticality, Kählerness, or the locus. *This is the form the identity should have been recorded in.*

**And the matrix statement is not stronger than the scalar one.** $K^2-\lambda K$ is a **polynomial in $K$**, so it commutes with $K$ and PSD is *equivalent* to the scalar inequality holding at every eigenvector of $K$. *The earlier rider that the matrix form "constrains the whole spectrum, not one Rayleigh quotient" is overstated: it adds only the step from the top eigenvector to all of them.* What arbitrary $v$ genuinely buys is non-spectral, $v=\mathbf1_S$ gives the **cut identity** $\lVert K\mathbf1_S\rVert^2-\lambda\,\mathbf1_S^{\mathsf T}K\mathbf1_S=\sum_{a\in S,\,b\notin S}K_{ab}^2$.

**The off-diagonal entries then have a closed form, and this half DOES use criticality.** At a critical point, reading the vertex equation at $(ab)$ gives $\lambda K_{ab}-K_{ab}^2=\lVert F_{(ab)}\rVert^2+(W^{\#})_{(ab),(ab)}$, hence

$$E_{ab}=\big[(K^2)_{ab}-(W^{\#})_{(ab),(ab)}\big]-\lVert F_{(ab)}\rVert^2,\qquad a\ne b,\quad\text{at a critical point,}$$

where $F$ is the off-diagonal part of $W$ as an operator on $\Lambda^2$ and $\lVert F_{(ab)}\rVert^2$ its row mass, using $(W^2)_{(ab),(ab)}=K_{ab}^2+\lVert F_{(ab)}\rVert^2$, itself exact. On the diagonal locus $F=0$ and $(K^2)_{ab}$ *is* the triangle sum $(W^{\#})_{(ab),(ab)}$, so both brackets vanish together. *This does NOT answer item 1, and the earlier claim here that it did was circular.* Reviewer 129: the vanishing of the bracket is $R_{ab}=0$ at every pair, which **is** the definition of the diagonal locus, and the record already holds that sharper as $t=2$ ([target.md](target.md)). The identity relocates the failure; it does not characterise anything.

**The per-pair equation carries a one-parameter family of contractions, and the vacuous one is its trivial member.** Weighting the $(ab)$ vertex equation $K_{ab}^2+\lVert F_{(ab)}\rVert^2+(W^{\#})_{(ab),(ab)}=\lambda K_{ab}$ by $v_av_b$ and summing gives, **for every $v\in\mathbb R^n$**,

$$\sum_{a<b}v_av_b\Big[K_{ab}^2+\lVert F_{(ab)}\rVert^2+(W^{\#})_{(ab),(ab)}\Big]=\tfrac{\lambda}2\,v^{\mathsf T}Kv .$$

Taking $v=\mathbf 1$ makes the right side vanish, because $K\mathbf1=0$, and recovers $t/2+(1-t/2)-1=0$, the contraction already recorded as carrying no information. *So that vacuity is a property of the single weight $\mathbf1\mathbf1^{\mathsf T}$ rather than of the vertex equation.* Verified at machine precision at $S^3{\times}S^3$, $S^4{\times}S^4$, $S^3{\times}S^4$, $S^2{\times}S^4$ and $S^5{\times}S^5$, all critical to $10^{-15}$. **A cold reviewer derived the same family independently**, in the equivalent form $\lambda(v^{\mathsf T}Kv)=\lVert Kv\rVert^2+\lVert G_{\mathrm{off}}\rVert^2+v^{\mathsf T}\hat Rv+[\cdots]$; two routes agreeing is what that is worth. The [norm budget](target.md) then bounds what any member of the family can deliver.

**The PSD statement is STRICT at sphere products, with a margin, and that is what decides how much frame rotation it tolerates.** A weighted Laplacian's kernel has one dimension per connected component of the graph $\{w_{ab}>0\}$. *The natural guess is that $S^p\times S^q$ has vanishing cross-block weights, making the graph disconnected, $\dim\ker=2$, and the second kernel direction unprotected, and that guess is FALSE.* Connectivity follows from the cross-block entry $c$ being **nonzero**, which it is. *The stronger reading, that the cross-block entries are the largest in $K$, is FALSE at every sphere product, and provably: $\lvert c\rvert/a_1=(p-1)/q$ and $\lvert c\rvert/a_2=(q-1)/p$ cannot both exceed $1$. At $S^2\times S^6$ the cross entry is $0.141$ against a maximum of $0.845$. Gate 2, reviewer 132.*

| | $S^3{\times}S^3$ | $S^3{\times}S^4$ | $S^4{\times}S^4$ | $S^6{\times}S^6$ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| $\max\lvert K_{ab}\rvert$ across blocks | $0.211$ | $0.183$ | $0.164$ | $0.112$ |
| components / $\dim\ker L$ | $1/1$ | $1/1$ | $1/1$ | $1/1$ |
| spectral gap $\varepsilon_1(L)$ | $0.267$ | $0.233$ | $0.214$ | $0.152$ |

So the weight graph is **connected**, the kernel is exactly the constant direction that $K\mathbf1=0$ already forces, and $K^2-\lambda K\succ0$ on $\mathbf1^\perp$ with a strictly positive margin. *That margin is $\varepsilon_1=\min\{pa_1^2+qc^2,\ qa_2^2+pc^2,\ nc^2\}$ in closed form, equal to $\mu^2/n$ exactly when $p-1\le q$ and $q-1\le p$. It does **not** decrease with $n$, the appearance that it did came from sampling only near-balanced products: at fixed $n=8$ it is $0.214$ at $S^4\times S^4$ and $0.0587$ at $S^2\times S^6$. Reviewer 132; the table below is balanced-family data and is labelled as such.*. **A frame rotation preserves PSD while it perturbs $K^2-\lambda K$ by less than $\varepsilon_1$ and breaks it beyond**, which is why bounded rotations and fully random frames give opposite answers at the same point, with no contradiction between them.

**So $K^2-\lambda K\succeq0$ is a property of diagonal CRITICAL points. REFUTED as a property of the diagonal locus.** *Gate 2, reviewer 129, with a witness built by hand.* At $n=8$, take two disjoint copies of the four-vertex weight $A_{12}=A_{34}=2c$, $A_{13}=A_{14}=A_{23}=A_{24}=-c$ at scales $c_1$ and $\delta$. Row sums vanish exactly, $\lVert K\rVert_F^2=2$, and $\theta\to0.866$, but the eigenvalue $4\delta$ sits **inside** $(0,\theta)$: at $\delta=0.001$, $\lambda_{\min}(K^2-\theta K)=-0.0034$ with criticality residual $0.002$. The proof needs the vertex equation at every $(ab)$, which is criticality, and the display above says so. **The stakes are concrete: diagonal branch 1 is an optimisation over exactly these matrices, so the false version licenses adding an invalid inequality to a relaxation.**

**And $K$ itself is a signed-graph Laplacian, with weights $-K_{ab}$.** $K\mathbf1=0$ and $K$ hollow is precisely that statement; the observation is free and the record did not carry it.

#### And that inequality is a Poincaré inequality on a weighted graph

Since $\sum_{ij}M_{ij}^2u_iu_j=\operatorname{tr}(MD_uMD_u)=\operatorname{tr}\big((MD_u)^2\big)$ with $D_u=\mathrm{diag}(u)$, and $\operatorname{tr}((MD_u)^2)=\|MD_u\|_F^2-\tfrac12\|[M,D_u]\|_F^2$ with $\|MD_u\|_F^2=\sum_iu_i^2s_i=2/n$ on the vertex-transitive locus,

$$u^{\mathsf T}M^{\circ2}u=\frac2n-\frac12\sum_{i,j}M_{ij}^2\,(u_i-u_j)^2 ,$$

so **B1 there is exactly**

$$\boxed{\ \sum_{i,j}M_{ij}^2\,(u_i-u_j)^2\ \ge\ \frac{2\mu_1^2}{n}\ }$$

, the Dirichlet energy of the top eigenvector $u$ of $M$, against edge weights $M_{ij}^2$ of total mass $2$ and uniform degree $2/n$. **Equality at both sphere products** ($0.533333333$ at $n=6$, $0.303030303$ at $n=12$, nine digits) and strict at the $C_5$ blow-up ($0.352313$ against $0.152313$).

#### So the diagonal residual has left curvature entirely

: it is a Poincaré inequality for a weighted graph, tested against the top eigenvector of the *signed* adjacency matrix whose entrywise square supplies the weights, with the conjectured maximiser as the equality case.

#### In its tightest form it is a spectral-gap statement

On the vertex-transitive locus $\tfrac n2M^{\circ2}$ is **doubly stochastic**, and since $u\perp\mathbf1$ is the Perron direction, writing $\nu_2$ for its second eigenvalue the residual is

$$\nu_2\Big(\tfrac n2M^{\circ2}\Big)\ \le\ 1-\frac{\mu_1^2}{2}\ =\ \frac12\sum_{i\ge2}\mu_i^2 .$$

Equality at both sphere products, where both sides are $1/(n-1)$, $0.2$ at $n=6$ and $0.090909$ at $n=12$, and strict at the $C_5$ blow-up. **A relation between the spectral gap of a Hadamard square and the spectrum of its signed square root**, with no reference to curvature, dimension parity, or criticality beyond what fixes the constant. Tersest form, since $\operatorname{tr}M^2=2$:

$$\mu_1(M)^2+2\,\nu_2\Big(\tfrac n2M^{\circ2}\Big)\ \le\ \operatorname{tr}M^2 .$$

#### Equality exactly at the sphere products

, $1.600000+0.400000$ at $n=6$ and $1.818182+0.181818$ at $n=12$, both summing to $2.000000$, and strict elsewhere: $1.823046$ at $S^4\times S^8$, $1.745356$ at the $C_5$ blow-up. Note $S^4\times S^8$ is **not** vertex-transitive and satisfies it anyway, so the statement may hold past the locus where it was derived; $\operatorname{tr}P=0$ throughout, since a zero diagonal survives the Hadamard square.

#### But it is not a general matrix fact, criticality is load-bearing

Over $15{,}000$ random symmetric $M$ with zero diagonal, $M\mathbf1=0$ and $\operatorname{tr}M^2=2$, the quantity reaches $3.50$ against the target $2$, with $\mu_1^2=0.95$ and $2\nu_2=2.55$ at the witness. So the linear constraints alone do not give it, and any proof must feed in the criticality equation $M^{\circ2}+M^2=\theta M+\mathrm{diag}(s_i)$ rather than only the conditions defining the locus. That is worth knowing before an attempt, since the inequality reads like a lemma about doubly stochastic matrices and is not one.

#### And feeding criticality in gives a rigidity worth stating on its own

On the vertex-transitive locus $s_i=2/n$, so the characterisation rearranges to

$$M^{\circ2}=\theta M-M^2+\tfrac2nI :$$

**the Hadamard square of $M$ is a quadratic polynomial in $M$.** Verified at $10^{-16}$ at $S^3\times S^3$, $S^6\times S^6$ and the $C_5$ blow-up, with $\|[M,M^{\circ2}]\|\sim10^{-18}$. So $M$ and $M^{\circ2}$ commute and share eigenvectors, and every eigenvalue of $P=\tfrac n2M^{\circ2}$ is

$$\nu(\mu)=1+\tfrac n2\,\mu(\theta-\mu),$$

which reproduces the measured spectra exactly, $(1,\,0.2,\,-0.3,\dots)$ at $n=6$, $(1,\,0.090909,\,-0.109091,\dots)$ at $n=12$, $(1,\,0.491895,\,0.491895,\,0.119217,\dots)$ at the blow-up. The residual then reads, for whichever eigenvalue attains $\nu_2$,

$$n\,\mu(\mu-\theta)\ \ge\ \mu_1^2 .$$

#### And that rigidity is exactly an association scheme

The standard characterisation, a space is the Bose–Mesner algebra of an association scheme iff it contains $I$ and $J$ and is closed under both ordinary and Hadamard multiplication ([Godsil's notes](https://www.math.uwaterloo.ca/~cgodsil/pdfs/assoc2.pdf), taken as a lead and checked here rather than cited from a summary), is satisfied by the algebra $\mathrm{span}\{I,M,\dots,M^d\}$ at the diagonal critical points, and the check separates them:

| point | distinct eigenvalues | $J$ in the algebra | Hadamard-closed | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| $S^3\times S^3$ | 3 | $5\times10^{-15}$ | $7\times10^{-16}$ | **2-class scheme**, strongly regular |
| $S^6\times S^6$ | 3 | $2\times10^{-13}$ | $3\times10^{-15}$ | **2-class scheme**, strongly regular |
| $C_5$ blow-up | 4 | $2\times10^{-13}$ | $2\times10^{-15}$ | **3-class scheme** |
| $S^4\times S^8$ | 4 | $3\times10^{-13}$ | $\mathbf{0.18}$ | **not a scheme** |

The unbalanced product fails Hadamard closure outright, and it is the one point of the four that is not vertex-transitive, which is where the rigidity was derived. **So on the vertex-transitive locus a diagonal critical point carries an association scheme, and the conjectured maximiser is the $2$-class case.** That places B1 in a classified world: two-class schemes are strongly regular graphs, **and on that locus B1 is now settled by enumeration.**

#### Lemma L96, REFUTED as stated: at even $n$ the weighted 2-class scheme maximises at $\theta_0(n)$, and not only at $K_{n/2,n/2}$

*Gate 2, [r661](../reviews/REVIEW_r661.md) and [r662](../reviews/REVIEW_r662.md) on identical briefs, **both REFUTED, agreeing on every count**.*

> **THE PARITY QUALIFIER WAS LOST BY THE NUMBERING COMMIT.** The body landed 2026-08-23 saying *"at every even $n$ from 6 to 24"*; **the heading was placed 2026-09-02 by a commit whose job was numbering, and it dropped the restriction.** At odd $n$ the claim fails three ways: $K_{n/2,n/2}$ does not exist, the odd balanced split is not regular so it is **not in the family at all**, and $\theta_0(n)>\Theta_n$ there, *so the heading asserts a maximum above what the locus attains, the same failure [L0](problem.md) was refuted for.* **At $n=9$ the true family maximum is $0.7698$ against $\theta_0(9)=1.1759$.**

> **THE ATTAINING SET IS LARGER THAN "ONLY AT", AND THE RECORD HOLDS THE WITNESS.** $\theta$ is complement-invariant, so **$2K_{n/2}$ attains $\theta_0$ at every even $n$**, $\mathrm{SRG}(6,2,1,0)$ at $n=6$, and $(12,5,4,0)$ screening to $1.236033$ with integral multiplicities. *[L74](diagonal-half.md), in this same file, states both members correctly and calls them "complements, hence one point up to sign"; L96 contradicts a point L74 had already audited, and names no "up to" group of its own.*

> **THE "SUPERSET, SO A FORTIORI" STEP IS FALSE.** $(k,k-1,0)$ lies in the stated superset for every $k$ and gives $\theta=\sqrt n\,\theta_0(n)$ at $k=n-2$, witnesses $(6,3,2,0)\to1.581139$ and $(24,22,21,0)\to6.493587$. **The hypothesis that actually works is multiplicity integrality $f,g\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}$, and it is never named.**

> **THE CLOSED FORM IS ALREADY ON THE RECORD, 130 LINES BELOW, IN THIS FILE.** Both reviewers derived $\theta(n,k,\lambda,\mu)=\lvert1+\lambda-\mu\rvert\sqrt{2(n-1)/(n\,k\,(n-1-k))}$ cold; it reproduces the block's two runner-up numbers exactly, returns $\theta_0(n)$ at the balanced point, and **is [L144](diagonal-half.md)'s formula verbatim.** *Under the block's own positive root, $K_{n/2,n/2}$ returns $-\theta_0$, as written, its named maximiser is the minimiser.*

> **THERE IS NO PROOF AND NO SCRIPT**, only a finite check at $6\le n\le24$, which is evidence rather than a theorem, while the word the block carries is *settled*. **The block is subsumed by [Lemma L57](problem.md)**, which proves the bound over the whole diagonal locus at every $n$, with constant $\Theta_n$, proved, and the rigidity group named.

*Nothing consumes L96; its blast radius is the next unnumbered paragraph, which declares a branch closed on its strength. It has no canon entry, and no heading check would catch that [L74](diagonal-half.md) and [L144](diagonal-half.md) already carry it, **L96 is the only wrong copy of the three.***

**The 2-class case, in full**

For a weighted $2$-class scheme, $M=\alpha A+\beta(J-I-A)$ with $A$ an $\mathrm{SRG}(n,k,\lambda,\mu)$; the row-sum and norm conditions fix $\alpha,\beta$ from $(n,k)$, and $A$'s eigenvalues $k,r,s$ with multiplicities $1,f,g$ give $M$'s as $0,\ \alpha r-\beta(1+r),\ \alpha s-\beta(1+s)$. So

$$\theta=\tfrac12\Big[n\big(k\alpha^3+(n-1-k)\beta^3\big)+f\mu_r^3+g\mu_s^3\Big]$$

is an explicit function of the parameters. **Maximising over every feasible parameter set gives exactly $\theta_0(n)$, attained only at $(k,\lambda,\mu)=(n/2,0,n/2)$, the complete bipartite $K_{n/2,n/2}$, which is the balanced two-block sphere product**, at every even $n$ from $6$ to $24$, matching $\theta_0$ to nine digits each time.

The enumeration runs over parameter sets satisfying integrality and $k(k-\lambda-1)=(n-k-1)\mu$, a **superset** of the realisable strongly regular graphs, so the bound holds a fortiori. The runners-up are the complete multipartite schemes and they reproduce the recorded multi-block values exactly: $0.829156$ at $(8,4,8)=K_{4,4,4}$ is $(S^4)^3$, and $0.638285$ at $(9,6,9)=K_{3,3,3,3}$ is $(S^3)^4$.

#### And the two halves of B1 now meet

The spectral cap $T_{\max}$ is attained only by the configuration "one large positive, $n-2$ equal negatives, one forced zero", **exactly three distinct eigenvalues**, which under the rigidity is precisely a $2$-class scheme. So:

* if $M$ attains the spectral cap, it is a $2$-class scheme and the enumeration above gives $\theta\le\theta_0$ with equality only at $K_{n/2,n/2}$, **closed**;
* otherwise $T<T_{\max}$ strictly, and what is owed is the trade-off $E-E_0\le T_{\max}-T$ with a *strict* spectral deficit to spend.

**What is left of B1** is therefore that second branch, $d\ge3$ classes, where the $C_5$ blow-up sits at $0.6708$, far below, together with the non-vertex-transitive points, where the rigidity fails outright ($S^4\times S^8$ misses Hadamard closure by $0.18$).

#### On the diagonal locus the two squares swap roles, which explains fence post F11

Writing $M^{\circ2}$ for the entrywise square,

$$\operatorname{tr}W^3=\tfrac12\langle M^{\circ2},M\rangle,\qquad \langle W,W^\#\rangle=\tfrac12\langle M^2,M\rangle,\qquad 2\theta=\langle M^{\circ2}+M^2,\ M\rangle,$$

exact at every test point. **The matrix square of $W$ becomes the *entrywise* square of $M$, and the sharp square becomes the *matrix* square**, the operations trade places. Fence post F1 *(cited here as F11 until 2026-09-03; no F11 exists, [r408](../reviews/REVIEW_r408.md))* recorded that $\langle W,W^\#\rangle$ carries almost all of $\theta$ where $\theta$ is large ($1.2136$ against $0.0225$ at $S^6\times S^6$) and treated it as a measurement; the swap is the reason. For a matrix whose weight is spread over many entries, $\langle M^2,M\rangle$ dwarfs $\langle M^{\circ2},M\rangle$, because the entrywise square of a spread matrix is tiny while its matrix square is not.

#### The case split survives as well

Once the negative graph is triangle-free, every triangle of $K_n$ carries at most two negative edges, so the triangle sum decomposes by that count: zero or two negatives contribute positively, one contributes negatively. ([b_diagonal_functional.py](../scripts/route-b/b_diagonal_functional.py))

#### Lemma L55, min-entry bound, sharp for even $n$

*Verified `HYPOTHESIS-DEBT`, repaired, [ROUND_L55](../verification/ROUND_L55.md); inequality, attainment and uniqueness machine-checked in [L55Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L55Statement.lean). [L93](bispectral.md) is this result under a withdrawn second number.*

**Statement.** Let $n\ge3$ and let $K$ be a real $n\times n$ matrix, hollow with zero row sums, and $t=\lVert K\rVert_F^2=\sum_{a,b}K_{ab}^2$ over ordered pairs. Then
\[
\Big|\min_{a\ne b}K_{ab}\Big|\;\ge\;\sqrt{\frac t{n(n-1)(n-2)}} .
\]
If moreover $K$ is symmetric and $t>0$, equality holds exactly when $K$ is a perfect-matching matrix, $K_{a\sigma(a)}=(n-2)c$ and $K_{ab}=-c$ for $b\notin\{a,\sigma(a)\}$, with $\sigma$ a fixed-point-free involution and $c>0$; such $K$ exist exactly at even $n$. *At $t=0$, $K=0$ attains equality at every $n$.*

**Proof.** *The inequality.* Let $m=\lvert\min_{a\ne b}K_{ab}\rvert$; the row sums vanish, so the minimum is at most $0$ and $-m$ bounds every off-diagonal entry. In a row the $n-1$ off-diagonal entries sum to $0$ and are each $\ge-m$, so each is $\le(n-2)m$, and every such entry $x$ satisfies $(x+m)\big((n-2)m-x\big)\ge0$, that is $x^2\le(n-3)mx+(n-2)m^2$. Summing over the row and using its zero sum gives $\sum_{b\ne a}K_{ab}^2\le(n-1)(n-2)m^2$, and summing over rows gives $t\le n(n-1)(n-2)m^2$. *Symmetry is not used.*

*Attainment.* The matching matrix with $c>0$ has minimum $-c$ and $t=n\big((n-2)^2+(n-2)\big)c^2=n(n-1)(n-2)c^2$. *At $c<0$ its minimum is $(n-2)c$ and the bound is missed by the factor $n-2$.*

*Uniqueness.* At equality every row bound and every pointwise bound is an equality, so each off-diagonal entry is $-m$ or $(n-2)m$. If $t>0$ then $m>0$, and in each row $\sum_{b\ne a}(K_{ab}+m)=(n-1)m$ with each term $0$ or $(n-1)m$, so exactly one entry of the row is $(n-2)m$; call its column $\sigma(a)\ne a$. **Symmetry** gives $K_{\sigma(a)a}=(n-2)m$, so the large entry of row $\sigma(a)$ is in column $a$, $\sigma(\sigma(a))=a$, and $\sigma$ is a fixed-point-free involution, which exists only at even $n$. $\square$ *Without symmetry the pairing need not be an involution: at $n=3$ the cyclic matrix with $K_{a,a+1}=m$, $K_{a,a+2}=-m$ is hollow with zero row sums and attains equality.*

#### The strongly regular graph solutions, reduced to one scalar condition

**[DGK 2023](../references/deninger-grundhoefer-kramer-2023-srg-quadratic/notes.md)'s equation and this file's diagonal criticality are the same, with no conversion factor.** Their conditions on a hollow symmetric $S$ are $\sum_kS_{ik}=0$. Ricci-flatness, and $\sum_kS_{ik}S_{kj}+S_{ij}^2=\theta S_{ij}$, which is $(M\circ M)_{ij}+(M^2)_{ij}=\theta M_{ij}$ verbatim. **What differs is normalisation**: DGK impose none and this record works at $\lVert W\rVert=1$, so *no $\theta$ from that paper is quotable here* and the usable object is the scale-free $\theta/\lVert W\rVert$.

**The ansatz is forced.** A two-eigenvalue hollow zero-row-sum solution on a strongly regular $\Gamma$ with parameters $(v,k,\lambda,\mu)$ is constant on edges and on non-edges, so $M=aA+b(J-I-A)$; zero row sums give $b=-ak/(v-1-k)$. Writing $t:=k/(v-1-k)$ and $N:=(1+t)A-t(J-I)$, we have $M=aN$ and

$$\lVert W\rVert^2=\sum_{i<j}M_{ij}^2=\frac{a^2vk(v-1)}{2(v-1-k)},\qquad\text{so}\qquad a^2=\frac{2(v-1-k)}{vk(v-1)}\ \text{ at unit norm.}$$

**Expanding $N^2$ on $\{I,A,J\}$** with $A^2=kI+\lambda A+\mu(J-I-A)$, $AJ=kJ$ and $(J-I)^2=(v-2)J+I$:

$$N^2=\big[(1+t)^2(\lambda-\mu)+2t(1+t)\big]A+\big[(1+t)^2(k-\mu)+t^2\big]I+\big[(1+t)^2\mu-2t(1+t)k+t^2(v-2)\big]J.$$

**Criticality then reads $\theta=aF_{ij}$ with $F_{ij}=\big[(N^2)_{ij}+N_{ij}^2\big]/N_{ij}$, and it requires $F$ to take the *same* value on edges and on non-edges**, one scalar equation in $(v,k,\lambda,\mu)$, which is DGK's admissibility condition expressed in this record's terms. With it, $\theta/\lVert W\rVert=F\sqrt{2(v-1-k)/(vk(v-1))}$ in closed form. *This is the object [I26](../plan.md) needs, and the equation is the next step.*

#### Lemma L74, $\theta$ in closed form for every strongly regular graph

> **THE SIGN OF $\theta$ AT A STRONGLY REGULAR GRAPH IS FIXED TWICE HERE, INCOMPATIBLY.** *This block's display carries $F$ signed, giving $\theta=-0.645497$ at the octahedron $\mathrm{SRG}(6,4,2,4)$ where $F=-5$; [Observation L144](#observation-l144-no-dgk-point-lies-in-the-band-except-the-balanced-product)'s carries $\lvert1+\lambda-\mu\rvert$ and gives $+0.645497$ at the same graph.* **Neither names the branch it is on.** *The condition that reconciles them is $\operatorname{sign}(a)=\operatorname{sign}(1+\lambda-\mu)$, since $\theta=aF$ and $F=(1+\lambda-\mu)(v-1)/w$ with $v-1,w>0$*, **so the sign of the scale is PARAMETER-DEPENDENT, which is why no fixed convention on it exists to be found.** *A $\theta>0$ convention is asserted in one prose aside of this file and is absent from [conventions.md](conventions.md), whose job it is.* **Both Lean files transcribe the split faithfully**: [L74Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L74Statement.lean)'s value is signed, [L96Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L96Statement.lean)'s carries an absolute value, and only the squares are reconciled.

**The admissibility condition is the graph identity itself.** Reducing $F_{\text{edge}}=F_{\text{non-edge}}$ with $w:=v-1-k$ gives $k(\lambda-\mu)+(v-1)\mu-k^2+k=0$, i.e.

$$k(k-\lambda-1)=(v-k-1)\mu,$$

**the standard strongly regular graph identity**, so it holds automatically and *every* strongly regular graph yields a critical point of the diagonal locus, with no further constraint. This is [DGK](../references/deninger-grundhoefer-kramer-2023-srg-quadratic/notes.md)'s one-to-one correspondence, re-derived in this record's own normalisation.

> **LEMMA L74.** *Let $\Gamma$ be strongly regular with parameters $(v,k,\lambda,\mu)$ and let $W$ be the unit-norm diagonal Weyl tensor it determines. Then $n=v$ and*
> $$\frac{\theta}{\lVert W\rVert}=\frac{(v-1)^2\lambda+2k(v-1)-k^2v+w^2}{w^2}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{2w}{vk(v-1)}},\qquad w=v-1-k.$$

**Gate 1(a), against a value already on the record.** The octahedron $K_{2,2,2}=\mathrm{SRG}(6,4,2,4)$ has $w=1$, $F=50+40-96+1=-5$ and $a=1/\sqrt{60}$, giving $\lvert\theta\rvert/\lVert W\rVert=5/\sqrt{60}=0.645497$, **exactly this record's catalogued $n=6$ maximal torus**. *Classified as agreement rather than discovery*: an independent route reaching a recorded point, with the sign being the $M\to-M$ convention. *The pentagon $C_5=\mathrm{SRG}(5,2,0,1)$ gives $F=0$ and its solution degenerates to $\theta=0$, and that is one member of an infinite subfamily: **every conference graph** has $k=(v-1)/2$, $\lambda=(v-5)/4$, $\mu=(v-1)/4$, which makes the numerator $(v-1)^2\cdot0$, so $\theta=0$ exactly. **The Petersen graph $\mathrm{SRG}(10,3,0,1)$ also has $\theta=0$ and is not a conference graph**, those need $2k=v-1$ and $2\cdot3\ne9$, so its vanishing is a separate solution of $F=0$.* [Reviewer 340](../reviews/REVIEW_r340.md).

> **AND $\mu=3$ WAS WRONG, THOUGH THE VALUE SURVIVED IT.** Two non-adjacent vertices of the octahedron are antipodal and share all four others, so $\mu=4$, and $4(4-2-1)=4=(6-4-1)\cdot4$ satisfies the graph identity, which $\mu=3$ does not. **The reason it went unnoticed is a live hazard: $\mu$ cancels out of $F_{\text{edge}}$**, so an inadmissible parameter set validates against that face alone while $F_{\text{non-edge}}$ disagrees ($-5$ against $+5/4$ here). *Any screen quoting $F_{\text{edge}}$ must check both faces.* [Reviewer 339](../reviews/REVIEW_r339.md). *The identification with the maximal torus is structural rather than a coincidence of $\theta$: on the $a<0$ branch $M$ carries $+4/\sqrt{60}$ on the three antipodal pairs and $-1/\sqrt{60}$ on the twelve edges, matching $\mathfrak{so}(2)^3\subset\mathfrak{so}(6)$ in eigenvalues, multiplicities **and** eigenvectors.*

**The vertex count is the dimension**, so this family populates the diagonal locus at every $n$ admitting a strongly regular graph, an infinite supply of exact critical points against which the record's census-built diagonal ceilings can be checked.

**The family's saturating members are exactly the balanced sphere products, and there is one of them at each even $n$.** The solution is diagonal, so its $\Lambda^2$ eigenvalues *are* the matrix entries, two of them, $a$ on edges and $-at$ on non-edges with $t=k/w$. Criticality is **odd** in $M$, so $\pm M$ are both critical with $\theta\mapsto-\theta$, and the ratio takes both values across the two branches:

$$\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lvert\lambda_{\min}\rvert}\;=\;\frac{w}{k}\quad(a>0),\qquad \frac{k}{w}\quad(a<0),\qquad w=v-1-k .$$

**Saturation of [Lemma L70](bounds.md) requires this to equal $n/(n-2)=v/(v-2)$**, the spectral half of [r323](../reviews/REVIEW_r323.md)'s characterisation, and each branch gives one linear condition:

$$\frac{k}{w}=\frac{v}{v-2}\iff 2k(v-1)=v(v-1)\iff k=\frac v2,\qquad \frac wk=\frac v{v-2}\iff k=\frac{v-2}2 .$$

**Exactly two parameter families satisfy the graph identity under those**: $\mathrm{SRG}(2m,m,0,m)=K_{m,m}$ and $\mathrm{SRG}(2m,m-1,m-2,0)=2K_m$, which are complements of each other and are the two sign branches of **one point**. There $\lambda_{\min}+(n-2)\lambda_{\max}=\sqrt{(2m-1)(m-1)}/m=\theta_0(2m)$, which the closed form returns as $\theta$, so the saturating member **is** the balanced sphere product $S^m\times S^m$, its complete bipartite graph being the cross-factor pairs and its non-edges the within-factor ones.
