> **SO THIS CONFIRMS [LEMMA L71](bounds.md) RATHER THAN TESTING IT, AND THE EARLIER READING WAS BACKWARDS.** *[Reviewer 339](../reviews/REVIEW_r339.md) and [Reviewer 340](../reviews/REVIEW_r340.md), independently, on identical briefs, reaching the same two parameter families.* This block previously read *no member saturates for $k\ge2$*, from three errors compounding: the saturation criterion was taken as $n-2$, which is [Lemma L73](bounds.md)'s **bound** and equals $n/(n-2)$ only at $n=4$; only the $a>0$ branch was considered, on which $\theta<0$ at every interesting member; and the resulting statement was **vacuous**, a conditional with no satisfied antecedent is not corroboration. **The repaired statement is the stronger one**: an infinite family of two-eigenvalue critical points, mostly *not* sphere products ([DGK](../references/deninger-grundhoefer-kramer-2023-srg-quadratic/notes.md), whenever $\Gamma$ and its complement are both connected), contains a saturating member at every even $n$ and it is the balanced sphere product every time, with nothing else saturating. *That is what Lemma L71 asserts, from a family its proof never saw.*

#### Lemma L129, the spectral gap is half-open: $\lambda$ is never an eigenvalue

*Contributed at Gate 2 by [r388](../reviews/REVIEW_r388.md), with [r387](../reviews/REVIEW_r387.md) reaching the same conclusion from the sphere-product spectra. **Hypotheses: $K$ hollow, symmetric, $K\mathbf1=0$, off-diagonal criticality, $\lambda>0$, no maximality, no band, no normalisation, no connectivity.***

**Statement.** At every pure critical point, $\operatorname{spec}(K)\cap(0,\lambda]=\varnothing$.

**Proof.** Off the diagonal criticality reads $(K^2-\lambda K)_{ab}=-K_{ab}^2$, and on the diagonal hollowness gives $(K^2-\lambda K)_{aa}=\sum_bK_{ab}^2$, so
$$K^2-\lambda K=L(w),\qquad w_{ab}=K_{ab}^2\ge0,$$
the weighted graph Laplacian, [Lemma L97](#lemma-l97-the-vertex-laplacian-is-psd-at-a-diagonal-critical-point-k2-lambda-ksucceq0)'s identity. $\ker L(w)$ is spanned by the indicators $\mathbf1_{C_t}$ of the connected components of $\{w_{ab}>0\}$, and **$K$ annihilates each of them**: for $a\in C_t$, $(K\mathbf1_{C_t})_a=\sum_{b\in C_t}K_{ab}=\sum_bK_{ab}=0$, since $K_{ab}=0$ off the component; for $a\notin C_t$ every term vanishes already. Now $Kx=\lambda x$ gives $L(w)x=K^2x-\lambda Kx=0$, so $x\in\ker L(w)$, so $Kx=0$, so $\lambda x=0$ and $x=0$. $\square$

**Two corollaries, both used elsewhere.** **(i) $\lambda_{\max}(K)>\lambda$ strictly at every pure critical point**, since $\operatorname{tr}K=0$ with $K\ne0$ forces $\lambda_{\max}>0$ and the half-open gap then forbids $\lambda_{\max}\le\lambda$ (machine-checked: `L129_cor_i` in [L129Cor.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L129Cor.lean)). **(ii) [Post F48](second-order.md)'s surviving half is a theorem**: at a padded pair $\widetilde N$ is itself a pure critical point at the same $\lambda$, so $\lambda\notin\operatorname{spec}(\widetilde N)$ at all, and the separation clause holds there, at *every* pure critical point, not at one witness. *[r388](../reviews/REVIEW_r388.md) gives a second, independent proof of (ii) through the Dirichlet identity of Constraint 2.*

#### Lemma L130, the four-set ratio in closed form, and the entry cap that closes the spread conversion

*Two independent cold derivations agreeing in every particular: [r401](../reviews/REVIEW_r401.md) and [r402](../reviews/REVIEW_r402.md), each reaching both statements without seeing the other. **Gate 2 passed on arrival.***

**(i) The ratio.** At every $(3,1)$ four-subset of $S^p\times S^q$,
$$\frac{3m_4-\tfrac12\sigma^2}{\theta^2}=\frac9{2(p-1)^2},$$
and the band forces $p\ge5$, so **the supremum over the two-factor family is exactly $9/32$**, attained for every $q\ge7$. *This upgrades [r400](../reviews/REVIEW_r400.md)'s screened $0.28125$ to a closed form and makes its $q\le200$ cutoff irrelevant. Scope: non-decay across the two-factor family, every point the record can evaluate, not across the class [I18](../plan.md)'s clause quantifies over.*

**(ii) The entry cap, on [L68](#lemma-l68-the-symmetry-defect-on-the-diagonal-locus-and-the-entry-cap-criticality-forces)'s hypotheses, criticality, symmetry, hollowness and $M\mathbf1=0$**, the last being what makes the off-diagonal criticality equation the one used here. Hollowness gives $\lVert M_a-M_b\rVert^2\ge2M_{ab}^2$, so with criticality
$$M_{ab}\le\frac{s_a+s_b}{2\theta}\qquad\text{at every pure critical point,}$$
which is [Lemma L68](#lemma-l68-the-symmetry-defect-on-the-diagonal-locus-and-the-entry-cap-criticality-forces)'s $g_{ab}\ge0$ with no hypothesis attached. **And criticality is load-bearing for the cap by an identity rather than by a sweep**: $2\theta M_{ab}-(s_a+s_b)=2\rho_{ab}-\tfrac12g_{ab}$ with $\rho$ the per-entry criticality residual, whose only constraint is $\sum M_{ab}\rho_{ab}=0$, so the cap fails **exactly** when $\rho_{ab}>\tfrac14g_{ab}$. *Contributed at Gate 2 by [r520](../reviews/REVIEW_r520.md), and it retires the search the block cited for the same point.* **Consequently $p_a:=M_{aj}+M_{ak}\le\dfrac{2s_a+s_j+s_k}{2\theta}\le\dfrac3{2\theta}\le\theta$ in the band, the last step being exactly $\theta^2\ge\tfrac32$**, with equality forcing $M$ to be a star and hence zero. Quantitatively $\theta-p_a\ge(1-s_a)/(2\theta)$.

**What (ii) settles.** The denominator of the entry-spread conversion $\lVert\tilde o|_I\rVert_\infty\le\sqrt{\max s_a}\,\lVert\tilde o\rVert/\min_a\lvert\theta-p_a\rvert$ **cannot vanish or be small**, so the conversion's price is bounded, and the count of indices with $p_a=\theta$ is **zero**, not "at most two". *The estimate is exactly tight on the two-factor family, since within-factor pairs are twin pairs.*

**And (i) settles what an $m_4$-only criterion can be.** Any such sufficient condition must imply $m_4<\theta^2/3$, since in-band $\sigma=0$ four-sets exist ($S^6\times S^7$, and the $(2,2)$ family at $q(q-1)+p(p-1)=4(p-1)(q-1)$); and that fails in band at $S^5\times S^{66}$, where $\sup m_4/\theta^2=3/8$. **So no reduction of the four-set clause to $m_4$ alone can work**, a theorem, not a caution.

#### Observation L144, no DGK point lies in the band except the balanced product

*Gate 2, [r675](../reviews/REVIEW_r675.md) and [r676](../reviews/REVIEW_r676.md) on identical briefs, **both GAP, and the gap runs in the block's favour**.*

> **THE OUTLINED DERIVATION IS UNAUDITED, AND THE TYPE STAYS `Observation` UNTIL AN AUDIT MOVES IT.** *The kernel-checked proof in the box below is independent of it: it uses neither $D>A$ nor complement invariance.* *[r810](../reviews/REVIEW_r810.md) and [r811](../reviews/REVIEW_r811.md) both REFUTED a promotion to `Lemma`, r810 on the rename reaching two files out of three and r811 on the derivations being unaudited; [r812](../reviews/REVIEW_r812.md) and [r813](../reviews/REVIEW_r813.md) then found the revert itself incomplete.* **What the derivation says:** In eigenvalue coordinates $\theta^2=2f^2\mu(\mu+B)/(ABCD)$, and using $D>A$, the AM–GM $A^2\ge4\mu r(S+1)$ and complement-invariance to assume $r\ge S$, the bound reduces to one inequality **affine in $\mu$** whose two coefficients both hold. **So $\theta<\tfrac12$ for every primitive strongly regular graph, at every $n$, with no census**, conference graphs giving $f=0$ exactly, and $\tfrac14$ the **sharp, never-attained** supremum, which is what the screened $0.4758$ was approaching. This derivation is [r676](../reviews/REVIEW_r676.md)'s own and has had no independent reader, and a reviewer's derivation entering the record as a verdict is the thing Gate 2 exists to catch. *The type is the audit's to move, on the kernel-checked proof or on this one.*

> **THE PARABOLA ROUTE ALONE FAILS, AND INTEGRALITY IS WHAT CARRIES IT.** $\theta^2$ **is** convex in $\mu$ at fixed $(n,k)$, but at $(100,50,1)$, admissible, with $\lambda=48.02\ge0$, it returns $\theta^2=1.8636>\tfrac32$. *So the endpoint argument is not the proof, and multiplicity integrality is the load-bearing hypothesis.*

> **THE CLOSED FORM IS CONFIRMED COLD, ABSOLUTE VALUE INCLUDED. AND THAT IS THE REPAIR [L96](diagonal-half.md) FAILED TO MAKE.** The positive root returns $-\theta_0$ at the balanced product. **Every strongly regular graph is a diagonal critical point**, and it is full-sphere criticality; the constant equation **is** the strongly-regular identity exactly, needing the unstated $0<k<v-1$.

> **THE IMPRIMITIVE HALF IS MISSING THE TWO QUALIFIERS THAT REFUTED [L96](diagonal-half.md). AND THEY ARE WRITTEN 150 LINES ABOVE, INSIDE L96'S OWN REFUTATION.** $\theta$ is **strictly increasing** in $g$ with no stationary point, and $g$ runs over divisors of $n$ with $g\le n/2$, so *"maximised uniquely at $g=n/2$"* needs **$n$ even** and holds only **up to complementation**. *The conclusion is unharmed: every $g\le n/3$ gives $\theta^2\le(n-1)(n-3)/n^2<1$, and the uniform bound one reviewer supplies covers the rest.*

> **"THE BAND" CARRIES TWO INEQUIVALENT SENSES ON THE RECORD**, and only one makes the headline non-vacuous, it is tabled in neither [conventions.md](conventions.md) nor [INDEX-traps.md](INDEX-traps.md).

*Provenance false: the same four claims sat in [routes.md](../routes.md) sixteen hours before the migration commit claimed this was the record's only copy, **and that copy is still live, stating the primitive bound with no hedge at all**, the exact overstatement this block exists to prevent. A **fourth, wrong copy** of the formula stood in [problem.md](problem.md) with the unnormalised $\theta=\lambda-\mu+1$; it is now corrected.*

*Migrated from [I18](../plan.md)(a) on 2026-09-03.

**The normalisation is pinned from criticality alone**, so no transcription of the DGK conventions is needed: the constant equation *is* the strongly-regular identity, hence every strongly regular graph is a diagonal critical point ([Lemma L74](#lemma-l74-theta-in-closed-form-for-every-strongly-regular-graph) proves this and gives the closed form in $(v,k,\lambda,\mu)$). In the parameters used here,

$$\theta(\Gamma)=\lvert1+\lambda-\mu\rvert\sqrt{\frac{2(n-1)}{nk(n-1-k)}} .$$

> **THE IMPRIMITIVE HALF, PROVED.** Imprimitive strongly regular graphs give $\theta=\sqrt{2(n-1)(g-1)/\big(n(n-g)\big)}$, **maximised uniquely at $g=n/2$**, which is the balanced product.

> **THE PRIMITIVE HALF, PROVED.** Primitive strongly regular graphs satisfy $\theta<\tfrac12$ at every $n$, kernel-checked as `primitive_thetaSq_lt_quarter` in [L144Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L144Statement.lean). The trace and multiplicity identities give $(f-g)(r-s)=-2k-(v-1)(\lambda-\mu)$. At $f=g$, $(v-1)(\mu-\lambda)=2k$ with $0<k<v-1$ makes the integer $\mu-\lambda$ equal to $1$, so $\theta=0$, the conference case. At $f\ne g$, $r$ and $s$ are rational roots of the monic $x^2-(\lambda-\mu)x-(k-\mu)$, hence integers, with $r\ge1$ and $s\le-2$ (at $s=-1$ the counting identity forces $\mu=0$), and with $B=-s-1$ the AM–GM $k^2\ge4\mu r(B+1)$ and $2(r-B)^2\le rB(r+1)(B+1)$ close it. *Multiplicity integrality is used exactly once, to make $r,s$ integers off the conference case. The screen to $n\le200$, maximum $0.4758$, and the non-sphere supremum $0.7059\,\Theta_v$ over $v\le300$ are consistent with it.*

**What it discharges.** With both halves proved, no DGK point lies in the band except the balanced product, so the falsifier [I18](../plan.md)(a) existed to supply **cannot exist**, and [R15](../routes.md)'s first owed step is discharged.

#### Lemma L176, the coordinate-diagonal locus has minimum degree two, so its $\Lambda^2$-rank is at least four

**Statement.** Let $W\ne0$ be a Weyl tensor coordinate-diagonal in some **orthonormal** frame $\{f_a\}$, *orthonormality is used and both audits found it unstated: it is what makes $K$ well defined and the $\Lambda^2$ action orthogonal*, with sectional matrix $K_{ab}=W_{abab}$, and let $G$ be the graph on $\{1,\dots,n\}$ whose edges are the pairs with $K_{ab}\ne0$. Then **every vertex of $G$ has degree $0$ or degree $\ge2$**, and $G$ has **at least four edges**, **in particular** $\operatorname{rank}(W:\Lambda^2\to\Lambda^2)\ge4$. *Only the edge bound is equivalent to the rank bound; the degree statement is strictly more than it.*

**Proof.** On the coordinate-diagonal locus $W$ acts on $\Lambda^2$ by $W(f_a\wedge f_b)=K_{ab}f_a\wedge f_b$, so its eigenvalues are the $K_{ab}$ and its rank is $\lvert E(G)\rvert$. The locus is *hollow symmetric with vanishing row sums*. Ricci-flatness of a Weyl tensor reads $\sum_bK_{ab}=0$ for each $a$ there, which is the whole of what the proof uses.

*Degree.* A vertex $a$ of degree $1$ with sole neighbour $b$ has $0=\sum_cK_{ac}=K_{ab}$, contradicting $\{a,b\}\in E$. So no degree is $1$.

*Edge count.* $W\ne0$ gives $\lvert E\rvert\ge1$. At $\lvert E\rvert\le2$ the degree sum is at most $4$, so at most two vertices carry support and a simple graph on two vertices has one edge, whose endpoints have degree $1$. At $\lvert E\rvert=3$ the degree sum is $6$ with every support degree $\ge2$, forcing **exactly three vertices of degree exactly two**, so $G=K_3$ on some $\{a,b,c\}$. Its three row sums are $K_{ab}+K_{ac}=0$, $K_{ab}+K_{bc}=0$, $K_{ac}+K_{bc}=0$; the first two give $K_{ac}=K_{bc}=-K_{ab}$ and the third then gives $K_{ab}=0$, so $W=0$. Hence $\lvert E\rvert\ge4$. $\square$

**The matrix theorem this lemma reduces to is machine-checked in full.** [L176Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L176Statement.lean) carries the sectional matrix as a structure, hollow, symmetric, vanishing row sums, over $\mathbb R$ at general $n$, and `four_le_card_edges` is $K\ne0\Rightarrow4\le\lvert E\rvert$ with no `sorry`. **The two bridges to geometry are proved for the coordinate frame** in [L176Op.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L176Op.lean): the coordinate-diagonal operator $W_K$ has $W_K(f_a\wedge f_b)=K_{ab}f_a\wedge f_b$ and is Weyl exactly when $K$ has zero row sums, and $\operatorname{rank}(W_K:\Lambda^2\to\Lambda^2)=\lvert E\rvert$, so `L176_op` is the lemma in that frame, and `L176_someFrame` in [FrameTransfer.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/FrameTransfer.lean) carries it to every orthonormal frame. *The file names no Weyl tensor, no metric and no $\Lambda^2$, so what it settles is the combinatorics the lemma rests on rather than the lemma.* **Its structure is a superset of the locus**, which makes its theorem the stronger of the two. *The three-edge case is reached there by edge membership rather than by the degree sum above: an edge's two endpoints each carry a second edge; those two are distinct because the endpoints of one are the first edge's lower vertex and a third vertex, neither of them the upper one; and that third vertex needs a second edge, which can only be the remaining one, closing the triangle.* **The two routes agree on the triangle and differ on how they reach it.**

> **THE BOUND IS SHARP, AND THE WITNESS IS A FOUR-CYCLE.** *At any $n\ge4$, put $K$ on the cycle $12,23,34,41$ with values $c,-c,c,-c$ and zero elsewhere.* **Every row sum cancels in pairs**, vertex $1$ reads $K_{12}+K_{14}=c-c=0$ and the other three read the same way, so this is a coordinate-diagonal Weyl tensor of $\Lambda^2$-rank exactly four. *The odd cycles are the ones the row sums kill: an odd closed walk forces the alternating sign pattern to contradict itself, which is the triangle case of the proof at greater length.*

**Corollary L176.1, a NONZERO Weyl tensor of $\Lambda^2$-rank at most three is coordinate-diagonal in no frame.** *The rank is a frame-free invariant and the lemma bounds it below on the locus, so no frame can be the one.* **The hypothesis $W\ne0$ is the lemma's own and cannot be dropped**: $\mathcal W_n$ is a linear subspace, so $0$ lies in it, has rank $0\le3$, and is coordinate-diagonal in every frame.

**Corollary L176.2, padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ is coordinate-diagonal in no frame, and this is analytic.** *$\mathbb{CP}^2$ is self-dual Einstein, so $W^-=0$; its $W^+$ is the degenerate case, eigenvalues proportional to $(2,-1,-1)$ on $\Lambda^+$ and each nonzero.* **So its $\Lambda^2$-rank is exactly three at $n=4$, and padding into $\mathcal W_n$ adds zero eigenvalues alone**. Corollary L176.1 applies. *This is what [I36](../plan.md) owed: the strict inequality in $\theta>\sqrt{3/2}$ is load-bearing, because padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ is a local maximum at $\theta=\sqrt{3/2}$ exactly ([r316](../reviews/REVIEW_r316.md)) and the item's conclusion fails at it.* **The obligation had rested on a computed $\kappa^2=1$ ([r102](../reviews/REVIEW_r102.md)); it now rests on the rank.**

**Corollary L176.3, $\kappa^2(W)<2\lVert M\rVert_F^2$ STRICTLY at every nonzero Weyl tensor of $\Lambda^2$-rank at most three, in [L94](LEDGER.md)'s own symbol.** *[L94](LEDGER.md) carries that bound with equality exactly at coordinate-diagonal, and the direction used here is equality $\Rightarrow$ coordinate-diagonal, which L176.1 excludes.* **Three conditions this rests on, all named rather than assumed.** *(i)* That direction is the harder half and is not argued here, it is [L94](LEDGER.md)'s, confirmed with a one-line proof by [r101](../reviews/REVIEW_r101.md) and [r102](../reviews/REVIEW_r102.md) on identical briefs. *(ii)* $\kappa^2$ and $\lVert M\rVert_F$ must be frame-invariant, since L176.1 negates diagonality over **all** frames; $\kappa^2=\max_Ft_F$ is a maximum over frames and so is invariant by construction. *(iii)* **The corollary stays in $\lVert M\rVert_F$ and does not convert to $\lVert W\rVert$**, on the locus $\lVert K\rVert_F^2=2$ at unit $W$, so the two differ by exactly the factor [W6](INDEX-traps.md) warns about, and read at the sectional matrix this bound is the slack $\kappa^2<4$ rather than $\kappa^2<2$. *A first draft of this corollary made that conversion silently and both audits caught it.* **At $W=0$ every candidate $\kappa$ vanishes and the strictness reads $0<0$, which is why the hypothesis is carried here too.**

**Reach, stated plainly.** *The criterion is a rank criterion and its range is small: it decides padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ and every other rank-$\le3$ tensor, and it says nothing about padded $\mathbb{HP}^2$*, whose curvature operator on $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^8$ has rank far above three. **The second survivor of [L24](second-order.md)'s cut is untouched by this lemma and its diagonality is still open.**

---

#### Lemma L177, the support graph is decided by the unsigned incidence matrix, and the locus dimension follows

**Statement.** For a graph $G=(V,E)$ let $N\in\{0,1\}^{V\times E}$ be its **unsigned** incidence matrix, $N_{ve}=1$ exactly when $v\in e$. Write $b(G)$ for the number of bipartite connected components of $G$. Then:

1. **The coordinate-diagonal Weyl tensors supported inside $G$ are exactly $\ker N$**, so their space has dimension $\lvert E\rvert-\lvert V\rvert+b(G)$.
2. **$G$ carries a coordinate-diagonal Weyl tensor with support exactly $G$** if and only if $\ker N$ contains a vector of full support.
3. **The locus's dimension at $G=K_n$, $n\ge3$, is $\binom n2-n$**, which is the count the record had been asserting.

**Proof.** *(1)* The locus is hollow symmetric with vanishing row sums: for a coordinate-diagonal $W$ the first Bianchi identity holds automatically, every term of $W_{abcd}+W_{acdb}+W_{adbc}$ has a mismatched index pair unless two indices coincide, and then antisymmetry kills it, so **total trace-freeness is the whole of the condition**, and $\sum_bW_{abcb}$ vanishes identically off $a=c$ and reads $\sum_bK_{ab}$ on it. *A weight $x\in\mathbb R^E$ has vanishing row sums exactly when $\sum_{e\ni v}x_e=0$ for every $v$, which is $Nx=0$.* **Two hypotheses are used throughout and were unstated**: the field has characteristic $\ne2$, without which the rank identity fails, and $G$ is loopless, which hollowness of $K$ supplies. The dimension is rank–nullity together with **$\operatorname{rank}N=\lvert V\rvert-b(G)$**, **which is classical and on file**: [Zaslavsky](../references/zaslavsky-2013-signed-graph-matrices/)'s Theorem IV.1, credited there to Jeurissen, gives $\operatorname{rank}H(\Sigma)=n-b(\Sigma)$ for a signed graph over any field of characteristic $\ne2$, and the **all-negative signing makes $H$ the unsigned incidence matrix with *balanced* meaning *bipartite***. *Its proof is the one that follows and it blocks over connected components first, which is the reading this block reached independently.* The argument: on a connected bipartite component the sign vector $\epsilon$ that is $+1$ on one part and $-1$ on the other satisfies $\epsilon^{\mathsf T}N=0$, since each edge crosses the parts; on a connected non-bipartite component an odd closed walk forces any left null vector to satisfy $\epsilon_v=-\epsilon_v$, so the left kernel is spanned by one vector per bipartite component and by nothing else. *(2)* is (1) read at the definition of support, **so it is a restatement rather than a criterion**, and it decides nothing on its own. *(3)* $K_n$ is connected and non-bipartite for $n\ge3$, so $b=0$ and $\dim=\binom n2-n$. $\square$

> **THIS EXPLAINS [L176](#lemma-l176-the-coordinate-diagonal-locus-has-minimum-degree-two-so-its-lambda2-rank-is-at-least-four)'S FOUR RATHER THAN MERELY PROVING IT, AND THE ARGUMENT RUNS PER COMPONENT.** *A support graph carries $x\in\ker N$ with **every** $x_e\ne0$ by definition, and $x$ restricted to a connected component lies in that component's kernel and is fully supported there*, **so $\ker N_i\ne0$ for every component, and $\lvert E_i\rvert\ge\lvert V_i\rvert-b_i+1$ holds componentwise rather than once for the whole graph.** **A bipartite component has $b_i=1$, so $\lvert E_i\rvert\ge\lvert V_i\rvert$ and it contains a cycle, of length at least four, being bipartite.** **A non-bipartite one has $b_i=0$, so $\lvert E_i\rvert\ge\lvert V_i\rvert+1$, and its odd cycle gives $\lvert V_i\rvert\ge3$**; three vertices carry at most three edges in a simple graph, so $\lvert V_i\rvert\ge4$ and $\lvert E_i\rvert\ge5$, attained by $K_4$ minus an edge. *Every component CARRYING AN EDGE therefore has at least four edges*, **the qualifier is needed, since an isolated vertex is a component with an empty kernel condition and no edges to count.** *And five is true as a lower bound but **not attained**: $K_4$ minus an edge realises five edges with kernel $(0,1,-1,-1,1)$, whose first entry is forced to zero, so no support graph has a non-bipartite component of five edges, **the smallest that is attained is the bowtie, at six**.* **So four is the minimum and $C_4$ is the shape attaining it. L176's bound was sharp for a reason its own proof could not see, having reached the triangle by counting.**

> **THE WHOLE-GRAPH CASE SPLIT IS NOT ENOUGH, WHICH IS WHY THE ARGUMENT IS WRITTEN THIS WAY.** *Splitting on whether the graph is all-bipartite leaves the mixed case open: a kernel vector could live entirely on the bipartite part, and a non-bipartite component would then carry zero weights.* **Full support is what rules that out, and it rules it out one component at a time.**

> **A NONZERO KERNEL DOES NOT GIVE A FULL-SUPPORT VECTOR, AND CLAIM (2) MUST NOT BE READ AS SAYING SO.** *The witness is the four-cycle with a pendant edge*: five vertices, five edges, connected and bipartite, so $\dim\ker N=5-5+1=1$, **and the pendant vertex has degree one, so its row sum forces that edge's weight to zero.** *The unique kernel direction is the cycle's alternating weight with a zero on the pendant, and the tensor it names has support $C_4$ rather than the whole graph.* **So (1) and (2) say different things**: the tensors supported **inside** $G$ are $\ker N$ whatever $G$ is, while carrying one with support **exactly** $G$ needs a kernel vector that vanishes nowhere. *Found at audit by [r771](../reviews/REVIEW_r771.md), against a reading this block does not make and a later reader easily would.*

> **WHAT IT SETTLES ABOUT THE TRIANGLE.** *$K_3$ is connected and non-bipartite, so $\operatorname{rank}N=3=\lvert E\rvert$ and $\ker N=0$*, **which is L176's $3\times3$ system, now as an instance rather than a calculation**, and the same sentence disposes of every odd cycle at once.

**Reach.** *The characterisation is complete for the support question and says nothing about which spectra are attainable*, the entries being free inside $\ker N$ subject only to full support. **[L176](#lemma-l176-the-coordinate-diagonal-locus-has-minimum-degree-two-so-its-lambda2-rank-is-at-least-four)'s corollaries about padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ are unaffected either way**, resting on the rank bound alone. *L177 rests on the same locus characterisation as L176, and both are Gate 2 landed: [r769](../reviews/REVIEW_r769.md) and [r770](../reviews/REVIEW_r770.md) on L176, [r771](../reviews/REVIEW_r771.md) and [r772](../reviews/REVIEW_r772.md) on this lemma.*

---

#### Lemma L178, every Weyl tensor of $\Lambda^2$-rank at most three has $\theta\le\sqrt{3/2}$, with equality exactly at $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$

**Statement.** Let $W\in\mathcal W_n$ be a nonzero Weyl tensor supported on $\Lambda^2V$ for a four-dimensional $V\subseteq\mathbb R^n$, with $\operatorname{rank}(W:\Lambda^2\to\Lambda^2)\le3$. Then $\theta(W)\le\sqrt{3/2}$, **with equality exactly when $W$ is $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ up to POSITIVE scale and an $O(n)$ frame change.** *The frame group must be $O(n)$ rather than $SO(n)$, so that the anti-self-dual mirror, which does attain the bound, is covered.* *Sign is not a symmetry of the equality case and was wrongly listed: $\theta$ is odd, so $-W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ attains $-\sqrt{3/2}$ rather than $+\sqrt{3/2}$.* **The sign-closed statement is $\lvert\theta\rvert\le\sqrt{3/2}$, with equality up to scale, sign and frame.** *By [L176](#lemma-l176-the-coordinate-diagonal-locus-has-minimum-degree-two-so-its-lambda2-rank-is-at-least-four) no such $W$ is coordinate-diagonal in any frame, so this is a family inside [I1](../plan.md)'s non-diagonal hypothesis, and it is the family where I1's supremum is attained.*

**Step 1, rank at most three forces (anti-)self-duality, and there is nothing else to check.** On $\Lambda^2V$ the operator splits as $W^+\oplus W^-$ with each summand self-adjoint and **trace-free on a three-dimensional space**, so each has rank $0$, $2$ or $3$, never $1$. A total rank of at most three therefore forces one summand to vanish. *Kernel-checked at $V=\mathbb R^4$ as `L178_split` in [L178Split.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L178Split.lean), with the splitting (`mixed_zero`) and the trace-freeness (`trace_blocks`).* *The rank-two case has a zero eigenvalue and $e_3=0$, so step 4 returns $\theta=0$ and it needs no separate treatment.*

**Step 2, the sharp square on $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ is the adjugate, and its constant is DERIVED.** Write $\Lambda^\pm\cong\mathfrak{su}(2)$ with structure constants $c_{ijk}=\gamma\epsilon_{ijk}$ in an orthonormal basis of $\Lambda^2$. The record's index formula is $(W^\#)_{ab}=\tfrac12\sum c_{acd}c_{bef}W_{ce}W_{df}$, which at $W=\mathrm{id}$ gives $(\mathrm{id}^\#)_{ab}=\tfrac12\gamma^2\sum_{c,d}\epsilon_{acd}\epsilon_{bcd}=\gamma^2\delta_{ab}$. **The record's anchor is $\mathrm{id}^\#=(n-2)\mathrm{id}$, which reads $2\,\mathrm{id}$ at $n=4$, so $\gamma^2=2$.** *Kernel-checked with the layer's own $\#$ as `sharp_sd` and `sharp_asd` in [L178Sharp.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L178Sharp.lean): for $W$ supported on $\Lambda^\pm$, $W^\#$ vanishes off that summand and is $2\operatorname{cof}W$ on it.* *The two identities are different operators, the computation's is $\Lambda^+$'s, three-dimensional, and the anchor's is $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^4$'s, six-dimensional, and the step that lets them be interchanged is that $\#$ is additive across the two ideals: with $P^\pm$ the projections, $(P^++P^-)^\#=(P^+)^\#+(P^-)^\#$ with no cross term, and the summands are alike, so $2\,\mathrm{id}=(P^+)^\#+(P^-)^\#$ forces $(P^\pm)^\#=2P^\pm$.* **That sentence was missing and is supplied by [r771](../reviews/REVIEW_r771.md) at audit, and its conclusion at $n=4$ is kernel-checked with the layer's own $\#$ as `sharp_sd`.** *The constant is pinned by an identity the record proves and not by any numeral it wrote.* **And the record's own crossover corroborates it independently**: $\theta_0(n)=\sqrt{3/2}$ has roots $6\pm2\sqrt7$ exactly, whereas $\gamma^2=1$ would put the crossover at $(9+\sqrt{33})/4$. *No other value of $\gamma^2$ is consistent with $6+2\sqrt7$.* For $W=\operatorname{diag}(\lambda_1,\lambda_2,\lambda_3)$ the same formula returns $W^\#=\gamma^2\operatorname{cof}(W)=2\operatorname{diag}(\lambda_2\lambda_3,\lambda_1\lambda_3,\lambda_1\lambda_2)$, so $\langle W,W^\#\rangle=6e_3$.

**Step 3, padding changes nothing, and the reason is the TOTAL ANTISYMMETRY of $c$.** *For $\alpha\notin\Lambda^2V$ the factors $W_{\gamma\epsilon}W_{\delta\zeta}$ already force $\gamma,\delta\in\Lambda^2V$, leaving $c_{\alpha\gamma\delta}=c_{\gamma\delta\alpha}=\langle[e_\gamma,e_\delta],e_\alpha\rangle$; the bracket lies in $\mathfrak{so}(V)$, so the component along $e_\alpha\perp\mathfrak{so}(V)$ vanishes.* **Two things license this and the block named neither.** *First, $\mathfrak{so}(V)$'s structure constants are **ambient-independent**, which is what permits reading an $n$-dependent anchor at $n=4$, invariance of $\theta$ under padding does not, since the padded identity satisfies $\mathrm{id}^\#=2\,\mathrm{id}$ rather than $(n-2)\mathrm{id}$, so the reason first written would have sanctioned a false reading.* **Second, the total antisymmetry above is what moves $\alpha$ into the bracket slot; subalgebra-ness alone is a non-sequitur.** So $W^\#$ is computed by $\mathfrak{so}(4)$'s own constants and stays supported on $\Lambda^2V$; $\theta$ is unchanged because $\lVert W\rVert^2$, $\operatorname{tr}W^3$ and $\langle W,W^\#\rangle$ all read off that block alone. *The same argument inside $\mathfrak{so}(4)$ is what confines $W^\#$ to $\Lambda^+$ when $W$ lives there, which step 2 needs to treat $W^\#$ as a $3\times3$ cofactor at all, and which this block did not say.* **$\theta$ is therefore an invariant of the four-dimensional piece**, which is why step 2's anchor is read at $n=4$ and not at $n$.

**Step 4, $\theta$ is nine times the third elementary symmetric function.** With $\lVert W\rVert^2=\operatorname{tr}(W^2)=p_2=1$ and $W$ trace-free, $e_1=0$, so Newton gives $\operatorname{tr}W^3=p_3=3e_3$. Adding step 2, $$\theta=\operatorname{tr}(W^3)+\langle W,W^\#\rangle=3e_3+6e_3=9e_3.$$ **The first Bianchi identity IS consumed, exactly once, in step 1**, total trace-freeness gives $\operatorname{tr}W^++\operatorname{tr}W^-=0$ and Bianchi gives $\operatorname{tr}W^+-\operatorname{tr}W^-=0$, and it takes both to make each summand trace-free on its own three-space. *[r771](../reviews/REVIEW_r771.md) reported it unused and [r772](../reviews/REVIEW_r772.md) refuted that; this block briefly carried the wrong reading.*

**Step 5, the bound is the reality of the roots.** $e_1=0$ and $p_2=1$ give $e_2=-\tfrac12$, so $\lambda_1,\lambda_2,\lambda_3$ are the roots of $x^3-\tfrac12x-e_3$. **Three real roots is exactly a nonnegative discriminant**, $-4(-\tfrac12)^3-27e_3^2=\tfrac12-27e_3^2\ge0$, hence $\lvert e_3\rvert\le\tfrac1{3\sqrt6}$ and $$\theta=9e_3\le\frac9{3\sqrt6}=\frac3{\sqrt6}=\sqrt{\tfrac32}.$$ *The inequality of steps 1 to 5 is kernel-checked at $V=\mathbb R^4$ for real $W$ as `L178_n4` in [L178Real.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L178Real.lean), and for the layer's rational $n=4$ model as `L178_n4` in [L178N4.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L178N4.lean), through `L178_theta_sd` and `L178_theta_asd` in [L178Theta.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L178Theta.lean), the discriminant as a Gram determinant of $I,W,W^2$, and the equality case in spectral form as `L178_n4_eq` in [L178Eq.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L178Eq.lean). The bound on $\mathbb R^n$ for $V$ the first four coordinates, steps 1 to 5 through the padding, is `L178_padded` in [L178Reduce.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L178Reduce.lean), and `L178_supported` in [L178Support.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L178Support.lean) states it for the operator, with the operator's rank read as its coefficient matrix's rank on the orthonormal basis of $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^4$, and `L178_frame` in [FrameTransfer.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/FrameTransfer.lean) carries it to every four-dimensional $V$. The equality case, up to positive scale and an $O(n)$ frame, is `L178_padded_eq_iff`, `L178_supported_eq_iff` and `L178_frame_eq_iff` in [L178Rigid.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L178Rigid.lean).* **Equality forces the discriminant to vanish, hence a repeated root**, and $x^3-\tfrac12x-\tfrac1{3\sqrt6}$ has roots $\tfrac1{\sqrt6}(2,-1,-1)$, which is $\mathbb{CP}^2$'s self-dual spectrum. $\square$

> **THE PROOF IS THE REALITY OF A CUBIC, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE OF IT.** *No criticality, no second variation, no representation theory, no fence.* **The competitor's spectrum is not an input**: it comes out as the unique equality case of a discriminant, which is why the bound is sharp and rigid at once. *[F11](second-order.md) requires $\operatorname{tr}W^3$ and $\langle W,W^\#\rangle$ to be treated together, and step 4 does not merely comply, on this family the two terms are the same function of the spectrum, in ratio $1:2$.*

> **WHAT IT DOES FOR [I1](../plan.md).** *I1 asks for $\theta\le\sqrt{3/2}$ on the non-diagonal critical set and its stated obstacle is a margin of $0.3\%$ against $S^5\times S^7$.* **On this family the margin is not consumed at all**, the family caps at $\sqrt{3/2}$ exactly, below $\theta_0(12)=1.236033$, so the band point is not a competitor here and no non-diagonality has to be priced. *That is a proper subfamily of I1 closed, sharply, and the first one closed by an argument rather than by a catalogue.*

**Reach, stated plainly.** *The hypothesis is rank $\le3$ **and** support on a four-dimensional subspace.* **Whether every rank-$\le3$ Weyl tensor is supported on a four-dimensional subspace is open and is not used**; the padded family is what the statement covers. **Padded $\mathbb{HP}^2$ is outside it**, its $\Lambda^2$-rank being far above three, so [L24](second-order.md)'s second survivor is untouched here as it was at [L176](#lemma-l176-the-coordinate-diagonal-locus-has-minimum-degree-two-so-its-lambda2-rank-is-at-least-four).

---

#### Lemma L179, the support of a low-rank Weyl tensor, and the first Bianchi identity read on the spectrum

**What this is for.** *[L178](#lemma-l178-every-weyl-tensor-of-lambda2-rank-at-most-three-has-thetalesqrt32-with-equality-exactly-at-w_mathbbcp2) assumes four-dimensional support and does not prove it, which is the exact width at which [I38](../plan.md) stays open.* **This lemma turns that assumption into an algebraic question about three $2$-forms.**

**(a) The support is the span of the image forms' supports.** Let $W\in\mathcal W_n$ have spectral decomposition $W=\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha\,\omega_\alpha\otimes\omega_\alpha$ over an orthonormal eigenbasis of $\Lambda^2$, and let $V\subseteq\mathbb R^n$ be the span of the supports of the $\omega_\alpha$ with $\lambda_\alpha\ne0$, a $2$-form's support being the image of the skew map it names. **Then $W$ is supported on $\Lambda^2V$**, *read in a frame adapted to $V$, which is the convention that makes "an index leaves $V$" mean anything and which this statement did not carry.* *Proof: $\operatorname{Im}W\subseteq\Lambda^2V$ by construction, so $W(f_a\wedge f_b)\in\Lambda^2V$ and $W_{abcd}=\langle W(f_a\wedge f_b),f_c\wedge f_d\rangle$ vanishes whenever $\{c,d\}\not\subseteq V$; the pair symmetry $W_{abcd}=W_{cdab}$ gives the other index pair.* $\square$

**(b) The first Bianchi identity is one equation on the nonzero spectrum.** The Bianchi obstruction is the map $\mathfrak b:S^2\Lambda^2\to\Lambda^4$ sending $\omega\odot\eta$ to $\omega\wedge\eta$, so $$\text{first Bianchi}\iff\sum_{\lambda_\alpha\ne0}\lambda_\alpha\,\omega_\alpha\wedge\omega_\alpha=0\ \in\Lambda^4.$$ **The zero eigenvalues drop out**, so at rank $r$ this is a condition on $r$ terms and no more, three, at the rank [L178](#lemma-l178-every-weyl-tensor-of-lambda2-rank-at-most-three-has-thetalesqrt32-with-equality-exactly-at-w_mathbbcp2) treats.

**(c) Rank one is impossible.** *A rank-one self-adjoint $W=\lambda\,\omega\otimes\omega$ has $\operatorname{tr}W=\lambda\lVert\omega\rVert^2$, which tracelessness forces to zero.* **So the nonzero ranks begin at two**, which is what [L178](#lemma-l178-every-weyl-tensor-of-lambda2-rank-at-most-three-has-thetalesqrt32-with-equality-exactly-at-w_mathbbcp2)'s step 1 uses on each of $W^\pm$ separately.

> **THE DECOMPOSABLE CASE IS BOUNDED AT SIX, AND ITS PLANES CANNOT BE ORTHOGONAL.** *A $2$-form satisfies $\omega\wedge\omega=0$ exactly when it is decomposable, $\omega=u\wedge v$, and its support is then the plane $\langle u,v\rangle$.* **So if all three image forms are decomposable, (b) holds automatically and (a) gives $\dim V\le6$.** *But the three planes cannot be mutually orthogonal*: an adapted frame would make such a $W$ coordinate-diagonal, and [L176](#lemma-l176-the-coordinate-diagonal-locus-has-minimum-degree-two-so-its-lambda2-rank-is-at-least-four) forbids rank three there. **THAT EXCLUDES ORTHOGONALITY AND NOT $\dim V=6$**, which is less than the paragraph reads as saying, *and the branch it leaves open is EMPTY, by [Lemma L185](#lemma-l185-the-all-decomposable-branch-is-empty). Ricci-flatness alone kills it at every width.* *This clause rests on the imported coordinate-diagonal rank bound and proves nothing without it.* **A non-decomposable image form has support of dimension at least four on its own.**

#### Lemma L200, WITHDRAWN THE DAY IT WAS NUMBERED: the corrected statement is already proved in the Lean layer

*Claimed $\mathrm{Alt}(A)=\tfrac13\sum_\alpha\mu_\alpha\,\omega_\alpha\wedge\omega_\alpha$. **[r844](../reviews/REVIEW_r844.md) and [r845](../reviews/REVIEW_r845.md) both `REFUTED AS STATED`, independently: the constant is $\tfrac16$.** The algebra, the projector, the spanning step, the sign and the two-directional [L179](#lemma-l179-the-support-of-a-low-rank-weyl-tensor-and-the-first-bianchi-identity-read-on-the-spectrum)(b) reading all survive, and the corrected identity is machine-checked already, so the id is spent and what survives is the note below. The estimate the block offered is false in the norm it named.*

---

> **AND $\mathrm{Alt}$ AND $\mathfrak b$ ARE ONE MAP, WITH THE FACTOR ALREADY MACHINE-CHECKED.** *$\mathrm{Alt}(A)=\tfrac16\,\mathfrak b(A)=\tfrac16\sum_\alpha\mu_\alpha\,\omega_\alpha\wedge\omega_\alpha$ for every symmetric $A$ on $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^n$*, which is [altCoeff_eq_third_bianchi](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Base/AltPart.lean)'s $\mathrm{Alt}=\tfrac13\,\mathrm{bianchi}$ composed with [bianchi_rankSumOp](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L179Statement.lean)'s $\mathrm{bianchi}=\tfrac12\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i\wedge\omega_i$. **So (b) above is the instance $\mu=\lambda$**, and [L199](bounds.md)'s $\Lambda^4$ term is $12\,\mathrm{Alt}(W^2)=2\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha^2\,\omega_\alpha\wedge\omega_\alpha$. *The record's wedge is twice the Pfaffian, fixed once for the layer at [wedgeSelf](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Base/SelfDual.lean); reading it as the Pfaffian doubles every constant here and was recorded that way for one revision.*

#### Lemma L185, the all-decomposable branch is empty

*Derived 2026-09-09. **Gate 2: [r798](../reviews/REVIEW_r798.md) and [r799](../reviews/REVIEW_r799.md), identical briefs, both CONFIRMED, and both independently corrected the first statement of it, which said `bounded at five` where the truth is `empty`.***

**Statement.** *There is no rank-three Weyl tensor whose three image forms are all decomposable.* Equivalently: unit decomposable $\omega_1,\omega_2,\omega_3$, orthonormal in $\Lambda^2$, with $\lambda_i\ne0$ and $W=\sum\lambda_i\,\omega_i\otimes\omega_i$ Ricci-flat, do not exist.

**Proof.** Ricci-flatness of $W$ is $\sum\lambda_i\omega_i\omega_i^{\mathsf T}=0$ ([Lemma L180](#lemma-l180-ricci-flatness-is-a-signed-psd-identity-and-it-names-the-support)(1)), and a unit decomposable $\omega_i$ is the area form of a plane $P_i$ with $\omega_i\omega_i^{\mathsf T}=\Pi_i$, the orthogonal projection. So **Ricci-flatness reads $\sum\lambda_i\Pi_i=0$**, and its trace gives $\sum\lambda_i=0$, so the signs are mixed, one against two, either way.

Group by sign, so that a sum of two positive multiples of projections equals a positive multiple of the third. **Squaring $\lambda_2\Pi_2+\lambda_3\Pi_3=-\lambda_1\Pi_1$ gives $\lambda_2\lambda_3(\{\Pi_2,\Pi_3\}-\Pi_2-\Pi_3)=0$, hence $(\Pi_2+\Pi_3)^2=2(\Pi_2+\Pi_3)$**, whose $2$-eigenspace is $P_2\cap P_3$ and has dimension $\operatorname{tr}/2=2$. So $P_2=P_3$, then $\Pi_1=\Pi_2$, and all three forms lie in the **one-dimensional** $\Lambda^2P$. *Three orthonormal vectors do not fit in a line.* $\square$ *Kernel-checked as `L185` in [L185Proof.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L185Proof.lean), the squaring step needing only $\lambda_2\lambda_3\ne0$ and closing through $(\Pi_2-\Pi_3)^2=0$.*

> **THE SAME CONCLUSION FOLLOWS FROM THE RECORD'S OWN [L180](#lemma-l180-ricci-flatness-is-a-signed-psd-identity-and-it-names-the-support)(3)**, which puts $V$ at the minority-sign form's support, a plane in the all-decomposable case, against that lemma's own $\dim V\ge4$. *The squaring route above is recorded because it imports no range fact about positive-semidefinite sums, so the two are independent.*

> **WHAT THIS CLOSES, AND WHAT IT DOES NOT.** *It empties the branch [I38](../plan.md) was open at, so the surviving question is only about image forms that are NOT all decomposable*, and a non-decomposable form has support of dimension at least four on its own. **It also reaches a sliver [L184](#lemma-l184-the-coincident-case-at-dim-v6-is-empty) does not**: the branch sits inside [L183](#lemma-l183-a-rank-three-weyl-tensor-is-an-orthonormal-triple-whose-images-are-collinear)'s coincident case with $A=\Pi_1$ of rank two, so L184's six-dimensional setup does not apply. *Padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$'s eigenforms are self-dual, hence non-decomposable, corroboration rather than counterexample.*

#### Lemma L186, no rank-three Weyl tensor has exactly one non-decomposable image form

*Derived 2026-09-09. **Gate 2: [r800](../reviews/REVIEW_r800.md) and [r801](../reviews/REVIEW_r801.md), identical briefs. Both CONFIRMED the statement first recorded here, $\dim V=4$, and both independently found it VACUOUS, by an argument two lines long that the first proof did not reach.***

**Statement.** *There is no rank-three Weyl tensor with exactly one non-decomposable image form.*

**Proof.** [L179](#lemma-l179-the-support-of-a-low-rank-weyl-tensor-and-the-first-bianchi-identity-read-on-the-spectrum)(b) reads the first Bianchi identity as $\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i\wedge\omega_i=0$. **A form is decomposable exactly when $\omega\wedge\omega=0$**, so the two decomposable forms contribute nothing and the identity collapses to $\lambda_1\,\omega_1\wedge\omega_1=0$ with $\lambda_1\ne0$, forcing $\omega_1$ decomposable, against hypothesis. $\square$ *Kernel-checked as `L186` in [L186Proof.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L186Proof.lean), with $\omega\wedge\omega=0\iff$ decomposable as `isDecomp_iff`.*

> **RICCI-FLATNESS, ORTHONORMALITY, THE SIGNS, THE RANKS AND THE SUPPORT ARE ALL UNUSED.** *The first proof recorded here ran through every one of them to reach $\dim V=4$, true, and true of nothing.* **This is the second consecutive lemma of this line that is correct and vacuous**: [L185](#lemma-l185-the-all-decomposable-branch-is-empty) by Ricci-flatness alone, this one by Bianchi alone. *Both were caught at Gate 2 and neither by the author.*

> **THE SIGN HYPOTHESIS THE FIRST PROOF ASSUMED IS A THEOREM, TWICE OVER.** *By [L180](#lemma-l180-ricci-flatness-is-a-signed-psd-identity-and-it-names-the-support)(3), a decomposable minority form makes $V$ a plane, putting three orthonormal forms in a one-dimensional $\Lambda^2V$; or by the rank count below, applied at this branch.* **The two halves of this block prove each other's assumption**, which is why neither needed carrying.

> **THE SAME RANK COUNT KILLS SAME-SIGNED WEIGHTS AT TWO NON-DECOMPOSABLE FORMS.** *If $\omega_1,\omega_2$ are both non-decomposable with $\lambda_1,\lambda_2$ of one sign, $\sum\lambda_i=0$ puts $\omega_3$ alone on the other side and Ricci-flatness reads $\lvert\lambda_1\rvert\omega_1\omega_1^{\mathsf T}+\lvert\lambda_2\rvert\omega_2\omega_2^{\mathsf T}=\lvert\lambda_3\rvert\Pi_3$*, **rank at least four against rank two.** *Kernel-checked as `same_sign_rank_impossible` in [L186Rank.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L186Rank.lean), with only $\omega_1$ non-decomposable.* *Positivity of BOTH weights is load-bearing: $A+(-1)A=0$ shows the count fails without it.* So the two carry opposite signs, and first Bianchi then gives $\omega_1\wedge\omega_1=c\,(\omega_2\wedge\omega_2)$ with $c=-\lambda_2/\lambda_1>0$, *proportionality with a positive ratio, which is [L183](#lemma-l183-a-rank-three-weyl-tensor-is-an-orthonormal-triple-whose-images-are-collinear)'s second alternative and the case [L184](#lemma-l184-the-coincident-case-at-dim-v6-is-empty) does not reach.*

> **AND THE TWO-CASE IS EMPTY TOO, BY [L178](#lemma-l178-every-weyl-tensor-of-lambda2-rank-at-most-three-has-thetalesqrt32-with-equality-exactly-at-w_mathbbcp2)'S OWN STEP 1.** *At $\dim V=4$ rank $\le3$ forces (anti-)self-duality, so every eigenform lies in $\Lambda^+$ or $\Lambda^-$, and a nonzero self-dual $2$-form on $\mathbb R^4$ satisfies $\omega^2=-\tfrac12\lVert\omega\rVert^2\mathrm{Id}$, hence has rank four.* **So at $\dim V=4$ ALL THREE image forms are non-decomposable**, and a configuration with exactly two forces $\dim V>4$, *which [L187](#lemma-l187-two-non-decomposable-image-forms-force-dim-vequiv0pmod4-with-dim-vge8)'s Pfaffian argument then contradicts, since it puts the two-case at $\dim V=4$.*

> **THE [L178](#lemma-l178-every-weyl-tensor-of-lambda2-rank-at-most-three-has-thetalesqrt32-with-equality-exactly-at-w_mathbbcp2) IMPORT ABOVE IS SOUND AND NOT CIRCULAR**, which was the author's own stated worry: step 1 is applied UNDER the hypothesis $\dim V=4$ to derive a contradiction with *exactly two non-decomposable*, so it is a conditional rather than an assumption of what is proved. *[r800](../reviews/REVIEW_r800.md) and [r801](../reviews/REVIEW_r801.md) checked it independently and both cleared it; the note sat under [L190](#lemma-l190-the-surviving-rank-three-case-is-inhabited-and-its-question-is-exactly-dim-v4) crediting that round's reviewers, and its subject is this step.*

> **THE CENSUS.** *Counting non-decomposable image forms:* **zero is empty** ([L185](#lemma-l185-the-all-decomposable-branch-is-empty), by Ricci); **one is empty** (this lemma, by Bianchi); **two is open** and is what [L187](#lemma-l187-two-non-decomposable-image-forms-force-dim-vequiv0pmod4-with-dim-vge8) attacks; **three is open and was unmentioned**, the first census here omitted it entirely while claiming the question closed. *So a rank-three Weyl tensor has AT LEAST TWO non-decomposable image forms.*

#### Lemma L187, two non-decomposable image forms force $\dim V\equiv0\pmod4$ with $\dim V\ge8$

*Derived 2026-09-09. **Gate 2: [r802](../reviews/REVIEW_r802.md) and [r803](../reviews/REVIEW_r803.md), identical briefs. Both REFUTED the statement first recorded here, $\dim V=4$, on the same defect, found independently: its premise $\dim V\le6$ is CIRCULAR, assuming the rank bound the argument exists to prove.***

**Statement.** *A rank-three Weyl tensor with exactly two non-decomposable image forms has $\dim V\equiv0\pmod4$ and $\dim V\ge8$.*

**What the $\sigma$ argument does establish.** On $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^6$ put $\sigma(\omega)=\star(\omega\wedge\omega)/2$; in an adapted frame it is the adjugate on pair spectra, whence $\sigma(\sigma(\omega))=\mathrm{Pf}(\omega)\,\omega$. *$\sigma$ is quadratic, so first Bianchi's $\sigma(\omega_1)=c\,\sigma(\omega_2)$ gives $\mathrm{Pf}(\omega_1)\,\omega_1=c^2\,\mathrm{Pf}(\omega_2)\,\omega_2$, and orthonormality forces both Pfaffians to vanish.* **The $\star$ signs were verified by hand on $\mathbb R^6$ by both reviewers and the identity is correct.** *A $\tau$-generalisation of it kills odd $m$ and $\dim V\in\{10,14,\dots\}$.*

**What it does not establish, and this is the defect.** *The argument needs $\dim V\le6$ to run, and that was asserted from $\operatorname{supp}\omega_1=\operatorname{supp}\omega_2+P_3$, which bounds $\dim V$ by $\operatorname{rank}\omega_2+2$, so $\dim V\le6$ ASSUMES $\operatorname{rank}\omega_2\le4$.* **That is the conclusion.** So the $\sigma$ route settles $\dim V=6$ and says nothing at $\dim V=8$.

> **THE CENSUS, AS IT NOW STANDS.** *Zero non-decomposable is empty ([L185](#lemma-l185-the-all-decomposable-branch-is-empty)); one is empty ([L186](#lemma-l186-no-rank-three-weyl-tensor-has-exactly-one-non-decomposable-image-form)); **two survives only at $\dim V\in\{8,12,\dots\}$**, being empty at $4$, $6$ and $\{10,14,\dots\}$; **three is open**.* **So TWO AND THREE BOTH REMAIN**, against the claim recorded here that three was the only case. *The census is structurally exhaustive this time, the earlier failure was in the emptiness verdicts rather than in the enumeration.*

#### Lemma L188, at $\dim V=8$ both forms are full rank, and first Bianchi factors

*Derived 2026-09-09. **Gate 2 pending.***

**Where it sits.** *[L187](#lemma-l187-two-non-decomposable-image-forms-force-dim-vequiv0pmod4-with-dim-vge8) empties the two-non-decomposable case at $\dim V\in\{4,6\}$ and [r802](../reviews/REVIEW_r802.md)'s $\tau$ route at $\{10,14,\dots\}$; it stops at $\dim V\equiv0\pmod4$ because $\omega^{m-1}$ is then an odd power.* **$\dim V=8$ is the first open dimension.**

**(1) Both non-decomposable forms have full rank eight.** *Wedging Bianchi's $\omega_1\wedge\omega_1=c\,(\omega_2\wedge\omega_2)$ with itself gives $\omega_1^{4}=c^2\omega_2^{4}$, that is $\mathrm{Pf}(\omega_1)=c^2\mathrm{Pf}(\omega_2)$.* If $\operatorname{rank}\omega_2<8$ then $\mathrm{Pf}(\omega_2)=0$, hence $\mathrm{Pf}(\omega_1)=0$ and $\operatorname{rank}\omega_1<8$, **against [L180](#lemma-l180-ricci-flatness-is-a-signed-psd-identity-and-it-names-the-support)(3), which puts $V=\operatorname{supp}\omega_1$ at dimension eight.**

**(2) First Bianchi FACTORS.** *Two-forms commute under the wedge, the degree product being even, so the cross terms cancel:* $$(\omega_1-\sqrt c\,\omega_2)\wedge(\omega_1+\sqrt c\,\omega_2)=\omega_1\wedge\omega_1-c\,(\omega_2\wedge\omega_2)=0.$$ **So with $\alpha=\omega_1-\sqrt c\,\omega_2$ and $\beta=\omega_1+\sqrt c\,\omega_2$ the open case reads $\alpha\wedge\beta=0$**, both nonzero, *either vanishing makes $\omega_1,\omega_2$ dependent against orthonormality*, with $\lVert\alpha\rVert^2=\lVert\beta\rVert^2=1+c$ and $\langle\alpha,\beta\rangle=1-c$.

**(3) And Ricci-flatness adds a second constraint, sharp.** *With both forms full rank, $\lambda_1A_1=\lvert\lambda_2\rvert A_2+\lvert\lambda_3\rvert\Pi_3$ says $\lambda_1A_1-\lvert\lambda_2\rvert A_2$ is positive semidefinite of rank EXACTLY TWO, two positive definite matrices differing, up to scale, by a rank-two term.* Read as a quadratic form at $x\perp P_3$ the projection term dies, leaving $$\lambda_1\lVert\omega_1x\rVert^2=\lvert\lambda_2\rvert\lVert\omega_2x\rVert^2\quad\text{for every }x\in P_3^\perp\cap V,$$ **so $\lVert\omega_1x\rVert/\lVert\omega_2x\rVert$ is the constant $\sqrt{\lvert\lambda_2\rvert/\lambda_1}$ on a SIX-DIMENSIONAL subspace**, two full-rank skew maps agreeing in norm up to one scalar, off a plane.

> **A QUADRATIC CONDITION ON ONE FORM HAS BECOME A BILINEAR CONDITION ON TWO.** *That is the shape the next attempt has to work with, and it is what the $\tau$ route could not reach at even $m$.* **The two constraints, $\alpha\wedge\beta=0$ and the constant norm ratio off a plane, have not been used together, and that is where the next attempt should start.**

> **THIS LEMMA STATES NO EMPTINESS AND CLAIMS NONE.** *Three lemmas of this line have now been correct about a class that was empty or about a premise that was circular; whether $\alpha\wedge\beta=0$ is satisfiable at these norms is exactly what is not settled here.*

#### Lemma L189, a rank-three Weyl tensor has three non-decomposable image forms

*Derived 2026-09-09 at Gate 2 on [L188](#lemma-l188-at-dim-v8-both-forms-are-full-rank-and-first-bianchi-factors) and reproduced independently before recording. **BOTH REVIEWERS REACHED THE EMPTINESS, BY DIFFERENT ROUTES**: [r804](../reviews/REVIEW_r804.md) by the determinant argument below, [r805](../reviews/REVIEW_r805.md) by splitting first Bianchi over $\mathbb R^8=P_3\oplus(P_3^\perp\cap V)$ into four equations, using Bianchi and orthonormality alone. **The determinant step and the strict monotonicity were screened here on integer skew matrices; the verdict was not taken on the report's authority.***

**Statement.** *The two-non-decomposable case is empty at every dimension, so a rank-three Weyl tensor has all three image forms non-decomposable.*

**Proof.** Put $N=\sqrt{\lambda_1}\,\omega_1$ and $M=\sqrt{\lvert\lambda_2\rvert}\,\omega_2$. **The same two square roots linearise both equations**: Ricci-flatness becomes $NN^{\mathsf T}=MM^{\mathsf T}+\lvert\lambda_3\rvert\Pi_3$ and first Bianchi becomes $N\wedge N=M\wedge M$.

*At $\dim V=2m$ with $m$ EVEN, $N^{\wedge4}=(N\wedge N)^{\wedge2}$, so Bianchi gives $\mathrm{Pf}(N)=\mathrm{Pf}(M)$ and hence $\det(NN^{\mathsf T})=\mathrm{Pf}(N)^4=\mathrm{Pf}(M)^4=\det(MM^{\mathsf T})$.* **But a nonzero positive-semidefinite bump on a positive definite matrix strictly raises the determinant**, so $\det(NN^{\mathsf T})>\det(MM^{\mathsf T})$. Contradiction. $\square$ *The monotonicity and the contradiction are machine-checked as `det_lt_det_add` and `L189_core` in [L189Det.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L189Det.lean).*

> **THE ONLY USE OF $\dim V=8$ IS THAT $m$ IS EVEN**, so this empties $\dim V\equiv0\pmod4$ ENTIRELY, the whole range [r802](../reviews/REVIEW_r802.md) left open. *With r802's $\tau$ route at the odd residue, the two-non-decomposable case is empty at every dimension.*

> **SO THE CENSUS CLOSES: ZERO, ONE AND TWO ARE EACH EMPTY, AND THREE IS THE SOLE SURVIVOR.** *[L187](#lemma-l187-two-non-decomposable-image-forms-force-dim-vequiv0pmod4-with-dim-vge8)'s block claimed this two lemmas early and was refuted for it; it is established here, by a route that block did not have.* **[L188](#lemma-l188-at-dim-v8-both-forms-are-full-rank-and-first-bianchi-factors)(1) is this same determinant identity stopped one line early**, and L188's own closing note, that the two constraints had not been used together, *is exactly what this proof does.*

> **AND [L188](#lemma-l188-at-dim-v8-both-forms-are-full-rank-and-first-bianchi-factors)'S FORWARD FRAMING IS WITHDRAWN.** *Its closing note called the two constraints `the shape the next attempt has to work with` and named where that attempt should start; used together they CLOSE the branch, so the ground it pointed at is not open.* **That note was written in the same edit as the constraints it described, and neither reviewer needed anything the block did not already contain.**

> **TWO HYPOTHESES WERE LOAD-BEARING AND UNSTATED, and both are theorems**: the $(+,-,-)$ sign pattern making $\omega_1$ the minority form, and $c>0$. *[L187](#lemma-l187-two-non-decomposable-image-forms-force-dim-vequiv0pmod4-with-dim-vge8) needed only $c\ne0$; L188's $\sqrt c$ genuinely needs positivity, and its block did not say so.*

#### Lemma L190, the surviving rank-three case is inhabited, and its question is exactly $\dim V>4$

*Derived 2026-09-09. **Gate 2 passed**, [r806](../reviews/REVIEW_r806.md) and [r807](../reviews/REVIEW_r807.md), identical briefs. **Both CONFIRMED the witness against every hypothesis, recomputing it rather than reading it off Lean; both REFUTED the block's account of WHY the determinant argument fails, and both scoped the cross term to $\dim V=8$.** Every repair below is theirs.*

**[L189](#lemma-l189-a-rank-three-weyl-tensor-has-three-non-decomposable-image-forms) leaves one case, and unlike its four predecessors it is INHABITED.** *Padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ has $W^-=0$, so its three eigenforms lie in $\Lambda^+\mathbb R^4$; a nonzero self-dual $2$-form on $\mathbb R^4$ satisfies $\omega^2=-\tfrac12\lVert\omega\rVert^2\mathrm{Id}$ and so has rank four.* **So all three image forms are non-decomposable there, at $\dim V=4$**, [Families.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Base/Families.lean) carries the tensor with its $\Lambda^2$ spectrum $(2,-1,-1,0,0,0)$ and $\theta=\sqrt{3/2}$ machine-checked. **The $\Lambda^\pm$ splitting of a Weyl operator is automatic at $n=4$**, so self-duality alone carries the claim and the Einstein condition is not consumed.

**AND THE CASE IS A FIVE-PARAMETER FAMILY RATHER THAN A POINT.** *Every $\omega\in\Lambda^+\mathbb R^4$ satisfies $\omega^2=-\tfrac12\lVert\omega\rVert^2\mathrm{Id}$ and $\omega\wedge\omega=\lVert\omega\rVert^2f_{1234}$, so ANY orthonormal basis of $\Lambda^+\mathbb R^4$ carrying ANY nonzero traceless weight vector is such a tensor*, three parameters in $SO(3)$ for the basis and two for $\lambda$ up to scale. **So the degenerate $(-1)$-eigenplane needs no choice of eigenbasis**, and [plan.md](../plan.md) already carried the family where this block cited a point.

**So [I38](../plan.md)'s question AT RANK THREE is exactly: can the three-non-decomposable case have $\dim V>4$?** *The other three image-form counts are empty, zero by [L185](#lemma-l185-the-all-decomposable-branch-is-empty), one by [L186](#lemma-l186-no-rank-three-weyl-tensor-has-exactly-one-non-decomposable-image-form), two by [L189](#lemma-l189-a-rank-three-weyl-tensor-has-three-non-decomposable-image-forms) at every dimension, and this one has a witness at four.* **The rank-TWO configuration at $\dim V\ge6$ is a second open question**, closed nowhere; [L180](#lemma-l180-ricci-flatness-is-a-signed-psd-identity-and-it-names-the-support)'s rank-two note below says exactly how far it reaches.

> **THE RESCALING THAT CLOSED [L189](#lemma-l189-a-rank-three-weyl-tensor-has-three-non-decomposable-image-forms) PUTS THIS CASE IN THE SAME SHAPE, AND BOTH LEGS OF ITS CONTRADICTION BREAK.** *With $N=\sqrt{\lambda_1}\,\omega_1$, $M_2=\sqrt{\lvert\lambda_2\rvert}\,\omega_2$, $M_3=\sqrt{\lvert\lambda_3\rvert}\,\omega_3$, Ricci-flatness reads $NN^{\mathsf T}=M_2M_2^{\mathsf T}+M_3M_3^{\mathsf T}$ and first Bianchi reads $N\wedge N=M_2\wedge M_2+M_3\wedge M_3$, the right sides now carrying TWO terms rather than one.* **On the RICCI leg the residual positive semidefinite bump that made L189's determinant step STRICT is gone**, and the right comparison becomes Minkowski's determinant inequality $\det(A+B)^{1/m}\ge\det(A)^{1/m}+\det(B)^{1/m}$ on positive semidefinite $A,B$, which padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ **saturates**, at $\det(NN^{\mathsf T})^{1/4}=1$ against $\det(M_iM_i^{\mathsf T})^{1/4}=\tfrac12$ each. *That is a dimension-free reason the determinant route is dead at $\dim V=4$, and it uses no wedge at all.* **On the BIANCHI leg, wedging with itself leaves a cross term $2(M_2^{\wedge2}\wedge M_3^{\wedge2})$, an element of $\Lambda^8V$, so it exists only at $\dim V\ge8$ and reads $0=0$ at $\dim V\in\{4,6\}$.** *The first open dimension is six, so the cross term is vacuous exactly where a successor would start, and a formula written with no dimension attached is the defect [L187](#lemma-l187-two-non-decomposable-image-forms-force-dim-vequiv0pmod4-with-dim-vge8) was refuted for.*

> **AT $\dim V=8$ THAT CROSS TERM IS THE WHOLE OBSTRUCTION, WHICH IS SHARPER THAN A TERM TO BE PRICED.** *Setting it to zero CLOSES the case against Minkowski, and it is bounded below by $2\sqrt{\mathrm{Pf}(M_2)\,\mathrm{Pf}(M_3)}$.* **[r807](../reviews/REVIEW_r807.md)'s, and unaudited**, recorded as the shape of the $\dim V=8$ question rather than as a step.

> **THE PAIRING IDENTITY IS A $\dim V=6$ STATEMENT AND REACHES NO FURTHER.** $\langle\sigma(\omega),\omega\rangle=3\,\mathrm{Pf}(\omega)$ holds on pair spectra at $\dim6$, each of the three terms being $s_1s_2s_3$, and it is $SO(6)$-invariant, so it is a genuine statement about $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^6$. *At $\dim V=2m$ the constant is $m$ rather than $3$, and $\langle\sigma(M_2),N\rangle$ does not typecheck at all for $m>3$.* **Pairing first Bianchi against a form converts it from an equation in $\Lambda^4$ into one in scalars**, at $\dim V=6$ it gives $3\,\mathrm{Pf}(N)=\langle\sigma(M_2),N\rangle+\langle\sigma(M_3),N\rangle$. *The relation is LINEAR in Bianchi where the cross term comes from SQUARING it, so the two instruments live in disjoint dimensions and neither reaches the other.* **Typed as `inner_sigma_self` in [L187Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L187Statement.lean).**

> **THE $(+,-,-)$ SIGN PATTERN IS WLOG HERE RATHER THAN FORCED.** *[L186](#lemma-l186-no-rank-three-weyl-tensor-has-exactly-one-non-decomposable-image-form)'s rank count proves it at TWO non-decomposable forms, where the third is decomposable; with all three non-decomposable, $-W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ realises $(+,+,-)$ and meets every hypothesis, as does any $\Lambda^+$ basis with $\lambda=(1,1,-2)$.* **The reduction is $W\mapsto-W$, and it is legitimate because the question is $\dim V$ and not $\theta$**, $\theta$ is odd under it and $\dim V$ is invariant. *Both reviewers found the pattern inherited from a neighbouring configuration, which is the [L187](#lemma-l187-two-non-decomposable-image-forms-force-dim-vequiv0pmod4-with-dim-vge8) failure mode.*

> **AND THIS LEMMA STATES NO EMPTINESS, BECAUSE THE CASE IS NOT EMPTY.** *Four consecutive results of this line were correct about a class that held nothing; this one exhibits its witness first and says what remains open second.*

#### Lemma L191, two forms with a common six-dimensional support and both coincidences are equal up to sign, so the rank-two branch is empty at $\dim V=6$

*Derived 2026-09-09 out of [L184](#lemma-l184-the-coincident-case-at-dim-v6-is-empty)'s open observation. **Gate 2 passed**, [r808](../reviews/REVIEW_r808.md) and [r809](../reviews/REVIEW_r809.md), identical briefs, both CONFIRMING the pair statement in all three cases and the entailment. **Both independently exhibited the same falsifier one dimension down**, which is what fixes the statement's reach.*

**Statement, in the rigidity form rather than the emptiness form.** *Let $\omega_1,\omega_2$ be $2$-forms with a common six-dimensional support, $\omega_1^2=\omega_2^2$ and $\omega_1\wedge\omega_1=\omega_2\wedge\omega_2$. Then $\omega_2=\pm\omega_1$.* **Orthogonality is spent once, at the last line, and equal norms are a consequence of equal squares rather than a hypothesis**, *so the emptiness of orthonormal such pairs is the corollary, and $\operatorname{tr}A=2$ is used nowhere.*

**Proof.** [L184](#lemma-l184-the-coincident-case-at-dim-v6-is-empty)'s three cases, each closing on two forms. *The split is on how many of $c_1^2,c_2^2,c_3^2$ agree in the spectrum of the common $A=-\omega_1^2$, and every input to it is read off ONE form*, **the second enters only by commuting with $A$, so it respects $A$'s eigenspaces for a reason rather than by inheritance.** Case 1: equal wedges give $s_{1j}s_{1k}=s_{2j}s_{2k}$ at all three pairs, which in $u_j=s_{1j}s_{2j}$ reads $u_ju_k=1$ and forces all $u_j$ equal, hence $\omega_2=\eta\omega_1$; *two of the three equations already suffice.* Case 2: linearity of $\star_V$ applied to $\star_V(J\wedge J)=2\epsilon_JJ$ gives $\omega_2=\pm\omega_1$, **injectivity being cited by L184's block and never used.** Case 3: the cross term in $\Lambda^2Q\otimes\Lambda^2P$ gives $\omega_2=\tau_1\tau_2\omega_1$ directly. $\square$

> **CASE 3, STATED EXACTLY.** *The three-signs count is not false for two, and ORTHOGONALITY substitutes; the count does fail, and what excludes the configuration it admits is the CROSS TERM.* **[r809](../reviews/REVIEW_r809.md) built the near-miss in closed form**: at $\rho_1\rho_2=-1$, $b=2a$, $a=\tfrac14$, the pair $\omega_{1,2}=\tfrac12(e^{12}+e^{34})\pm\tfrac1{\sqrt2}e^{56}$ is orthonormal with a common six-dimensional support and equal squares, and **satisfies orthogonality**, failing only in the two cross-term components $e^{1256}$ and $e^{3456}$. *So a fold that dropped the cross term would have been wrong, and the sequencing, cross term before orthogonality, is load-bearing.*

> **WHAT THIS SETTLES FOR A RANK-TWO WEYL TENSOR.** *At rank two, tracelessness gives $\lambda_2=-\lambda_1$; [L180](#lemma-l180-ricci-flatness-is-a-signed-psd-identity-and-it-names-the-support)(1) then reads $\omega_1^2=\omega_2^2$ and [L179](#lemma-l179-the-support-of-a-low-rank-weyl-tensor-and-the-first-bianchi-identity-read-on-the-spectrum)(b) reads $\omega_1\wedge\omega_1=\omega_2\wedge\omega_2$*, **exactly the hypotheses above, with the common support DERIVED from $\ker\omega=\ker\omega^2$ rather than assumed.** *Both readings are checked against the general-rank forms of those lemmas, so no three-form relation is carried.* **So the rank-two branch is empty at $\dim V=6$, and the ledger row is that and not "rank two closed".**

> **AND THE BRANCH IS INHABITED AT $\dim V=4$, WHICH BOTH REVIEWERS FOUND INDEPENDENTLY.** *Any orthonormal pair in $\Lambda^+\mathbb R^4$ satisfies every hypothesis, $\omega_i^2=-\tfrac12\mathrm{Id}$ and $\omega_i\wedge\omega_i=f_{1234}$ hold across the whole of $\Lambda^+$, and each gives an honest rank-two Weyl tensor.* **So six-dimensionality is SHARP, and no dimension-free formula is available in the pair argument.** *Which is the same shape as [L190](#lemma-l190-the-surviving-rank-three-case-is-inhabited-and-its-question-is-exactly-dim-v4)'s: a witness at four, emptiness above it, and the question living higher.*

> **WHAT SURVIVES IS $\dim V\ge8$ WITH A REPEATED EIGENVALUE.** *Case 1's argument is dimension-free ([r808](../reviews/REVIEW_r808.md)): at rank two the stratum where $-\omega_1^2$ has $m$ pairwise distinct eigenvalues is empty at every $\dim V=2m\ge4$.* **Cases 2 and 3 are strictly six-dimensional**, the first because $\star_V(J\wedge J)$ lands in $\Lambda^2V$ only at $\dim V=6$, the second because its splitting is $4+2$. *And nothing bounds the survivor: $\sum c_j^2=1$ is satisfiable at every $m$, so there is no analogue of [L187](#lemma-l187-two-non-decomposable-image-forms-force-dim-vequiv0pmod4-with-dim-vge8)'s congruence.*

> **THE FOUR `HYPOTHESIS-DEBT`S ARE DISCHARGED, WITH ONE STEP ADDED.** *[L184](#lemma-l184-the-coincident-case-at-dim-v6-is-empty)'s items (i)–(iv) survive both audits: the three $\star$ signs are $+$ at the fixed orientation, the base constant is $2$, and $\epsilon_J$ is well defined because $\det_{\mathbb R}U=\lvert\det_{\mathbb C}U\rvert^2=1$ on $U(3)$.* **(iv) left implicit that a compatible $J$ admits an adapted orthonormal frame at all**, supplied by [r808](../reviews/REVIEW_r808.md) in two lines. *No machine check of $\star_V(J\wedge J)=2\epsilon_JJ$ exists in either direction, r808's attempt exceeded its screening budget and was stopped, so the identity rests on the hand computation.*

#### The rank-three support question, and everything the record has that bears on it

*These notes were carried inside [plan.md](../plan.md)'s I38 item, which grew to 18 kB against a task list whose entries are meant to be an identifier, a status, a route and what is owed. **The mathematics belongs here**, where the subject lives, and the plan keeps the task.*

**Why the family is the right one.** *[Lemma L176](findings/diagonal-half.md) says rank $\le3$ is disjoint from the coordinate-diagonal locus*, so this is a subfamily of I1's non-diagonal hypothesis, **and it is not an arbitrary one: padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ has rank exactly three and attains I1's supremum.** *So the item asks I1's question where its answer is known to be tight, which is the only place a sharp inequality can be tested.*

**The cube half is done and is elementary.** *The $\Lambda^2$ operator of a Weyl tensor is trace-free, so a rank-three $W$ has three eigenvalues with $\sum\lambda_i=0$ and $\sum\lambda_i^2$ fixed by the norm; maximising $\sum\lambda_i^3$ under those two constraints gives $\tfrac1{\sqrt6}$ at unit norm, attained only at $(2,-1,-1)/\sqrt6$*, **which is $\mathbb{CP}^2$'s spectrum, so the cube term is already sharp at the intended equality case and rigid there.**

**What is owed is the sharp term.** *A bound $\langle W,W^\#\rangle\le\tfrac2{\sqrt6}$ on the rank-three family, sharp at the same point.* **[F11](findings/second-order.md) says any route must treat $\operatorname{tr}W^3$ and $\langle W,W^\#\rangle$ together, and this item complies by pricing them at the same spectrum rather than separately.** *The record's identities $\operatorname{tr}W^3=\tfrac12\langle M^{\circ2},M\rangle$ and $\langle W,W^\#\rangle=\tfrac12\langle M^2,M\rangle$ hold **on the diagonal locus**, which L176 puts outside this family, so they are unavailable and a general expression is needed.*

**First sub-step, and it is a convention rather than mathematics: pin the normalisation.** *Whether $\theta=\operatorname{tr}W^3+\langle W,W^\#\rangle$ at unit norm depends on which norm $\lVert W\rVert$ is and which trace form pairs the $\Lambda^2$ operators, and this record's recurring defect is exactly a quantity read under a neighbouring convention.* **Settle it against a derived identity, never against a numeral the record wrote.**

**CLOSED THE SAME DAY ON THE PADDED FAMILY, as [Lemma L178](findings/diagonal-half.md).** *The convention sub-step is discharged against the record's own $\mathrm{id}^\#=(n-2)\mathrm{id}$ rather than any numeral: it forces $\gamma^2=2$ at $n=4$, hence $W^\#=2\operatorname{cof}W$ on $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ and $\langle W,W^\#\rangle=6e_3$.* **Then $\theta=3e_3+6e_3=9e_3$ and the bound is the reality of $x^3-\tfrac12x-e_3$**, $\lvert e_3\rvert\le\tfrac1{3\sqrt6}$, so $\theta\le3/\sqrt6=\sqrt{3/2}$, equality at a repeated root, which is $\mathbb{CP}^2$'s spectrum. *The competitor comes out of a discriminant rather than going in as an input.*

**THE ALL-DECOMPOSABLE BRANCH IS CLOSED, 2026-09-09, as [Lemma L185](findings/diagonal-half.md).** *Ricci-flatness alone empties it at every width: $\sum\lambda_i\Pi_i=0$ at unit decomposable forms forces the three planes to coincide, and three orthonormal forms do not fit in the one-dimensional $\Lambda^2P$.* **The record had this branch as *bounded at six, and its planes cannot be orthogonal*, which excluded orthogonality and not the dimension.** *So the surviving question is only about image forms that are NOT all decomposable, and such a form has support of dimension at least four on its own.* **Gate 2: [r798](reviews/REVIEW_r798.md), [r799](reviews/REVIEW_r799.md), both CONFIRMED, both correcting the first statement from `bounded at five` to empty.**

**WHAT REMAINS OF THE ITEM IS ONE QUESTION, AND [Lemma L179](findings/diagonal-half.md) MAKES IT ALGEBRAIC.** *Is every $\Lambda^2$-rank-$\le3$ Weyl tensor supported on a four-dimensional subspace?* **L178 assumes that support and does not prove it**, so the item stays open at exactly that width. **L179 reduces it to three $2$-forms**: the support is the span of the image forms' supports, the first Bianchi identity is the single equation $\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i\wedge\omega_i=0$ in $\Lambda^4$ over the nonzero spectrum alone, and rank one is impossible. *So the question is whether an orthonormal triple with weights summing to zero can satisfy that equation and Ricci-flatness while spanning more than four dimensions.* **The all-decomposable case is already bounded at six and constrained**: those three planes cannot be mutually orthogonal, since an adapted frame would make $W$ coordinate-diagonal and [L176](findings/diagonal-half.md) forbids rank three there. **[Lemma L180](findings/diagonal-half.md) then supplies Ricci-flatness and narrows the question to ONE CASE.** *It is the identity $\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i^2=0$, a signed combination of positive semidefinite $A_i=\omega_i\omega_i^{\mathsf T}$ of trace $2$, with tracelessness as its trace rather than a separate condition. At rank three the signs cannot all agree, so moving the minority-sign term leaves a PSD sum equal to a PSD matrix, and range additivity puts the other two supports inside the minority form's.* **So $V$ is that form's support, of even dimension at least four**, $\dim V=2$ making $\Lambda^2V$ one-dimensional. **[L178](findings/diagonal-half.md) covers $\dim V=4$ in full, so $\dim V\ge6$ IS THE WHOLE OF WHAT REMAINS**, *and [Lemma L181](findings/diagonal-half.md) empties the first family one would build there.* **On a common orthogonal splitting into planes, Ricci-flatness is the diagonal of $M^{\mathsf T}\Lambda M$ and first Bianchi is its off-diagonal, so both together force that matrix to vanish, and $MM^{\mathsf T}=I$ then gives $\Lambda=0$.** *The block-structured analogue of this record's sphere products is therefore empty at every $n$, every rank and every number of blocks, and a witness would have to be a triple with no common block splitting.*

**THE TWO CONDITIONS ARE THE CLASSICAL CURVATURE DECOMPOSITION READ SPECTRALLY, WHICH IS WHERE A LITERATURE ATTACK WOULD START.** *$S^2(\Lambda^2)$ splits as the Weyl part plus a symmetric part plus $\Lambda^4$, and [L179](findings/diagonal-half.md) and [L180](findings/diagonal-half.md) identify those last two on a spectral decomposition: the $\Lambda^4$ component is $\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i\wedge\omega_i$ and the symmetric component is $\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i^2$.* **So "rank-three Weyl tensor" is "a rank-three element of $S^2(\Lambda^2)$ lying in the Weyl summand"**, and the question is whether that summand meets the rank-three locus outside $\Lambda^2$ of a four-space. *[r774](reviews/REVIEW_r774.md) is fetching a source for the decomposition, which is the right place to look before deriving further.*

**A PARAMETER COUNT SAYS TO EXPECT EMPTINESS, AND IT IS A COUNT RATHER THAN A PROOF.** *At $\dim V=6$: the orthonormal triple in the $15$-dimensional $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^6$ is a Stiefel manifold of dimension $45-6=39$, and the unit weights summing to zero add one, giving $40$. The constraints are a symmetric $6\times6$ equation whose trace is automatic, so $20$, plus $\binom64=15$ from $\Lambda^4$, $35$ in all, leaving $5$.* **But $O(6)$ has dimension $15$ and acts on the solution set, so any solution with finite stabiliser needs a $15$-dimensional orbit inside a $5$-dimensional set.*

> **THE PARAMETER COUNT IS INVALID, AND A FAMILY THE RECORD ALREADY OWNS REFUTES IT OUTRIGHT.** *Gate 2 passed on this box* ([r824](../reviews/REVIEW_r824.md), [r825](../reviews/REVIEW_r825.md)). **Take $P\in Gr(4,6)$ and any orthonormal triple in $\Lambda^+P$**: every unit $\omega\in\Lambda^+P$ has $\omega^2=-\tfrac12\Pi_P$ and $\omega\wedge\omega=\mathrm{vol}_P$, so both constraints read $\big(\sum_i\lambda_i\big)\times(\text{one object})=0$ and **every unit traceless $\lambda$ solves them.** *That family has dimension $8+3+1=12$ where the count predicts $5$, so the count is false by seven, on a locus the record has carried since [L178](#lemma-l178-every-weyl-tensor-of-lambda2-rank-at-most-three-has-thetalesqrt32-with-equality-exactly-at-w_mathbbcp2)*, **and no equivariance, submersion or orbit argument is needed to see it** ([r825](../reviews/REVIEW_r825.md)).

> **WHY IT FAILS, AND BY HOW MUCH.** *The count reads $40-35=5$, which is the solution set's dimension only where the constraint map is a submersion.* **It never is**: the map is $O(6)$-equivariant with invariant zero set, so every orbit lies inside the zero set and in the kernel of the differential. **The stabiliser bound is sharper than first written**, $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^6$ being $\mathfrak{so}(6)$, so a triple's stabiliser algebra is the centraliser of the $3$-plane it spans, of dimension at most $4$ rather than $9$. *So orbits have dimension at least $11$, the rank is at most $29$, and at least SIX of the $35$ constraints are dependent at every solution.* **THE BOUND RUNS THE OTHER WAY ON THE FINITE-STABILISER CASE: IT CAPS THE STABILISER, SO IT PERMITS ZERO AND THE $15$-DIMENSIONAL ORBIT WITH IT.** *And that case is inhabited among orthonormal triples: $\omega_1=(e^{12}+2e^{34}+4e^{56})/\sqrt{21}$, $\omega_2=e^{13}$, $\omega_3=e^{15}$ has trivial centraliser* ([r826](../reviews/REVIEW_r826.md)). **The kill is two-step and needs no computation.** *$[\omega_2,\omega_3]=-e^{35}$, so $\omega_2,\omega_3$ generate $\mathfrak{so}(3)$ on $\operatorname{span}(e_1,e_3,e_5)$ and Schur leaves the commutant's skew part as $\mathfrak{so}(3)$ on $\operatorname{span}(e_2,e_4,e_6)$; bracketing $ae^{24}+be^{26}+ce^{46}$ against $\omega_1$ gives $-ae^{14}-2ae^{23}-be^{16}-4be^{25}-2ce^{36}-4ce^{45}$, six distinct basis elements, so $a=b=c=0$.* **The exhibit says nothing about whether a CONSTRAINED triple has trivial stabiliser, which is the open question.** *What it kills is the note's reasoning and every claim that no triple has a finite stabiliser.

> **TWO CORRECTIONS TO THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS REFUTATION, BOTH FOUND BY BOTH REVIEWERS.** *It read $\operatorname{rank}dF_p\le r$ as giving $\dim S\ge40-r$, which runs backwards: a rank bound bounds a fibre from ABOVE, and $\lVert x\rVert^2$ has rank $0$ at a zero set that is a point.* **The numbers survive for a different reason, the orbit is contained in $S$ outright, and the true constant is $11$, not $6$.** *And it concluded that the count says nothing about emptiness, which is too strong.*

> **THE COUNT'S CONCLUSION HOLDS, AND WHETHER ANY COUNT CAN CARRY IT IS DISPUTED.** *[r824](../reviews/REVIEW_r824.md) repairs it by quotienting first: $\dim(M/G)$ against the $35$ constraints leaves an expected deficit of about ten, so the note's conclusion comes back stronger than it went in on the principal stratum.* **[r825](../reviews/REVIEW_r825.md) argues no count can settle it**, the $\dim V=6$ branch being an OPEN subset of a solution set whose other component is the inhabited padded family, so any count concluding emptiness empties that too. *Exactly one of these is wrong and finding out which is the next task on this line, not an inconvenience.* **THE TWO POSITIONS ARE COMPATIBLE, AND THE SETTLEMENT THAT SAID OTHERWISE IS WITHDRAWN.** *[r827](../reviews/REVIEW_r827.md) refuted it: a dimension count applies STRATUM BY STRATUM, so a failure on one stratum does not refute it on another.* **The counterexample is explicit**, $F(v,x)=(v,\,x\lVert v\rVert^2,\,\lVert v\rVert^4)$ on $\mathbb R^2\oplus\mathbb R$ with $SO(2)$ rotating $v$, where the count fails on the singular stratum by the padded family's own mechanism and is exactly right on the principal one. *So [r824](../reviews/REVIEW_r824.md) localising at a finite-stabiliser point and [r825](../reviews/REVIEW_r825.md) exhibiting the padded failure are both correct, and "exactly one of these is wrong" was the error.* **The arithmetic was wrong too**: $\dim(M/G)=25$ is the finite-stabiliser slice, and at a padded point the orbit is $11$, so the repaired count reads $29$ against $35$ and expects $-6$ where the truth is $+1$. *And the padded family is TWO orbits times the circle rather than one, $\mathrm{Ad}$ acting on $\Lambda^\pm P$ by inner automorphisms so frame orientation is invariant.*
