> **THE CONCLUSION SURVIVES ON DIFFERENT GROUNDS.** *No count decided this branch while its stabiliser dimension was unknown*, **not because a count refuted anywhere is refuted everywhere, but because every version of it fails at the padded locus, the properly stratified one included, and the branch could not then be identified with the principal stratum** ([r827](../reviews/REVIEW_r827.md)).

> **CLOSING THE STRATIFICATION PUTS THE COUNT BACK IN SCOPE, AS EVIDENCE.** *Every solution at $\dim V=6$ now has trivial stabiliser, so the branch IS the principal stratum, which is exactly where [r827](../reviews/REVIEW_r827.md)'s counterexample found the count exact rather than false.* **So [r824](../reviews/REVIEW_r824.md)'s repair applies to it**: $\dim(M/G)=25$ against $35$ constraints expects a deficit of ten and predicts emptiness, with the padded family's objection landing on a stratum the branch no longer meets. *A count that survives its own objection is still a count*, it predicts and does not prove, **but it now points the same way as the census instead of being excluded from the question.**

> **AND "NOTHING POINTS EITHER WAY" WAS FALSE.** *Both reviewers refused it.* **The record's own $\dim V=6$ census points**, every attacked sub-case having closed empty, [L181](#lemma-l181-ricci-flatness-and-first-bianchi-are-the-two-halves-of-one-matrix-and-together-they-empty-the-simultaneously-block-diagonal-family), [L184](#lemma-l184-the-coincident-case-at-dim-v6-is-empty), [L185](#lemma-l185-the-all-decomposable-branch-is-empty), [L186](#lemma-l186-no-rank-three-weyl-tensor-has-exactly-one-non-decomposable-image-form), [L189](#lemma-l189-a-rank-three-weyl-tensor-has-three-non-decomposable-image-forms), [L191](#lemma-l191-two-forms-with-a-common-six-dimensional-support-and-both-coincidences-are-equal-up-to-sign-so-the-rank-two-branch-is-empty-at-dim-v6), *and no witness above $\dim V=4$ exists anywhere in the record.* **The count carries no weight; the census does.**

> **A NEW EXCLUSION, SUPPLIED BY [r824](../reviews/REVIEW_r824.md) AT REVIEW.** *Every rank-three triple at $\dim V=6$ whose stabiliser has dimension at least three is now excluded*: the $\mathfrak t^3$ case by [L181](#lemma-l181-ricci-flatness-and-first-bianchi-are-the-two-halves-of-one-matrix-and-together-they-empty-the-simultaneously-block-diagonal-family), the $\mathfrak{so}(3)$ case by the support, and the $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ case by a half-page computation new to the record. **So a surviving solution has stabiliser of dimension at most two**, and with dimensions two and one since closed it has TRIVIAL stabiliser and orbit exactly $15$. 

> **THE DIMENSION-TWO CASE NARROWS, AND DOES NOT CLOSE.** *A stabiliser algebra is a subalgebra of $\mathfrak{so}(6)$, which is compact, so it is reductive; a two-dimensional reductive algebra is abelian, and $\mathfrak z(U)$ at dimension two is therefore a $2$-torus.* **A REGULAR $2$-torus closes the case**: no root of $D_3$ vanishes on a regular $\mathfrak t^2$, since the roots are $\pm e_i\pm e_j$ and none has two zero coordinates, so $\mathfrak z(\mathfrak t^2)=\mathfrak t^3$, *and $U\subseteq\mathfrak z(\mathfrak z(U))=\mathfrak t^3$ with $\dim U=3$ forces $U=\mathfrak t^3$, whose centraliser is $\mathfrak t^3$ itself at dimension three, against the hypothesis.* **What survives is a SINGULAR $2$-torus**, one on which a root vanishes, for instance $\{(a,a,b)\}$, where $e_1-e_2$ dies and the centraliser grows to the five-dimensional $\mathfrak u(2)\oplus\mathfrak u(1)$, leaving room for a $3$-plane inside it. *The regular case's argument wants a REGULAR ELEMENT $H$ in the torus, giving $\mathfrak z(\mathfrak t^2)\subseteq\mathfrak z(H)=\mathfrak t^3$; "no root has two zero coordinates" proves only that no COORDINATE $2$-plane is singular, which is weaker* ([r827](../reviews/REVIEW_r827.md)).

> **AND THE SINGULAR CASE IS CLOSED AT REVIEW, WHICH CLOSES DIMENSION TWO ENTIRELY.** *For an orthonormal triple in $\mathfrak k=\mathbb Ra\oplus\Lambda^-P\oplus\mathbb R\varepsilon$, writing $\omega_i=\alpha_ia+m_i+c_i\varepsilon$, the two constraints are exactly $$\alpha^{\mathsf T}\Lambda\alpha=c^{\mathsf T}\Lambda c=\alpha^{\mathsf T}\Lambda c=0,\qquad M^{\mathsf T}(\Lambda\alpha)=M^{\mathsf T}(\Lambda c)=0.$$ The first three say $\operatorname{span}(\alpha,c)$ is totally isotropic for a form of signature $(2,1)$, hence at most a line, and the rank-one shape of $MM^{\mathsf T}=I-(p^2+q^2)uu^{\mathsf T}$ kills the line.* **So $\alpha=c=0$, the triple spans $\Lambda^-P$, and NO SOLUTION HAS A TWO-DIMENSIONAL STABILISER AT ANY $\dim V$** ([r827](../reviews/REVIEW_r827.md), audited and independently re-derived by [r826](../reviews/REVIEW_r826.md)). *With [r824](../reviews/REVIEW_r824.md)'s exclusion above it and the dimension-one case closed below, a witness at $\dim V=6$ has trivial stabiliser and orbit $15$.*

> **TWO STEPS CARRY THE CLOSURE AND NEITHER IS THE ISOTROPY, WHICH IS THE EASY HALF.** *The reduction is the first*: $\dim\mathfrak z(U)=2$ makes $\mathfrak z(U)$ abelian, hence toral in a compact algebra, and a SINGULAR $2$-torus is conjugate to $\ker(e_1-e_2)$ because $D_3$ is simply laced and its Weyl group is transitive on roots. *So $U\subseteq\mathfrak z(\mathfrak z(U))=\mathfrak k$, and $\mathfrak k$ is the ONLY shape available, no two root pairs of $D_3$ being proportional and therefore no $2$-plane killing more than one.* **Without it the computation is about one $\mathfrak k$ and the claim about all of them.**

> **THE SECOND IS THE DEGENERATE BRANCH OF THE RANK-ONE KILL, WHERE THE ARGUMENT IS TIGHT.** *Writing $\alpha=pu$, $c=qu$ on the isotropic line and $\gamma^2=p^2+q^2$, orthonormality is $MM^{\mathsf T}=I_3-\gamma^2uu^{\mathsf T}$, a Gram matrix, so $\gamma^2\lvert u\rvert^2\le1$ and the branches are exhaustive.* **Below equality $M$ is invertible and $M^{\mathsf T}(\Lambda u)=0$ gives $\Lambda u=0$ at once**, hence $u=0$ at nonzero weights. *At equality $M$ is singular and that step is unavailable*: $\lVert M^{\mathsf T}u\rVert^2=\lvert u\rvert^2(1-\gamma^2\lvert u\rvert^2)=0$ puts $u$ in $\ker M^{\mathsf T}=\mathbb Ru$ exactly, so $\Lambda u=\kappa u$, and $u^{\mathsf T}\Lambda u=\kappa\lvert u\rvert^2=0$ forces $\kappa=0$, then $\lambda_iu_i=0$ at every $i$ and $u=0$ against $\gamma^2\lvert u\rvert^2=1$. **The equality case is attainable a priori**, so it is closed rather than excluded ([r826](../reviews/REVIEW_r826.md)). *$\lambda_i\ne0$ is load-bearing in both branches and is the rank-three setting's own hypothesis.*

> **AND DIMENSION ONE IS EMPTY TOO, SO A WITNESS AT $\dim V=6$ HAS TRIVIAL STABILISER EXACTLY.** *A one-dimensional $\mathfrak z(U)=\mathbb RX$ puts $U$ inside $\mathfrak z(X)$, and the centraliser of a nonzero element of $\mathfrak{so}(6)$ takes exactly four shapes, by the eigenvalue pattern of its normal form: $\mathfrak t^3$, $\mathfrak k$, $\Lambda^2Q\oplus\Lambda^2Q^\perp$ at $\dim Q=2$, and $\mathfrak u(3)$.* **The first two contradict themselves on dimension**, $\mathfrak t^3$ being its own centraliser and $\mathfrak k$ forcing $U=\Lambda^-P$ with a four-dimensional centraliser; **the seven-dimensional branch closes by the rank-one lemma with Sylvester on $\mathcal U\Lambda\mathcal V^{\mathsf T}=0$** ([r826](../reviews/REVIEW_r826.md), audited and re-derived by [r828](../reviews/REVIEW_r828.md) and [r829](../reviews/REVIEW_r829.md)).

> **THE $\mathfrak u(3)$ BRANCH CLOSES IN THREE STEPS, AND EVERY ONE IS AN EQUALITY CASE.** *Identify $\mathbb R^6$ with $\mathbb C^3$ and write $\omega_i=iH_i$ with $H_i$ hermitian, so orthonormality is $\operatorname{tr}(H_iH_j)=\delta_{ij}$, Ricci is $\sum_i\lambda_iH_i^2=0$, and first Bianchi is $\sum_i\lambda_i\operatorname{adj}(H_i)=0$, an EXTRA condition rather than an implied one.*

> - **Bianchi forces $U\subseteq\mathfrak{su}(3)$.** *Cayley–Hamilton turns $\operatorname{adj}(H)$ into $H^2-(\operatorname{tr}H)H+e_2(H)I$, and Ricci kills the square term, leaving $\sum_i\lambda_i\tau_iH_i=\tfrac12\big(\sum_i\lambda_i\tau_i^2\big)I$ at $\tau_i=\operatorname{tr}H_i$.* **Its trace reads $\sum\lambda_i\tau_i^2=\tfrac32\sum\lambda_i\tau_i^2$, so both sides vanish**, and the $H_i$ being orthonormal are independent, giving $\lambda_i\tau_i=0$ and hence $\tau_i=0$ at nonzero weights.
> - **Ricci then forces a common square.** *At traceless unit hermitian $H$ on $\mathbb C^3$, Newton's identities give $\operatorname{tr}H^4=\tfrac12$, so $\lVert(H^2)_0\rVert^2=\tfrac12-\tfrac13=\tfrac16$ at EVERY such $H$.* **With $\sum\lambda_i=0$ the display $\sum_i\lambda_i(H_i^2)_0=0$ is a triangle inequality at equality**, three vectors of one length and weights summing to zero, so $H_1^2=H_2^2=H_3^2$.
> - **Equal squares classify.** *A common square with distinct eigenvalues has only commuting square roots, spanning at most the two-dimensional traceless diagonal algebra, which cannot hold three orthonormal elements.* **So the square is $\tfrac12I_2\oplus0$ and $H_i=\tfrac1{\sqrt2}(n_i\cdot\vec\sigma)\oplus0$ at an orthonormal $n_i\in S^2$**, inhabited, and its forms share a kernel, so $\dim V=4$ and the padded family is recovered rather than a witness at six.

> **SO AT $\dim V=6$ A SOLUTION HAS $\mathfrak z(U)=0$ AND ORBIT $15$**, and the finite-stabiliser stratum the count reasoned about is the only one left. *Both reviewers reached the closure independently on identical briefs, and the parent reproduced the whole chain by its own route before applying it.*

> **THREE DEFECTS IN THE WRITE-UP THE AUDIT QUOTED, EACH FOUND BY BOTH READERS.** *$\mathcal V\mathcal V^{\mathsf T}$ should read $\mathcal V^{\mathsf T}\mathcal V$, the two sides living in different copies of $\mathbb R^3$; the appeal to [r824](../reviews/REVIEW_r824.md) §7 in the $\operatorname{rank}\mathcal U=0$ branch is the rank-one lemma's own special case and is avoidable; and "§5 applies verbatim" is true of §5.1 to §5.4 only, §5.5's reduction being the sole consumer of $\dim\mathfrak z(U)=2$.* **The conclusion stands at every one of them.**

> **THAT CLOSURE IS A REVIEWER'S OWN DERIVATION AND IS UNAUDITED.** *It arrived inside a Gate 2 report, which is where the record has twice before taken a reviewer's construction as a verdict.* **It owes its own round**, and until it has one it is cited as r827's and not as the record's, *the same footing [L184](#lemma-l184-the-coincident-case-at-dim-v6-is-empty)'s pair form was held on until [L191](#lemma-l191-two-forms-with-a-common-six-dimensional-support-and-both-coincidences-are-equal-up-to-sign-so-the-rank-two-branch-is-empty-at-dim-v6) audited it.*

**THE CLAIM RESTS ON [L178](findings/diagonal-half.md) AND NOT ON [L179](findings/diagonal-half.md)–[L180](findings/diagonal-half.md) ALONE, WHICH AN AUDIT ESTABLISHED THE HARD WAY.** *[r773](reviews/REVIEW_r773.md) and [r774](reviews/REVIEW_r774.md) each refuted "$\dim V\ge6$ is the whole of what remains" in closed form, independently, with the same exhibit, $2\,\omega_1\otimes\omega_1-\omega_2\otimes\omega_2-\omega_3\otimes\omega_3$ on the self-dual forms of an $\mathbb R^4$, rank three with $\dim V=4$.* **Both refutations are correct against the brief they were given and land on nothing in the record**, because that brief withheld L178, which settles $\dim V=4$. *The exhibit is $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ up to scale and is L178's own equality case.* **What the episode shows is that the sentence is only true with L178 in scope, so it is written that way here and nowhere alone.**

**THE $\dim V=6$ CASE HAS A CONCRETE FORM, AND THE ADJUGATE COMES BACK.** *At $\dim V=6$ the Hodge star is an isomorphism $\Lambda^4\to\Lambda^2$, and on $\omega=\sum_jc_j\varepsilon_j$ in normal form it sends $\omega\wedge\omega$ to $2(c_2c_3\varepsilon_1+c_1c_3\varepsilon_2+c_1c_2\varepsilon_3)$*, **the Pfaffian adjugate**, written $\mathrm{Padj}(\omega)=\tfrac12\star(\omega\wedge\omega)$. *So the remaining case asks for an orthonormal triple in $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^6$ with weights summing to zero satisfying two quadratic conditions of the same shape in different targets:* $$\textstyle\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i^2=0\ \text{in }S^2,\qquad \sum_i\lambda_i\,\mathrm{Padj}(\omega_i)=0\ \text{in }\Lambda^2.$$ **This is the same operator that runs [L178](findings/diagonal-half.md)**, where $W^\#=2\operatorname{cof}W$ on $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ and the bound came out of a discriminant, *at $\dim V=4$ the adjugate is the whole of $\#$, and at $\dim V=6$ it is what Bianchi becomes.* **The echo is a reason to attack this case with $\#$'s own algebra rather than with a fresh construction.** **AND THE RECORD NOW HOLDS TWO INDEPENDENT INSTANCES OF RICCI AND BIANCHI BEING TIED, WHICH NEITHER BLOCK CROSS-REFERENCES.** *In [L166](findings/ungraded-slack.md) they are the **same** linear condition $am+b(m+2)=0$ on the Kähler family; in [L181](findings/diagonal-half.md) they are **complementary** halves of one matrix, the diagonal and the off-diagonal.* **The two are different phenomena, coincidence in one, partition in the other, and what they share is that a pair of conditions the programme prices separately turns out to be one object.** *That is the shape to look for at $\dim V=6$: not a candidate triple, but the object whose two parts these are.* **AND [Lemma L183](findings/diagonal-half.md) FINDS IT.** *With $\Phi(\omega)=(\omega^2,\omega\wedge\omega)$, Ricci and Bianchi are the single equation $\sum_i\lambda_i\Phi(\omega_i)=0$, and weights summing to zero and vanishing nowhere make that exactly **collinearity of the three images**.* **So a rank-three Weyl tensor IS an orthonormal triple with collinear images, the weights fixed up to scale by the ratio.** *It explains $n=4$: there the images **coincide**, every $\omega_i^2=-\tfrac12\mathrm{Id}$ and every $\omega_i\wedge\omega_i=f_{1234}$, so every weight vector works and [L178](findings/diagonal-half.md)'s family is two-parameter.* **The concrete next question is the coincident sub-case at $\dim V=6$**: three orthonormal complex structures on $\mathbb R^6$, pairwise trace-orthogonal, sharing a common $J\wedge J$, *The block first called those quaternionic relations and so found a dimension obstruction; that conflated trace-orthogonality with **anticommutation**, and only the latter wants $4\mid n$.* **So no dimension obstruction is available, and the question is open.** **BUT THE SCALAR SUB-CASE CLOSES**, *on $\mathbb R^6$ every compatible complex structure has $\star(J\wedge J)=2\epsilon_JJ$, so equal $J_i\wedge J_i$ forces $J_i=\pm K$ for one $K$, and then $\operatorname{tr}(J_iJ_j)=\mp6\ne0$ against trace-orthogonality.* **AND [Lemma L184](findings/diagonal-half.md) CLOSES THE COINCIDENT CASE ENTIRELY AT $\dim V=6$. GATE 2 PASSED, and by a route using NO WEYL HYPOTHESIS AT ALL** ([r781](reviews/REVIEW_r781.md), [r782](reviews/REVIEW_r782.md), both deriving the key identities independently). *What it proves is that no orthonormal triple of $2$-forms with a common six-dimensional support has both its squares and its self-wedges coincident; one mechanism runs all three cases, the hypotheses forcing $\omega_i=\pm\omega_1$.*, in three separable cases set by how many eigenvalues of the common $A=-\omega_i^2$ agree. *Pairwise distinct: each $\omega_i$ preserves $A$'s eigenplanes, so $\omega_i=\sum_js_{ij}c_j\varepsilon_j$ and equal wedges force the sign products $s_{ij}s_{ik}$ to be $i$-independent, giving $\omega_i=\eta_i\omega_1$ against orthogonality. All equal: complex structures, and $\star_V(J\wedge J)=2\epsilon_JJ$ collapses them to $\pm K$. Exactly two equal: the CROSS TERM in $\Lambda^2Q\otimes\Lambda^2P$ gives $\omega_i=\tau_i\tau_1\omega_1$, and orthogonality then reduces to $4a=-2b$.* **Case 1's appeal to [L181](findings/diagonal-half.md) is retired, that lemma's conclusion is about a $W\in\mathcal W_n$ and cannot reach a bare triple, and case 3 closes on the cross term rather than on a count of three signs, which is false for two.** **So what remains of [I38](../plan.md#section-1-advance-the-proofs) is ONE case, the images distinct and collinear.** *Polarised, that case is concrete: $\Phi$ is quadratic, so with $u=\omega_1-\omega_3$, $v=\omega_1+\omega_3$, $p=\omega_2-\omega_3$, $q=\omega_2+\omega_3$, collinearity reads* $$\{u,v\}=t\,\{p,q\}\ \text{in }S^2,\qquad u\wedge v=t\,p\wedge q\ \text{in }\Lambda^4$$ *for one scalar $t\notin\{0,1\}$, the anticommutator and the wedge being the two polarisations of $\omega\mapsto\omega^2$ and $\omega\mapsto\omega\wedge\omega$.* **Recorded as a reformulation, unaudited, settling nothing.** *Nothing above reaches $\dim V\ge8$, where case 1's argument survives whenever the $c_j^2$ are pairwise distinct and the other two are untried.* **At $\dim V\ge8$ the trichotomy does not apply at all**, it is a split on the three values six dimensions supply, and at eight there are four, a pattern like $(a,a,b,b)$ falling outside every case; only case 1's argument transfers. *Every contradiction uses only a **pair**, and that is now [Lemma L191](findings/diagonal-half.md), Gate 2 passed by [r808](reviews/REVIEW_r808.md) and [r809](reviews/REVIEW_r809.md)*: **two $2$-forms with a common six-dimensional support, equal squares and equal self-wedges satisfy $\omega_2=\pm\omega_1$**, orthogonality being spent once at the last line. *[r783](reviews/REVIEW_r783.md) flagged it and declined to adopt it, which was right at the time; the audit has since been run.* *A first draft claimed equal squares alone force a complex structure; they do not, only $A=cI$ does, which is why the closure reaches the scalar case and no further.* **Gate 2 in flight on [L183](findings/diagonal-half.md), with all four repairs sent to both reviewers mid-round.** *Derived 2026-09-08 and UNAUDITED; it is a reformulation and settles nothing on its own.* *That is a reason to expect no witness and no reason to believe it; the count assumes the constraints are independent, which is exactly what a degenerate locus violates.* **It is recorded here rather than in [findings/](findings/) because it establishes nothing.**, an orthonormal triple with weights summing to zero satisfying both the PSD identity and $\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i\wedge\omega_i=0$, spanning six dimensions or more. *Padded $\mathbb{HP}^2$ is outside the family either way.* **Gate 2 owed on L178 (in flight) and on L179.**

**WHERE THE ITEM STANDS AFTER GATE 2 ON [L190](findings/diagonal-half.md), 2026-09-09. TWO OPEN QUESTIONS RATHER THAN ONE.** *At RANK THREE the image-form census is complete: zero non-decomposable is empty by [L185](findings/diagonal-half.md), one by [L186](findings/diagonal-half.md), two by [L189](findings/diagonal-half.md) at every dimension, and three is INHABITED at $\dim V=4$ by a five-parameter family, any orthonormal basis of $\Lambda^+\mathbb R^4$ with any nonzero traceless weight vector.* **So the rank-three question is exactly whether that case reaches $\dim V>4$.** *At RANK TWO the two forms share a support, and [L191](findings/diagonal-half.md), the audited pair form of [L184](findings/diagonal-half.md), empties $\dim V=6$: Ricci gives $\omega_1^2=\omega_2^2$ and Bianchi gives $\omega_1\wedge\omega_1=\omega_2\wedge\omega_2$, which are exactly its hypotheses.* **The branch is INHABITED at $\dim V=4$**, any orthonormal pair in $\Lambda^+\mathbb R^4$ meets every condition, both reviewers finding it independently, **and OPEN at $\dim V\ge8$**, where what survives is the stratum with a repeated eigenvalue of $-\omega_1^2$, case 1's argument being dimension-free. *Nothing bounds that survivor: $\sum c_j^2=1$ is satisfiable at every $m$, so there is no analogue of [L187](findings/diagonal-half.md)'s congruence.*

**AND THE $\dim V=8$ QUESTION HAS A SHAPE.** *Under [L189](findings/diagonal-half.md)'s rescaling both legs of its contradiction break: Ricci loses the residual semidefinite bump that made the determinant step strict, and Minkowski's determinant inequality replaces it, saturated at padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$, so the determinant route is dead at $\dim V=4$ for a dimension-free reason.* **On the Bianchi leg the cross term $2(M_2^{\wedge2}\wedge M_3^{\wedge2})$ lives in $\Lambda^8V$**, so it is $0=0$ at $\dim V\in\{4,6\}$ and at $\dim V=8$ it is the whole obstruction, *setting it to zero closes the case, and [r807](reviews/REVIEW_r807.md) bounds it below by $2\sqrt{\mathrm{Pf}(M_2)\,\mathrm{Pf}(M_3)}$, unaudited.* **The first open dimension is six, where neither instrument the block offered has a referent.**

#### Lemma L192, the Bianchi defect IS the weighted Laplacian of the Pfaffian, in every even dimension

*Gate 2 passed: [r817](../reviews/REVIEW_r817.md) and [r818](../reviews/REVIEW_r818.md), identical briefs. **Both CONFIRMED the two numbered statements and every constant in them, and both REFUTED the block's account of its own scope**, independently and on the same three points. The general identity below is theirs; the first version of this block had it at $\dim V=6$ alone and called the other dimensions vacuous.*

**Statement.** Let $W=\sum_{i=1}^3\lambda_i\,\omega_i\otimes\omega_i$ be a rank-three element of $\mathcal W_n$ supported on $\Lambda^2V$ with $\dim V=2m$, write $w(x)=\sum_i x_i\omega_i$, put $$L=\sum_{i=1}^3\lambda_i\frac{\partial^2}{\partial x_i^2},\qquad \mathcal B=\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i\wedge\omega_i\in\Lambda^4V,$$ so that $\mathcal B=0$ is first Bianchi ([L179](#lemma-l179-the-support-of-a-low-rank-weyl-tensor-and-the-first-bianchi-identity-read-on-the-spectrum)(b)).

1. **Ricci-flatness IS $L\big[w(x)^2\big]=0$**, an identity between CONSTANT matrices: $L[w^2]=2\sum_i\lambda_i\omega_i^2$ carries no $x$.
2. **THE WEIGHTED LAPLACIAN OF THE PFAFFIAN IS THE BIANCHI DEFECT, TESTED:** $$L\big[\mathrm{Pf}(w(x))\big]=\frac{1}{(m-2)!}\,\star\big[\mathcal B\wedge w(x)^{m-2}\big]$$ **at every $m\ge2$.** *So first Bianchi implies $L[\mathrm{Pf}(w)]=0$ in every even dimension, and the implication is not a $\dim V=6$ phenomenon.*

**What $m=3$ supplies is not the identity but the TEST SPACE.** *At $m=3$ the factor $w^{m-2}$ is $w$ itself, so harmonicity tests $\mathcal B$ against $w(x)$ and, over all $x$, against the three-dimensional span and nothing more*, three of the fifteen conditions $\mathcal B=0$ carries in $\Lambda^4\mathbb R^6$.

**THE CONVERSE HOLDS AT $m=2$ AND THE REFORMULATION IS SHARPEST THERE.** *At $\dim V=4$ the factor is $w^0=1$, so $L[\mathrm{Pf}(w)]=\star\mathcal B$ is the Bianchi defect entire and harmonicity is EQUIVALENT to first Bianchi.* **The first version of this block called that case vacuous, having read "$L$ applied to a quadratic returns a constant" as "returns no information"; the constant IS the defect.**

**Proof.** *(1)* $\partial_i^2\big(w^2\big)=2\omega_i^2$, so $L[w^2]=2\sum_i\lambda_i\omega_i^2$, and [L180](#lemma-l180-ricci-flatness-is-a-signed-psd-identity-and-it-names-the-support)(1) is that this vanishes. Both directions are immediate, the left side carrying no $x$ to quantify over.

*(2)* $\mathrm{Pf}(w)=\star\big(w^{m}/m!\big)$, and $\partial_i^2\,w^{m}=m(m-1)\,\omega_i\wedge\omega_i\wedge w^{m-2}$ since the wedge of $2$-forms is commutative. Summing against the weights gives $L[\mathrm{Pf}(w)]=\tfrac1{(m-2)!}\star\big[\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i\wedge\omega_i\wedge w^{m-2}\big]$, which is the display. $\square$

> **IN COORDINATES AT $m=3$ IT IS THREE LINEAR EQUATIONS ON ONE SYMMETRIC ARRAY.** *With $c_{ijk}=(\omega_i\wedge\omega_j\wedge\omega_k)/\mathrm{vol}$ totally symmetric, harmonicity reads $\sum_i\lambda_i\,c_{iik}=0$ for $k=1,2,3$*, three conditions on the ten independent entries. **RELABEL SO THAT $\omega_1$ CARRIES THE MINORITY SIGN**, which the weight pattern permits in either case; then [L180](#lemma-l180-ricci-flatness-is-a-signed-psd-identity-and-it-names-the-support)(3) makes $V$ its support and $c_{111}=6\,\mathrm{Pf}(\omega_1)\ne0$ at $\dim V=6$. *Without that clause the index is wrong under $(+,+,-)$, where the minority form is $\omega_3$ and $\mathrm{Pf}(\omega_1)$ may vanish, a rank-four non-decomposable $\omega_1$ being permitted by [L189](#lemma-l189-a-rank-three-weyl-tensor-has-three-non-decomposable-image-forms); both reviewers found it.*

> **WHETHER HARMONICITY IS STRICTLY WEAKER THAN BIANCHI AT $m=3$ IS OPEN, AND THE FIRST VERSION ASSERTED IT.** *A dimension count over $\Lambda^2$ is not an argument on the constrained set.* **[r817](../reviews/REVIEW_r817.md) proves the two EQUIVALENT on the record's coordinate-diagonal family**, where the three forms share one orthogonal splitting into planes, and **[r818](../reviews/REVIEW_r818.md) exhibits strictness off the Ricci-flat class** at $e^{12}$, $e^{34}$, $\tfrac1{\sqrt2}(e^{13}+e^{56})$ with $\lambda=(1,1,-2)$. *So the question is exactly whether strictness survives Ricci-flatness, and that is unanswered.*

> **WHAT IT DOES NOT DO.** *It settles nothing: (1) restates a hypothesis the record carries and (2) is a consequence of another.* **What it changes is the shape of the question**, a rank-three Weyl tensor at $\dim V=6$ is an orthonormal triple whose Pfaffian cubic is harmonic for a signature-$(+,-,-)$ Laplacian with $c_{111}\ne0$, *and it hands the open $\dim V=8$ branch the same reformulation, where the test element is $w\wedge w$.* **Recorded as a reformulation and not citable as a closure.**

#### Lemma L193, the Pfaffian adjugate satisfies the adjugate law, $\omega\,\sigma(\omega)=-\mathrm{Pf}(\omega)\,\mathrm{Id}$

*Derived 2026-09-09. **Gate 2 passed**, [r820](../reviews/REVIEW_r820.md) confirmed the identity, the sign, the normal-form proof with its degeneracies and all four coefficients of the polarisation, recomputing them from `star6` and `pf6` rather than from this block; [r821](../reviews/REVIEW_r821.md) confirmed the debt reduction and independently derived the extremal configuration behind [F63](fence-posts.md). **[r819](../reviews/REVIEW_r819.md) was dispatched and died mid-round**, which nothing caught, and the watchdog the skill asks for is now armed at dispatch.* Screened on random skew forms at $\dim6$ before recording, the off-diagonal residue being at machine zero, and the screen is reported as a screen.

**Statement.** On $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^6$, with $\sigma(\omega)=\tfrac12\star(\omega\wedge\omega)$ the Pfaffian adjugate, $$\omega\,\sigma(\omega)=-\mathrm{Pf}(\omega)\,\mathrm{Id}_6$$ as a product of skew matrices, at every $\omega$.

**This is $A\,\mathrm{adj}(A)=\det(A)\,\mathrm{Id}$ for the Pfaffian**, and it is STRICTLY STRONGER than [L187](#lemma-l187-two-non-decomposable-image-forms-force-dim-vequiv0pmod4-with-dim-vge8)'s $\sigma\circ\sigma=\mathrm{Pf}\cdot\mathrm{id}$, which it implies in five lines: *transpose, take determinants to get $\mathrm{Pf}\circ\sigma=\mathrm{Pf}^2$, apply the law at $\sigma(\omega)$, invert, extend by density* ([r820](../reviews/REVIEW_r820.md)). **The converse is unavailable**, L187 living on a pair-spectrum model with no matrix product in it. *An earlier version said neither follows from the other by inspection, which is one-directionally wrong.* **At $V\subsetneq\mathbb R^n$ the identity reads $\mathrm{Id}_V$ and not $\mathrm{Id}_n$**, being false off $V$.

**Proof.** Take a normal frame for $\omega$, so that $\omega=\sum_{j=1}^3c_j\varepsilon_j$ with $\varepsilon_j$ the area form of the $j$-th plane. On pair spectra $\sigma$ is the adjugate, so $\sigma(\omega)=\sum_j\big(\textstyle\prod_{k\ne j}c_k\big)\varepsilon_j$. **The two are simultaneously block-diagonal for that splitting**, and on the $j$-th plane $\omega$ acts as $c_jJ$ and $\sigma(\omega)$ as $\big(\prod_{k\ne j}c_k\big)J$, so their product is $c_1c_2c_3\,J^2=-\mathrm{Pf}(\omega)\,\mathrm{Id}_2$. *The value is the same on all three planes*, which is what makes the sum a multiple of the identity rather than a block-diagonal matrix. $\square$

> **WHY THE THREE BLOCKS AGREE IS THE WHOLE OF IT.** *Each plane contributes $c_j\cdot\prod_{k\ne j}c_k$, which is the full product $c_1c_2c_3$ however the factor is split*, so the identity says that the adjugate is exactly the factor which, plane by plane, completes $\omega$ to its own Pfaffian.

> **THE POLARISED FORM, WHICH IS WHAT THREE DISTINCT FORMS NEED.** *Writing $\beta(\alpha,\eta)=\star(\alpha\wedge\eta)$, so that $\sigma(\omega)=\tfrac12\beta(\omega,\omega)$, the identity polarises to* $$\alpha\,\beta(\eta,\zeta)+\eta\,\beta(\alpha,\zeta)+\zeta\,\beta(\alpha,\eta)=-\big\langle\beta(\alpha,\eta),\zeta\big\rangle\,\mathrm{Id}_6$$ **at every three $2$-forms on $\mathbb R^6$**, totally symmetric in them. *Screened on random triples, the off-diagonal residue at machine zero; the screen is reported as a screen.* **L193 is the case $\alpha=\eta=\zeta$**, and the polarised form is what reaches a sum over a triple, where L193 alone reaches only its diagonal terms.

> **AND IT TURNS HARMONICITY INTO A MATRIX IDENTITY.** *Setting $\alpha=\eta=\omega_i$ and $\zeta$ arbitrary, then summing against the weights, the left side is $2\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i\beta(\omega_i,\zeta)+\zeta\star\!\mathcal B$ and the right is $-\langle\star\mathcal B,\zeta\rangle\mathrm{Id}$, where $\mathcal B$ is the Bianchi defect.* **At $\zeta$ in the span the right side vanishes by harmonicity** ([L192](#lemma-l192-the-bianchi-defect-is-the-weighted-laplacian-of-the-pfaffian-in-every-even-dimension)), leaving $$\zeta\,\star\!\mathcal B=-2\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i\,\beta(\omega_i,\zeta)\qquad\text{for }\zeta\in\mathrm{span}(\omega_1,\omega_2,\omega_3).$$ *So the defect is pinned by a sum the Ricci condition constrains, which is the first place the two Weyl conditions meet as matrices rather than as scalars.* **Whether that closes $\dim V=6$ is unanswered, and the sum on the right is what a successor has to price.**

> **AND IT DISCHARGES [L184](#lemma-l184-the-coincident-case-at-dim-v6-is-empty)'S STANDING HYPOTHESIS-DEBT.** *For a complex structure $J$ on $V$, $J^2=-\mathrm{Id}$ gives $J^{-1}=-J$, and the Statement becomes $\star(J\wedge J)=2\,\mathrm{Pf}(J)\,J$*, **which is L184 case 2's owed $\star_V(J\wedge J)=2\epsilon_JJ$, with the constant, the base sign and $\epsilon_J=\pm1$ all settled** ([r820](../reviews/REVIEW_r820.md)). *That debt was carried as an unproved `Prop` in the Lean layer and named in four places.* **The reduction is typed** as `starWedge_of_adjugate`, taking the adjugate law and $\mathrm{Pf}(J)=\pm1$ as hypotheses and returning the identity, *so the two debts are one*, and both are proved: the adjugate law as `adjugate_law` and $\mathrm{Pf}(J)=\pm1$ as `pf6_complexStructure` in [I67PfSign.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I67PfSign.lean), closing the identity as `starWedgeIdentity_holds`.

**THE THREE UNKNOWNS ARE COMPUTED, 2026-09-17** ([screen_I67_star_wedge.py](../scripts/misc/screen_I67_star_wedge.py)), and the Lean statement `StarWedgeIdentity` is now PROVED (below). *The layer once carried it as an unproved `Prop`, with the docstring saying neither the constant, nor the base sign, nor the two-orbit fact behind $\epsilon_J$'s well-definedness had a computation on file.* **All three now do.**

- **The anchor** $\star e_{0123}=e_{45}$, which the layer flagged as taken from the record and unchecked, holds EXACTLY and with no other entries.
- **$\epsilon_J=\mathrm{Pf}(J)$, and the base sign is $-1$ UNDER THE LAYER'S OWN $\varphi=E_{ba}-E_{ab}$**, a convention that must be stated wherever the value is, since $\mathrm{Pf}$ is odd in degree three on $\mathbb R^6$ so the opposite form-to-matrix convention gives $+1$ ([r990](../reviews/REVIEW_r990.md), [r991](../reviews/REVIEW_r991.md), both reading the other orientation from a brief that never fixed $\varphi$). *Settled in the KERNEL: the proved `sigma_0_1` gives $\sigma(\mathrm{stdJ6})_{01}=+1$ while $\mathrm{stdJ6}_{01}=-1$, so $\sigma(J)=-J$.* At `stdJ6 = φ₀₁+φ₂₃+φ₄₅`, that matrix being three blocks $\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&-1\\1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)$ so $\mathrm{Pf}=(-1)^3$. *This confirms the formula above rather than the layer's neutral $\pm1$: $\star_6(J\wedge J)=-2J$ at `stdJ6`.*
- **Both signs occur, one per $SO(6)$ orbit**, with $\mathrm{Pf}(RAR^{\mathsf T})=\det(R)\mathrm{Pf}(A)$ checked in each, so $\epsilon_J$ is genuinely two-valued and well defined.

**AND THE IDENTITY REDUCES TO THE LAYER'S OWN `triple6`.** *Since $\star_6$ and `triple6` share `levi6`, antisymmetry makes the three wedge terms equal and collapses the $1/24$ against the $1/48$:*
$$\sum_{a,b}\big(\star_6(X\wedge X)\big)_{ab}Z_{ab}=12\,\mathrm{triple6}(Z,X,X)\qquad\text{at EVERY }X,Z,$$

*the pairing being the FULL double sum: the constant is $12$ over $\sum_{a,b}$ and $6$ over $\sum_{a<b}$, and both readers flagged that the brief left it unfixed* ([r990](../reviews/REVIEW_r990.md), [r991](../reviews/REVIEW_r991.md)),
**with no hypothesis whatever, skewness included**, verified to $1.8\cdot10^{-14}$ on arbitrary pairs. *The proof is three relabellings, and the signs are pinned. With $A=\sum\varepsilon_{abcdef}Z_{ab}X_{cd}X_{ef}$, $B=\sum\varepsilon Z_{ab}X_{ce}X_{df}$ and $C=\sum\varepsilon Z_{ab}X_{cf}X_{de}$: transposing $d$ with $e$ gives $B=-A$, transposing $e$ with $f$ gives $C=-B$, and composing the two gives $C=+A$, so the wedge's $X_{cd}X_{ef}-X_{ce}X_{df}+X_{cf}X_{de}$ contracts to $3A$. Then the left side is $\tfrac1{24}\cdot2\cdot3A=\tfrac A4$ and the right is $12\cdot\tfrac1{48}A=\tfrac A4$.* **Those two NEIGHBOURING transpositions are the whole of what a Lean proof needs**, and they are recorded here so a later cycle does not rediscover the signs. **The substitution carrying $C$ to $A$ is the CYCLE $(d,e,f)\mapsto(f,d,e)$, which is EVEN**, so the two sign flips cancel and the two swaps `Finset.sum_comm` supplies are exactly enough. *Reading it instead as the reversal $(d,e,f)\mapsto(f,e,d)$ costs a cycle: that permutation is odd, needs three adjacent swaps, and a proof built on it fails while the numerics keep confirming the target, which reads as the statement being wrong rather than the route.* *So `StarWedgeIdentity` is exactly $6\,\mathrm{triple6}(Z,J,J)=\mathrm{pf6}(J)\,\langle J,Z\rangle$, a statement about objects [Pfaffian6.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Base/Pfaffian6.lean) already carries with its additivity and homogeneity lemmas.* **That reduction is the whole of what [I67](../plan.md) still owes, and Gate 2 is owed on it.**

**AND THE LAYER'S STATED REASON FOR ITS `sorry` IS WRONG, WHICH MOVES THE DEBT.** *[L193](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L193Statement.lean)'s `adjugate_law`, $X\sigma(X)=-\mathrm{Pf}(X)\mathrm{Id}$, carries a `sorry` justified as needing "the real skew normal form together with the $SO(6)$-equivariance of $\star$, $\wedge$ and the Pfaffian", with the note that Mathlib carries neither in a usable shape.* **That describes the frame-adapted proof strategy, not the statement.** **The law is PROVED**, not screened: sympy expands $X\sigma(X)+\mathrm{Pf}(X)\mathrm{Id}$ over the $15$ free entries to identically zero, with controls that $\mathrm{Pf}$ and $\sigma$ are nonzero and that the OPPOSITE sign fails. *Both Gate 2 readers supplied their own proofs and both quantified why the parent's $300$ samples were never enough: each entry is a homogeneous cubic in $15$ variables, and those span $\binom{17}3=680$ dimensions, so $300$ points leave a $\ge380$-dimensional space of forms fitting the data* ([r990](../reviews/REVIEW_r990.md), [r991](../reviews/REVIEW_r991.md)). **[r990](../reviews/REVIEW_r990.md)'s argument is the one to keep**: every entry is degree $\le1$ per variable, since $\sigma(X)_{kj}$ is a Pfaffian on the complement of $\{k,j\}$ and cannot contain the $X_{ik}$ multiplying it, and a multilinear form vanishing on $\{0,1\}^{15}$ vanishes identically. **A polynomial identity in finitely many variables needs no normal form and no equivariance, only a finite expansion**, and `levi6` is nonzero on just the $4!=24$ all-distinct tuples per index pair, so each $\sigma_6$ entry is $24$ quadratic terms.

*So the debt is not "machinery Mathlib lacks" but "an expansion Lean must be made to run", which is a different and smaller thing.* **`starWedge_of_adjugate` derives the identity from this law together with $\mathrm{Pf}(J)=\pm1$**, and the second input was in the end proved by orthogonal normal form rather than by the expansion (see `StarWedgeIdentity` IS PROVED below).

**THE EXPANSION IS DONE FOR $\sigma_6$ AND THE REMAINING OBSTACLE IS NAMED.** *All fifteen entries $\sigma_6(X)_{ij}$, $i<j$, are typed and kernel-checked as the Pfaffian of the complementary four-set signed by the permutation* ([I67AdjugateLaw.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I67AdjugateLaw.lean)), each closing by `simp` and `ring` in about eighty seconds for the file. **What stops `adjugate_law` is `simp`'s step limit and nothing else**: $\mathrm{pf6}=\mathrm{triple6}(X,X,X)$ is SIX nested sums, $6^6=46656$ terms against $\sigma_6$'s $6^4$, and it exceeds the limit even at $2\cdot10^7$ steps.

**And the way round reuses the entries.** *Pairing the reduction at $Z=X$ gives $\langle\sigma_6X,X\rangle=6\,\mathrm{pf6}(X)$, so*
$$\mathrm{pf6}(X)=\tfrac13\sum_{a<b}\sigma_6(X)_{ab}\,X_{ab},$$
**a thirty-term sum whose every entry is already a theorem**, screened to $2.6\cdot10^{-13}$ over four hundred skew draws. *What that route still owes in Lean is the $\star_6$-to-`triple6` index identity itself.*

**THE LAW IS NOW TYPED ENTRY BY ENTRY, 2026-09-17** ([I67AdjugateEntries.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I67AdjugateEntries.lean), $1655$ lines, standard axioms). *All thirty-six entries of $X\sigma_6(X)=-\mathrm{Pf}(X)\mathrm{Id}$ in explicit polynomial form: six diagonal ones carrying the Pfaffian and thirty vanishing, each from the fifteen $\sigma_6$ entries together with $X$'s skewness.*

**The skewness was the whole difficulty and it is worth recording why.** *Row $0$ touches only upper-triangular entries of $X$, so its entry closes without skewness at all, which is why the first probe succeeded and misled. Every other row contains $X_{i0}$ while the Pfaffian polynomial is written in upper entries only, and `ring` cannot bridge the two unaided.* **Five of six diagonal entries failed until the skewness rewrites were supplied.**

**So the remaining debt is ONE SCALAR IDENTITY**, $\mathrm{pf6}(X)$ against that polynomial, and no longer the law. *`pf6` is `triple6 X X X`, six nested sums over $\mathrm{Fin}\,6$, and `simp` exceeds its step ceiling on it staged or not.* **The way round remains the pairing**, $\mathrm{pf6}(X)=\tfrac13\sum_{a<b}\sigma_6(X)_{ab}X_{ab}$, whose thirty terms are all now theorems.

**THREE ROUTES TO $\mathrm{pf6}$'s CLOSED FORM ARE NOW CLOSED, and the pairing is the only one left.** *Direct expansion of `triple6 X X X` exceeds `simp`'s step ceiling. Staging it, the sums first and `levi6` second in separate calls, hits the same ceiling, so the budget is not shared between them and the six-fold sum alone exceeds it. And the TRILINEAR route is closed too: `triple6` cannot be evaluated even at a single CONCRETE basis triple such as `triple6(\varphi_{01},\varphi_{23},\varphi_{45})`, so expanding $X$ in the fifteen elementary skew matrices and using the layer's `add` and `smul` lemmas does not help either.* **That last one is the informative failure**: concreteness does not rescue the expansion, so the obstacle is the sum's arity and not the generality of its arguments. *What remains is the pairing, which needs one index identity between `star6` and `triple6`, the three relabellings whose signs are pinned above.*

**BOTH RELABELLINGS ARE NOW TYPED, 2026-09-17** ([I67Relabel.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I67Relabel.lean), standard axioms). *`levi6_swap_de` and `levi6_swap_ef` each close in one line, `unfold levi6; rw [sgnR_swap _ _]; ring`, the same shape as the layer's own `levi6_swap01`. `relabel_de` proves $B=-A$ and `relabel_fe` proves $C=-B$ by `Finset.sum_comm` at the $(d,e)$ and $(e,f)$ levels, and `relabel_df` composes them to $C=A$ in one `rw`.*

**THESE PROOFS EXPAND NOTHING, WHICH IS WHY THEY SIDESTEP THE CEILING THAT CLOSED THE OTHER THREE ROUTES.** *Each peels the six sums with `Finset.sum_congr` and rewrites one `levi6` factor under the binders, so the $6^6$ terms are never enumerated and the arity of the sum never enters the proof at all.* **A route blocked by a step limit is unblocked by reindexing rather than by a larger limit**, and that is the transferable half.

**What the pairing still owes is the collection**, $\langle\star_6(X\wedge X),Z\rangle=12\,\mathrm{triple6}(Z,X,X)$ from `relabel_de` and `relabel_df`, which is the $\tfrac1{24}\cdot2\cdot3A$ against $12\cdot\tfrac1{48}A$ arithmetic above with the three wedge terms replaced by $3A$. Then $\mathrm{pf6}$'s closed form at $Z=X$, and then [L193](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L193Statement.lean)'s `sorry`. **Gate 2 owed on [I67Relabel.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I67Relabel.lean).**

**AND THE ENTRYWISE FILE OWES AN ASSEMBLY AS WELL AS THE SCALAR IDENTITY**, [r992](../reviews/REVIEW_r992.md). *Granting $\mathrm{pf6}(X)$ equals the diagonal polynomial, [L193](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L193Statement.lean)'s `adjugate_law` is a MATRIX equation and the thirty-six lemmas are scalar ones, so closing it needs an `ext` and a case split against `Matrix.one_apply` on top.* **Routine, and not nothing**: the phrase "one scalar identity" invites a reader to think the file is one `rw` from discharging L193, and it is two steps.

**AND THE WHOLE CHAIN CLOSES, 2026-09-17: [L193](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L193Statement.lean)'s `sorry` IS DISCHARGED.** *Three files carry it, all standard axioms.* **[I67Relabel.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I67Relabel.lean)** proves the pairing $\sum_{a,b}\big(\star_6(X\wedge X)\big)_{ab}Z_{ab}=12\,\mathrm{triple6}(Z,X,X)$ at every $X$ and $Z$, no hypothesis, from the two `levi6` middle-slot swaps and the three relabellings. **[I67Pf6.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I67Pf6.lean)** takes that at $Z=X$, where the left side is thirty-six-term double sum whose six diagonal terms vanish and whose thirty others reduce by skewness to the FIFTEEN $\sigma_6$ entries that are already theorems, and reads off $\mathrm{pf6}(X)$ in the fifteen free entries. **[L193Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L193Statement.lean)'s `adjugate_law`** then assembles the thirty-six `adj` lemmas by `ext` and a case split, exactly the step [r992](../reviews/REVIEW_r992.md) said was owed beyond the scalar identity.

**THE TRANSFERABLE HALF IS THE METHOD, NOT THE RESULT.** *`pf6 X` is `triple6 X X X`, six nested sums whose $6^6$ terms exceed `simp`'s step ceiling at any budget, staged, unstaged or at a concrete basis triple.* **A route blocked by a step limit is unblocked by REINDEXING rather than by a larger limit**: the pairing peels the sums with `Finset.sum_congr` and rewrites one `levi6` factor under the binders, so the terms are never enumerated and the arity never enters, and the Pfaffian is then READ OFF THE ADJUGATE instead of expanded.

**One index shape cost three build cycles and is worth recording.** *`fin_cases i` produces $\langle k,\_\rangle$ rather than the numeral `k`, and `simp only` and `rw` match syntactically, so all thirty-six `adj` rewrites silently failed while `simp` reassociated the goal around them and reported only "unsolved goals".* **The fix is that `exact` matches up to definitional equality where `rw` does not**, so the thirty-six branches close as focus blocks with `simpa using adj_i_j X hX`.

**GATE 2 PASSED ON ALL THREE FILES, [r993](../reviews/REVIEW_r993.md), sole reviewer by design, ten rows of ten CONFIRMED and nothing refuted.** *It recomputed the fifteen signed perfect matchings itself and compared them against `pf6_eq`'s cubic as signed SETS rather than term by term, so a missing or spurious monomial could not pass, then pinned the OVERALL sign, which no term-by-term check can settle: at $X=e_{01}+e_{23}+e_{45}$ all $48$ nonzero terms of `triple6 X X X` are $+1$, so the cubic is $+\mathrm{Pf}$ in the layer's own normalisation.* **AND IT CLOSED THE CONSTANT [r991](../reviews/REVIEW_r991.md) LEFT AMBIGUOUS**: the $12$ belongs to the ORDERED double sum and reads $6$ over $a<b$, which is what the Lean writes. *Its only findings were two stale docstrings, both applied: the relabelling file listed as owed the pairing it proves, and `starWedge_of_adjugate` called the adjugate law unproved sixty lines below its proof.*

**`StarWedgeIdentity` IS PROVED, 2026-09-23**, as `starWedgeIdentity_holds` in [I67PfSign.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I67PfSign.lean), on the three standard axioms. *Every complex structure on $\mathbb R^6$ is orthogonally conjugate to $J_0=\varphi_{01}+\varphi_{23}+\varphi_{45}$, built one $J$-invariant plane at a time since $J$ is skew and orthogonal, so $\mathrm{Pf}(J)=\det Q\cdot\mathrm{Pf}(J_0)=\pm1$ by the transformation law $\mathrm{Pf}(QXQ^{\mathsf T})=\det Q\,\mathrm{Pf}(X)$, and `starWedge_of_pf` closes the predicate with $\varepsilon_J=\mathrm{Pf}(J)$.* **The route runs through neither $\mathrm{Pf}(X)^2=\det X$ nor $\det J=1$**, and it makes [L184](#lemma-l184-the-coincident-case-at-dim-v6-is-empty)'s case 2 unconditional in Lean.

#### L233 WITHDRAWN: $\det J=1$ was ALREADY PROVED in the layer, and what is kept here is a second route

**THE NUMBER IS WITHDRAWN.** *`det_complex_structure` in [I67PfSquared.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I67PfSquared.lean) has proved this since before the session, by continuity of the rotation family and the intermediate value theorem. The block above it in this file said the result was OWED, which was stale, and a numbered result was spent on it.* **THE CAUSE IS THE ONE [lean_id_audit.py](../scripts/misc/lean_id_audit.py) EXISTS FOR**: classifying against [findings/](.) SUCCEEDS and returns something true, while the layer already carries the theorem. *The record was read and the layer was not.*

**WHAT IS KEPT IS THE PROOF, AS A SECOND ROUTE, BECAUSE IT IS STRICTLY SIMPLER.** *The existing one needs a continuous family, its non-vanishing, continuity of $\det$ and the intermediate value theorem. This one is three matrix identities over $\mathbb R$ and uses no topology at all.*

**Statement.** *If $J$ is a real $2n\times2n$ SKEW matrix with $J^2=-I$, then $\det J=1$.* *The claim holds without skewness, by the superseded argument below, and the proof that is MACHINE-CHECKED uses it, which is why the statement carries it.* **No orthogonality and no compatible metric are needed**, and [SelfDual.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Base/SelfDual.lean)'s `IsComplexStructure` supplies skewness outright.

**Proof, and it never leaves $\mathbb R$.** With $J$ skew, $\det(I+J)=\det\big((I+J)^{\mathsf T}\big)=\det(I-J)$. Next $(I+J)(I-J)=I-J^2=2I$, so $\det(I+J)\det(I-J)=2^{2n}$ and neither factor vanishes. Finally $J(I+J)=J+J^2=-(I-J)$, so $\det J\cdot\det(I+J)=(-1)^{2n}\det(I-J)=\det(I+J)$. Cancel the nonzero factor: $\det J=1$. $\square$

**A FIRST VERSION OF THIS PROOF WENT THROUGH $\mathbb C$ AND IS SUPERSEDED.** *It read off the eigenvalues as $\pm i$ with equal multiplicities by conjugation, which is correct and needs a complexification the Lean layer does not carry, so it left a `sorry` where this one leaves none.* **The skew route uses only $\det M^{\mathsf T}=\det M$ and three matrix identities**, and it is the one machine-checked as `det_eq_one_of_complexStructure`. *The cost of the first version was that it assumed the obstacle was the mathematics when it was the choice of argument.*

**WHY THE OBVIOUS ROUTE IS CIRCULAR AND THIS ONE IS NOT.** *Taking determinants in $J^2=-I$ gives $(\det J)^2=(-1)^{2n}=1$, so $\det J=\pm1$ and the sign is exactly what is wanted.* **Pinning it by $\det J=\mathrm{Pf}(J)^2\ge0$ assumes the very identity $\mathrm{Pf}(X)^2=\det X$ that this lemma is meant to feed**, so that route proves nothing. *The conjugation argument uses only that $J$ is real, which is where the content sits.*

**Where it stands.** `StarWedgeIdentity` is proved by another route, above, so this lemma feeds only the general identity $\mathrm{Pf}(X)^2=\det X$ at skew $X$.


> **THE CONTRACTION AGAINST RICCI IS WITHDRAWN, AND WHAT IT DISCARDED IS THE FINDING.** *This box claimed that left-multiplying the harmonicity identity by $\lambda_k\omega_k$ and summing gave a new constraint in which both Weyl conditions are consumed.* **Both reviewers refuted it** ([r822](../reviews/REVIEW_r822.md), [r823](../reviews/REVIEW_r823.md)): running the polarised law at $(\omega_i,\omega_i,\omega_k)$ and discharging nothing gives, at EVERY triple, $$2\mathcal M+R\,\star\!\mathcal B+\sum_k\lambda_kh_k\,\omega_k=0,$$ with $\mathcal M$ the contracted sum, $R=\sum_k\lambda_k\omega_k^2$ the Ricci defect and $h_k=\langle\star\mathcal B,\omega_k\rangle$ the harmonicity defect. *On the harmonic locus this reads $\mathcal M=-\tfrac12R\,\star\!\mathcal B$, so at $R=0$ it holds for EVERY value of $\mathcal B$*, **the constraint excludes no $\mathcal B$ and provably cannot force $\mathcal B=0$.** *The box said as much out loud without hearing it: the left side is the zero matrix times $\star\mathcal B$.* **And on the class the display quantifies over the conclusion is immediate without Ricci at all**, a rank-three Weyl tensor having $\mathcal B=0$ outright.

> **WHAT SURVIVES IS THE UNCONDITIONAL IDENTITY, AND THE BOX HAD A SPECIALISATION OF IT.** *At EVERY triple of $2$-forms and every weights, with no hypothesis whatever,* $$2S+\Big(\sum_k\lambda_k\omega_k^2\Big)\star\!\mathcal B=-\sum_k\lambda_k\big\langle\star\mathcal B,\omega_k\big\rangle\,\omega_k,\qquad S=\sum_{i,k}\lambda_i\lambda_k\,\omega_k\,\omega_i\,\beta(\omega_i,\omega_k).$$ **Ricci kills the middle term, so $S=0$ is EQUIVALENT to the three harmonicity pairings vanishing** ([r823](../reviews/REVIEW_r823.md)), *the constraint is harmonicity restated and cannot be stronger than the hypothesis it came from.* **Under Ricci, $S$ lands in the span, so it is three conditions and not thirty-six**, and its trace is $0=0$ at every triple whatsoever, $R$ being symmetric and $\star\mathcal B$ skew. *It does cut on the Ricci-flat class: r823 exhibits a Ricci-flat orthonormal triple at $\lambda=(1,1,-2)$ with $S=-2\sqrt3\,\omega_3\ne0$, so it is not vacuous as a condition, it is simply not new.*

> **WHAT THE CONTRACTION THREW AWAY IS THREE MATRIX EQUATIONS.** *The harmonicity identity gives $\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i\,\beta(\omega_i,\omega_k)=0$ for $k=1,2,3$ SEPARATELY, and summing them against $\lambda_k\omega_k$ keeps one scalar's worth of it.* **So the uncollapsed system is the object, and the collapse was recorded as the finding** ([r822](../reviews/REVIEW_r822.md)). *That system is three matrix equations in six dimensions, and whether it forces $\mathcal B=0$ is [L192](#lemma-l192-the-bianchi-defect-is-the-weighted-laplacian-of-the-pfaffian-in-every-even-dimension)'s open question restated, not a new one.*

> **AND THE PRIORITY CLAIM WAS FALSE.** *"The first constraint in which both Weyl conditions are consumed" is refuted twice over*: [L181](#lemma-l181-ricci-flatness-and-first-bianchi-are-the-two-halves-of-one-matrix-and-together-they-empty-the-simultaneously-block-diagonal-family) carries them as the diagonal and off-diagonal of one matrix and needs both to reach $\Lambda=0$, and [L183](#lemma-l183-a-rank-three-weyl-tensor-is-an-orthonormal-triple-whose-images-are-collinear) fuses them into the single equation $\sum_i\lambda_i\Phi(\omega_i)=0$. **This file already said so**, L181's own box calling the two complementary halves of one object. *Both reviewers found it independently.*

> **WHAT IT GIVES [I38](../plan.md).** *[L192](#lemma-l192-the-bianchi-defect-is-the-weighted-laplacian-of-the-pfaffian-in-every-even-dimension) leaves the question of whether harmonicity is strictly weaker than first Bianchi ON THE RICCI-FLAT CLASS, and Ricci-flatness is a statement about the $\omega_i^2$ while Bianchi is one about the $\sigma(\omega_i)$.* **It is the relation the record carries in a usable matrix form**; [SelfDual.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Base/SelfDual.lean) carries two others, `StarWedgeIdentity` and the decomposable-locus pair, and an earlier version of this box called it the only one. *So it is where a proof tying the two conditions would start.* *It is recorded as a tool and closes nothing.*

#### Lemma L180, Ricci-flatness is a signed PSD identity, and it names the support

**Statement.** Let $W=\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i\otimes\omega_i$ be a nonzero element of $\mathcal W_n$ in spectral form, the $\omega_i$ orthonormal $2$-forms and every $\lambda_i\ne0$. Writing each $2$-form as a skew matrix and setting $A_i=-\omega_i^2=\omega_i\omega_i^{\mathsf T}$:

1. **Ricci-flatness is exactly $\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i^2=0$**, an identity in $S^2(\mathbb R^n)$, equivalently $\sum_i\lambda_iA_i=0$ with **every $A_i$ positive semidefinite of trace $2$**.
2. **Tracelessness is its trace** and is therefore not an extra condition, *and the first Bianchi identity is used nowhere in this lemma, which is Ricci-only*: $\operatorname{tr}(\omega_i^2)=-2\lVert\omega_i\rVert^2=-2$, so the trace of (1) is $-2\sum_i\lambda_i$.
3. **At rank three the support $V$ is the support of the single form carrying the minority sign**, and $\dim V$ is even and at least four.

**Proof.** *(1)* With $W_{abcd}=\sum_i\lambda_i(\omega_i)_{ab}(\omega_i)_{cd}$, the Ricci contraction is $\sum_bW_{abcb}=\sum_i\lambda_i\sum_b(\omega_i)_{ab}(\omega_i)_{cb}=\big(\sum_i\lambda_i\omega_i\omega_i^{\mathsf T}\big)_{ac}$, and $\omega^{\mathsf T}=-\omega$. **$A=\omega\omega^{\mathsf T}$ is positive semidefinite for the same reason any Gram matrix is**, and $\operatorname{tr}A=\sum_{a,b}\omega_{ab}^2=2\lVert\omega\rVert^2$.

*(3)* The $\lambda_i$ are nonzero and sum to zero by (2), so their signs are not all equal: up to relabelling the pattern is $(+,+,-)$ or $(+,-,-)$. Moving the minority-sign term to the other side leaves **a sum of positive semidefinite matrices equal to a positive semidefinite matrix**, and **the range of a sum of PSD matrices is the sum of their ranges, which was stated here with no argument and is now sourced**: [Fillmore–Williams](../references/fillmore-williams-1971-operator-ranges/) Theorem 2.2 gives $\mathcal R(A)+\mathcal R(B)=\mathcal R(\sqrt{AA^*+BB^*})$, and [Yang](../references/yang-2024-semidefinite-optimization/) states the finite-dimensional PSD form directly. So in the first pattern $\operatorname{supp}\omega_1+\operatorname{supp}\omega_2=\operatorname{supp}\omega_3$ and in the second $\operatorname{supp}\omega_2+\operatorname{supp}\omega_3=\operatorname{supp}\omega_1$. *Here $\operatorname{range}(A)=\operatorname{range}(\omega)=\operatorname{supp}\omega$, since $\omega^2x=0$ forces $\lVert\omega x\rVert^2=-x^{\mathsf T}\omega^2x=0$.* **In both patterns the minority form's support already contains the other two, so it is $V$**, and a $2$-form's support has even dimension. *At $\dim V=2$ the space $\Lambda^2V$ is one-dimensional and the rank is at most one, so $\dim V\ge4$.* **Three steps used here were unstated and are now named**: that $\operatorname{supp}\omega\subseteq V$ gives $\omega\in\Lambda^2V$; that $V$ does not depend on the choice of eigenbasis inside a degenerate eigenspace, which matters because the equality case has $\lambda_2=\lambda_3$; and the passage from $\ker A=\ker\omega$ to equal ranges, which is the self-adjointness of $A$. $\square$

> **WHAT (3) PROVES AT EVERY RANK, AND WHERE THE EVENNESS STOPS.** *The general content is $\sum_{\lambda_i>0}\operatorname{supp}\omega_i=\sum_{\lambda_i<0}\operatorname{supp}\omega_i=V$, the positive and negative sides span the same space, at any rank.* **The step from there to "$V$ is ONE form's support", and so to evenness, needs one side to be a single form, which is what rank three supplies and rank four does not**: at the pattern $(+,+,-,-)$ both sides are sums of two, and $\dim V$ may be odd. *So (3) is a rank-three statement and not the first case of a pattern.*

> **WHAT THIS LEAVES [I38](../plan.md).** *Combining with [L179](#lemma-l179-the-support-of-a-low-rank-weyl-tensor-and-the-first-bianchi-identity-read-on-the-spectrum), a rank-three Weyl tensor is governed by three conditions on an orthonormal triple: $\sum_i\lambda_iA_i=0$ with $A_i\succeq0$, the Bianchi equation $\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i\wedge\omega_i=0$ in $\Lambda^4$, and $\lVert\omega_i\rVert=1$.* **The support question is now one line: is $\dim V=4$ forced, or is there such a triple with $\dim V\ge6$?** *[L178](#lemma-l178-every-weyl-tensor-of-lambda2-rank-at-most-three-has-thetalesqrt32-with-equality-exactly-at-w_mathbbcp2) covers $\dim V=4$ completely and the even case $\dim V=2$ is impossible, so **$\dim V\ge6$ is the whole of what remains**.*

> **AND THE RANK-TWO CASE IS SETTLED AT TWO DIMENSIONS AND OPEN ABOVE THEM.** *At rank two, $\lambda_2=-\lambda_1$ and (1) reads $A_1=A_2$, so $\omega_1$ and $\omega_2$ have the **same** support, which is $V$.* **At $\dim V=4$ the branch is INHABITED**, any orthonormal pair in $\Lambda^+\mathbb R^4$ meets every condition, and [L178](#lemma-l178-every-weyl-tensor-of-lambda2-rank-at-most-three-has-thetalesqrt32-with-equality-exactly-at-w_mathbbcp2)'s step 1 returns $\theta=0$ there, *that lemma carrying a four-dimensional $V$ as a hypothesis and reaching no further.* **At $\dim V=6$ it is EMPTY, by [L191](#lemma-l191-two-forms-with-a-common-six-dimensional-support-and-both-coincidences-are-equal-up-to-sign-so-the-rank-two-branch-is-empty-at-dim-v6)**. Ricci gives $\omega_1^2=\omega_2^2$ and Bianchi gives $\omega_1\wedge\omega_1=\omega_2\wedge\omega_2$, which are exactly that lemma's hypotheses. *At $\dim V\ge8$ it is open, and what survives there is the stratum where $-\omega_1^2$ has a repeated eigenvalue, case 1's argument being dimension-free.* **The reach of the citation was found by [r807](../reviews/REVIEW_r807.md) at Gate 2 on L190, where the block claimed every other branch closed.**

---

#### Lemma L181, Ricci-flatness and first Bianchi are the two halves of one matrix, and together they empty the simultaneously-block-diagonal family

**Statement.** Fix an orthogonal decomposition $\mathbb R^n=P_1\oplus\dots\oplus P_m\oplus P_0$ with each $P_j$ a plane, and let $\varepsilon_j$ be the unit area-form of $P_j$. Call $W\in\mathcal W_n$ **simultaneously block-diagonal** for this splitting when every eigen-$2$-form with nonzero eigenvalue is a combination of the $\varepsilon_j$. **Then $W=0$.**

**Proof.** Write $\omega_i=\sum_{j=1}^mM_{ij}\varepsilon_j$ for the $r$ eigenforms with eigenvalues $\lambda_i\ne0$, and $\Lambda=\operatorname{diag}(\lambda)$. Orthonormality of the $\omega_i$ is $MM^{\mathsf T}=I_r$, the $\varepsilon_j$ being orthonormal.

*Ricci-flatness is the diagonal.* As a skew matrix $\varepsilon_j$ is supported on $P_j$, so $\omega_i\omega_i^{\mathsf T}$ is block-diagonal with $M_{ij}^2\,\mathrm{Id}_{P_j}$ on the $j$-th plane. By [L180](#lemma-l180-ricci-flatness-is-a-signed-psd-identity-and-it-names-the-support), Ricci-flatness is $\sum_i\lambda_i\omega_i^2=0$, which reads $$\textstyle\sum_i\lambda_iM_{ij}^2=0\quad\text{for each }j\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad\operatorname{diag}\big(M^{\mathsf T}\Lambda M\big)=0.$$

*First Bianchi is the off-diagonal.* $\varepsilon_j\wedge\varepsilon_j=0$, so $\omega_i\wedge\omega_i=2\sum_{j<k}M_{ij}M_{ik}\,\varepsilon_j\wedge\varepsilon_k$, and the $\varepsilon_j\wedge\varepsilon_k$ are linearly independent in $\Lambda^4$. By [L179](#lemma-l179-the-support-of-a-low-rank-weyl-tensor-and-the-first-bianchi-identity-read-on-the-spectrum), first Bianchi is $\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i\wedge\omega_i=0$, which reads $$\textstyle\sum_i\lambda_iM_{ij}M_{ik}=0\quad\text{for each }j<k\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad\big(M^{\mathsf T}\Lambda M\big)_{j\ne k}=0.$$

*So $M^{\mathsf T}\Lambda M=0$.* Multiplying by $M$ on the left and $M^{\mathsf T}$ on the right and using $MM^{\mathsf T}=I_r$ gives $\Lambda=M\big(M^{\mathsf T}\Lambda M\big)M^{\mathsf T}=0$, contradicting $\lambda_i\ne0$ unless there are no such eigenvalues at all. $\square$

> **AND THE HALVES ARE EXACTLY A SYMMETRIC MATRIX'S ENTRIES.** *Ricci fixes the $m$ diagonal entries and Bianchi the $\binom m2$ off-diagonal ones, which together are the $\binom{m+1}2$ entries of $M^{\mathsf T}\Lambda M$ and nothing else.* **Neither condition is redundant, and Bianchi is load-bearing**: $\Lambda=\operatorname{diag}(1,-1)$ with $M=\tfrac1{\sqrt2}\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\1&-1\end{pmatrix}$ gives a nonzero block-diagonal trace-free $W$ whose Bianchi sum is $2\,\varepsilon_1\wedge\varepsilon_2\ne0$, *so Ricci alone does not empty the family.* **The one exception is $m=1$, where $\Lambda^4$ contributes nothing and the Ricci half closes it alone.** *Both from [r776](../reviews/REVIEW_r776.md).*

> **THE TWO CONDITIONS ARE COMPLEMENTARY HALVES OF ONE OBJECT, WHICH IS THE CONTENT.** *Ricci-flatness and the first Bianchi identity are usually carried as unrelated hypotheses and priced separately.* **On this family they are the diagonal and the off-diagonal of the single matrix $M^{\mathsf T}\Lambda M$**, so imposing both is imposing that one matrix vanishes, and a matrix congruent to $\Lambda$ through an isometry cannot vanish unless $\Lambda$ does. *No inequality, no spectrum, and no dimension enters: the statement holds at every $n$, every rank and every number of blocks.*

**What it does for [I38](../plan.md), stated exactly.** *[L180](#lemma-l180-ricci-flatness-is-a-signed-psd-identity-and-it-names-the-support) leaves $\dim V\ge6$ as the whole of what remains, and the first family one would build there is three forms sharing a block structure, the analogue of the balanced products that populate this record.* **That family is empty**, at $\dim V=6$ and at every larger even dimension. *What it does not reach is a triple with no common block splitting*, which is where a witness would now have to live.

**Two checks that it is not vacuous, both argued rather than asserted, and the reason a naming of particular forms cannot work is general.** *Every $2$-form is block-diagonal for **some** splitting, by the real skew normal form*, so no argument of the shape "this form is not a combination of area forms" can succeed for any choice of form. **The quantifier over all splittings has to be discharged by a count, and it is.**

*The span of the $\varepsilon_j$ is $m$-dimensional*, so the hypothesis needs $r\le m$, which $MM^{\mathsf T}=I_r$ already forces, and $m\le\lfloor n/2\rfloor$. **$W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ escapes by that count alone**: at $n=4$ the span is at most two-dimensional while its three eigenforms span $\Lambda^+$, so no splitting can contain them. *This also fixes where the lemma has content at rank three: $r=3$ needs $m\ge3$, hence $n\ge6$, **exactly [I38](../plan.md)'s remaining case**, and nothing at $n=4$, which [L178](#lemma-l178-every-weyl-tensor-of-lambda2-rank-at-most-three-has-thetalesqrt32-with-equality-exactly-at-w_mathbbcp2) already settles.*

**A coordinate-diagonal $W$ escapes it too, and the reason is decomposability.** *If $e_{ab}=\sum_ja_j\varepsilon_j$ then, the $\varepsilon_j$ having orthogonal supports, a sum with two or more nonzero terms has rank at least four, so $e_{ab}=\pm\varepsilon_j$ for a single $j$.* **Two eigenforms sharing an index would then need their planes both orthogonal and intersecting**, and [L176](#lemma-l176-the-coordinate-diagonal-locus-has-minimum-degree-two-so-its-lambda2-rank-is-at-least-four) puts at least four edges in any support graph, whose minimum degree two makes two of them share a vertex.

---

#### Lemma L183, a rank-three Weyl tensor is an orthonormal triple whose images are collinear

**Statement.** Define $\Phi:\Lambda^2\mathbb R^n\to S^2(\mathbb R^n)\oplus\Lambda^4\mathbb R^n$ by $\Phi(\omega)=(\omega^2,\ \omega\wedge\omega)$. Then $W=\sum_{i=1}^3\lambda_i\,\omega_i\otimes\omega_i$ with the $\omega_i$ orthonormal and every $\lambda_i\ne0$ lies in $\mathcal W_n$ **if and only if the three points $\Phi(\omega_1),\Phi(\omega_2),\Phi(\omega_3)$ either all coincide or are distinct and collinear**, *collinear **jointly** in the direct sum, which is strictly stronger than collinear in each factor, and which the first statement did not say.* **On the distinct branch the weights are determined up to scale by the collinearity ratio; on the coincident branch they are not**, *there every vector summing to zero serves, so the clause holds on one branch only and the first repair left it unqualified.* *The determination on the distinct branch is asserted here and not argued.*

> **ON THE WEYL SIDE, TWO EQUAL IMAGES FORCE ALL THREE EQUAL.** *If $\Phi_1=\Phi_2$ then $\sum_i\lambda_i\Phi_i=0$ with $\lambda_1+\lambda_2=-\lambda_3$ gives $\lambda_3(\Phi_3-\Phi_1)=0$, and $\lambda_3\ne0$.* **So exactly-two-coincident never occurs for a genuine rank-three Weyl tensor**, the configuration exists among triples of $2$-forms and is exactly what the criterion must exclude, which is why the two branches are the whole of it. *[r779](../reviews/REVIEW_r779.md).*

> **BARE COLLINEARITY IS NOT ENOUGH, AND THE FIRST DRAFT SAID IT WAS.** *If exactly two images coincide, say $\Phi_2=\Phi_3=P$ and $\Phi_1=Q\ne P$, the triple is collinear, yet $\sum_i\lambda_i\Phi_i=\lambda_1(Q-P)$ vanishes only at $\lambda_1=0$, which the rank-three hypothesis forbids.* **So the excluded configuration is exactly-two-coincident**, and the statement above names it.

**Proof.** By [L180](#lemma-l180-ricci-flatness-is-a-signed-psd-identity-and-it-names-the-support) Ricci-flatness is $\sum_i\lambda_i\omega_i^2=0$ and by [L179](#lemma-l179-the-support-of-a-low-rank-weyl-tensor-and-the-first-bianchi-identity-read-on-the-spectrum) first Bianchi is $\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i\wedge\omega_i=0$, so together they are the single equation $\sum_i\lambda_i\Phi(\omega_i)=0$, **and tracelessness, being Ricci's trace, adds $\sum_i\lambda_i=0$ rather than a further constraint.**

*Three vectors carrying weights that sum to zero and vanish nowhere are affinely dependent, hence collinear.* Writing $\lambda_3=-(\lambda_1+\lambda_2)$ turns $\sum\lambda_i\Phi_i=0$ into $\lambda_1(\Phi_1-\Phi_3)=-\lambda_2(\Phi_2-\Phi_3)$, so $\Phi_1-\Phi_3$ and $\Phi_2-\Phi_3$ are parallel, **and neither difference can vanish alone**, since $\lambda_1,\lambda_2\ne0$. *Conversely*, all three coinciding lets any weight vector summing to zero serve; and from $\Phi_1-\Phi_3=t(\Phi_2-\Phi_3)$ with the three distinct, $t\ne0$ and $t\ne1$ follow, and $(1,-t,t-1)$ sums to zero, vanishes nowhere and annihilates the triple. $\square$

> **MUTUAL ORTHOGONALITY IS UNUSED; UNIT NORM IS NOT.** *The affine-dependence argument uses no pairing between distinct $\omega_i$, so mutual orthogonality is stated and unused.* **But the unit norms are consumed**, tracelessness is the trace of the Ricci condition only because $\operatorname{tr}(\omega_i^2)=-2$, and dropping the normalisation breaks that step. *A second repair here claimed the equivalence holds for any three $2$-forms; that overshoots.* **The correct scope is any three $2$-forms of EQUAL NONZERO NORM, with linear independence added by hand if rank three is to survive.** *[r780](../reviews/REVIEW_r780.md)'s witness: take three unit self-dual forms on $\mathbb R^4$ with weights $(1,1,-2)$ and rescale $\omega_3'=2\omega_3$, $\lambda_3'=-\tfrac12$, the same tensor, still Weyl, but $\Phi$ being quadratic gives $\Phi(\omega_3')=4P$ against $\Phi_1=\Phi_2=P$, which is exactly the two-coincident configuration the criterion excludes.* **What orthonormality supplies beyond that is the spectral reading**, that the $\lambda_i$ are $W$'s eigenvalues and the rank is three.

> **AND IT EXPLAINS WHY $n=4$ IS EASY: THERE THE THREE IMAGES COINCIDE.** *On the self-dual forms of $\mathbb R^4$ every $\omega_i^2=-\tfrac12\mathrm{Id}_4$ and every $\omega_i\wedge\omega_i=f_{1234}$*, so $\Phi_1=\Phi_2=\Phi_3$ and **every** weight vector summing to zero gives a Weyl tensor. *The counts, stated precisely rather than as one number: the weight plane at a fixed frame is $2$-dimensional, the tensor set $5$-dimensional, and the scale-free moduli $1$-dimensional, the block first called all of it "$2$-parameter".* **And $\theta$ there is a function of the weights alone.** *The $n=4$ coincidence itself holds for **every** unit self-dual form and not only a basis, the $J_k$ anticommuting and $\omega\wedge\omega=\langle\omega,\star\omega\rangle f_{1234}=\lVert\omega\rVert^2f_{1234}$.*

> **WHAT IT ASKS AT $\dim V=6$, WHICH IS [I38](../plan.md)'S REMAINING CASE.** *The coincident sub-case is the one to try first, being what $n=4$ does: three orthonormal $\omega_i$ with equal $\omega_i^2$ and equal $\omega_i\wedge\omega_i$.* **Equal squares alone do NOT force a complex structure, and a first draft said they did.** *$\omega_i\omega_i^{\mathsf T}=A$ with $A$ merely positive definite of trace $2$ has solutions $\omega$ for many non-scalar $A$; what forces the structure is $A=cI_6$, which says $\omega/\sqrt c$ is both orthogonal and skew.* **So the scalar case is a sub-case of the coincident case, not the whole of it**, and it is the one that settles. > **THE SCALAR SUB-CASE AT $\dim V=6$ IS EMPTY.** *On $\mathbb R^6$ every metric-compatible complex structure satisfies $\star(J\wedge J)=2\epsilon_JJ$ with $\epsilon_J=\pm1$ according to whether $J$'s orientation agrees with the fixed one*, in a $J$-adapted frame $J=\varepsilon_1+\varepsilon_2+\varepsilon_3$, so $J\wedge J=2(\varepsilon_1\varepsilon_2+\varepsilon_1\varepsilon_3+\varepsilon_2\varepsilon_3)$ and each $\star$ returns the missing area form. **So equal $J_i\wedge J_i$ forces $\epsilon_iJ_i$ all equal, hence $J_i=\pm K$ for one $K$**, and then $\operatorname{tr}(J_iJ_j)=\pm\operatorname{tr}(K^2)=\mp6\ne0$, **contradicting trace-orthogonality.** *Derived 2026-09-08 and unaudited. It closes the scalar sub-case and leaves the non-scalar coincident case and the distinct-collinear case both open.*

**The quaternionic relations are ANTICOMMUTATION, $J_iJ_j=-J_jJ_i$, and those do need a dimension divisible by four, but trace-orthogonality is strictly weaker and the two must not be conflated**, which a first draft of this paragraph did. *So no dimension obstruction is available here, and whether three trace-orthogonal complex structures on $\mathbb R^6$ can share a $4$-form is open rather than blocked.* *Unaudited, and the non-coincident sub-case is untouched.*

---

#### Lemma L194, the distinct branch of L183 is empty, so a rank-three Weyl tensor's three images coincide

*Derived 2026-09-10. **GATE 2 PASSED**, [r832](../reviews/REVIEW_r832.md) and [r833](../reviews/REVIEW_r833.md), each deriving the identity at every $n$ from the skew normal form and each confirming the constant and the norm. The elementary step is typed and proved in [CollinearSphere.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/CollinearSphere.lean) with no gap and no curvature in it. **The identity is machine-checked at every $n$** as `phi_sphere` in [PhiSphereN.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/PhiSphereN.lean), and the lemma as `L194` in [L194Proof.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L194Proof.lean).*

**Statement.** *Let $W=\sum_{i=1}^3\lambda_i\,\omega_i\otimes\omega_i\in\mathcal W_n$ with the $\omega_i$ orthonormal and every $\lambda_i\ne0$.* **Then $\Phi(\omega_1)=\Phi(\omega_2)=\Phi(\omega_3)$**, for $\Phi(\omega)=(\omega^2,\ \omega\wedge\omega)$. *Equivalently, [L183](#lemma-l183-a-rank-three-weyl-tensor-is-an-orthonormal-triple-whose-images-are-collinear)'s distinct-and-collinear branch is empty, at every $n$.*

**Proof.** [L183](#lemma-l183-a-rank-three-weyl-tensor-is-an-orthonormal-triple-whose-images-are-collinear) gives that the three points either coincide or are distinct and collinear. **$\Phi$ sends the unit sphere of $\Lambda^2$ into a sphere**: at every skew $\omega$,
$$\operatorname{tr}(\omega^4)+\lVert\omega\wedge\omega\rVert_\perp^2=2\,\langle\omega,\omega\rangle^2,$$
in the multi-index-orthonormal norm on $\Lambda^4$ recorded in [conventions.md](conventions.md), and the left side is exactly $\lVert\Phi(\omega)\rVert^2$ with the Frobenius norm on $S^2$, since $\omega^2$ is symmetric and $\operatorname{tr}((\omega^2)^2)=\operatorname{tr}(\omega^4)$. *So $\lVert\Phi(\omega_i)\rVert^2=2$ for each $i$.* **A line meets a sphere in at most two points**, so three collinear points of equal norm are not distinct. *Collinearity is affine and therefore independent of the norm used to state it, while lying on a sphere is not, which is why the identity's own inner product is named.* $\square$

> **THE ELEMENTARY STEP, STATED SO IT CAN BE CHECKED WITHOUT THE GEOMETRY.** *Parametrise the line through $\Phi_3$ and $\Phi_2$ as $p(s)=\Phi_3+s(\Phi_2-\Phi_3)$, so the three points are $s=0,1,t$. Equal norms at $s=0$ and $s=1$ give $2\langle\Phi_3,\Phi_2-\Phi_3\rangle=-\lVert\Phi_2-\Phi_3\rVert^2$, and equal norms at $s=t$ then give $t(t-1)\lVert\Phi_2-\Phi_3\rVert^2=0$.* **So $t=0$, $t=1$, or $\Phi_2=\Phi_3$**, and [L183](#lemma-l183-a-rank-three-weyl-tensor-is-an-orthonormal-triple-whose-images-are-collinear)'s own note that exactly-two-coincident never occurs then forces all three equal.

> **SCREENED AGAINST THE KNOWN WITNESS.** *Padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ has rank exactly three, and every unit $\omega\in\Lambda^+\mathbb R^4$ has $\omega^2=-\tfrac12\mathrm{Id}_4$ and $\omega\wedge\omega=\mathrm{vol}_4$, so its three images COINCIDE.* **It sits in the surviving branch and is no counterexample**, which is the check this lemma most needed.

> **JOINTNESS IS THE ONE HYPOTHESIS THIS CANNOT LOSE.** *[L183](#lemma-l183-a-rank-three-weyl-tensor-is-an-orthonormal-triple-whose-images-are-collinear)'s collinearity is joint in the direct sum, and under a factorwise reading there is no single line to intersect with the sphere.* **And L183 fixes no inner product on $S^2\oplus\Lambda^4$ at all**, its proof being an affine dependence and nothing more, so no conversion between the two norms is owed and "collinearity is norm-independent" understates itself ([r833](../reviews/REVIEW_r833.md)).

> **A SHORTER ROUTE REACHES ALL THREE IN ONE STEP** ([r833](../reviews/REVIEW_r833.md)). *Writing $v_i=\Phi_i-\Phi_3$, pairing $\lambda_1v_1+\lambda_2v_2=0$ with $\Phi_3$ and using the equal norms gives $\tfrac{\lambda_2}{\lambda_1}(\lambda_1+\lambda_2)\lVert v_2\rVert^2=0$, and $\lambda_1+\lambda_2=-\lambda_3\ne0$ finishes it.* **No case split and no appeal to the exactly-two-coincident note.**

> **WHAT IT CLOSES.** *The weights being "determined up to scale by the collinearity ratio" was a statement about the empty branch, so it constrains nothing.* **Every rank-three Weyl tensor is a coincident triple**, and [L184](#lemma-l184-the-coincident-case-at-dim-v6-is-empty) empties that at $\dim V=6$, **so the rank-three branch at $\dim V=6$ is EMPTY.** *The composition is exact: L184's hypotheses are precisely what L194 delivers.* **It also routes through [L191](#lemma-l191-two-forms-with-a-common-six-dimensional-support-and-both-coincidences-are-equal-up-to-sign-so-the-rank-two-branch-is-empty-at-dim-v6), whose hypotheses L194 also delivers and which needs only two of the three forms**, *though not as INDEPENDENT corroboration, L191's proof being L184's three cases run on pairs* ([r832](../reviews/REVIEW_r832.md)).

> **AND IT REPROVES [L189](#lemma-l189-a-rank-three-weyl-tensor-has-three-non-decomposable-image-forms) GIVEN [L185](#lemma-l185-the-all-decomposable-branch-is-empty).** *Coincidence makes the three self-wedges equal, so the image forms are all decomposable or none is, and L185 empties the first* ([r833](../reviews/REVIEW_r833.md)).

#### Lemma L195, a common square with two distinct eigenvalues forces two forms equal up to sign

*Gate 2: [r834](../reviews/REVIEW_r834.md) and [r835](../reviews/REVIEW_r835.md) independently. The first version derived the constraint below, kept only a strict weakening of it, and built a weighted count on that, reaching "empty up to $\dim V=12$, first possible at $14$" where the constraint itself gives emptiness at every dimension. **Gate 2 passed in this form.***

**Statement.** *Let $\omega_1,\omega_2$ be $2$-forms with $\omega_1^2=\omega_2^2=-A$ for $A$ positive definite, and $\omega_1\wedge\omega_1=\omega_2\wedge\omega_2$.* **If $A$ has at least two distinct eigenvalues then $\omega_2=\pm\omega_1$.** *No dimension hypothesis, no Weyl hypothesis, and no orthogonality: equal norms come from the common square rather than being assumed.*

**Proof.** *Each $\omega_i$ commutes with $\omega_i^2=-A$, so it preserves every eigenspace $E_j$ of $A$ and restricts there to $c_j$ times a complex structure $J_{ij}$, with $c_j^2$ the eigenvalue.* **The wedge's $\Lambda^2E_j\otimes\Lambda^2E_k$ component is $2c_jc_k\,J_{ij}\wedge J_{ik}$**, a rank-one tensor, so equality across $i$ at a pair $j\ne k$ makes $J_{2j}\wedge J_{2k}=J_{1j}\wedge J_{1k}$, *and a rank-one tensor determines its factors up to reciprocal scalars, which complex structures having fixed norm pins to $J_{2j}=\varepsilon J_{1j}$ and $J_{2k}=\varepsilon J_{1k}$ with one sign $\varepsilon=\pm1$.*

**The sign is the same at every eigenspace.** *Writing $\sigma_j$ for it, the pair condition says $\sigma_j\sigma_k=1$ at every $j\ne k$, so $\sigma$ is constant.* Hence $\omega_2=\pm\omega_1$. $\square$ *Kernel-checked as `L195` in [L195Proof.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L195Proof.lean), the rank-one step read off the wedge evaluated on $E_j\times E_k$ with no $\Lambda^4$.*

> **WHAT IT SUBSUMES.** *[L184](#lemma-l184-the-coincident-case-at-dim-v6-is-empty)'s cases 1 and 3 are the instances at $\dim V=6$ with three and with two distinct eigenvalues*, and the first version of this lemma is the all-simple instance. **All of them are one five-line argument with no dimension in it.**

> **AND IT LEAVES THE COINCIDENT QUESTION AS EXACTLY ONE PROBLEM.** *Two forms of a coincident triple are orthogonal, so $\omega_2\ne\pm\omega_1$, and the lemma forces $A$ to have a SINGLE eigenvalue: $A=c\,\mathrm{Id}_V$ and $\omega_i=\sqrt c\,J_i$ for complex structures $J_i$.* **So the whole of the rank-three question is: for which $m$ do three pairwise trace-orthogonal orthogonal complex structures on $\mathbb R^{2m}$ share a common $J\wedge J$?** *Empty at $m=1$; INHABITED at $m=2$, which is padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$; empty at $m=3$ by [L184](#lemma-l184-the-coincident-case-at-dim-v6-is-empty)'s case 2; and OPEN at $m\ge4$.* **The diagonal quaternionic triple on $\mathbb R^8$ fails the wedge condition, by the same rank-one obstruction** ([r835](../reviews/REVIEW_r835.md)).

> **AT $\dim V=8$ THE SURVIVING SHAPE IS ALREADY NARROW, ON A REVIEWER'S OWN DERIVATION AND UNAUDITED.** *The rank-one reading closes the splittings $4+4$, $6+2$ and $2+2+2+2$ outright, leaving only $A=c\,\mathrm{Id}_8$; and a hard-Lefschetz remark excludes ANTICOMMUTING pairs of complex structures at every $\dim V\ge6$, which removes the quaternionic $\mathbb H^2$ and the octonionic candidates* ([r834](../reviews/REVIEW_r834.md)). **So a witness at $\dim V=8$ would be three commuting-in-no-obvious-way complex structures with a shared self-wedge**, and the obvious constructions are the ones ruled out.

#### Lemma L196, two complex structures with a common self-wedge agree up to sign at every $m\ge3$, so the coincident case is four-dimensional

*Gate 2 passed: [r836](../reviews/REVIEW_r836.md) and [r837](../reviews/REVIEW_r837.md), identical briefs. Both found the same defect in the first version, a hypothesis it imposed and never used, and dropping it is what closes the branch.*
