**Statement.** *Let $J_1,J_2$ be orthogonal complex structures on $\mathbb R^{2m}$ with $\omega_1\wedge\omega_1=\omega_2\wedge\omega_2$.* **Then $J_2=\pm J_1$ at every $m\ge3$.** *Consequently no three pairwise trace-orthogonal such structures share a self-wedge above $m=2$, and by [L195](#lemma-l195-a-common-square-with-two-distinct-eigenvalues-forces-two-forms-equal-up-to-sign) and [L194](#lemma-l194-the-distinct-branch-of-l183-is-empty-so-a-rank-three-weyl-tensors-three-images-coincide) **every rank-three Weyl tensor is supported on a FOUR-dimensional subspace.***

**Proof.** *Equal self-wedges is a difference of squares: with $\alpha=\omega_1-\omega_2$ and $\beta=\omega_1+\omega_2$, and $\omega_i\wedge\omega_j$ symmetric at even degree,* $\omega_1\wedge\omega_1=\omega_2\wedge\omega_2\iff\alpha\wedge\beta=0$. **Put $T=-J_1J_2$, orthogonal, with $J_1\pm J_2=J_1(I\pm T)$**, so $\alpha$ and $\beta$ are degenerate exactly at $T$ having eigenvalue $+1$, respectively $-1$.

*On a symplectic vector space $(\mathbb R^{2m},\gamma)$ the operator $L=\gamma\wedge\cdot$, contraction with the INVERSE bivector, and their commutator form an $\mathfrak{sl}_2$, and $L^{m-k}:\Lambda^k\to\Lambda^{2m-k}$ is an isomorphism for $0\le k\le m$*, **needing only nondegeneracy, so $L:\Lambda^2\to\Lambda^4$ is injective at every $m\ge3$.** *At $m=2$ it maps six dimensions to one and has a five-dimensional kernel, which is where the witness lives.* **So a nondegenerate $\alpha$ or $\beta$ finishes it**, giving $\omega_2=\mp\omega_1$.

**Both degenerate at once is impossible.** *$J_1TJ_1^{-1}=-J_2J_1=T^{-1}$, so $E_+=\ker(T-I)$ and $E_-=\ker(T+I)$ are $J_1$-invariant, and on them $J_2=J_1$ and $J_2=-J_1$ respectively.* **Hence $\alpha$ ANNIHILATES $E_+$ and $\beta$ annihilates $E_-$**, so the $\Lambda^2E_-\otimes\Lambda^2E_+$ component of $\alpha\wedge\beta$ is the single term $\alpha|_{E_-}\wedge\beta|_{E_+}$, with nothing to cancel it. *Both restrictions are nondegenerate on their own pieces, which are nonzero precisely when both $\alpha$ and $\beta$ are degenerate.* **So that component is nonzero against $\alpha\wedge\beta=0$, whatever the rest of the spectrum does.** $\square$

> **THE FIRST VERSION IMPOSED A HYPOTHESIS AND NEVER USED IT.** *It ran the last step only where $T$ had no eigenvalues off $\pm1$, and concluded that a nontrivial rotation part survived, leaving the question "open at $m\ge4$".* **The rotation part is irrelevant: it contributes nothing to that component.** *Both reviewers found this independently, and it is the third time on this branch that a block derived the right relation and used less than it gave.*

> **AND THE SURVIVING-LOCUS SENTENCE WAS FALSE AT $m=2$ AS WELL.** *The witness there has $T^2=-\mathrm{Id}$ and so neither $\pm1$ eigenvalue*, against a sentence saying both are present. **Its $\alpha$ is nondegenerate and lies in $\ker L_\beta$**, which is exactly what $m\ge3$ forbids and $m=2$ permits ([r837](../reviews/REVIEW_r837.md)).

> **STRENGTHENINGS RECORDED, EACH A REVIEWER'S OWN AND UNAUDITED.** *An elementary Darboux argument gives the injectivity without citing hard Lefschetz, consuming $m\ge3$ visibly and returning the five-dimensional kernel at $m=2$.* **And EVERY eigenvalue of $T$ has even multiplicity**, by the quaternionic structure $\mathrm{conj}\circ J_1$ on each eigenspace, *which at $m=4$ would have forced $\operatorname{spec}T=\{1,1,-1,-1,i,i,-i,-i\}$ at all three pairs, had the case survived* ([r837](../reviews/REVIEW_r837.md)).

> **AND IT CLOSES RANK TWO, WHICH IS A COINCIDENT PAIR AND NOTHING ELSE.** *At $\Lambda^2$-rank two, trace-freeness gives $\lambda_2=-\lambda_1$, so Ricci-flatness is $\lambda_1(\omega_1^2-\omega_2^2)=0$ and first Bianchi is $\lambda_1(\omega_1\wedge\omega_1-\omega_2\wedge\omega_2)=0$*, **both coincidences, at $\lambda_1\ne0$, and no further condition.** *So [L195](#lemma-l195-a-common-square-with-two-distinct-eigenvalues-forces-two-forms-equal-up-to-sign) forces the common square to be $c\,\mathrm{Id}$ and this lemma then gives $\omega_2=\pm\omega_1$ at every $m\ge3$, against orthogonality.* **Rank two is therefore empty at every $\dim V\ge6$**, which retires [L187](#lemma-l187-two-non-decomposable-image-forms-force-dim-vequiv0pmod4-with-dim-vge8)'s surviving case. *This trace is the parent's and is UNAUDITED; the three lemmas it composes are each Gate 2 passed.*

> **SO THE WHOLE OF [I38](../plan.md) FOLLOWS, AND IT IS THE ITEM'S OWN THEOREM.** *Rank one is impossible, trace-freeness forcing the single eigenvalue to vanish; rank two and rank three are empty above four dimensions; and [L178](#lemma-l178-every-weyl-tensor-of-lambda2-rank-at-most-three-has-thetalesqrt32-with-equality-exactly-at-w_mathbbcp2) caps $\theta$ on a four-dimensional support.* **Every $W\in\mathcal W_n$ with $\Lambda^2$-rank at most three satisfies $\theta\le\sqrt{3/2}$, with equality exactly at padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$.** *What this does NOT do is reach [I1](../plan.md): the bound is ATTAINED here, so the family is the sharpest test of the conjecture rather than a step toward it, and the reduction from general rank is [I68](../plan.md), which has no method.*

> **TWO PLACES THE BLOCK STILL OWES A LINE.** *The $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ isomorphism holds for $0\le k\le m$ and the statement did not say so; and the cross-term paragraph should name the $\Lambda^2R$ block it silently covers* ([r836](../reviews/REVIEW_r836.md)). **And $\operatorname{tr}T=0$ in full says more than was used**: it forces $\dim E_+=\dim E_-=m$ when $R=0$ and forbids $R=0$ outright at odd $m$. *A fourth instance on this branch of taking a strict weakening of a derived relation.*

> **NO STEP USES RICCI-FLATNESS, FIRST BIANCHI OR MEMBERSHIP OF $\mathcal W_n$.** *Both Weyl conditions are automatic on the coincident branch: common squares make $\sum_i\lambda_i\omega_i^2=-A\sum_i\lambda_i=0$ and common self-wedges make $\sum_i\lambda_i\,\omega_i\wedge\omega_i=\Omega\sum_i\lambda_i=0$, each by trace-freeness alone.* **So this branch is pure linear algebra**, which is why no curvature machinery has ever helped on it.

#### Lemma L184, the coincident case at $\dim V=6$ is empty

*Stands beside [L183](#lemma-l183-a-rank-three-weyl-tensor-is-an-orthonormal-triple-whose-images-are-collinear). **AUDITED FOUR TIMES**: [r781](../reviews/REVIEW_r781.md) and [r782](../reviews/REVIEW_r782.md), then [r832](../reviews/REVIEW_r832.md) and [r833](../reviews/REVIEW_r833.md), each re-deriving the three cases separately and each confirming all of them. **The header read `Unaudited` until 2026-09-10 and the label propagated**, into [L194](#lemma-l194-the-distinct-branch-of-l183-is-empty-so-a-rank-three-weyl-tensors-three-images-coincide)'s closing box and into the brief of the round that caught it.*

**Statement, in the form both audits established rather than the one first written.** *No orthonormal triple $\omega_1,\omega_2,\omega_3$ of $2$-forms with a common six-dimensional support has both its squares and its self-wedges coincident.* **No Weyl hypothesis is used anywhere**, in particular there is no rank-three $W\in\mathcal W_n$ with $\dim V=6$ whose three images under $\Phi$ coincide, which is the corollary the programme wanted.

> **AND THE MECHANISM IS ONE THING IN ALL THREE CASES: THE HYPOTHESES FORCE $\omega_i=\pm\omega_1$, WHICH THREE ORTHONORMAL FORMS CANNOT SATISFY.** *Case 2 by $\star$ being injective, case 3 by the cross term, and case 1 by orthogonality alone once the wedge hypothesis is kept, equal wedges make $s_{ij}s_{ik}$ independent of $i$, so $\omega_i=\eta_i\omega_1$ and $\operatorname{tr}(\omega_1\omega_2)=\mp2\ne0$.* **That retires case 1's appeal to [L181](#lemma-l181-ricci-flatness-and-first-bianchi-are-the-two-halves-of-one-matrix-and-together-they-empty-the-simultaneously-block-diagonal-family) and with it the odd-$n$ question**, *which was never the lemma's anyway: the brief's rendering of L181 dropped its remainder summand $P_0$, and L181 as stated covers odd $n$, both of its own reviewers having found the remainder inert.* **Supplied by [r781](../reviews/REVIEW_r781.md) and [r782](../reviews/REVIEW_r782.md), independently.**

**Setup.** Write $A=-\omega_i^2$, common by hypothesis, positive definite of trace $2$ on $V$. *Each $\omega_i$ commutes with $\omega_i^2$, hence preserves every eigenspace of $A$*, and $A$'s eigenvalues come in equal pairs, $\omega_i$ being skew with eigenvalues $\pm\mathrm ic_j$. **So $A$ has spectrum $\{c_1^2,c_1^2,c_2^2,c_2^2,c_3^2,c_3^2\}$ and the three cases are set by how many of the $c_j^2$ agree.** *Every input to that split is Weyl-free: the doubling is skewness, positive definiteness is the common six-dimensional support, and $\operatorname{tr}A=2$ is the unit norm, so the three cases exhaust the possibilities for any three positive reals and the split survives the strengthened statement, where there is no tensor to constrain the spectrum.* **And the corollary follows because a rank-three Weyl tensor's eigenforms are exactly such a triple**, *equal squares already force a common support, so no reconciliation step is needed and nothing is lost by dropping the Weyl hypothesis.* **The bridge from [L183](#lemma-l183-a-rank-three-weyl-tensor-is-an-orthonormal-triple-whose-images-are-collinear)'s language is that $\Phi(\omega)=(\omega^2,\omega\wedge\omega)$, so "the images coincide" IS "the squares and the self-wedges coincide"**, *a definition this block used and did not carry, which both reviewers recorded as debt rather than guessing at.*

**Case 1, the $c_j^2$ pairwise distinct.** *$A$ is positive definite on $V$ because $V=\operatorname{im}A$, and its multiplicities **on $V$** are all even, off $V$ the zero eigenvalue has multiplicity $n-6$, so the evenness is a statement about $A|_V$ and was written as though about $A$.* **$A$'s eigenspaces are then three planes $P_1,P_2,P_3$, each preserved by every $\omega_i$, and a skew map on a plane is a multiple of its area form**, so $\omega_i=\sum_js_{ij}c_j\varepsilon_j$ with $s_{ij}=\pm1$. *Equal wedges force $s_{ij}s_{ik}c_jc_k$ to be independent of $i$, and $c_jc_k>0$ cancels, so $s_{ij}s_{ik}$ is independent of $i$; setting $\eta_i=s_{i1}s_{11}$ gives $s_{ij}=\eta_is_{1j}$ for **every** $j$, hence $\omega_i=\eta_i\omega_1$ exactly.* **Then $\operatorname{tr}(\omega_1\omega_2)=-2\eta_2=\mp2\ne0$, against orthogonality.** *Individual $s_{ij}$ is not canonical, each $\varepsilon_j$ being fixed up to sign, but the independence claim and $\eta_i$ both are.* **This closes case 1 with no Weyl input and no appeal to [L181](#lemma-l181-ricci-flatness-and-first-bianchi-are-the-two-halves-of-one-matrix-and-together-they-empty-the-simultaneously-block-diagonal-family)**, *whose conclusion is about a $W\in\mathcal W_n$ and so cannot reach a bare triple at all, which is why the appeal had to go rather than merely being improved on.* $\square$

**Case 2, all three equal, $A=cI_6$.** *Then $\omega_i/\sqrt c$ is orthogonal and skew, a complex structure, and unit norm forces $c=\tfrac13$, so $\omega_i^2=-\tfrac13\mathrm{Id}_6$ and $c$ is not a free parameter.* **The Hodge star here is $V$'s, not $\mathbb R^n$'s**: $\star(J\wedge J)$ lands in $\Lambda^2$ only in six dimensions, and read on $\mathbb R^n$ for $n>6$ the identity is false. *On $V$, every compatible complex structure satisfies $\star_V(J\wedge J)=2\epsilon_JJ$ with $\epsilon_J=\pm1$ by orientation.* **Equal $J_i\wedge J_i$ forces $J_i=\pm K$ for a single $K$**, *and nothing weaker survives, since $\star$ is injective, so equal wedges give $\omega_j=\pm\omega_1$ exactly.* **The contradiction is then linear independence rather than orthogonality**: $J_i=\pm K$ leaves the three forms spanning a line, so the rank cannot be three. *[r780](../reviews/REVIEW_r780.md) supplied both strengthenings; the trace condition $\operatorname{tr}(J_iJ_j)\ne0$ is not needed **for the triple**, where linear independence does it, *for a pair it is exactly what is needed, linear independence having nothing to bite on, and this clause was written without that scope*, and $\epsilon_J$ is well defined because $U(3)\subset SO(6)$ preserves orientation, so the $J$-induced orientation is frame-independent, *a reviewer proposed $SO(6)$'s connectedness instead and this block adopted it; that is a different statement and does not supply the well-definedness, so the original attribution was right.* **This closes nothing outside $\dim V=6$**, where alone $\star_V(J\wedge J)$ lands in $\Lambda^2V$, the dimension count $\binom{2m}4=\binom{2m}2$ holding at $2m=6$ and nowhere else. *The constraint is on $\dim V$ and not on $n$, which the block conflated twice, once in the case body and again in the entailment box below, the second surviving the first repair.* $\square$

**Case 3, exactly two agree, spectrum $(a,a,b)$ with $a\ne b$.** *$A$'s eigenspaces are a $4$-plane $Q$ and a plane $P$, both preserved, so $\omega_i=\sqrt a\,J_i\oplus\tau_i\sqrt b\,\varepsilon_P$ with $J_i$ a complex structure on $Q$ and $\tau_i=\pm1$.* **Equal wedges force $J_i=\sigma_iJ$ for one $J$**: the wedge splits into a $\Lambda^4Q$ part $a\,J_i\wedge J_i$ and a cross term $2\tau_i\sqrt{ab}\,J_i\wedge\varepsilon_P$ in $\Lambda^2Q\otimes\Lambda^2P$, the $\varepsilon_P\wedge\varepsilon_P$ term vanishing. **$J\wedge J=2\eta\,\mathrm{vol}_Q$ with $\eta=\pm1$ according to whether $J$'s orientation matches $Q$'s. NOT $+2$ always, which a first draft asserted.** *The cross term is what carries the conclusion*: equating it across $i$ gives $\tau_iJ_i$ all equal, hence $J_i=\sigma_iJ$ for one $J$, **and $\rho_i=\sigma_i\tau_i$ is constant**, which retires the three-signs step and gives $2a=-b$ at once. *Writing that as "$\sigma_i=\tau_i$" is choice-dependent, $J$ and $-J$ swap it, and $\rho_i$ constant is the invariant form ([r783](../reviews/REVIEW_r783.md)).* **With $J:=\tau_1J_1$ the two parts carry the same sign, so $\omega_i=\tau_i\tau_1\omega_1$ and case 3 exhibits the common mechanism too**, in a line the case itself did not take. **The $\Lambda^4Q$ condition is then automatic, but only in that order, and the sequencing is load-bearing.** *Read alone the $\Lambda^4Q$ equality says $\eta_1=\eta_2=\eta_3$, which is a real restriction; it becomes vacuous only after the cross term has already given $J_i=\sigma_iJ$, since $(-J)\wedge(-J)=J\wedge J$ leaves $\eta$ unchanged, *so the corrected sign costs the argument nothing, and the block had the right conclusion for a reason it stated wrongly.* **Then orthogonality is a sign identity that has no solution.** *With $\operatorname{tr}(J^2)=-4$ on $Q$ and $\varepsilon_P^2=-\mathrm{Id}_P$, $\operatorname{tr}(\omega_i\omega_j)=-4a\sigma_i\sigma_j-2b\tau_i\tau_j$, so orthogonality reads $4a\,\sigma_i\sigma_j=-2b\,\tau_i\tau_j$ at all three pairs. Setting $\rho_i=\sigma_i\tau_i$, the ratio $\sigma_i\sigma_j/(\tau_i\tau_j)=\rho_i\rho_j$ must be one constant, and three signs with all pairwise products equal force that constant to be $+1$*, **whence $4a=-2b$, impossible for $a,b>0$.** $\square$

> **THE OBSERVATION THAT BECAME [L191](#lemma-l191-two-forms-with-a-common-six-dimensional-support-and-both-coincidences-are-equal-up-to-sign-so-the-rank-two-branch-is-empty-at-dim-v6).** *[r783](../reviews/REVIEW_r783.md) noticed that every contradiction above uses only a **pair** and flagged it for its own audit rather than adopting it, which is the discipline that round was convened to enforce, and the audit is now on the record.* **Traced through: case 1's equal wedges give $s_{1j}s_{1k}=s_{2j}s_{2k}$ at all three pairs, which in the sign variables $u_j=s_{1j}s_{2j}$ reads $u_ju_k=1$**, *three equations in three signs, forcing all $u_j$ equal, so $s_{2j}=\eta s_{1j}$ for every $j$ and $\omega_2=\eta\omega_1$, whence $\operatorname{tr}(\omega_1\omega_2)=-2\eta\ne0$. The count is not two free ratios against three constraints, which is what it looks like before the substitution;* case 2 gives $\omega_2=\pm\omega_1$ **from the identity $\star_V(J\wedge J)=2\epsilon_JJ$ alone**, *not from $\star$ being injective, which the block cites and never uses, since applying any linear map to an equality suffices*; and case 3's cross term gives $\omega_2=\tau_2\tau_1\omega_1$.** **ONE of the triple version's closings is genuinely three-form and a pair version must REWRITE it**: case 2's rank argument, which orthogonality replaces. *Case 3's "three signs with all pairwise products equal force $+1$" is indeed false for two, $\rho_1\rho_2=-1$ admits $b=2a$, and what excludes that configuration is the CROSS TERM rather than orthogonality; the corrected $\rho_i$-constant sentence in the case body already retires the count and is pair-valid.* **[r809](../reviews/REVIEW_r809.md) built the admitted configuration in closed form and it satisfies orthogonality**, so a fold that leaned on orthogonality there would have been wrong. **And the identity $\star_V(J\wedge J)=2\epsilon_JJ$ is used in case 2 only**, and it is proved: `starWedgeIdentity_holds` in [I67PfSign.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I67PfSign.lean), with $\epsilon_J=\mathrm{Pf}(J)$. *Each closes on two forms, and the case split needs only the common square.* **So the lemma proves the strictly stronger pair statement, and stronger still**: *two $2$-forms with a common six-dimensional support, equal squares and equal self-wedges satisfy $\omega_2=\pm\omega_1$*, **a rigidity statement, orthogonality being spent once at the last line.** *That is [L191](#lemma-l191-two-forms-with-a-common-six-dimensional-support-and-both-coincidences-are-equal-up-to-sign-so-the-rank-two-branch-is-empty-at-dim-v6), Gate 2 passed by [r808](../reviews/REVIEW_r808.md) and [r809](../reviews/REVIEW_r809.md), and it empties the rank-two Weyl branch at $\dim V=6$.* **The one rewrite a pair version needs is case 2's, where orthogonality replaces the rank argument.** **Four `HYPOTHESIS-DEBT`s were named across the round, and all four are discharged here.** *Each is short, which is why they were asserted; none was on file, which is why they were debts.*

> **(i) The $\varepsilon_j\wedge\varepsilon_k$ are independent.** *For $j<k$ they are the distinct basis $4$-forms $e_{2j-1,2j,2k-1,2k}$, orthonormal in $\Lambda^4$.*
> **(ii) $\alpha\mapsto\alpha\wedge\varepsilon_P$ is injective on $\Lambda^2Q$.** *$Q\perp P$, so the map is $\alpha\mapsto\alpha\otimes\varepsilon_P$ into $\Lambda^2Q\wedge\Lambda^2P$ under the natural isomorphism, and $\varepsilon_P\ne0$.*
> **(iii) $\star_V$ is an isomorphism $\Lambda^4V\to\Lambda^2V$.** *The Hodge star is an isometry $\Lambda^k\to\Lambda^{\dim V-k}$ for every $k$; at $\dim V=6$ that is $\Lambda^4\to\Lambda^2$. "Injective" was the weaker word and the wrong reason.*
> **(iv) The base constant is $2$, computed.** *In a $J$-adapted frame $J=\varepsilon_1+\varepsilon_2+\varepsilon_3$, so $J\wedge J=2(\varepsilon_1\varepsilon_2+\varepsilon_1\varepsilon_3+\varepsilon_2\varepsilon_3)$, the diagonal terms vanishing. Each star returns the missing area form*, $\star e_{1234}=e_{56}$, $\star e_{1256}=e_{34}$, $\star e_{3456}=e_{12}$, **all three signs $+$ when the frame's orientation is the fixed one**, *so $\star(J\wedge J)=2J$, and $-2J$ when it is not.* **The sign is that comparison and nothing else.**
> **Well-definedness of $\epsilon_J$ is the $U(3)$ fact**: *two $J$-adapted frames differ by a $J$-unitary map, $U(3)\subset SO(6)$ preserves orientation, so the $J$-induced orientation does not depend on the frame.* **Connectedness of $SO(6)$ is a different statement and does not supply this**, a clause of this block credited it and was wrong.

> **WHAT IS ENTAILED.** *At $\dim V=6$ the coincident case is empty, and [L194](#lemma-l194-the-distinct-branch-of-l183-is-empty-so-a-rank-three-weyl-tensors-three-images-coincide) empties the other branch of [L183](#lemma-l183-a-rank-three-weyl-tensor-is-an-orthonormal-triple-whose-images-are-collinear) at every $n$, so **NOTHING remains there and the rank-three branch at $\dim V=6$ is empty**.* **This sentence read that the distinct-and-collinear case survives until 2026-09-10**, which was true of the record before L194 and false fifteen lines below it.* **At $\dim V\ge8$ the case split does not even apply**, being a trichotomy on the three values $c_1^2,c_2^2,c_3^2$ that six dimensions supply, *at $\dim V=8$ there are four, and a pattern such as $(a,a,b,b)$ falls outside all three cases entirely.* **Only case 1's argument transfers, to the pairwise-distinct stratum**, and the rest is untouched, *cases 2 and 3 being strictly six-dimensional, the first because $\star(J\wedge J)$ lands in $\Lambda^2$ only at $n=6$ and the second because its splitting is $4+2$.* **A first version of this box read "one case left" while its own next sentence said nothing reaches $\dim V\ge8$; the two contradicted each other**, and [r782](../reviews/REVIEW_r782.md) caught it. *A ledger row taken from the inflated form would have been wrong, and rows are what later work is built on.*

## Analysis the plan's items carried
*Step 7 leaves each item its identifier, theorem, grade, route and what is owed, and the reasoning comes here. Each was checked against the record first and none was a duplicate.*

### I36, every local maximiser above $\sqrt{3/2}$ is coordinate-diagonal

**Read the grade with care: `attackable` is the uncertain column**, and the item is filed at $1$ on a *shape* rather than a mechanism. **GIVEN A MECHANISM 2026-09-07, as [Lemma L174](findings/second-order.md).** *[L94](findings/LEDGER.md)'s equality case is an "iff", $\kappa^2\le2\lVert M\rVert_F^2$ with equality **exactly** at coordinate-diagonal, so at unit norm the item's shape claim is the scalar claim $\kappa^2=2$, and the two are equivalent under the outer quantifier.* **So the `attackable` column can be re-read**: "is diagonal in some frame" is an existential over frames, while $\kappa^2=2$ is an extremal condition on a continuous function the record already estimates. *The item is no less open, the same question is now asked of a number.* **And its threshold sits below $\theta_0(12)$**, since $\tfrac{220}{144}>\tfrac32$, so $\theta>\sqrt{3/2}$ is the weaker hypothesis and covers strictly more maximisers. **AND THE STRICT INEQUALITY IS LOAD-BEARING**, which the item did not say: padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ is a local maximum at $\theta=\sqrt{3/2}$ **exactly** ([r316](reviews/REVIEW_r316.md)) with $\kappa^2=1$ ([r102](reviews/REVIEW_r102.md)), so at $\theta\ge\sqrt{3/2}$ the item is **false**. **THAT DEBT IS DISCHARGED 2026-09-08 as [Lemma L176](findings/diagonal-half.md)**, the competitor has $\Lambda^2$-rank three ($W^-=0$, $W^+\propto(2,-1,-1)$) and the coordinate-diagonal locus has rank at least four, its vanishing row sums killing every graph on three or fewer edges. *The obligation had rested on a computed $\kappa^2=1$ and now rests on the rank.* **L176 is Gate 2 landed**, [r769](../reviews/REVIEW_r769.md) and [r770](../reviews/REVIEW_r770.md) confirm the lemma in full, every defect they found sitting in its corollaries.

**THE BRIDGE THIS ITEM RESTS ON IS MACHINE-CHECKED, AND THE PLAN RECORDED IT AS OWED, 2026-09-19.** *The reframing above turns "diagonal in some frame" into the scalar claim $\kappa^2=2\lVert M\rVert_F^2$ through [L94](LEDGER.md)'s equality case, and [plan-lean.md](../plan-lean.md) carried `L94` in its hypothesis-debt table as a claim proved nowhere.* **`kappaSq_eq_iff` in [L94Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L94Statement.lean) PROVES it**, standard three axioms and no `sorry`: at every $n$ and every Weyl $W$, $\kappa^2(W)=2\lVert M\rVert_F^2$ holds exactly when some frame makes $W$ coordinate-diagonal, with `IsWeyl W` the only hypothesis. **At unit norm that is `kappaSq_eq_two_iff`, $\kappa^2=2$**, which is the form the item uses. *So the existential over frames really is an extremal condition on one scalar, and the item's own note that "the same question is now asked of a number" is secured rather than conditional.*

**WHAT THE DEBT TABLE'S `L94` ROW IS ABOUT, WHICH IS A DIFFERENT OBJECT.** *The `Prop`s named `L94` in [L174.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L174.lean) and [L174Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L174Statement.lean) are ABSTRACT, quantified over arbitrary types with `IsCoordDiag`, `kappaSq` and `IsUnit` as parameters.* **BOTH ARE NOW DISCHARGED**, `l94_concrete` and `l94_concrete'` in [I36Budget.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I36Budget.lean), one term serving both types ([r1002](../reviews/REVIEW_r1002.md)), *and the abstract binder in `shape_iff_budget` correctly stays, `L94` being false at `Weyl = Frame = Unit` with `kappaSq = 0`, so it excludes something.* **What the instantiation does NOT buy is any I36 content beyond `l94_concrete` itself**: `t` and `IsLocalMax` are passed through and never read, so the link to the plan item is a naming rather than a typing until $\theta$ and local-maximality are defined at these types, which no file does. *The table also listed [L94Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L94Statement.lean) as a CONSUMER of the claim it in fact proves.* *That is the fourth count of this shape found in two days, all of them a name searched for rather than a binder.*

**AND THE REASON THE LINK IS A NAMING IS STRUCTURAL: THE TWO LEAN LAYERS DO NOT MEET, 2026-09-19.** *[L94](LEDGER.md)'s objects live on `Tensor4 n`, which is `Fin n → Fin n → Fin n → Fin n → ℝ`, carrying $\kappa^2$, `normM2`, `IsFrame`, `rotate` and `CoordinateDiagonal`. The record's $\theta$, $P$, $\lVert\cdot\rVert$ and the whole diagonal-locus development live on OPERATORS, `Matrix ι ι ℝ → Matrix ι ι ℝ`.* **Three files in the tree mention `Tensor4` and NONE of them mentions `RealLayer`**, so no declaration anywhere converts one to the other. *That is why `shape_iff_budget_concrete` can only quantify $\theta$ away: there is no $\theta$ at those types to supply.*

**What the bridge would be**, and it is [I108](../plan.md): a map from a curvature operator on $\Lambda^2$ to its four-index tensor, with $\mathrm{IsWeyl}$ carried across in both directions, after which $\theta$, local-maximality and the threshold are all expressible at the types L94 is stated over. *Until it exists, every statement joining L94's $\kappa^2$ to the record's $\theta$ is a statement in prose.*

**WHAT I36'S FORMALISATION IS NOW BLOCKED ON, AND IT IS ONE MISSING INSTANCE, 2026-09-19.** *[I108](../plan.md) carried the bridge onto the bundled operators, where `CurvLin` is `Matrix ι ι ℝ →ₗ[ℝ] Matrix ι ι ℝ` and $\mathcal W_n$ is a finite-dimensional submodule.* **`CurvLin` CARRIES NO TOPOLOGY**, Mathlib supplying one on `ContinuousLinearMap` and not on `LinearMap`, so `IsLocalMaxOn` does not elaborate there at all. *The layer's only use of local-maximality, [L9Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L9Statement.lean)'s `L9`, is therefore on the BARE function type, whose product topology is the topology of pointwise convergence.*

**THE DEFECT CUTS BOTH WAYS AND THE DIRECTION IS DECIDED BY QUANTIFIER POSITION.** *A coarser topology has fewer and larger neighbourhoods, so the neighbourhood filter is coarser and `IsLocalMaxOn` is HARDER to satisfy, which makes it a STRONGER predicate.* **In a hypothesis, as in I36's "every local maximiser is coordinate-diagonal", a stronger predicate makes the theorem WEAKER** ([r1003](../reviews/REVIEW_r1003.md)). **In an existential conclusion, as in L9's "there exists a local maximiser with nonzero defect", it makes the claim STRONGER than the record means**, and L9 carries a `sorry`, so it is an open obligation harder than intended. *r1003 named the first direction and the second follows from the same fact.*

**What would settle it.** *A norm on $\mathcal W_n$, which the record already has in `innerOp`: the layer defines the pairing, proves it symmetric and computes it, and nothing turns it into an instance.* **Finite-dimensionality makes the choice free**, every Hausdorff vector topology on a finite-dimensional real space being the same one, so the instance fixes a convention rather than a fact. *Until it exists, every statement in the layer quantifying over local maximisers means something other than what the record means.*

**A HANDLE ON COORDINATE-DIAGONALITY, 2026-09-10: A FAMILY OF $\Lambda^4$ CONDITIONS.** *Working notes, not a numbered result.* **GATE 2 by [r846](../reviews/REVIEW_r846.md) and [r847](../reviews/REVIEW_r847.md) on identical briefs, and [r848](../reviews/REVIEW_r848.md) on the Lean file.** *The witness stands and every step of (ii) is confirmed by both; four conclusions drawn around them did not, and one of the two reviewers closed the block's own open question at $n=4$ in the affirmative. Each finding was reproduced independently before it was applied.* **What the item lacks is any computable test for "diagonal in some frame", an existential over frames**, and $\mathrm{Alt}$ supplies a candidate because it is frame-covariant and vanishes on the locus.

**(i) DERIVED, and the closure is diagonality in a FIXED basis rather than the vanishing of four-distinct entries.** *A $W$ diagonal in the coordinate $2$-plane basis has every power diagonal in that same basis, since diagonal operators compose entrywise, and a diagonal operator has $W_{abcd}=0$ at every four-distinct entry.* **So $\mathrm{Alt}(W^k)=0$ for every $k\ge1$ at every coordinate-diagonal $W$**, with no Weyl hypothesis and no criticality. *The vanishing of four-distinct entries is NOT by itself closed under powers, and the block first argued through it*: [r847](../reviews/REVIEW_r847.md) exhibits $M=uv^{\mathsf T}+vu^{\mathsf T}+vw^{\mathsf T}+wv^{\mathsf T}$ on $\varphi_{12},\varphi_{13},\varphi_{34}$ with $M_{1234}=0$ and $(M^2)_{1234}=1$, reproduced here. **The same middle step stood at [L199](bounds.md)'s block and is corrected there too**, and (i) itself is that block's own paragraph re-derived, credited to [r843](../reviews/REVIEW_r843.md) and uncited here until now. *Machine-checked as `bianchi_diagOp_iterate` in [I36DiagTest.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I36DiagTest.lean), where the composition through `diagOp_iterate` is exactly the repair; [r848](../reviews/REVIEW_r848.md) caught that the two halves were proved and never composed.*

**(ii) DERIVED, and it collapses the family to one condition per eigenvalue.** *Write $\mathrm{Alt}(A)=\tfrac16\sum_\alpha\mu_\alpha\,\omega_\alpha\wedge\omega_\alpha$ and group the spectrum by distinct value: $\mathrm{Alt}(W^k)=\tfrac16\sum_\mu\mu^k\,\mathfrak b(P_\mu)$ with $P_\mu$ the eigenprojector.* **The Vandermonde matrix on the distinct nonzero $\mu$ is invertible**, so $$\mathrm{Alt}(W^k)=0\ \text{ for every }k\ge1\iff \mathfrak b(P_\mu)=0\ \text{ for every eigenvalue }\mu\ne0.$$ *Both reviewers confirm every step, and both note that invertibility needs distinctness **and** nonvanishing, both of which the grouping supplies.* **Only $m\le\binom n2$ powers are consumed**, so "every $k\ge1$" is what the statement says and more than the proof spends. *$\mathfrak b(P_\mu)$ is independent of the orthonormal basis chosen inside a degenerate eigenspace, which is what makes the test well defined and which the per-eigenform reading would not give.* **The zero eigenvalue drops out and $\mathfrak b(P_0)$ is then forced**, since $\mathfrak b(\mathrm{Id})=0$.

**(iii) THE TEST DOES NOT CHARACTERISE THE LOCUS AMONG SYMMETRIC OPERATORS, by an explicit witness.** *Take $n=4$, fix $t$ with $\sin2t\ne0$, put $g_2=\cos t\,e_2+\sin t\,e_3$ and $g_3=-\sin t\,e_2+\cos t\,e_3$, and let $W$ have the six orthonormal eigenforms $$e_1\wedge g_2,\quad e_1\wedge g_3,\quad e_1\wedge e_4,\quad e_2\wedge e_3,\quad e_2\wedge e_4,\quad e_3\wedge e_4$$ with any six distinct nonzero eigenvalues.* **Every one is decomposable, so $\mathfrak b(P_\mu)=0$ at every $\mu$ and $\mathrm{Alt}(W^k)=0$ for every $k$**, and the six are an orthonormal basis of $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^4$ because $e_2\wedge e_3=g_2\wedge g_3$. *Both reviewers verified orthonormality, spanning and decomposability in closed form, and both completed the non-diagonality argument the block had asserted.*

> **THE COMPLETE NON-DIAGONALITY ARGUMENT, WHICH THE BLOCK FIRST ASSERTED.** *Saying "the three forms sharing a line" singles out nothing, since two lines lie in three supports each, $\mathbb Re_1$ and $\mathbb Re_4$* ([r846](../reviews/REVIEW_r846.md)). **The count is the kill**: of the fifteen pairwise support intersections exactly two are lines lying in three planes, and a frame needs four such lines, one per vector. *[r847](../reviews/REVIEW_r847.md) completed the remaining case $f_1=\pm e_4$ and checked that the witness survives zero-padding to every $n\ge4$, so the statement is about $\mathcal W_n$ rather than about $n=4$ alone.*

> **THE WITNESS LEAVES $\mathcal W_4$ AT EVERY ADMISSIBLE PARAMETER, AND IN CLOSED FORM RATHER THAN BY MEASUREMENT.** *First Bianchi is $\mathrm{Alt}(W)=0$, which is FREE here*, the all-decomposable set satisfies it identically, whatever the eigenvalues, and both reviewers confirm it. **Ricci-flatness is what fails, and the record's own [L180](#lemma-l180-ricci-flatness-is-a-signed-psd-identity-and-it-names-the-support) gives the entry**: $\mathrm{Ric}_{23}=\tfrac12\sin2t\,(\mu_1-\mu_2)$, nonzero at every admissible parameter, replacing a screened $\lvert\mathrm{Ric}\rvert=10$ whose eigenvalues the block never named ([r846](../reviews/REVIEW_r846.md)). *Equivalently and in two lines: $\star$ sends $e_1\wedge g_2$ to $g_3\wedge e_4$, which is not an eigenform* ([r847](../reviews/REVIEW_r847.md)). **Screened against the closed form at $t=0.7$ and $t=0.4$, agreeing to six decimals.**

> **AND TUNING THE FAMILY INTO $\mathcal W_4$ FAILS BY PRODUCING A DIAGONAL POINT.** *Ricci-flatness is linear in the eigenvalues, and on this family its solution space is ONE-DIMENSIONAL, spanned by $(\tfrac12,\tfrac12,-1,-1,\tfrac12,\tfrac12)$*, screened, and derived by [r846](../reviews/REVIEW_r846.md). **So it forces $\mu_1=\mu_2$ and $\mu_5=\mu_6$**, and then the eigenspaces are $e_1\wedge\langle e_2,e_3\rangle$ and $\langle e_2,e_3\rangle\wedge e_4$, each frame-independent inside its plane, *so the point is coordinate-diagonal after all.* **The obvious attack on the Weyl case is therefore closed, and it closes in the direction the test predicts.**

> **AND THE SAME THING HAPPENS AT EVERY $n$, DERIVED RATHER THAN SCREENED.** *The witness generalises by the trick that builds it: rotate $e_2,e_3$ by $t$, take the planes through $e_1$ from the rotated frame and every other plane from the standard one, giving an orthonormal basis of $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^n$ of decomposable forms at every $n$.* **Ricci-flatness there is exactly $\{w$ hollow symmetric, zero row sums, $w_{12}=w_{13}\}$, of dimension $\binom n2-n-1$**, $1,4,8,13$ at $n=4,5,6,7$, *and $\mu_1=\mu_2$ follows from the single $(2,3)$ entry at every $n$, in two lines* ([r849](../reviews/REVIEW_r849.md), [r850](../reviews/REVIEW_r850.md), each deriving what the block had screened). **That equality collapses the witness**: the $\mu_1$-eigenspace becomes $e_1\wedge\langle e_2,e_3\rangle$, a pencil and so entirely decomposable, and a decomposable basis of it may be chosen inside the standard frame.

> **THE CRUX IS COORDINATE-DIAGONALITY ITSELF, AND IT IS PROVED AT $n=4$ AND OPEN AT $n\ge5$.** *[L180](#lemma-l180-ricci-flatness-is-a-signed-psd-identity-and-it-names-the-support)(1) specialises in one line: $A_\alpha=\omega_\alpha\omega_\alpha^{\mathsf T}$ is $uu^{\mathsf T}+vv^{\mathsf T}$ at a decomposable $\omega_\alpha=u\wedge v$, which is the orthogonal projection $\Pi_{P_\alpha}$ onto its plane, so on the all-decomposable set Ricci-flatness reads $\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha\Pi_{P_\alpha}=0$.* **The obligation is EXISTENTIAL over decomposable eigenbases**: that *some* decomposable orthonormal eigenbasis of $W$ lies in a common frame. *Which is coordinate-diagonality, so it absorbs what this block earlier called a free fourth step.* **[r850](../reviews/REVIEW_r850.md) proves it at $n=4$** via $\Lambda^\pm$: $\Pi_P=\tfrac12\mathrm{Id}-\sum a_ib_j\sigma_i\bar\sigma_j$, so the identity is $\sum\lambda_\alpha=0$ together with the vanishing of the $\Lambda^+\otimes\Lambda^-$ block, whence $W=A\oplus C$ with each $a_\alpha,b_\alpha$ an eigenvector, and a three-case split on $\operatorname{spec}A$ exhibits the frame. *That argument has no analogue at $n\ge5$, which is the whole of what [I36b](../plan.md) still owes.*

> **THE UNIVERSAL FORM OF THE CRUX IS FALSE, AND THE BLOCK'S OWN WITNESS REFUTES IT.** *It read that $\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha\Pi_{P_\alpha}=0$ over the planes of an orthonormal basis of decomposable forms forces THOSE planes into a common frame.* **At $n=4$ the witness at weight $(\tfrac12,\tfrac12,-1,-1,\tfrac12,\tfrac12)$ satisfies the identity with every weight nonzero, at every $\sin2t\ne0$, and its six planes lie in no common frame**, only two lines sit in three of the six supports and a frame needs four, which is this block's own count. *The identity constrains $W$ and not a chosen basis, and the $\tfrac12$-eigenvalue has multiplicity four.* **So the universal reading is the error the paragraph below diagnoses in its own predecessor, at a second address** ([r849](../reviews/REVIEW_r849.md), [r850](../reviews/REVIEW_r850.md), independently and with the same witness).

> **AND THERE IS NO OFF-DIAGONAL "DETECTOR", WHICH THE BLOCK ASSERTED AS A CONVERSE.** *It read that a coordinate-diagonal operator has Ricci on the diagonal and nothing off it, therefore an off-diagonal entry detects frame mixing and nothing else.* **That converse is false**: $\varphi_{12}\otimes\varphi_{12}$ is coordinate-diagonal and its Ricci has $(2,3)$ entry $-\tfrac12\sin2t$ read in the rotated frame, reproduced here as $-0.492725$ at $t=0.7$. **And it is vacuous where it was used**: Ricci-flatness is frame-independent, so at the point the obligation quantifies over, every off-diagonal entry is zero and there is nothing to read. *What is true and is all that was used: the equation $\mathrm{Ric}_{23}=\tfrac12\sin2t\,(\lambda_1-\lambda_2)=0$ FORCES $\lambda_1=\lambda_2$ on this family, which is a consequence of the identity rather than a diagnostic on an arbitrary operator.*

> **PROVENANCE, AND TWO HYPOTHESES INHERITED WITHOUT NOTICE.** *The specialisation $\omega\omega^{\mathsf T}=\Pi_P$ is [L185](#lemma-l185-the-all-decomposable-branch-is-empty)'s own proof line, recorded 2026-09-09 and uncited here until now* ([r849](../reviews/REVIEW_r849.md), [r850](../reviews/REVIEW_r850.md)). **It holds for exactly one normalisation**, $u\wedge v=vu^{\mathsf T}-uv^{\mathsf T}$, which is the record's own $\varphi_{ab}=E_{ba}-E_{ab}$; *the $1/\sqrt2$ wedge returns $\tfrac12\Pi_P$ and breaks L180's trace-$2$ clause.* **And L180 assumes every $\lambda_i\ne0$ while this block sums over a full eigenbasis carrying zeros**, the extension is free, L180(1)'s proof spending no nonvanishing, and it was unstated. *A byproduct neither reviewer found on the record: $\sum_\alpha\Pi_{P_\alpha}=(n-1)\mathrm{Id}$ for any orthonormal decomposable basis of $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^n$, checked at $n=4,5,6,7$.*

> **THE EARLIER FORM OF THE CRUX IS FALSE TOO, WHICH THE RECORD'S OWN WITNESS SHOWS.** *It read that Ricci-flatness forces equal eigenvalues on two decomposable eigenforms whose planes share a line.* **The four-cycle refutes it**, being coordinate-diagonal and Ricci-flat with $e_1\wedge e_2$ and $e_1\wedge e_3$ sharing $\mathbb Re_1$ at eigenvalues $1$ and $0$. *And the zero-eigenvalue objection to that witness is closed*: the $K_4$ weights $(2,1,-3,-3,1,2)$ are Ricci-flat with all six eigenvalues nonzero and all row sums zero, reproduced here ([r850](../reviews/REVIEW_r850.md)).

**(iv) An earlier attempt, kept because its collapse says why the working witness needed six eigenforms rather than two.** *At $n=4$ take $E=\mathrm{span}\{\omega_1,\omega_2\}$ with $\omega_1=(e_{12}+e_{34})/\sqrt2$ self-dual and $\omega_2=(e_{13}+e_{24})/\sqrt2$ anti-self-dual, so that $\mathrm{Pf}(\omega_1)+\mathrm{Pf}(\omega_2)=\tfrac12-\tfrac12=0$ and $\mathfrak b(P_E)=0$, and set $W=\nu\,\mathrm{Id}+(\mu-\nu)P_E$, whose other eigenprojector inherits $\mathfrak b(P_{E^\perp})=\mathfrak b(\mathrm{Id})-\mathfrak b(P_E)=0$.* **It is diagonal**: $\omega_1\pm\omega_2$ are decomposable, $(e_2+e_3)\wedge(e_4-e_1)$ and $-(e_2-e_3)\wedge(e_1+e_4)$ up to scale, orthogonal as forms, **and their two planes are mutually orthogonal**, all four inner products checked by both reviewers, so the frame $\tfrac1{\sqrt2}(e_2+e_3,\,e_4-e_1,\,e_2-e_3,\,e_1+e_4)$ makes $E=\mathrm{span}\{f_1\wedge f_2,\,f_3\wedge f_4\}$. *That the planes are orthogonal is a fact about this pair and not a pattern across a spectrum, which the block's box overstated* ([r847](../reviews/REVIEW_r847.md)).

> **WHAT THE COLLAPSE SAID, AND IT POINTED STRAIGHT AT (iii).** *Non-decomposability of individual eigenforms is not an obstruction, because $\mathfrak b(P_E)=0$ lets a two-dimensional $E$ carry a decomposable pair even when no basis vector chosen first is decomposable.* **The working witness of (iii) instead makes every eigenvalue simple**, which turns each condition into "$\omega_\mu$ is decomposable" and leaves the frame free to fail on one pair.

> **THE FENCES, WITH THE CITATION CORRECTED.** *[F37](fence-posts.md) forbids higher variations and the test uses none, and [F6](fence-posts.md) forbids a moment argument and $\mathfrak b$ is not a moment; both clear.* **[F44](second-order.md) lives in [second-order.md](second-order.md) rather than in [fence-posts.md](fence-posts.md)**, which the block and [plan.md](../plan.md) both mis-cited, *and what clears it is the TEST being a frame-covariant condition rather than the refutation in (iii)* ([r846](../reviews/REVIEW_r846.md), [r847](../reviews/REVIEW_r847.md)).

> **SUB-STEP (a) HAS ITS CLASSIFICATION, SOURCED, AND IT BOUNDS THE PROBLEM RATHER THAN SOLVING IT.** *[Ghorpade–Patil–Pillai](../references/decomposable-subspaces-grassmann-codes/notes.md) Theorem 6, the Structure Theorem for Decomposable Subspaces: a subspace of $\Lambda^\ell V$ is decomposable exactly when it is close, of type I or type II.* **At $\ell=2$ that reads: a subspace of $\Lambda^2V$ every element of which is decomposable is either a PENCIL $f\wedge U$, of any dimension, or $\Lambda^2U$ for a THREE-dimensional $U$, and nothing else.** *At subspace dimension two the two types COINCIDE and the alternative is a single one*: a plane $W\subseteq\Lambda^2U$ with $\dim U=3$ corresponds under $\star$ to a plane of $U$, whose orthogonal complement is a line lying in every support, so $W=f\wedge U$ ([r853](../reviews/REVIEW_r853.md), verified here). **The dichotomy is genuine from dimension three**, where $\Lambda^2U$'s three supports pairwise meet in lines with no line common to all, and the paper says as much in its own words, that for two-dimensional subspaces the two notions are identical. *That is the classical $\alpha$/$\beta$ dichotomy for linear subspaces of the Grassmann cone, here with a proof; the paper records the low-dimensional cases as classical, citing Marcus, and the general-$\ell$ theorem as new.* **The [2026-09-02 sweep](../reviews/2026-09-02-LIT-step8-sweep.md) searched the decomposable TEST $v\wedge v=0$ and found nothing; searching the CLASSIFICATION found this in one query.**

> **AND THE SCOPE LIMIT IS THE WHOLE OF WHAT STILL SEPARATES IT FROM I36b.** *Theorem 6 classifies subspaces every element of which is decomposable. An eigenspace of a $W$ passing the $\Lambda^4$ test needs only a decomposable BASIS, which is strictly weaker.* **[r849](../reviews/REVIEW_r849.md)'s obstruction is exactly a subspace of the second kind that is not of the first**: $\mathrm{span}\{e_1\wedge e_2,e_3\wedge e_4\}$ has the decomposable basis $\{e_1\wedge e_2,e_3\wedge e_4\}$ while $\cos t\,e_1\wedge e_2+\sin t\,e_3\wedge e_4$ has $\mathrm{Pf}=\tfrac12\sin2t\ne0$ off the axes. *Two things it does not say, both refuted at Gate 2.* **RESELECTION IS FREE IN BOTH TYPES, not constrained inside $\Lambda^2U$**: the Hodge star inside $U$ sends any orthonormal basis of $\Lambda^2U$ to $\{h_2\wedge h_3,h_3\wedge h_1,h_1\wedge h_2\}$ for an orthonormal frame of $U$, so **every all-decomposable eigenspace is frame-compatible on its own and the whole difficulty is CROSS-eigenspace compatibility** ([r851](../reviews/REVIEW_r851.md), [r852](../reviews/REVIEW_r852.md), independently). *The only asymmetry is frame consumption, a line against a three-space.* **AND SUB-STEP (a) OWES NO CLASSIFICATION**: the class of subspaces carrying a decomposable basis contains $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^n$ itself, with the coordinate basis, so it constrains nothing, and the chain is three strict conditions, all-decomposable, then an orthonormal decomposable basis, then a decomposable basis, separated at the middle by $\mathrm{span}\{e_1\wedge e_2,\,e_3\wedge e_4,\,(e_1+e_3)\wedge(e_2+e_4)\}$ at $\mathfrak b(P_E)=-\tfrac12$, and what coordinate-diagonality actually needs is the third thing again, a FRAME-COMPATIBLE basis. *At $n=4$ and $r=2$ the decomposable-basis planes are a full-dimensional open family in $\mathrm{Gr}(2,6)$ while the all-decomposable ones are a five-dimensional sliver, so the residue is not a classification.*

> **ONE OBJECT CARRIES THE WHOLE OF IT, AND IT IS $\Theta(E)$ RATHER THAN THE STRUCTURE THEOREM.** *Set $\Theta(E)=\sum_i\omega_i\omega_i^{\mathsf T}$ over an orthonormal basis of a subspace $E\subseteq\Lambda^2\mathbb R^n$.* **It is basis-independent, additive over orthogonal sums, and of trace $2\dim E$**, since $\sum_i\omega_i\otimes\omega_i=\Pi_E$ is basis-free and $\omega\omega^{\mathsf T}$ is linear in $\omega\otimes\omega$. *So [L180](#lemma-l180-ricci-flatness-is-a-signed-psd-identity-and-it-names-the-support)(1) reads* $$\sum_\mu\lambda_\mu\,\Theta(E_\mu)=0,$$ *on ANY orthonormal eigen-decomposition, with the kernel's terms $0\cdot\Theta$ and no decomposability hypothesis anywhere.* **Three earlier claims of this section are its values**: $\Theta(f\wedge U')=r\,ff^{\mathsf T}+\Pi_{U'}$ at unit $f$ and orthonormal $u_i$, $\Theta(\Lambda^2U)=(\dim U-1)\Pi_U$, and $\Theta(\Lambda^2\mathbb R^n)=(n-1)\mathrm{Id}$ from $\varphi_{ab}\varphi_{ab}^{\mathsf T}=E_{aa}+E_{bb}$ with each index in $n-1$ pairs. *Verified: trace $4$ at $\dim E=2$, and $\Theta(\Lambda^2\mathbb R^4)=3\,\mathrm{Id}$ exactly* ([r854](../reviews/REVIEW_r854.md), [r855](../reviews/REVIEW_r855.md)).

> **THE ALL-DECOMPOSABLE HYPOTHESIS IS WITHDRAWN, AND IT WAS WORSE THAN NARROW: IT EXCLUDED REAL OBJECTS.** *The $\Lambda^4$ test is necessary only for the MIDDLE of the three nested conditions, so upgrading to all-decomposable is two strict steps and the Structure Theorem supplies packaging only.* **The witness is a coordinate-diagonal critical point**: at $n=4$, $\lambda_{12}=\lambda_{34}=2$ with the other four $-1$ is Ricci-flat, Bianchi and traceless with spectrum $\{2,2,-1,-1,-1,-1\}$, and its $\lambda=2$ eigenspace $\mathrm{span}\{\varphi_{12},\varphi_{34}\}$ has an orthonormal decomposable basis while a mixed element has self-wedge $1$ ([r854](../reviews/REVIEW_r854.md), reproduced here). *And the pencil sum was FALSE without $\lVert f\rVert=1$: the rescaling $(f,u_i)\mapsto(tf,u_i/t)$ fixes every wedge while $r\,ff^{\mathsf T}$ moves by $t^2$, giving $\mathrm{diag}(12,1,1,1,0)$ against a true $\mathrm{diag}(3,1,1,1,0)$ at $n=5$* ([r855](../reviews/REVIEW_r855.md)).

> **AND ON THE ALL-DECOMPOSABLE LOCUS THERE IS NOTHING TO SAY, WHICH IS WHY THE HYPOTHESIS GOES.** *At $n=4$ a Weyl tensor commutes with $\star$, so an all-decomposable eigenspace containing $\omega$ contains the self-dual $\omega+\star\omega$, whose self-wedge is nonzero*, verified at $\varphi_{01}+\varphi_{23}$. **And no nonzero coordinate-diagonal Ricci-flat $W$ at $n=4$ has all-decomposable eigenspaces**, the diagonal Ricci system being $K_4$'s unsigned incidence matrix of rank $4$, so every solution pairs opposite planes ([r854](../reviews/REVIEW_r854.md)). *With I36 proved at $n=4$ the locus is $\{0\}$ there.* **At every $n$ the kernel's own all-decomposability caps it at $n-1$, forcing $\Lambda^2$-rank $\ge\binom{n-1}2$, so the locus excludes padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ everywhere** ([r855](../reviews/REVIEW_r855.md)). *So "a reduction of the remaining half" is withdrawn outright: it was one-directional, it left the frame existential untouched, and its locus omits what the conjecture turns on.*

> **WHAT STANDS, AND WHAT IS OWED.** *Standing: $\Theta$ and its three values, the equation $\sum_\mu\lambda_\mu\Theta(E_\mu)=0$ at any orthonormal eigen-decomposition, and the trace identity $\sum_\mu\dim E_\mu=\binom n2$ with the kernel counted.* **The two counts as first written are withdrawn**, having been read off the total over all of $\Lambda^2$ while the equation omits the kernel, the correct restriction being $\binom n2-\dim\ker W$. **Owed, and named by [r854](../reviews/REVIEW_r854.md) without being opened**: whether the all-decomposable locus has a coordinate-diagonal member at $n=5,6,7$, which on the diagonal is a partition of $E(K_n)$ into stars and triangles carrying a nonzero vertex-sum-zero weighting. *Empty would make this sub-locus an emptiness theorem; nonempty would give the equation its first nonzero instance. Every hand case that reviewer tried collapsed to zero.*

**AN INTEGRALITY CONDITION CARRIES THE FRAME EXISTENTIAL, 2026-09-10.** *Working notes, Gate 2 pending.* **The configuration of an operator with an orthonormal decomposable eigenbasis is its set of planes $P_\alpha$, and the whole of coordinate-diagonality is one condition on the PAIRS of them**: every two planes either share a line or are mutually orthogonal. *That condition is an equality case, so the existential over frames becomes the extremal case of a scalar inequality.* **Nothing below uses Weyl, Ricci-flatness or criticality**: it is linear algebra about orthonormal bases of $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^n$ by decomposable forms. *The inner product on the $\Pi_\alpha$ is the plain $\langle A,B\rangle=\operatorname{tr}(AB)$ with no factor $\tfrac12$, which every constant below requires and which the block did not declare* ([r857](../reviews/REVIEW_r857.md)). **Typed in [L201Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L201Statement.lean): ONE identity, the per-index one, and the scalar equality case, all three gap-free, with the theorem's proof the file's one visible gap.** *$\Xi$, its bound and the $[0,1]$ range are absent from the file, the range entering the scalar lemmas as a hypothesis* ([r856](../reviews/REVIEW_r856.md), [r857](../reviews/REVIEW_r857.md), and the account this sentence replaces claimed two identities and the bound).

> **THE TWO EXACT IDENTITIES, BOTH UNCONDITIONAL.** *Write $\Pi_\alpha=\omega_\alpha\omega_\alpha^{\mathsf T}$, the orthogonal projection onto $P_\alpha$, so that $\Theta$'s value $\sum_\alpha\Pi_\alpha=(n-1)\mathrm{Id}$ is the completeness relation.* **Then $\sum_{\beta\ne\alpha}\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_\alpha\Pi_\beta)=2(n-2)$ at every $\alpha$**, since $\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_\alpha((n-1)\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_\alpha))=2(n-1)-2$. *And $\Lambda^2$-orthogonality of two decomposable unit forms says exactly that one principal angle between their planes is a right angle, because $\langle u\wedge v,u'\wedge v'\rangle=\cos\vartheta_1\cos\vartheta_2$.* **So $\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_\alpha\Pi_\beta)=\cos^2\vartheta$ of the remaining angle, lying in $[0,1]$, equal to $1$ exactly when the planes share a line and to $0$ exactly when they are mutually orthogonal**, the second because $\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_\alpha\Pi_\beta)=\lVert\Pi_\alpha\Pi_\beta\rVert_F^2$.

> **THE BRIDGE, AND IT MAKES THIS THE RECORD'S OWN $T$-CALCULUS RATHER THAN A NEW LANGUAGE.** **GATE 2 PASSED** ([r997](../reviews/REVIEW_r997.md), [r998](../reviews/REVIEW_r998.md), identical briefs, *agreeing on ten of eleven rows independently*), **and STRENGTHENED to a basis-free identity**: $\lVert[\omega_\alpha,\omega_\beta]\rVert^2=\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_\alpha\Pi_\beta)-2\langle\omega_\alpha,\omega_\beta\rangle^2$ at EVERY pair of decomposable unit forms, of which the statement below is the $\Lambda^2$-orthogonal case. *Principal position comes from an SVD of the $2\times2$ inner-product matrix, so it exists at every pair rather than being available by fortune, and $\Lambda^2$-orthogonality is EQUIVALENT to $\cos\vartheta_1\cos\vartheta_2=0$.* **Equal planes cannot occur under the hypothesis**, so that case is vacuous rather than unhandled. **For $\Lambda^2$-orthogonal decomposable unit forms, $\lVert[\omega_\alpha,\omega_\beta]\rVert^2=\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_\alpha\Pi_\beta)$.** *Proof in four lines from $[a\wedge b,c\wedge d]=\langle a,d\rangle\,c\wedge b-\langle b,d\rangle\,c\wedge a-\langle a,c\rangle\,d\wedge b+\langle b,c\rangle\,d\wedge a$, which follows from $(a\wedge b)z=\langle a,z\rangle b-\langle b,z\rangle a$: put the two planes in principal position, $P=\mathrm{span}\{u,v\}$ and $u'=\cos\vartheta_1u+\sin\vartheta_1x$, $v'=\cos\vartheta_2v+\sin\vartheta_2y$ with $x,y\perp P$ and $x\perp y$, where orthogonality is $\cos\vartheta_1\cos\vartheta_2=0$ and kills $\vartheta_2$. Then three of the four coefficients vanish and $[\omega,\omega']=\cos\vartheta_1\,(v\wedge y)$, of norm $\lvert\cos\vartheta_1\rvert$, against $\operatorname{tr}(\Pi\Pi')=\cos^2\vartheta_1$.* **So $\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_\alpha\Pi_\beta)$ is the record's $S_{\alpha\beta}=\sum_\gamma T_{\alpha\beta\gamma}$** ([bounds.md](bounds.md)), and three things follow at once. *The per-index identity is [Lemma L153](bounds.md)'s row marginal $\sum_\beta\lVert[v,v_\beta]\rVert^2=2(n-2)\lVert v\rVert^2$ composed with the bridge, so it is corroboration of a recorded lemma by a second route and not a new identity.* **[Lemma L31](stratum-two-negative.md)'s pair cap SHARPENS from $2$ to $1$ on the decomposable locus, with equality exactly when the two planes share a line**, *and it needs only ONE argument decomposable, since $\lVert\operatorname{ad}(\omega)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=1$ at every decomposable unit $\omega$ with no orthogonality at all* ([r997](../reviews/REVIEW_r997.md), [r998](../reviews/REVIEW_r998.md)). **Equality at $2$ is what requires both arguments of rank $4$, which is why the sharpening is forced**, which the recorded equality case implies without stating: it needs both arguments of rank $4$, and a decomposable form has rank $2$. *And the bracket is itself a decomposable form, $\cos\vartheta_1(v\wedge y)$, so integrality says every bracket of two basis elements is either zero or a UNIT bivector.*

> **WHICH GIVES THE THEOREM ITS LIE-ALGEBRAIC FORM, THE ONE A READER CAN HOLD.** *An orthonormal basis of $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ by rank-two elements, whose pairwise brackets are each either zero or of unit norm, is the standard basis $\{\varphi_{ab}\}$ of some orthonormal frame, up to signs.* **The two hypotheses are the two ways a pair can commute**: the bracket vanishing is mutual orthogonality of the planes, and the unit bracket is a shared line. *The standard basis satisfies it because $[\varphi_{ab},\varphi_{bc}]=\pm\varphi_{ac}$ lands in the basis, so the conclusion is attained and the statement is not vacuous.*

> **SO THE COORDINATE CONFIGURATION IS THE EQUALITY CASE OF ONE SCALAR QUARTIC.** *Summing the per-index identity gives $\sum_{\alpha\ne\beta}\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_\alpha\Pi_\beta)=n(n-1)(n-2)$, fixed across every configuration, which is also $\operatorname{tr}((\sum\Pi)^2)-\sum\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_\alpha^2)=n(n-1)^2-n(n-1)$.* **Every term lies in $[0,1]$, so $t^2\le t$ termwise, and the commutator identity below proves the same bound as positivity of a sum of squares:** $$\Xi(\mathcal P):=\Big\lVert\sum_\alpha\Pi_\alpha\otimes\Pi_\alpha\Big\rVert^2=\sum_{\alpha,\beta}\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_\alpha\Pi_\beta)^2\ \le\ n(n-1)(n-2)+2n(n-1)=n^2(n-1),$$ *with equality exactly at integrality.* **The clause below that equality holds at no other configuration IS the theorem, so the bound is not independent support for it** ([r856](../reviews/REVIEW_r856.md), [r857](../reviews/REVIEW_r857.md)). **RE-DERIVED UNWITTINGLY 2026-09-12, WHICH MAKES THIS BLOCK ONE TO READ BEFORE WORKING [I36](../plan.md).** *A cycle looking for a method for the frame link reconstructed the forced first moment, the termwise $t^2\le t$ step and the equality case, and proposed "kill $\sum_{\alpha\ne\beta}S_{\alpha\beta}(1-S_{\alpha\beta})$" as a handle.* **That quantity is this bound's slack, so killing it is the theorem restated and not a route to it**, which is what the sentence above already says. *The re-derivation agrees on every constant and adds nothing, and it is recorded here because a statement this easy to reach independently is one the next cycle will reach again.* **$\Xi$ is defined on orthonormal decomposable bases of $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^n$ and on nothing wider**, it takes the value $n^2(n-1)$ at every coordinate configuration, and by the theorem below it takes it at no other. *So "diagonal in SOME frame", an existential over $O(n)$, is the equality case of a single scalar bound.*

> **THE PAIRWISE TRACES ARE A GRAM MATRIX, WHICH CONFIRMS THE CONSTANT BY A SECOND ROUTE.** **GATE 2 PASSED** ([r997](../reviews/REVIEW_r997.md), [r998](../reviews/REVIEW_r998.md), *both recomputing the Johnson spectrum two ways*). $S_{\alpha\beta}=\langle\Pi_\alpha,\Pi_\beta\rangle_F$ *is the Gram matrix of the projections inside $\mathrm{Sym}^2\mathbb R^n$, so it is positive semidefinite of rank $\dim\mathrm{span}\{\Pi_\alpha\}\le\tfrac{n(n+1)}2$, of trace $n(n-1)$, and completeness makes the all-ones vector an eigenvector at $2(n-1)$.* **At the coordinate configuration $S=A(J(n,2))+2\,\mathrm{Id}$ for the Johnson graph, whose eigenvalues $2(n-2),n-4,-2$ give $S$ the spectrum $2n-2,\ n-2,\ 0$ at multiplicities $1,\ n-1,\ \binom n2-n$**, so $\lVert S\rVert_F^2=4(n-1)^2+(n-1)(n-2)^2=n^2(n-1)$, *which is $\Xi$'s claimed extremal value reached without the termwise argument and with no code in common.* **And it says the extremum is a RANK statement as well as a norm one**: the coordinate configuration has $\operatorname{rank}S=n$, the projections spanning only the frame's diagonal matrices, against $\operatorname{rank}S=5>4$ at the rotated witness. **AND $\operatorname{rank}S\ge n$ AT EVERY CONFIGURATION, WITH EQUALITY EXACTLY AT THE COORDINATE ONES, IS PROVED BELOW** ([the rank block](#from-i74-ricci-flatness-is-the-kernel-of-the-projections-gram-matrix-and-its-rank-is-at-least-n)), *by splitting the all-ones eigenvalue off and applying Cauchy-Schwarz to the remainder against the $\Xi$ cap.* **The sentence that stood here called it open**, which was true when this paragraph was written and false from the moment the split was found, *and a top-down reader met the stale copy seventy lines before the proof.*

#### Lemma L201, integrality of the pairwise plane traces forces a common frame

**Gate 2 PASSED** ([r856](../reviews/REVIEW_r856.md), [r857](../reviews/REVIEW_r857.md), identical briefs), *both confirming the chain link by link and both finding the same two elisions in the four-step induction the proof no longer uses.* **Every finding was validated here before it was applied**: the $n=4$ exclusion by solving $K_4$'s zero-row-sum system, the $n=5$ non-emptiness by the $S_5$-irreducibility of the Ricci-flat weightings, and the sharp values at $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ by recomputing $P=6abc+12abc$ from the per-chirality sharp.

> **THE THEOREM: INTEGRALITY IS COMMUTATIVITY OF THE PROJECTIONS, SO A COMMON FRAME FOLLOWS IN TWO LINES.** *Let $\{\omega_\alpha\}$ be an orthonormal basis of $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^n$, $n\ge3$, by decomposable forms, and suppose $\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_\alpha\Pi_\beta)\in\{0,1\}$ at every pair. Then some orthonormal frame of $\mathbb R^n$ has the $P_\alpha$ as exactly its coordinate $2$-planes.* **The proof is the identity $[\Pi_\alpha,\Pi_\beta]=\sqrt{S_{\alpha\beta}}\,(uu'^{\mathsf T}-u'u^{\mathsf T})$ and nothing else.** *In principal position, $P_\alpha=\mathrm{span}\{u,v\}$ and $P_\beta=\mathrm{span}\{u',y\}$ with $u'=\cos\vartheta_1u+\sin\vartheta_1x$ and $x,y\perp P_\alpha$, orthogonality having killed the second angle. Then $\Pi_\alpha\Pi_\beta=\cos\vartheta_1\,uu'^{\mathsf T}$, whose trace is $\cos^2\vartheta_1=S_{\alpha\beta}$, and* $$\operatorname{tr}\big([\Pi_\alpha,\Pi_\beta]^{\mathsf T}[\Pi_\alpha,\Pi_\beta]\big)=2S_{\alpha\beta}(1-S_{\alpha\beta}).$$ *The general identity, which the round supplied, is $\operatorname{tr}([\Pi_\alpha,\Pi_\beta]^{\mathsf T}[\Pi_\alpha,\Pi_\beta])=2\operatorname{tr}(T-T^2)=\tfrac12\sum_i\sin^2(2\vartheta_i)$ at $T=\Pi_\alpha\Pi_\beta\Pi_\alpha$, which equals $2S(1-S)$ exactly on the $\Lambda^2$-orthogonal locus* ([r862](../reviews/REVIEW_r862.md)). **And the orthogonality hypothesis is LOAD-BEARING, with a closed-form object to show it**: an isoclinic pair at $45^\circ$ has $S=\cos^2 45^\circ+\cos^2 45^\circ=1$, integral, while its commutator has norm $1$, so integrality without orthogonality does not give commutativity. *The norm here is the PLAIN Frobenius trace and the factor $2$ needs it: under the record's own $\Lambda^2$ product $\langle X,Y\rangle=\tfrac12\operatorname{tr}(X^{\mathsf T}Y)$ the constant would read $1$, and the commutator is skew, so the hazard is real* ([r862](../reviews/REVIEW_r862.md), [r863](../reviews/REVIEW_r863.md); the Lean instance pins it in the plain convention). **So integrality at every DISTINCT pair says exactly that the projections COMMUTE pairwise**, whence they are simultaneously diagonalisable, whence each $\Pi_\alpha$ is a sum of two rank-one diagonal projections in one frame and $P_\alpha$ is a coordinate plane of it. *The diagonal is not integral: $S_{\alpha\alpha}=2$* ([r862](../reviews/REVIEW_r862.md)). **And "exactly its coordinate planes" needs one more line than commutativity gives**: distinct planes because $\Lambda^2P$ is a line and the forms are orthonormal, then the count $\binom n2$, *which is the only place the family being a BASIS is spent* ([r863](../reviews/REVIEW_r863.md)). *The converse is immediate: diagonal matrices commute.* **The identity is an instance of the classical two-projection theory**, where the commutator of two orthogonal projections is measured by $T(I-T)$ at $T=\Pi_\alpha\Pi_\beta\Pi_\alpha$ and vanishing means the two subspaces decompose compatibly. *What the setting adds is that $\Lambda^2$-orthogonality collapses the two operator angles to one, so the trace is a single $\cos^2$ and integrality becomes commutativity. A citation for the classical half is owed: two search passes returned the two-subspace theory as a lead and nothing citable, so no source is filed.* **The four-step induction this replaced was correct and is deleted** ([r856](../reviews/REVIEW_r856.md) and [r857](../reviews/REVIEW_r857.md) confirmed it step by step and repaired two elisions in it), *since a proof a reader can hold in one line supersedes one that needs four steps, a base case and a pressure point.* **The commutator form also PROVES the bound**: summing it gives $$\sum_{\alpha\ne\beta}\lVert[\Pi_\alpha,\Pi_\beta]\rVert_F^2=2\big(n^2(n-1)-\Xi\big)\ \ge\ 0,$$ *so $\Xi\le n^2(n-1)$ is positivity of a sum of squares and its equality case is the theorem's hypothesis, which is why the two were never independent.* **Gate 2 covers the STATEMENT and this proof is new**, so it is owed a round of its own. *The factor $2$ is machine-checked at an instance whose angle is neither zero nor right, $\operatorname{tr}(\Pi\Pi')=9/25$ against $\lVert[\Pi,\Pi']\rVert_F^2=288/625$, in [L201Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L201Statement.lean) and carrying no gap axiom.*

> **WHAT IT BUYS I36, AND EXACTLY HOW MUCH OF THE EXISTENTIAL IS LEFT.** *Integrality is a condition on the CHOSEN decomposable orthonormal eigenbasis rather than on $W$, and the record's rotated witness is the proof: after its collapse $\mu_1=\mu_2$ the operator IS coordinate-diagonal while the rotated basis of its $\mu_1$-eigenspace is not integral.* **So $\Xi$ is a function of the configuration, and coordinate-diagonality of $W$ is $\sup\Xi=n^2(n-1)$ over the reselections admissible inside the eigenspaces**, a supremum over $\prod_\mu O(\dim E_\mu)$ rather than an existential over $O(n)$, with a scalar equality as the target. *Where the spectrum is simple the eigenbasis is unique up to signs, $\Xi$ is a function of $W$ alone, and coordinate-diagonality is exactly the single equation $\Xi(W)=n^2(n-1)$.* **A scalar characterisation was already on the record and this one differs in what it still quantifies over**: [L94](bispectral.md)'s equality case makes coordinate-diagonality the extremal condition $t=\lVert K\rVert_F^2=2$, whose equality case is a supremum over frames at every spectrum, while $\Xi$ carries no existential at all once the spectrum is simple. **THE SIMPLE-SPECTRUM BRANCH IS EMPTY AT $n=4$ AND LIVE FROM $n=5$, WHICH IS THE ROUND'S ONE REFUTATION.** *A coordinate-diagonal Ricci-flat $W$ at $n=4$ has $\lambda_{12}=\lambda_{34}$, $\lambda_{13}=\lambda_{24}$ and $\lambda_{14}=\lambda_{23}$, so its spectrum is $a,a,b,b,c,c$ and is never simple: simplicity and coordinate-diagonality EXCLUDE each other there, and proving [I73](../plan.md) at $n=4$ would refute I36's conclusion rather than deliver it* ([r856](../reviews/REVIEW_r856.md), [r857](../reviews/REVIEW_r857.md), independently, and reproduced here by solving $K_4$'s zero-row-sum system). **At $n=5$ the branch is non-empty**, the Ricci-flat weightings forming the $5$-dimensional irreducible of $S_5$, which lies inside no collision hyperplane $\lambda_e=\lambda_f$ because equivariance would then put it inside all of them and hence inside the constants, so the generic weighting has ten distinct values. *So the payoff clause reads at $n\ge5$, which is where the conjecture lives.* *The remaining obligation is to establish integrality, or the equality $\sup\Xi=n^2(n-1)$, at a critical point above $\sqrt{3/2}$ carrying an orthonormal decomposable eigenbasis.*

> **AND THE TWO RECORDED PER-INDEX RELATIONS TAKE A CLOSED FORM HERE, WHICH IS WHERE $\theta$ ENTERS.** *Gate 2 pending.* **Ricci-flatness contracted against $\Pi_\alpha$ IS relation (B)**: $\sum_\beta\lambda_\beta\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_\alpha\Pi_\beta)=0$ with $S_{\alpha\alpha}=2$ gives $$\sum_{\beta\ne\alpha}\lambda_\beta S_{\alpha\beta}=-2\lambda_\alpha,$$ *so the $\Theta$ equation and the record's relation (B) are one statement read at one row.* **And writing $[\omega_\alpha,\omega_\beta]=\pm\sqrt{S_{\alpha\beta}}\,\eta_{\alpha\beta}$ at a unit decomposable $\eta_{\alpha\beta}$ gives $T_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=S_{\alpha\beta}\langle\eta_{\alpha\beta},\omega_\gamma\rangle^2$, so [relation (C)](target.md) becomes** $$\sum_{\beta\ne\alpha}\lambda_\beta S_{\alpha\beta}\,r_{\alpha\beta}=2\lambda_\alpha(\theta-\lambda_\alpha),\qquad r_{\alpha\beta}=\langle W\eta_{\alpha\beta},\eta_{\alpha\beta}\rangle.$$ *So (C) is (B) with each term weighted by $W$'s Rayleigh quotient at the bracket's direction, and on the coordinate configuration $\eta_{ab,bc}=\pm\varphi_{ac}$ makes every $r$ an eigenvalue, which is the diagonal-locus form.* **This is where $\theta$ can enter and the marginals cannot**, [bounds.md](bounds.md)'s post holding that every $\lambda$-weighted contraction of $S$ is a $\{p,q,0\}$ pattern while $\theta$'s dependence is the $\{1,1,1\}$ pattern. *So the integrality obligation is to be attacked through $r$, and the question it turns on is when $\eta_{\alpha\beta}$ is itself a basis element.*

> **AND THE OFF-DIAGONAL CRITICALITY FAMILY IS THE RIGHT INPUT FOR INTEGRALITY, WHICH IS NOT WHAT IT WAS SCREENED AGAINST.** *Gate 2 pending.* **[bounds.md](bounds.md) records the $3\binom N2$ off-diagonal relations of $Q(W)=\theta W$ as carrying no spectral content, every right side being zero, so they constrain the signs of $c$ and are not a lever on the $\theta$ statement** ([r841](../reviews/REVIEW_r841.md)). *The distinction that reopens them: integrality is a condition on the marginals $S_{\alpha\beta}=\sum_\gamma c_{\alpha\beta\gamma}^2$ and on which $\eta_{\alpha\beta}$ are basis elements, so it is a GEOMETRIC condition on the $c$'s rather than a bound on $\theta$, and sign data is exactly the kind of input it consumes.* **So the screen that removed them from [I1](../plan.md) does not remove them from the integrality obligation**, and the obligation now has two named inputs: the off-diagonal relations as sign data, and relation (C)'s Rayleigh form above as the one place $\theta$ enters.

> **AND WHAT I36 DELIVERS AT $n=4$ IS EXACTLY NOTHING, WHICH IS THE RIGHT ANSWER THERE.** *On the coordinate-diagonal locus at $n=4$ the forced pairing leaves three free weights with $a+b+c=0$, and then $\lVert W\rVert^2=2q$ at $q=a^2+b^2+c^2$, $\operatorname{tr}(W^3)=6abc$, $\langle W,W^\#\rangle=12abc$ through the per-chirality sharp, so* $$\theta=\frac{18abc}{(2q)^{3/2}},\qquad (abc)^2\le\frac{q^3}{54},$$ **capping $\theta$ at $\theta_0(4)=\sqrt3/2$ with equality at $(-t,-t,2t)$, strictly below $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$'s $\sqrt{3/2}$** ([r857](../reviews/REVIEW_r857.md), reproduced here at the equality case). *So the maximiser at $n=4$ is NOT coordinate-diagonal, I36's hypothesis $\theta>\sqrt{3/2}$ is vacuous there, and the item's content is entirely about $n\ge5$.* **That is also the sharpest available statement of why the conjecture needs a dimension threshold.**

> **AND THE ROUTE'S FIRST LINK IS UNNAMED AND FAILS AT A REAL LOCAL MAXIMUM.** *Everything in this section, the $\Lambda^4$ test included, is conditional on the maximiser having an orthonormal decomposable eigenbasis, equivalently on $\mathfrak b(P_\mu)=0$ at every eigenvalue, equivalently on $\mathrm{Alt}(W^k)=0$ for every $k$.* **That does not follow from criticality**, and $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ at $n=4$ is the instance: it is self-dual, so $\mathrm{Alt}(W^2)=\tfrac16\big(\operatorname{tr}(W^2\Pi_+)-\operatorname{tr}(W^2\Pi_-)\big)\mathrm{vol}=\tfrac16\big(\lVert W^+\rVert^2-\lVert W^-\rVert^2\big)\mathrm{vol}$, whose trace difference is $\lVert W\rVert^2\ne0$ ([r857](../reviews/REVIEW_r857.md), the factor $\tfrac16$ being what the sentence dropped). *Its blocks are $\mathcal A=c\,\mathrm{diag}(2,-1,-1)$ and $\mathcal B=0$, giving $\theta=18c^3/(6c^2)^{3/2}=\sqrt{3/2}$ exactly and $P=\operatorname{tr}(W^3)+\langle W,W^\#\rangle=6c^3+12c^3$ through [F2c](conventions.md)'s per-chirality $W^\#=2\mathcal A^2-\operatorname{tr}(\mathcal A^2)\mathrm{Id}_3$.* **So the link must consume the strict threshold, which is [R6](../routes.md)'s own clearance criterion**, and the separator the record has for it is [L176](#i36-every-local-maximiser-above-sqrt32-is-coordinate-diagonal)'s $\Lambda^2$-rank rather than the value. *A coordinate-diagonal Ricci-flat $W$ at $n=4$ has $\mathcal A=\mathcal B$, its opposite-plane weights being forced equal, so it satisfies the test and the contrast is sharp.*

> **WHAT THE ROUND GAVE BACK, AND IT SHARPENS THE ITEM RATHER THAN THE SEARCH.** *$\mathfrak b(P_E)$ is the trace of $\mathrm{Pf}$ restricted to $E$, so the $\Lambda^4$ test is NECESSARY for an eigenspace to carry an orthonormal decomposable basis, and [I36b](../plan.md)'s open half is exactly its converse read per eigenspace* ([r852](../reviews/REVIEW_r852.md)). **An all-decomposable subspace of $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^n$ has dimension at most $n-1$**, so the pencil is capped rather than of any dimension. *And the proofs are field-free, so the dichotomy holds over $\mathbb R$, which the block did not say.* **The provenance is corrected too**: [Remark 8](../references/decomposable-subspaces-grassmann-codes/notes.md) credits the PRELIMINARY lemmas, half of them to Bourbaki, and says nothing about a low-dimensional case of Theorem 6, so the earlier "classical, citing Marcus" is withdrawn.

> **AND THE WITNESS SETTLES A RECORDED INSTRUMENT AS SUFFICIENT RATHER THAN CHARACTERISING.** *Every eigenspace of it is one-dimensional and decomposable, the Plücker condition in full, and no frame exists*, so the Plücker instrument is strictly sufficient, which belongs in [diagonality.md](diagonality.md)'s instrument table ([r846](../reviews/REVIEW_r846.md)).

#### Lemma L205, the $\kappa=1$ stratum has exactly one element and it is a product of two-spheres

> **THE $\kappa=1$ CASE IS CLOSED AFFIRMATIVELY AT EVERY EVEN $n$, IN THREE LINES, FOR AN $A$ CARRYING A DECOMPOSABLE ORTHONORMAL BASIS.** *Gate 2 by [r880](../reviews/REVIEW_r880.md), which upheld every step and named that hypothesis as used twice and stated nowhere.* **It is load-bearing rather than cosmetic**: a decomposable orthonormal eigenbasis makes first Bianchi automatic, since $\mathfrak b(W)\propto\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha\,\omega_\alpha\wedge\omega_\alpha=0$ term by term, *so isotropy alone does not encode Bianchi and an object satisfying isotropy without it need not be a Weyl tensor at all.* **When the positive eigenspace has dimension $a=n/2$, isotropy reads $\sum_{\alpha\in A}\Pi_\alpha=\mathrm{Id}_n$, and multiplying by $\Pi_\alpha$ gives $\sum_{\beta\ne\alpha}\Pi_\alpha\Pi_\beta=0$**, whose trace is $\sum_{\beta\ne\alpha}\lVert\Pi_\alpha\Pi_\beta\rVert_F^2=0$ with every term nonnegative, *so every product vanishes and the $n/2$ planes are mutually orthogonal.* **They sum to $\mathbb R^n$, so an orthonormal basis chosen in each gives a frame with $\omega_\alpha=f_{2\alpha-1}\wedge f_{2\alpha}$, and at two eigenvalues $W=\lambda_-\mathrm{Id}+(\lambda_+-\lambda_-)\Pi_A$ is determined by $\Pi_A$ alone, hence coordinate-diagonal in that frame.** *No dimension count, no divisibility and no rank argument enters, which is why it holds at every even $n$ rather than at the ones the counts reach.* **AND IT IS A CLASSIFICATION WITH A ONE-ELEMENT ANSWER, NOT AN EMPTY THEOREM**: the unique solution up to $O(n)$ is the perfect-matching tensor, $\lambda_+=n-2$ on $n/2$ disjoint planes and $-1$ elsewhere ([r880](../reviews/REVIEW_r880.md)). *Ricci-flatness reproduced here in one line from $\Theta(\Lambda^2\mathbb R^n)=(n-1)\mathrm{Id}$: $(n-2)\mathrm{Id}-((n-1)\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Id})=0$.* **So the $\kappa=1$ stratum contains exactly one object and it is a product of two-spheres**, which is a sphere product and carries a small $\theta$, so the stratum is settled rather than merely bounded.

> **THE SIMPLEX BOUND AND THIS RECORD'S PER-INDEX RELATION HAVE THE SAME RIGHT-HAND SIDE, AND THE RELATION IS THE STRONGER OF THE TWO.** *Derived 2026-09-12 as [I80](../plan.md)'s first sub-step, on route [R21](../routes.md). **Gate 2 PASSED**, [r889](../reviews/REVIEW_r889.md) and [r890](../reviews/REVIEW_r890.md) on identical briefs, agreeing on every claim and each refuting three of the parent's.* **[Casazza and Fickus](../references/tight-fusion-frames-packing/notes.md)'s Theorem 5.1 gives, for $M$ subspaces of dimension $c$ in $\mathbb F^d$,** $\max_{j\ne j'}\operatorname{tr}(P_jP_{j'})\ge\tfrac{c(Mc-d)}{d(M-1)}$, *with equality exactly at an equi-chordal tight fusion frame.* **At $c=2$, $d=n$ that is $\max_{\alpha\ne\beta}S_{\alpha\beta}\ge\tfrac{2(2M-n)}{n(M-1)}$, and the identity $\sum_{\beta\ne\alpha}S_{\alpha\beta}=2(\kappa-1)$ with $\kappa=2M/n$ has exactly that as its mean**, the symbolic difference being zero.

> **THEY ARE NOT ONE STATEMENT, AND THE DIFFERENCE RUNS IN THE RECORD'S FAVOUR.** *The simplex bound holds for every family; the per-index relation needs $\sum_\alpha\Pi_\alpha=\kappa\,\mathrm{Id}$.* **On the isotropic class the relation is strictly stronger, bounding the mean at EVERY index rather than the global maximum once** ([r889](../reviews/REVIEW_r889.md)). *So the literature supplies no constraint here that the record did not already have, and the parent's claim that the two are one statement is refuted as stated.*

> **AND EQUALITY IS NOT ISOTROPY: IT IS ISOTROPY PLUS EQUAL OVERLAPS, AND ISOTROPY IS FREE.** *Equality needs the equi-chordal condition, and the coordinate family is isotropic at every $n\ge4$ while $\max S=1>\tfrac4{n+1}$, so it satisfies isotropy and misses equality.* **Worse for the identification: at $M=\binom n2$ isotropy is a THEOREM rather than a hypothesis**, since $\sum_\alpha A_\alpha^2$ is the same operator for every orthonormal basis of $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ and so equals its coordinate value $-(n-1)\mathrm{Id}_n$, forcing $\kappa=n-1$ ([r890](../reviews/REVIEW_r890.md)). *Identifying the packing criterion with isotropy therefore identified a real condition with an empty one.*

> **THE BOUND IS ATTAINED AT $n=4$ BY A $\Lambda^2$-ORTHONORMAL DECOMPOSABLE BASIS, SO THE GAP TO $1$ IS NOT SLACK.** *Both reviewers built it independently and in closed form: six unit vectors of the icosahedral Seidel matrix give $G_\pm=I\pm\tfrac1{\sqrt5}S_{\mathrm{Sei}}$, $u$ in $\Lambda^+\cong\mathbb R^3$ and $v$ in $\Lambda^-$ with Gram $2I-G_u$, and $\omega=(u+v)/\sqrt2$.* **The result is an orthonormal decomposable basis of $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^4$ with $\sum_\alpha\Pi_\alpha=3\,\mathrm{Id}_4$ and EVERY $S_{\alpha\beta}=\tfrac45=\tfrac4{n+1}$.** *So $\tfrac45$ is the true optimum of $\max S$ at $n=4$, the coordinate basis is not optimal there, and reading the distance from $\tfrac4{n+1}$ to $1$ as slack in the bound is wrong.*

> **NO MULTIPLICITY CAP IS OBTAINABLE, AND THAT IS NOW SETTLED RATHER THAN CASE-CHECKED.** *The right-hand side $R(M)=\tfrac{2(2M-n)}{n(M-1)}$ is INCREASING in $M$, derivative numerator $n-2$, with limit $\tfrac4n$.* **So $R<1$ at every $n\ge4$ and every finite $M$, and no pairing of the bound with $S_{\alpha\beta}\le1$ can ever cap $M$** ([r889](../reviews/REVIEW_r889.md), [r890](../reviews/REVIEW_r890.md)). *Pairing them gives only $M(4-n)\le n$, vacuous for $n\ge4$; the parent's threshold of $n>4$ was one step short, since the bound at $n=4$ is $\tfrac{M-2}{M-1}<1$ too.* **[R21](../routes.md)'s stated payoff, a cap on eigenvalue multiplicities, does not follow from this machinery at all.**

> **AND THE EQUI-ISOCLINIC OPTIMA ARE STRUCTURALLY UNREACHABLE ON THE DECOMPOSABLE LOCUS.** *$\Lambda^2$-orthonormality of unit decomposables forces $\cos\theta_1\cos\theta_2=0$ on every pair, so an equi-isoclinic $\Lambda^2$-orthonormal family has $\sigma^2=0$ and hence $M=n/2$* ([r890](../reviews/REVIEW_r890.md)). **So the six icosahedral complex lines at $n=6$ HAD to fail $\Lambda^2$-orthonormality**, which the $n=6$ work above discovered by construction, *and the reachable class is equi-chordal but not equi-isoclinic, of which the $n=4$ family above is an instance.* **A successor must say what such a family buys, since $\Lambda^2$-orthonormality already forbids the isoclinic optimum.**

> **A COROLLARY WORTH KEEPING, AND A SCOPE CORRECTION TO THE IDENTITY ITSELF.** *The mean overlap is at most $\tfrac4{n+1}$ always, with equality exactly at a full basis* ([r889](../reviews/REVIEW_r889.md)). **And the per-index identity does NOT need $\Lambda^2$-orthonormality**: its derivation uses only rank-two projections summing to $\kappa\,\mathrm{Id}$, so it holds at the icosahedral family too. *What $\Lambda^2$-orthonormality buys is distinct planes, $S\le1$ and $M\le\binom n2$, and nothing in the identity.*

> **ONE SCOPE NOTE, BECAUSE IT IS WHERE THE RECORD'S OWN BRIDGE IS CONDITIONAL.** *$S_{\alpha\beta}\in[0,1]$, $S=1$ exactly at a shared line, and $\lVert[\omega_\alpha,\omega_\beta]\rVert^2=S_{\alpha\beta}$ all require $\Lambda^2$-orthonormality.
