In general $\lVert[\omega_\alpha,\omega_\beta]\rVert^2=S_{\alpha\beta}-2\langle\omega_\alpha,\omega_\beta\rangle^2$ and $S$ ranges over $[0,2]$, two planes in $\mathbb R^4$ sharing a line giving $\tfrac32$* ([r890](../reviews/REVIEW_r890.md), screened to $6\times10^{-16}$ over $1200$ pairs at $n=4$ to $7$). **The record's uses are inside that hypothesis and so are legitimate**, and $S\le1$ is a consequence of $\Lambda^2$-orthonormality rather than a general fact.

> **SO $n=6$ REDUCES TO ONE EXPLICIT FINITE QUESTION.** *The rank count gives $a=n/2$ or $a\ge n/2+2$, the complement gives the same for $15-a$, and the swap identifies $a$ with $15-a$.* **DIVISIBILITY $n\mid2a$ MAY NOT BE USED HERE, BECAUSE IT PRESUPPOSES THE CONCLUSION**: it is necessary only for a COORDINATE configuration, where each frame direction lies in a whole number of planes ([r880](../reviews/REVIEW_r880.md)). *So the window at $n=6$ is $a\in\{3,5,6,7\}$ up to swap, at $\kappa=1,\tfrac53,2,\tfrac73$, which is THREE open cases rather than one, and the count grows fast with $n$: ten open cases at $n=8$ and twenty-six at $n=12$.* **That kills the dimension-by-dimension framing more thoroughly than the $\kappa$ reading did**, since going up in $n$ adds cases rather than resolving them. **$a=3$ is the $\kappa=1$ case and is now closed**, so what remains at $n=6$ is exactly: *six $\Lambda^2$-orthonormal decomposable forms in $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^6$ with $\sum_\alpha\Pi_\alpha=2\,\mathrm{Id}_6$, and whether their planes must be the coordinate planes of a frame.* **The overlap identity is general: $\sum_{\beta\ne\alpha}t_{\alpha\beta}=2(\kappa-1)$ at every $\kappa$**, from $\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_\alpha\cdot\kappa\mathrm{Id})-\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_\alpha^2)$, *so the three-line theorem above is exactly its $\kappa=1$ instance and the two arguments are one* ([r880](../reviews/REVIEW_r880.md)). **At $\kappa=2$ it gives $2$,** *and the coordinate solutions are precisely the $2$-regular graphs on six vertices, the hexagon and the two triangles, which spend that overlap as $1+1$.* **By [Lemma L201](#lemma-l201-integrality-of-the-pairwise-plane-traces-forces-a-common-frame) a non-coordinate configuration must spend it non-integrally**, across four or more partners, *and the symmetric ansatz $t_{\alpha\beta}=\tfrac25$ has a positive semidefinite Gram matrix with spectrum $4,\tfrac85^{(5)}$.* **Settling that ansatz would NOT settle $n=6$**: it is the barycentre of a nine-dimensional polytope of overlap patterns ([r880](../reviews/REVIEW_r880.md)). **And the insufficiency of a positive semidefinite Gram is now shown by CONSTRUCTION rather than asserted**: the six icosahedral complex lines in $\mathbb C^3=\mathbb R^6$ realise the whole $t=\tfrac25$ datum with $\sum_\alpha\Pi_\alpha=2\,\mathrm{Id}_6$ and $\Theta(A)=2\,\mathrm{Id}_6$, failing only $\Lambda^2$-orthonormality, every pair being isoclinic at $\cos^2=\tfrac15$ so that $\langle\omega_\alpha,\omega_\beta\rangle=\tfrac15$, *and that failure propagates to first Bianchi through $\sum_{\alpha\ne\beta}\omega_\alpha\wedge\omega_\beta=\Omega\wedge\Omega$ at the nondegenerate $\Omega=2J$.* **The complex-line construction is excluded pair by pair, and that scope is the whole of what it says**: the argument is pair-local and carries no force until a single complex structure $J$ is fixed for the entire configuration ([r880](../reviews/REVIEW_r880.md)): two orthogonal decompositions of $\mathbb R^6$ into complex lines would sum to $2\,\mathrm{Id}$, but complex lines are isoclinic, so $\Lambda^2$-orthogonality forces their planes orthogonal and no line can be orthogonal to a spanning triple.

> **THE SPLIT CONSTRUCTION AT $n=6$ PRODUCES NOTHING NEW, AND THE REASON IS A FACT ABOUT $O(3)$.** *Working notes, Gate 2 pending, derived 2026-09-12.* **Write $\mathbb R^6=\mathbb R^3\oplus\mathbb R^3$ and build six planes as two graph families, $P_i=\mathrm{span}\{e_i,Af_i\}$ and $Q_i=\mathrm{span}\{e_i,A'f_i\}$ at $A,A'\in O(3)$.** *Each family sums to $\mathrm{Id}_6$, so the six sum to $2\,\mathrm{Id}_6$; within a family the planes are mutually orthogonal; and across families every pair is automatically $\Lambda^2$-orthogonal for $i\ne j$, since the cross Gram determinant has both terms zero.* **The one real condition is at $i=j$: $\langle\omega_{P_i},\omega_{Q_i}\rangle=(A^{\mathsf T}A')_{ii}$, so $\Lambda^2$-orthonormality demands that $B=A^{\mathsf T}A'$ have ZERO DIAGONAL**, and then the pairwise traces are $t_{P_iQ_j}=B_{ij}^2$.

> **AND EVERY $3\times3$ ORTHOGONAL MATRIX WITH ZERO DIAGONAL IS A SIGNED PERMUTATION, SO $B_{ij}^2\in\{0,1\}$ AND THE CONFIGURATION IS COORDINATE.** *Four lines: rows are $(0,a,b)$, $(c,0,d)$, $(e,f,0)$ with unit norms, and orthogonality gives $bd=af=ce=0$. If $b=0$ then $a=\pm1$, forcing $f=0$, then $e=\pm1$, forcing $c=0$ and $d=\pm1$; the branch $d=0$ runs the same way. Either way $B$ is a signed permutation.* **So the integrality [Lemma L201](#lemma-l201-integrality-of-the-pairwise-plane-traces-forces-a-common-frame) needs holds automatically here, and the six planes are the coordinate planes of the frame $\{e_i\}\cup\{Af_i\}$.** *The construction is therefore closed as a source of counterexamples, which matters because it is the first place one would look: it is the $n=6$ analogue of the two-decomposition idea that the isoclinic argument kills for complex lines.* **What it does NOT close is the general case**, where the six planes need not split across a $\mathbb R^3\oplus\mathbb R^3$ decomposition at all, and that remains the one open question at $n=6$.

> **THE SMALLEST OPEN CASE IS $n=5$ AT $\kappa=2$, AND ITS SYMMETRIC PART REDUCES TO THREE EQUATIONS.** *Working notes, Gate 2 pending, derived 2026-09-12.* **The window at $n=5$ is $a=5$ alone, so $\kappa=2$, and the pentagon realises it: five $\Lambda^2$-orthonormal decomposable forms in $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^5$ with $\sum_\alpha\Pi_\alpha=2\,\mathrm{Id}_5$.** *For a $\mathbb Z_5$-symmetric configuration, written as the orbit of one plane under a generator $g$, equivariance makes $\sum_\alpha\Pi_\alpha$ block-scalar on $\mathbb R^5=L\oplus V_1\oplus V_2$, and averaging collapses isotropy to TWO conditions, $(\Pi_P)_{00}=\tfrac25$ and $\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_P|_{V_1})=\tfrac45$, the third following from the trace.* **With the two $\Lambda^2$-orthogonality conditions $\langle\omega_0,\omega_1\rangle=\langle\omega_0,\omega_2\rangle=0$ that is four conditions on the six-dimensional $\mathrm{Gr}(2,5)$ against a two-dimensional centraliser torus, so the symmetric solutions are ISOLATED.**

> **THE TWO CYCLIC ANSATZE GIVE ONLY THE PENTAGON AND THE PENTAGRAM, AND BOTH ARE COORDINATE.** *In the complex model $\mathbb R^5=\mathbb R\oplus\mathbb C\oplus\mathbb C$ with $g=(1,\zeta,\zeta^2)$ at $\zeta=e^{2\pi i/5}$, take $P=\mathrm{span}\{p,g^mp\}$ at $p=(a,z_1,z_2)$.* **Isotropy then FORCES $a^2=\tfrac15$ and $\lvert z_1\rvert^2=\lvert z_2\rvert^2=\tfrac25$, leaving no magnitude freedom and only the two phases the torus moves**, and orthonormality $a^2+\lvert z_1\rvert^2\cos\tfrac{2\pi m}5+\lvert z_2\rvert^2\cos\tfrac{4\pi m}5=0$ then holds IDENTICALLY in $m$, *screened to machine zero at every $m$.* **So $m=1$ is the pentagon and $m=2$ the pentagram, both coordinate $5$-cycles in relabelled frames, and the ansatz can produce nothing else.**

> **WHAT IS LEFT AT $n=5$, WRITTEN OUT SO IT CAN BE SOLVED.** *Rotating inside $P$ so that $q$ carries no component on the invariant line, and using the torus to make $z_1,z_2$ real, everything reduces to one magnitude $x=\lvert z_1\rvert^2$ and two phases, with $a^2=\tfrac25$ forced and* $$\lvert z_2\rvert^2=\tfrac35-x,\quad\lvert w_1\rvert^2=\tfrac45-x,\quad\lvert w_2\rvert^2=\tfrac15+x.$$ **The remaining system is $A\cos\beta_1+B\cos\beta_2=0$ together with $\det M(\alpha)=0$ at $\alpha=1,2$**, where $A=\sqrt{x(\tfrac45-x)}$, $B=\sqrt{(\tfrac35-x)(\tfrac15+x)}$ and $M(\alpha)$ is the $2\times2$ Gram of $(p,q)$ against $(g^\alpha p,g^\alpha q)$. *The pentagon sits at $x=\tfrac{3+\sqrt5}{10}$, where every identity above was screened to machine zero, which is what says the parametrisation is right.* **THE SYMMETRIC CASE IS NOW SETTLED AND IT ADMITS ONLY THE COORDINATE CONFIGURATIONS.** *Solved 2026-09-12 with the user's approval: $652$ of $800$ random starts converge to residual $\sim10^{-20}$, and EVERY one lands on the same overlap pattern, ten zeros and ten ones with integrality defect exactly $0$, which is the $5$-cycle.* **So by [Lemma L201](#lemma-l201-integrality-of-the-pairwise-plane-traces-forces-a-common-frame) every $\mathbb Z_5$-symmetric solution is coordinate, and the pentagon and pentagram are the whole of it.** *The residual is validated at the pentagon, where it is exactly zero and the overlap matrix is the $5$-cycle's.* **What this is and is not**: a numerical exhaustion over basins rather than a proof, and silent about configurations carrying no $\mathbb Z_5$ symmetry. *The earlier reading that this solve was reserved is superseded: the footing now waits only on runs whose cost cannot be stated in advance.* **What the solve would have decided** : a third solution makes [I76](../plan.md) false at $n=5$ and ends the dimension-by-dimension route at the bottom, while only the two known solutions closes the symmetric case and moves the front to non-symmetric configurations.

> **WHAT THE GENERAL $n=6$ CASE WOULD NEED, NAMED RATHER THAN ATTEMPTED.** *The two natural families are closed, the per-plane condition does not bite, and no pointwise constraint links the three overlaps of a triple: with $P_1$ fixed, the principal directions of $P_2$ and $P_3$ into $P_1$ fix $t_{12}$ and $t_{13}$ while $t_{23}$ stays free in the four-dimensional complement.* **So the obstruction, if there is one, is global and the remaining question is a search over the configuration variety**: six $\Lambda^2$-orthonormal decomposable forms in $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^6$ with $\sum_\alpha\Pi_\alpha=2\,\mathrm{Id}_6$, asking whether any lies off the $O(6)$-orbits of the hexagon and the two triangles. *This record is on an analysis-only footing, so that search is not run here.* **What each outcome would decide**: a witness makes [I76](../plan.md) FALSE at $n=6$ and ends the dimension-by-dimension route, which would be as informative as a proof; an exhaustive absence leaves $n=6$ affirmative and the next open dimension $n=8$, where the divisibility and rank counts admit more sizes.

> **AND THE STRUCTURE THE TWO COORDINATE SOLUTIONS HAVE IS WORTH RECORDING, BECAUSE THEY DIFFER.** *The two triangles give $A=\Lambda^2U_1\oplus\Lambda^2U_2$ for an orthogonal splitting $\mathbb R^6=U_1\oplus U_2$ into three-spaces, which is a SUBALGEBRA $\mathfrak{so}(3)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(3)$ closed under brackets, and $\Theta(\Lambda^2U)=(\dim U-1)\Pi_U$ returns $2\Pi_{U_1}+2\Pi_{U_2}=2\,\mathrm{Id}_6$ exactly.* **The hexagon is not closed under brackets**, $[\varphi_{12},\varphi_{23}]=\pm\varphi_{13}$ being a chord rather than an edge, *yet it satisfies every constraint equally.* **So the constraint set does not see the Lie structure**, which is one reason a classification has not fallen out of it: any invariant that distinguished subalgebras from the rest would already have separated these two. *And $A$ is never all-decomposable at $n=6$: a pencil caps at $n-1=5$ dimensions and $\Lambda^2U$ at three, so the Structure Theorem excludes a six-dimensional all-decomposable subspace outright, which is why the weaker decomposable-BASIS condition is the right one throughout.*

#### From [I76](../plan.md): the integrality defect CANNOT certify non-coordinacy, and the refutation attempt fails on its own logic

**A non-integral decomposable eigenbasis does not make $W$ non-coordinate-diagonal, and at $n=5$ it never can.** *Gate 2 by [r881](../reviews/REVIEW_r881.md) and [r882](../reviews/REVIEW_r882.md) on identical briefs; the first constructed the frame and the attempt is withdrawn.* **The logical gap: at two eigenvalues the positive eigenvalue has multiplicity $a$, so EVERY orthonormal basis of $A$ is an eigenbasis, while $W=\lambda(\Pi_A-\Pi_{A^\perp})$ depends only on the SUBSPACE $A$.** *[Lemma L201](#lemma-l201-integrality-of-the-pairwise-plane-traces-forces-a-common-frame) constrains a configuration of planes; coordinate-diagonality is a property of the eigenspace. One non-integral eigenbasis says nothing about $W$.*

> **THE MECHANISM, AND THE RECORD ALREADY HELD IT.** *Two pentagon edges sharing a vertex span $f_a\wedge\langle f_b,f_c\rangle$, a PENCIL, which is entirely decomposable* ([r851](../reviews/REVIEW_r851.md) and [r852](../reviews/REVIEW_r852.md) recorded that reselection is free inside both types of the Structure Theorem, and a pencil is one of them). **So rotating inside that pencil keeps the basis orthonormal and decomposable, moves the pairwise traces off the integers, and leaves $A$ untouched.** *Every vertex of $C_5$ has degree two, so such a pencil exists at every vertex and the defect test can NEVER certify non-coordinacy at $n=5$* ([r881](../reviews/REVIEW_r881.md)).

> **THE EXACT WITNESS THAT SETTLES IT.** *$\omega_1=e_1\wedge e_2$, $\omega_2=e_2\wedge e_4$, $\omega_4=e_1\wedge e_3$, $\omega_3=\tfrac15(3e_3-4e_4)\wedge e_5$, $\omega_5=\tfrac15(4e_3+3e_4)\wedge e_5$: isotropy, orthonormality and every Pfaffian exact in rational arithmetic, pairwise traces $\tfrac{16}{25}$ and $\tfrac9{25}$ so the defect is $\tfrac9{25}$, and yet $\mathrm{span}A$ is the coordinate pentagon's span.* **So a non-integral basis of a coordinate span is an ordinary object rather than a curiosity**, and the numerical witness of this cycle is one: its $W$ lies in the $O(5)$ orbit of the coordinate pentagon, the five coordinate bivectors landing in $A$ to $1.07\times10^{-60}$ at seventy digits, with the frame constructed by linear algebra from the witness itself. *A quaternionic reduction gives $p^2+q^2+r^2=1$ with $2pqr=0$, two moduli matching the measured Jacobian nullity of $17-15$, and reproduces all ten traces from two of them to $10^{-46}$.*

> **WHAT THE ATTEMPT ESTABLISHED ANYWAY, SINCE THE OBJECT IS REAL.** *The configuration is a genuine element of $\mathcal W_5$, verified exactly: isotropy forces the complement's sum through $\Theta(\Lambda^2\mathbb R^5)=4\,\mathrm{Id}$ so Ricci-flatness is identical, $\mathrm{Alt}(\mathrm{Id})=0$ gives first Bianchi from the decomposability of $A$ alone, and the equal-and-opposite spectrum gives trace-freeness.* **Its $\theta$ is $0$ EXACTLY, both $\operatorname{tr}(W^3)$ and $\langle W,W^\#\rangle$ vanishing, and it IS a critical point of $P$ on the unit sphere with multiplier zero**, as the coordinate pentagon already was ([r881](../reviews/REVIEW_r881.md), [r882](../reviews/REVIEW_r882.md), independently). *And $\kappa=2$, so nothing here touches the $\kappa=1$ theorem, whose $a=n/2$ is non-integral at odd $n$.* **The complement is the complementary coordinate pentagram of the same frame**, which is why its decomposability was never evidence of anything.

> **WHAT IS ACTUALLY OPEN AT $n=5$, STATED SO IT CANNOT BE ANSWERED BY ANOTHER WITNESS.** *Whether a configuration exists with ALL ten pairwise traces strictly below $1$.* **That is the only surviving route to a non-coordinate two-eigenvalue point, and it needs a derivation about the subspace $A$ rather than a search over bases** ([r881](../reviews/REVIEW_r881.md)). *Every configuration found this cycle has some trace equal to $1$, which is exactly the pencil that permits the reselection, so a witness with no trace at $1$ would have no pencil to hide in.* **The $n=6$ configurations of this cycle inherit the same defect and are withdrawn with it**, their spans untested and their integrality defects therefore uninformative.

#### From [I74](../plan.md): Ricci-flatness is the kernel of the projections' Gram matrix, and its rank is at least $n$

**Working notes, Gate 2 pending, derived 2026-09-10 as [I74](../plan.md)'s first step.** *Write $S_{\alpha\beta}=\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_\alpha\Pi_\beta)$ for the pairwise plane traces of an orthonormal decomposable basis, which [Lemma L201](#lemma-l201-integrality-of-the-pairwise-plane-traces-forces-a-common-frame) reads as the Gram matrix of the projections inside $\mathrm{Sym}^2\mathbb R^n$.* **Then Ricci-flatness of $W=\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha\,\omega_\alpha\otimes\omega_\alpha$ is exactly $$S\lambda=0,$$** since $M=\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha\Pi_\alpha$ lies in $\mathrm{span}\{\Pi_\beta\}$ and $\langle A,B\rangle=\operatorname{tr}(AB)$ is positive definite on $\mathrm{Sym}^2\mathbb R^n$, so a vector of that span orthogonal to all of it is zero. *Both named inputs are what the reverse direction consumes and neither was stated* ([r858](../reviews/REVIEW_r858.md), [r859](../reviews/REVIEW_r859.md)). **Everything here needs $n\ge3$ and the hypothesis is that the WHOLE eigenbasis is decomposable, kernel included**, since the cap and [L201](#lemma-l201-integrality-of-the-pairwise-plane-traces-forces-a-common-frame) both need a full basis and on a partial configuration the rank bound has no proof. *So the Ricci-flat weightings of a fixed configuration are precisely $\ker S$, of dimension $\binom n2-\operatorname{rank}S$, and a NONZERO critical point requires a degenerate Gram matrix.*

> **THE RANK IS AT LEAST $n$, WITH EQUALITY EXACTLY AT THE COORDINATE CONFIGURATIONS.** *$S$ is positive semidefinite of trace $n(n-1)$ with $\operatorname{rank}S=\dim\mathrm{span}\{\Pi_\alpha\}$, and completeness makes the all-ones vector an eigenvector at $2(n-1)$, which is the top eigenvalue since the entries are nonnegative.* **Split that eigenvalue off**: the remaining eigenvalues sum to $n(n-1)-2(n-1)=(n-1)(n-2)$ and there are $r-1$ of them, so Cauchy–Schwarz gives $\sum_{i\ge2}\sigma_i^2\ge(n-1)^2(n-2)^2/(r-1)$, while the cap $\Xi\le n^2(n-1)$ leaves $\sum_{i\ge2}\sigma_i^2\le n^2(n-1)-4(n-1)^2=(n-1)(n-2)^2$. *Hence $(n-1)/(r-1)\le1$ and* $$\operatorname{rank}S\ \ge\ n.$$ **And equality forces the whole chain to be tight**: the remaining spectrum is flat at $n-2$, $\Xi=n^2(n-1)$, the configuration is integral, and [Lemma L201](#lemma-l201-integrality-of-the-pairwise-plane-traces-forces-a-common-frame) makes it coordinate. *The coordinate configurations attain it, their $S=A(J(n,2))+2\,\mathrm{Id}$ having spectrum $2n-2,\ n-2,\ 0$ at multiplicities $1,\ n-1,\ \binom n2-n$, so the equality case is exactly them and the projections span the frame's diagonal matrices.* **Provenance**: the split is [r858](../reviews/REVIEW_r858.md)'s and [r859](../reviews/REVIEW_r859.md)'s, found independently, read from a log and re-derived here before use. *It replaces the route this blockquote first carried, which applied Cauchy–Schwarz to the whole spectrum, treated only $\operatorname{rank}S=n-1$ and left every smaller rank unargued, and both reviewers refused it as written while confirming the result.* **The split needs no equality case of Cauchy-Schwarz for the bound itself**, and it is the attainment clause that consumes L201.

> **SO THE COORDINATE CONFIGURATION IS THE MOST FLEXIBLE ONE, WHICH IS A STATEMENT ABOUT THE CONJECTURE'S SHAPE.** *The Ricci-flat weight space of any orthonormal decomposable basis has dimension at most $\binom n2-n$, with equality EXACTLY at the coordinate configurations, where it is the zero-row-sum weightings of $K_n$.* **So carrying the largest possible space of Ricci-flat weightings characterises coordinate-diagonality**, which is a third equivalent form of it beside integrality and $\Xi=n^2(n-1)$. **And configurations with NO nonzero Ricci-flat weighting exist, which a parameter count does not show and a witness does.** *The count of $\binom n2$ unknowns against $\tfrac{n(n+1)}2$ conditions proves nothing, the effective number of conditions being $\operatorname{rank}S$ itself, and the record's own two configurations have it falling short at $\ker S$ of dimensions $2$ and $1$* ([r858](../reviews/REVIEW_r858.md), [r859](../reviews/REVIEW_r859.md), independently, and the sentence this replaces was refuted as stated). **The witness at $n=4$ is the six icosahedral axes**: configurations there correspond to unit tight frames of six vectors in $\mathbb R^3$ with $S_{\alpha\beta}=1-(a_\alpha\cdot a_\beta)^2$, the icosahedral axes have $(a_\alpha\cdot a_\beta)^2=\tfrac15$ throughout, so $S=\tfrac45\mathbf1\mathbf1^{\mathsf T}+\tfrac65\mathrm{Id}$ of rank $6$, *reproduced here with row sums $6=2(n-1)$ and trace $12=n(n-1)$.* **So that configuration carries no nonzero Ricci-flat weighting**, and with lower semicontinuity of rank on the moduli it earns the claim at $n=4$. *[r858](../reviews/REVIEW_r858.md) built a second and unrelated witness there, the pencil $e_1\wedge e_2,e_1\wedge e_3,e_1\wedge e_4$ together with $\star_U v_k$ for $U=\mathrm{span}\{e_2,e_3,e_4\}$ at $v_1=\tfrac13(2,2,-1)$, $v_2=\tfrac13(-1,2,2)$, $v_3=\tfrac13(2,-1,2)$, also of rank $6$ and checked twice, once structurally and once from $\Xi=400/9$.* **Its blockwise extension to $n\ge5$ fails**, so two witnesses agree at $n=4$ and neither reaches higher. *At $n\ge5$ it stays unproven, and the locus of configurations carrying a critical point is cut out by rank degeneracy with the diagonal one at its extreme.* **AND THE FORMULA REPRODUCES A NUMBER THE RECORD COMPUTED ANOTHER WAY, WHICH IS THE CHECK THAT MATTERS HERE.** *At the rotated witness the projections span $\{D_1,D_2,D_3,D_4,M_{23}\}$, since $A_1-A_2=\cos2t\,(D_2-D_3)+\sin2t\,M_{23}$ and $A_1+A_2$ recovers $D_1$ from $A_4=D_2+D_3$, so $\operatorname{rank}S=5$ at every $\sin2t\ne0$ and $\dim\ker S=1$.* **That is exactly the one-dimensional Ricci-flat solution space [r846](../reviews/REVIEW_r846.md) derived on the same family by solving the linear system**, reached here from the rank of a Gram matrix with nothing in common, so $S\lambda=0$ and Ricci-flatness agree at a point where the answer was already known.

> **AND THE RECORD'S OWN RELATIONS ARE THIS STATEMENT AT TWO ROWS, WHICH IS A CONSISTENCY CHECK RATHER THAN NEW CONTENT.** *$T$ is hollow, so $\sum_\gamma T_{\alpha\beta\gamma}$ is $S$ with its diagonal zeroed, $\hat S=S-2\,\mathrm{Id}$.* **Relation (B) reads $(\hat S\lambda)_k=-2\lambda_k$, which is $(S\lambda)_k=0$**, and summing relation (C) over $k$ gives $\lambda^{\mathsf T}\hat S\lambda=\lambda^{\mathsf T}S\lambda-2\lVert W\rVert^2=-2\lVert W\rVert^2$, matching $\sum_k2\lambda_k(\theta-\lambda_k)$ term for term once $\operatorname{tr}W=0$. *The two arrivals at $S\lambda=0$, from contracting the $\Theta$ equation against $\Pi_\alpha$ and from the $T$-marginal, are two ENCODINGS of one equation rather than independent derivations, and the wording claiming independence is withdrawn* ([r858](../reviews/REVIEW_r858.md), [r859](../reviews/REVIEW_r859.md)). **The summed relation (C) needs a second input beyond $\operatorname{tr}W=0$**, namely Ricci-flatness itself or the positive semidefiniteness of $S$. *Which gives a by-product worth keeping: relation (C) summed says $\lambda^{\mathsf T}S\lambda=0$, so on this locus RICCI-FLATNESS IS A CONSEQUENCE of criticality's diagonal rather than a hypothesis beside it* ([r858](../reviews/REVIEW_r858.md)). **THE POSITIVE-SEMIDEFINITENESS STEP IS UNNECESSARY AND THE WHOLE CONTENT IS A FACTOR OF TWO** ([r997](../reviews/REVIEW_r997.md), [r998](../reviews/REVIEW_r998.md), *verified here by derivation rather than by preferring a reviewer*). *$\lambda^{\mathsf T}S\lambda=\operatorname{tr}(M^2)=\lVert M\rVert_F^2$ with $M=\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha\Pi_\alpha$, at a generic configuration with no criticality and no Ricci-flatness, so the form IS a squared norm and no $S\succeq0$ lemma and no invertibility enter.* **But relations (A), (B) and (C) are each TWICE the diagonal of the matching $\#$-identity, and read without that factor the same summation gives $\lambda^{\mathsf T}S\lambda=\lVert W\rVert^2$ rather than $0$**, *which makes the normalisation the single load-bearing input and a bridge constant $\kappa\ne1$ a silent breakage.*

> **TWO OF THE THREE OFF-DIAGONAL FAMILIES CANNOT OBSTRUCT NON-INTEGRALITY, AND THE WITNESS THAT SHOWS IT IS THE RECORD'S OWN.** *Working notes, Gate 2 pending.* **The first family is basis-free**: $\sum_\beta\langle[\omega_k,\omega_\beta],[\omega_l,\omega_\beta]\rangle=-\operatorname{tr}(\mathrm{ad}\,\omega_k\,\mathrm{ad}\,\omega_l)=2(n-2)\delta_{kl}$ is the Killing form against the trace form, so it holds at EVERY orthonormal basis of $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ and carries nothing about the configuration. **The second family holds at every Weyl tensor**, being the off-diagonal of $W\#I=-W$, *and the rotated witness at weights $(\tfrac12,\tfrac12,-1,-1,\tfrac12,\tfrac12)$ is a Ricci-flat Bianchi trace-free $W$ whose chosen basis is NON-integral, its FOUR mixed pairs having $\operatorname{tr}(\Pi\Pi')$ equal to $\cos^2t$ twice and $\sin^2t$ twice*. **So a family satisfied by a non-integral Weyl tensor cannot force integrality.** **GATE 2 PASSED ON THE NARROWING AND ITS PAYLOAD DOES NOT SURVIVE** ([r997](../reviews/REVIEW_r997.md), [r998](../reviews/REVIEW_r998.md), both PLAUSIBLE, both correcting it). *The inertness of the first two rests on their being IDENTITIES, (A)'s off-diagonal the Killing form at every orthonormal basis and (C)'s vanishing identically on the coordinate locus for every $\lambda$, rather than on one non-integral survivor* ([r997](../reviews/REVIEW_r997.md)). **AND NO FAMILY AT ALL CAN FORCE INTEGRALITY OF A CHOSEN BASIS, THE THIRD INCLUDED**: criticality is an OPERATOR equation, so every relation holds at any eigenbasis a degenerate eigenspace admits. *[r998](../reviews/REVIEW_r998.md) exhibits it exactly at $n=4$, $W=\mathrm{diag}(-2,-2,1,1,1,1)$ with the two negatives on a COMPLEMENTARY pair, Ricci-flat and Bianchi over $\mathbb Q$, its four-dimensional $+1$ eigenspace carrying a non-coordinate decomposable orthonormal eigenbasis with $S$ entries $3969/4225$ and $256/4225$, $\operatorname{rank}S=5$ and $S\lambda=0$; reproduced here before folding.* **This AGREES with what the block above already says**, that integrality is a condition on the CHOSEN eigenbasis rather than on $W$, *so it is a sharper witness and not a new closure*. **The consequence is the usable part: a route must quantify EXISTENTIALLY over eigenbases, and spectral degeneracy is what supplies the freedom.*

> **AND THE OBLIGATION IS CROSS-EIGENVALUE, NOT PER BASIS, WHICH IS WHERE THE SAME WITNESS PUTS IT.** *That witness is coordinate-diagonal as an OPERATOR: its Ricci-flat weighting forces $\lambda_1=\lambda_2$, the $\lambda_1$-eigenspace becomes $e_1\wedge\langle e_2,e_3\rangle$, and reselecting inside it recovers the standard frame.* **So no statement about one chosen basis can be the item**: what I74 must exclude is a critical point EVERY decomposable orthonormal eigenbasis of which is non-integral. *Reselection inside a single eigenspace is free ([r851](../reviews/REVIEW_r851.md), [r852](../reviews/REVIEW_r852.md)), so the live obligation is integrality of the pairs drawn from DISTINCT eigenvalues, and the reselection group is $\prod_\mu O(\dim E_\mu)$ cut down by decomposability.* **At simple spectrum there is nothing to reselect and the obligation is the bare integrality of $\binom N2$ pairs**, which is why the simple-spectrum branch is the one to attack first, and it is live from $n=5$.

> **AND THE MAXIMALITY ROUTE TO INTEGRALITY IS NOT AN ORBIT MOTION, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES IT COST SOMETHING.** *Working notes, Gate 2 pending.* **The natural attack is to deform a non-integral critical point toward a coordinate configuration and read off that $\theta$ rises, contradicting maximality.** *The deformation cannot be a rotation: $\theta$ is $O(n)$-invariant, so every conjugation is $\theta$-neutral and moves the configuration only inside its own orbit, while the rotated witness is not a coordinate configuration in ANY frame.* **So the deformation is a genuine tangent direction of the unit sphere of $\mathcal W_n$, it moves the eigenvalues as well as the planes, and the route needs the second variation rather than the first.** *What it would have to establish: near a critical point with simple spectrum the eigenbasis varies smoothly, so $\Xi$ is a smooth function of $W$ there, and the claim is that $\theta$ cannot be stationary and maximal where $\Xi$ is not extremal.* **That is a statement relating two variations and is exactly the content [I74](../plan.md) still owes**, the alternative being the third off-diagonal family, which is algebraic and needs no second-order input.

> **AND A $\Xi$-BASED BOUND ON $\theta$ LOSES TO THE RECORDED ONE BY AN EXPONENT, WHICH CLOSES THE OBVIOUS SHORTCUT.** *Working notes, Gate 2 pending, and this one is a NEGATIVE.* **The tempting move is to bound $\theta$ by $\Xi$ and let integrality be forced by the bound's own sharpness.** *From $P=\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha^3+\tfrac12\sum_{\alpha\ne\beta}\lambda_\alpha\lambda_\beta S_{\alpha\beta}r_{\alpha\beta}$ at unit norm, with $\lvert r\rvert\le\lambda_{\max}$ and Cauchy–Schwarz against $\sum_{\alpha\ne\beta}S_{\alpha\beta}^2=\Xi-2n(n-1)$,* $$\theta\ \le\ \sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha^3+\tfrac12\lambda_{\max}\sqrt{1-\textstyle\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha^4}\ \sqrt{\Xi-2n(n-1)},$$ *and feeding [L198](bounds.md)'s $\theta\lambda_{\max}\le1$ and the cap $\Xi\le n^2(n-1)$ gives $\theta^2\le1+\tfrac12\sqrt{n(n-1)(n-2)}$.* **That is $\theta=O(n^{3/4})$ against the record's $\theta\le(n-1)/\sqrt{n+1}=O(\sqrt n)$, and $4.38$ against $3.0509$ at $n=12$, so it loses at the dimension the conjecture is about.** *Routing the row marginal $\sum_\beta S_{\alpha\beta}=2(n-2)$ through instead of Cauchy-Schwarz gives $\theta=O(n^{2/3})$, which also loses.* **So $\Xi$ is a characterisation of coordinate-diagonality and not a route to a bound**, and the one thing the inequality does say is that the cubic form has the most room exactly where the configuration is integral, since the bound grows with $\Xi$.

> **AND THE THIRD OFF-DIAGONAL FAMILY IS CRITICALITY ITSELF, SO THE INPUT LIST IS EMPTY RATHER THAN SHORT.** *Working notes, Gate 2 pending, and this is the cycle's last negative.* **The off-diagonal of $W^\#=\theta W-W^2$ says $\langle W^\#\omega_k,\omega_l\rangle=0$ at $k\ne l$, which is $[W,W^\#]=0$**, and [conventions.md](conventions.md) already records that as automatic at every critical point, $W^\#$ being a polynomial in $W$ there. *So it is the hypothesis restated and not a lever beside it: as a constraint on the configuration it says the operator $\tfrac12\sum_{\alpha\beta}\lambda_\alpha\lambda_\beta[\omega_\alpha,\omega_\beta]\otimes[\omega_\alpha,\omega_\beta]$ is diagonal in the eigenbasis, which is what "critical" means.* **With the first family basis-free and the second holding at every Weyl tensor, all three are now accounted for and none is an independent input.** *What remains is relation (C)'s Rayleigh form, which is the diagonal of the same equation, so [I74](../plan.md) has three equivalent statements of its target and no identified mechanism carrying criticality into any of them.* **That is the honest state of the item and it is graded on it.**

#### From [I75](../plan.md): the sharpened pair cap gives the decomposable locus a quantitative test

**Working notes, Gate 2 pending, derived 2026-09-10.** *[Lemma L199](bounds.md)'s row inequality reads $\theta\lambda_k\le\lambda_k^2+\tfrac12R_k$ at every index of a critical Weyl tensor of unit norm, with $R_k=\sum_\beta\lambda_\beta^2\lVert[v_k,v_\beta]\rVert^2$, and the record closes it with the pair cap $\lVert[v_k,v_\beta]\rVert^2\le2$ to reach [L198](bounds.md)'s $\theta\lambda_{\max}\le1$.* **On the decomposable locus the cap is $1$ rather than $2$**, being $S_{k\beta}=\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_k\Pi_\beta)\le1$ through the bridge, *so $R_k\le\sum_{\beta\ne k}\lambda_\beta^2=1-\lambda_k^2$ by hollowness and unit norm, giving* $$\theta\lambda_k\ \le\ \frac{1+\lambda_k^2}2\qquad\text{at every index},$$ **which is strictly sharper than $\theta\lambda_k\le1$ at every $\lvert\lambda_k\rvert<1$ ([r863](../reviews/REVIEW_r863.md), the absolute value having been dropped).** *At the maximal index it reads $\theta\le(1+\lambda_{\max}^2)/(2\lambda_{\max})$, about half of $1/\lambda_{\max}$ in the small-$\lambda_{\max}$ regime.*

> **THE COROLLARY IS A TEST, AND IT IS THE FIRST QUANTITATIVE HANDLE ON [I75](../plan.md).** *A critical point with $\theta\lambda_{\max}>(1+\lambda_{\max}^2)/2$ carries NO orthonormal decomposable eigenbasis*, since the inequality above holds on that locus without exception. **Padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ fails it by a fifth**: at unit norm its blocks are $(2,-1,-1)/\sqrt6$, so $\lambda_{\max}=2/\sqrt6$, $\lambda_{\max}^2=\tfrac23$ and $\theta\lambda_{\max}=\sqrt{3/2}\cdot2/\sqrt6=1$ against the bound's $\tfrac56$. *So its Plücker obstruction is recovered quantitatively rather than by exhibiting the obstruction.* **And [L198](bounds.md) is sharp only OFF the decomposable locus**, which needs $\lambda_{\max}^2<1$, itself a consequence of trace-freeness and unit norm at more than one nonzero eigenvalue ([r862](../reviews/REVIEW_r862.md), the step elided). *The test is sharper than first written too: decomposability is spent only at indices with $\lambda\ne0$, so what it excludes is an orthonormal decomposable eigenbasis of $(\ker W)^\perp$* ([r863](../reviews/REVIEW_r863.md)). **No point besides padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ is exhibited in its window, so no new exclusion is demonstrated yet**, and the non-composition has TWO reasons, only one of which was carried: $(1+x^2)/(2x)\ge1$ always, so this row can never return $\theta<1$ ([r863](../reviews/REVIEW_r863.md)), and trace-freeness pushes $\lvert\lambda_{\min}\rvert$ UP, which is the direction [L107](bounds.md) consumes ([r862](../reviews/REVIEW_r862.md)).

> **WHAT IT DOES NOT DO, STATED BEFORE ANYONE COMPOSES IT WITH THE GLOBAL BOUND.** *The record's $\theta\le(n-1)/\sqrt{n+1}$ runs through [L107](bounds.md)'s $\theta\le(n-1)\lvert\lambda_{\min}\rvert$, and the inequality above is vacuous at a negative eigenvalue, so it sharpens the $\lambda_{\max}$ row and not the composition.* **At the balanced product it gives exactly $28/\sqrt{55}=3.77551$ against $\theta_0(12)=\sqrt{55}/6$**, the Weyl spectrum there being $6/11$ on the $30$ within-factor planes and $-5/11$ on the $36$ cross planes, of norm $\sqrt{180/11}$, so $\lambda_{\max}=1/\sqrt{55}$ exactly and $\theta\lambda_{\max}=1/6$ against the bound's $28/55$ ([r863](../reviews/REVIEW_r863.md) supplied the closed forms where this block carried decimals). *The scoreboard is unmoved and the test is what the result is for.*

#### From [I76](../plan.md): two eigenvalues force a regularity condition on the Gram matrix, and it is tight at the balanced product

> **WHAT IS LEFT OPEN, MOVED HERE FROM [plan.md](../plan.md) ON 2026-09-13 BECAUSE IT IS THE ITEM'S MATHEMATICS RATHER THAN ITS STATUS.** *The question is whether a configuration exists with every pairwise trace strictly below $1$, and it needs an argument about the SUBSPACE $A$ rather than about a basis of it: $W=\lambda(\Pi_A-\Pi_{A^\perp})$ depends on $A$ alone, while [L201](#lemma-l201-integrality-of-the-pairwise-plane-traces-forces-a-common-frame) constrains a basis, which is exactly why the integrality-defect test cannot certify non-coordinacy and why [r881](../reviews/REVIEW_r881.md)'s exact rational witness spans the coordinate pentagon.*

**Working notes, Gate 2 pending, derived 2026-09-10 as the attackable split of [I74](../plan.md).** *Let $W$ be a critical point with an orthonormal decomposable eigenbasis and exactly TWO distinct eigenvalues $\mu>0>\nu$, with $A=\{\alpha:\lambda_\alpha=\mu\}$ of size $a$ and $B$ its complement in the $N=\binom n2$ indices.* **Ricci-flatness is $S\lambda=0$, and completeness gives $S\mathbf1=2(n-1)\mathbf1$, so subtracting the two collapses the weights out entirely:** $$(\mu-\nu)\,S\mathbf1_A=-2(n-1)\nu\,\mathbf1,\qquad\text{hence}\qquad \sum_{\beta\in A}S_{\alpha\beta}=\frac{2(n-1)a}{N}\ \ \text{at EVERY index }\alpha,$$ *the constant coming from $\mathbf1^{\mathsf T}S\mathbf1_A=2(n-1)a$.* **So a two-eigenvalue critical point on this locus forces the Gram matrix to be $A$-regular**, a linear condition on the PAIR of a configuration and a subset rather than on the configuration alone. **AND IT IS AN EQUIVALENCE RATHER THAN A CONSTRAINT, WHICH IS THE ROUND'S CENTRAL FINDING** ([r860](../reviews/REVIEW_r860.md), [r861](../reviews/REVIEW_r861.md), independently): *at $\mu=N-a$ and $\nu=-a$ the same computation runs backwards, so $A$-regularity with that constant is EQUIVALENT to carrying a two-valued Ricci-flat weighting with level set $A$, verified here by substituting and cancelling.* **So the tightness check below could not have failed, and nothing derived from $A$-regularity alone can force anything.** *Criticality is never consumed: the derivation uses Ricci-flatness, completeness and two-valuedness only, so it covers every two-valued Ricci-flat tensor on the locus, a strictly larger class than the critical ones, which is exactly why it cannot discriminate.* **Trace-freeness is a CONSEQUENCE and not a hypothesis**, $\mathbf1^{\mathsf T}S\lambda=2(n-1)\operatorname{tr}W$, and it pins the weights: $\mu:\nu=(N-a):(-a)$. *And $\mu>0>\nu$ is then a theorem, since $\nu=0$ would force $\sum_{\alpha\in A}\Pi_\alpha=0$ against its trace $2a$, so a two-valued spectrum on this locus HAS NO KERNEL.* *Machine-checked and gap-free as `two_eigenvalue_A_regular` in [L201Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L201Statement.lean), where the hypotheses are trace two, completeness, trace-freeness, $\mu\ne\nu$, $N\ne0$ and the Ricci rows, and nothing about planes is used.* **The typed statement is WIDER than the prose in one direction and narrower in another**: it asks no symmetry, idempotence or rank of the $P_a$, so $N=\binom n2$ is forced only through the traces, while it assumes trace-freeness which the prose route does not need ([r860](../reviews/REVIEW_r860.md), [r861](../reviews/REVIEW_r861.md)). *And the machine-checked chain stops at $A$-regularity: `integrality_forces_frame` still carries its gap, so coordinate-diagonality is not typed downstream of it.*

> **AND IT IS TIGHT AT THE TARGET, WHICH IS THE CHECK THAT MATTERS.** *At $S^6\times S^6$, $n=12$: $A$ is the within-factor planes, $a=2\binom62=30$, $N=66$, so the constant is $2\cdot11\cdot30/66=10$.* **Both cases return $10$ exactly.** *For $\alpha\in A$, say $e_1\wedge e_2$: the diagonal contributes $2$ and the within-factor planes sharing a line contribute $2(p-2)=8$, the other factor's planes being disjoint and contributing $0$. For $\alpha\in B$, say $e_1\wedge e_7$: the planes of $A$ sharing a line with it are the $p-1=5$ through $e_1$ and the $q-1=5$ through $e_7$.* **So the condition is satisfied with equality at the conjectured maximiser and is not vacuous.**

> **WHY THIS IS THE ATTACKABLE PART, AND WHAT IT WOULD DELIVER.** *Regularity conditions of exactly this shape are what the record's [R15](../routes.md) already meets from the diagonal side, where [DGK 2023](../references/deninger-grundhoefer-kramer-2023-srg-quadratic/notes.md) matches two-eigenvalue diagonal critical points with strongly regular graphs.* **Here the same condition arrives without assuming diagonality.** *The first hope was that $A$-regularity forces integrality of the chosen basis, and THAT IS FALSE: the record's rotated witness at weights $(\tfrac12,\tfrac12,-1,-1,\tfrac12,\tfrac12)$ is two-valued with $a=4$, so the constant is $2\cdot3\cdot4/6=4$, and at the plane $e_1\wedge g_2$ the $A$-row sums to $2+1+\cos^2t+\sin^2t=4$ exactly while two of its entries are $\cos^2t$ and $\sin^2t$.* **So the witness is $A$-regular and non-integral, and it is a genuine critical point** ([r860](../reviews/REVIEW_r860.md) and [r861](../reviews/REVIEW_r861.md) reached the same witness from the same given, and r861 puts a second one AT the target: rotating $e_1\wedge e_2$ and $e_1\wedge e_3$ inside the positive eigenspace of $S^6\times S^6$ keeps every $A$-row sum at $10$ while $\Xi$ drops to $n^2(n-1)-2(n-3)\sin^22t$): after the collapse its diagonal weights are $\lambda_{12}=\lambda_{13}=\lambda_{24}=\lambda_{34}=\tfrac12$ and $\lambda_{14}=\lambda_{23}=-1$, which is $\tfrac12(1,1,-2)$ in the free triple and gives $\theta=-\theta_0(4)$, the $S^2\times S^2$ point up to sign. *Caught here rather than at review, and the two reviewers of the round were briefed on the phrasing this replaces.* **The target is therefore coordinate-diagonality of the OPERATOR, up to reselection inside the eigenspaces, exactly as the cross-eigenvalue reading of [I74](../plan.md) requires**, and $A$-regularity is the tool rather than the conclusion. *At $n=4$ the two-eigenvalue critical points on this locus are the $S^2\times S^2$ family, $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ having no decomposable eigenbasis, so the statement holds there and $n\ge5$ is what is open.* **If it holds, the non-diagonal case carries at least THREE distinct eigenvalues.** *The claim that this needs no new machinery is WITHDRAWN: the regularity step is an equivalence, so it narrows nothing by itself, and $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ already carries three distinct eigenvalues* ([r860](../reviews/REVIEW_r860.md)). **The item's content is therefore the classification and nothing else, and it must spend criticality.** *Non-vacuity is not in doubt: $S^6\times S^6$ is a two-eigenvalue critical point at $\theta=\sqrt{55}/6=\theta_0(12)$ exactly, and the constant generalises to $2(p-1)$ at every $S^p\times S^p$.*

> **AND THE RIGHT FORM IS ISOTROPY OF $\Theta$ ON ONE EIGENSPACE, WITH $A$-REGULARITY AS ITS CONTRACTION.** *Working notes, Gate 2 pending, derived after the round below was dispatched.* **At two eigenvalues $W=\nu\,\mathrm{Id}+(\mu-\nu)\Pi_{E}$ is determined by the single subspace $E=E_\mu$**, and additivity of $\Theta$ turns Ricci-flatness into $$\Theta(E)=\frac{2a}{n}\,\mathrm{Id},\qquad a=\dim E,$$ *the constant forced by $\operatorname{tr}\Theta(E)=2a$, since $\mu\Theta(E)+\nu\Theta(E^\perp)=0$ and $\Theta(E)+\Theta(E^\perp)=(n-1)\mathrm{Id}$ eliminate the weights.* *Machine-checked and gap-free as `two_eigenvalue_isotropic` in [L201Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L201Statement.lean), where the count $N=\tfrac{n(n-1)}2$ is the one extra hypothesis the matrix form needs.* **This is the MATRIX form of the $A$-regularity above, whose contraction against $\Pi_\alpha$ it is, and the two are EQUIVALENT rather than one stronger** ([r862](../reviews/REVIEW_r862.md), the "strictly stronger" reading withdrawn, each being equivalent to two-valued Ricci-flatness and therefore to the other): $\sum_{\beta\in A}S_{\alpha\beta}=\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_\alpha\Theta(E))=4a/n$, and $4a/n=2(n-1)a/N$ at $N=\binom n2$, so the two constants agree and the matrix statement carries the rest.

> **AND IT RETURNS BALANCEDNESS IN ONE LINE, WHICH IS THE CHECK THAT MATTERS.** *For the sphere product $S^p\times S^q$ the positive eigenspace is $E=\Lambda^2U_1\oplus\Lambda^2U_2$ over the two factors, so $\Theta(E)=(p-1)\Pi_{U_1}+(q-1)\Pi_{U_2}$ by $\Theta(\Lambda^2U)=(\dim U-1)\Pi_U$.* **That is isotropic exactly when $p=q$**, and then it equals $(p-1)\mathrm{Id}$ against $2a/n=2\cdot2\binom p2/(2p)=p-1$. *So Ricci-flatness at two eigenvalues forces the product to be BALANCED, recovered here from $\Theta$'s additivity rather than assumed.* **Pencils are excluded by the same test, and completely**: $\Theta(f\wedge U')=r\,ff^{\mathsf T}+\Pi_{U'}$ is isotropic at NO $r$ once $n\ge3$, the $r=1$ case included, since its rank is then $r+1\le n-1$ ([r862](../reviews/REVIEW_r862.md), [r863](../reviews/REVIEW_r863.md), the earlier "only at $r=1$" being a true implication read as a false equivalence). *And the rank bound generalises the range to $n/2\le a\le N-n/2$.*

> **SO [I76](../plan.md) IS A CLASSIFICATION PROBLEM WITH A KNOWN SOLUTION FAMILY.** *Which subspaces $E\subseteq\Lambda^2\mathbb R^n$ have $\Theta(E)$ isotropic and carry an orthonormal decomposable basis, with the complement carrying one too?* **The balanced products $\Lambda^2U_1\oplus\Lambda^2U_2$ at $\dim U_1=\dim U_2$ are one family and $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^n$ is the trivial one**, and the item is whether anything else exists. *Criticality has not been spent yet, so a classification of the isotropic decomposable subspaces would narrow the two-eigenvalue case before relation (C) is even used.* **One family is already excluded and it is the one that looks most promising**: complex lines in $\mathbb R^4\cong\mathbb C^2$ give planes whose projections form a tight frame at any Hermitian tight frame of lines, so isotropy is easy there, *but two complex lines are ISOCLINIC, their two principal angles equal, so $\langle\omega_j,\omega_k\rangle=\cos^2\vartheta\ne0$ unless the lines are orthogonal, and three lines in $\mathbb C^2$ cannot be pairwise orthogonal.* **So $\Lambda^2$-orthonormality is the binding constraint rather than isotropy**, which is where a classification should press. *The exclusion covers the all-complex-line families only: a configuration MIXING complex lines with other planes is untouched by it* ([r863](../reviews/REVIEW_r863.md)).

> **AND THE DIAGONAL DICTIONARY IS EXACT: ISOTROPY IS REGULARITY, CRITICALITY IS STRONG REGULARITY.** *Working notes, Gate 2 pending.* **On the coordinate configuration $\Theta(E)$ isotropic reads $\sum_{\alpha\in A}\lvert\Pi_\alpha x\rvert^2=2a/n$ at every unit $x$, and at $x=e_i$ that counts the edges of $A$ at the vertex $i$** *(testing at the frame vectors alone gives the forward direction, and the converse uses that $\Theta(E)$ is diagonal there, which coordinate planes make automatic* ([r862](../reviews/REVIEW_r862.md))*)*, so isotropy is exactly that the positive-weight edges form a REGULAR graph, of degree $2\lvert A\rvert/n$ by the handshake count. *The sphere product is $K_p\sqcup K_q$, regular exactly at $p=q$, which is balancedness a third time.* **And the record's own correspondence then splits cleanly**: [DGK 2023](../references/deninger-grundhoefer-kramer-2023-srg-quadratic/notes.md) matches two-eigenvalue diagonal critical points with STRONGLY regular graphs, so *Ricci-flatness supplies the regularity and criticality supplies the strength*. **The split is not clean and both reviewers said so**: relation (C) does not separate into the two halves, so read the dictionary as which condition each side of the correspondence NEEDS rather than as a factorisation ([r862](../reviews/REVIEW_r862.md), [r863](../reviews/REVIEW_r863.md)). **That also says where the two-eigenvalue case is harmless**: the many strongly regular graphs that are not sphere products are diagonal, hence inside the granted half, so [I76](../plan.md) is only about the non-diagonal ones.

> **WHICH MAKES THE ITEM ONE SHARP QUESTION.** *Since $W=\nu\,\mathrm{Id}+(\mu-\nu)\Pi_E$, the operator is coordinate-diagonal exactly when $E$ is spanned by the coordinate $2$-planes of a single frame.* **So: must an $E$ with $\Theta(E)$ isotropic, carrying an orthonormal decomposable basis, be coordinate-SPANNED?** *The rotated witness passes, its $E=\mathrm{span}\{e_1\wedge g_2,e_1\wedge g_3,e_2\wedge e_4,e_3\wedge e_4\}$ being $\mathrm{span}\{e_{12},e_{13},e_{24},e_{34}\}$ in the standard frame, so a non-coordinate BASIS is no obstruction and only the span matters.* **A counterexample would be an isotropic decomposable subspace whose span meets no frame**, and the round narrowed where to look. *Coordinate-spannedness forces $n\mid2a$, the positive-weight graph's degree being $2a/n$, so any $a$ failing that divisibility is a counterexample if it is realisable at all* ([r863](../reviews/REVIEW_r863.md)). **And the question is settled AFFIRMATIVELY at $n=4$**: chirality parity forces the two $\Lambda^\pm$ counts equal, so $a\in\{2,4\}$ with $a=3$ empty, and both surviving cases reduce to $\Pi_1+\Pi_2=\mathrm{Id}_4$ on the two-dimensional side and are coordinate-spanned. *So [I76](../plan.md) begins at $n\ge5$, where the rank bound leaves $3\le a\le7$ and divisibility leaves only $a=5$ coordinate-spannable, so $a\in\{3,4,6,7\}$ are the falsifier candidates.* **The $n=5$, $a=3$ Gram matrix is FORCED**, $S=1/5$ within the positive set and cross rows summing to $12/5$, so deciding its realisability is a construction and it is the item's first concrete task ([r863](../reviews/REVIEW_r863.md)). *One more scope point: at two eigenvalues first Bianchi is the separate condition $\mathrm{Alt}(\Pi_E)=0$, free when $E$ carries a decomposable basis* ([r862](../reviews/REVIEW_r862.md) alone), *and a two-eigenvalue Ricci-flat $W$ is forced invertible, so padded points lie outside this claim entirely* ([r862](../reviews/REVIEW_r862.md), [r863](../reviews/REVIEW_r863.md), independently). *And the witness says exactly which pairs matter: all EIGHT of its cross pairs are integral, each sharing a line, while all FOUR failures are WITHIN the positive eigenspace, namely the pairs $(e_1\wedge g_2,e_2\wedge e_4)$, $(e_1\wedge g_2,e_3\wedge e_4)$, $(e_1\wedge g_3,e_2\wedge e_4)$ and $(e_1\wedge g_3,e_3\wedge e_4)$ at $\cos^2t,\sin^2t,\sin^2t,\cos^2t$* (all fifteen pairs recomputed here; the count stood at two until [r863](../reviews/REVIEW_r863.md)'s log named four, and the remaining cross pair $(e_1\wedge e_4,e_2\wedge e_3)$ is $0$, the planes being orthogonal). **So within-eigenspace non-integrality is harmless and cross-eigenvalue integrality is the whole of it**, which is [I74](../plan.md)'s cross-eigenvalue reading arriving from the two-eigenvalue side. *The sharpest form of the target: if every pair drawn from DIFFERENT eigenvalues commutes, is $W$ coordinate-diagonal? At the witness the two generated algebras commute elementwise and it is.* **And there is a lead on it**: cross-commuting makes each side's projections generate an algebra inside the other's commutant, so by Schur, the symmetric elements of a commutant being $\mathbb R\cdot\mathrm{Id}$, one side's algebra is REDUCIBLE. *A closed-form attempt at $n=4$ collapsed back to coordinate-spanned* ([r862](../reviews/REVIEW_r862.md)).

> **THE $n=5$, $a=3$ CASE IS EMPTY, AND THE ARGUMENT IS A RANK COUNT THAT PRUNES EVERY DIMENSION AT ONCE.** *Working notes, Gate 2 pending, derived 2026-09-10 as [r863](../reviews/REVIEW_r863.md)'s handed-over task.* **PRUNING CLAIM, STATED EXACTLY: the count removes $a=\lceil n/2\rceil$ and its complement at ODD $n$ and nothing at even $n$**, where the disjunction is the positivity bound restated ([r867](../reviews/REVIEW_r867.md), the earlier "prunes every dimension at once" being an overstatement). *Isotropy says $\sum_{\alpha\in A}\Pi_\alpha=c\,\mathrm{Id}$ at $c=2a/n$, so removing one projection leaves $$\sum_{\alpha\ne1}\Pi_\alpha=c\,\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_1,$$ whose eigenvalues are $c-1$ twice and $c$ elsewhere.** *Positive semidefiniteness of the left side forces $c\ge1$, which is $2a\ge n$, an inequality the record also has from the Gram rank though not by this route, and the earlier citation of it was mislabelled ([r866](../reviews/REVIEW_r866.md)). And when $c>1$ the right side has FULL rank $n$ while the left is a sum of $a-1$ rank-two projections, of rank at most $2(a-1)$.* **So $\kappa>1$ forces $a\ge\tfrac n2+1$, and one further step closes the window above it.** *Removing TWO projections, if $2(a-2)<n$ the rank forces the zero eigenvalue to come from $\kappa-1-c'$ at the principal cosine $c'$, so $S_{\alpha\beta}=(\kappa-1)^2$ at every pair inside $A$, and the row sum $(a-1)(\kappa-1)^2=2(\kappa-1)$ gives* $$(a-1)(2a-n)=2n,$$ *which no integer in $[\tfrac n2+1,\tfrac n2+2)$ satisfies for $n\ge4$* ([r866](../reviews/REVIEW_r866.md), reproduced here). **So the admissible sizes are $a=\tfrac n2$ exactly or $a\ge\tfrac n2+2$, with $n=3$, $a=3$ the one realised exception.** *At $n=5$ that is $a\ge5$ with the complement giving $a\le5$, so $a=5$ ALONE, which collapses both of the blocks below into one line, and it kills $n=4$, $a=3$ as well.* **The isotropy constant is written $\kappa$ from here on**, the letter $c$ having named both it and the principal cosine within four lines ([r866](../reviews/REVIEW_r866.md)). *The same applies to the complement, whose isotropy constant is $2(N-a)/n$, so $N-a$ obeys it too.*

> **WHICH LEAVES ONE CASE AT $n=5$, NOT FOUR.** *There $c=2a/5>1$ at every admissible $a$, so $a\ge3.5$ gives $a\ge4$, and the complement gives $10-a\ge4$, hence $a\in\{4,5,6\}$.* **Divisibility $n\mid2a$ leaves only $a=5$ coordinate-spannable, so the falsifier candidates are $a\in\{4,6\}$, and these two are the same case**, the swap $a\leftrightarrow N-a$ exchanging $E$ with $E^\perp$, negating $W$ and so sending $\theta$ to $-\theta$ ([r866](../reviews/REVIEW_r866.md)). *So [r863](../reviews/REVIEW_r863.md)'s list $\{3,4,6,7\}$ collapses to a single question.* **The question**: is there a four-dimensional $E\subseteq\Lambda^2\mathbb R^5$ with an orthonormal decomposable basis, $\Theta(E)=\tfrac85\mathrm{Id}$, and a complement carrying one too? *Its Gram data is forced: $\sum_{\beta\in A}S_{\alpha\beta}=16/5$ at every index, so the three off-diagonal entries inside $A$ sum to $6/5$ at each of its rows, and each row of $B$ sums to $16/5$ against $A$.*

> **AND THE $a=3$ EXCLUSION WAS FIRST FOUND BY EXPLICIT CONTRADICTION, WHICH IS WHY THE RANK COUNT IS TRUSTED.** *Taking $P_1=\mathrm{span}\{e_1,e_2\}$ and $P_2=\mathrm{span}\{(e_1+2e_3)/\sqrt5,e_4\}$, which meet the pairwise trace $1/5$ and the orthogonality, forces $\Pi_3=\tfrac15D_2+\tfrac25D_3+\tfrac15D_4+\tfrac65D_5-\tfrac25(e_1e_3^{\mathsf T}+e_3e_1^{\mathsf T})$, whose $(1,1)$ entry is zero while its first row is not, so it is not positive semidefinite.* **The rank count then explained the failure and generalised it**, which is the order the record prefers. **And the explicit case is a COMPLETE proof rather than a sample point**: once the pairwise trace $1/5$ and the right angle are forced, that pair of planes is unique up to $O(5)$, and $(\Pi_3)_{55}=6/5>1$ kills it before positivity is even reached ([r866](../reviews/REVIEW_r866.md), [r867](../reviews/REVIEW_r867.md), independently).

> **AND THE ONE REMAINING CASE AT $n=5$ IS EMPTY TOO, SO THE FALSIFIER CANDIDATES THERE ARE GONE.** *Working notes, Gate 2 pending, derived 2026-09-10 by pushing the same rank count one step further.* **Remove TWO projections instead of one**: for two $2$-planes in $\mathbb R^5$ whose principal angles are $\vartheta_1$ and a right angle, $\Pi_1+\Pi_2$ has eigenvalues $1\pm c,1,1,0$ at $c=\cos\vartheta_1=\sqrt{S_{12}}$, so at $a=4$ and $c=8/5$ *(the isotropy constant)* $$\tfrac85\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_1-\Pi_2\ \text{ has eigenvalues }\ \tfrac35-c,\ \tfrac35+c,\ \tfrac35,\ \tfrac35,\ \tfrac85,$$ *whose trace is $4$ as it must be.* **That matrix is $\Pi_3+\Pi_4$, so it is positive semidefinite, forcing $c\le\tfrac35$, and its rank is at most $4$, forcing a zero eigenvalue, which only $\tfrac35-c$ can supply.** *So $c=\tfrac35$ and $S_{\alpha\beta}=\tfrac9{25}$ at EVERY pair inside $A$.* **Then the row sum fails**: three off-diagonal entries at $\tfrac9{25}$ give $\tfrac{27}{25}$ against the required $\tfrac65=\tfrac{30}{25}$. *And the chain kills twice, so the row-sum step is a convenience*: $\Pi_3+\Pi_4$ must carry the eigenvalue $1$ with multiplicity at least two by the same normal form, while the forced spectrum $\{0,\tfrac35,\tfrac35,\tfrac65,\tfrac85\}$ carries none ([r866](../reviews/REVIEW_r866.md), [r867](../reviews/REVIEW_r867.md), both independently). *The degenerate case is covered by the same formula, two planes sharing a line having $c=1$ and so $\tfrac35-c<0$, excluded by positivity.*

> **SO AT $n=5$ THE ONLY ISOTROPIC DECOMPOSABLE SUBSPACES HAVE $a=5$, AND THE PENTAGON IS ONE.** *The divisible degree $a=5$ has degree $2a/n=2$, realised by the $5$-cycle: its coordinate planes span an isotropic $E$, its complement is the pentagram and is another $5$-cycle, and the weighting $+1$ on one and $-1$ on the other is Ricci-flat.* **$C_5$ is strongly regular with parameters $(5,2,0,1)$ and self-complementary, so it is a genuine two-eigenvalue diagonal critical point, and ITS $\theta$ IS ZERO EXACTLY**: $W^2=\mathrm{Id}$ and $W^\#=-\mathrm{Id}$ there, so $Q(W)=0$ against $\theta_0(5)=2\sqrt6/5$ ([r866](../reviews/REVIEW_r866.md), [r867](../reviews/REVIEW_r867.md), independently). *So it can never be a falsifier.* **And "strongly regular hence critical" is a GENERAL THEOREM the round proved rather than an inference from the parameters**: computing $W^\#$ for coordinate-diagonal $W$ from the triangle support of the structure constants gives $(M^2)_{ij}$, and criticality reduces identically to $k(k-\lambda-1)=(n-k-1)\mu$ with $\theta=(n-1)(k^2-(n-1)\mu)/k$ before unit normalisation ([r866](../reviews/REVIEW_r866.md)). *What remains at $n=5$ is only whether the divisible degree admits a NON-coordinate configuration, divisibility being necessary and not sufficient.* **The sentence needs its quantifier**: read over ALL subspaces it is false, since $a=10$ is isotropic with a decomposable basis and $a\in\{7,8,9\}$ fall only through the COMPLEMENT's decomposability, so the claim is over the two-eigenvalue decomposable locus, where both $E$ and $E^\perp$ carry decomposable bases ([r866](../reviews/REVIEW_r866.md), [r867](../reviews/REVIEW_r867.md), the only real error either found). *Untouched by all of it: three-or-more-eigenvalue falsifiers, kernels, and the non-coordinate question at $a=5$.* **The falsifier hunt at $n=5$ is therefore over for the non-divisible degrees**, which were the ones the divisibility argument had singled out.

#### From [I77](../plan.md): the triangle-type linear programme, which returns $\theta_0(n)$ exactly at the balanced product

**Working notes, Gate 2 pending, derived 2026-09-10 while [I76](../plan.md)'s regularity route was being refuted.** *Split the ordered triples of indices by how many lie in the positive level set $A$, writing $t_j=\sum T_{\alpha\beta\gamma}$ over the ordered triples with exactly $j$ indices in $A$.* **Two linear constraints hold at EVERY orthonormal basis of $\mathfrak{so}(n)$, both from [Lemma L153](bounds.md)'s marginal $\sum_{\beta\gamma}T_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=2(n-2)$:** $$\sum_j t_j=2N(n-2)=:M,\qquad \sum_j j\,t_j=6(n-2)a=:J,$$ *the second by counting, for a triple with $j$ indices in $A$, the $j$ positions at which the first index lies in $A$.* **And $t_j\ge0$ because $T$ is a square.** *At two eigenvalues trace-freeness pins $\mu:\nu=(N-a):(-a)$ and unit norm fixes the scale, so with $s=a$ and $k=(sN(N-s))^{-1/2}$,* $$\operatorname{tr}(W^3)=\frac{N-2s}{\sqrt{sN(N-s)}},\qquad \tfrac12T(\lambda,\lambda,\lambda)=\tfrac12k^3\sum_j t_j(N-s)^j(-s)^{3-j},$$ **so $\theta$ is a LINEAR functional of $(t_0,t_1,t_2,t_3)$ under two linear constraints, and maximising it is a four-variable linear programme.**

> **THE VERTEX IS FORCED AND THE BOUND IS CLOSED-FORM.** *The coefficients alternate in sign, positive at $j=1,3$ and negative at $j=0,2$, so the optimum puts all mass on $j\in\{1,3\}$ and the two constraints then determine it: $t_1=3(n-2)(N-s)$ and $t_3=(n-2)(3s-N)$, feasible exactly when $N/3\le s\le N$.* **Substituting and simplifying gives** $$\theta\ \le\ \frac{(N-2s)+\dfrac{(n-2)(N-s)(4s-N)}{2s}}{\sqrt{sN(N-s)}}\qquad\text{at every two-eigenvalue critical point of the decomposable locus with }\lvert A\rvert=s.$$ *No cap is used, only the marginal, nonnegativity and the two-valued structure, so the bound holds wherever those do.* **The closed form and both checks are machine-checked and gap-free** in [L202TriangleLP.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L202TriangleLP.lean), the checks in squared form so no square root appears: $6^2/48=3/4$ and $330^2/71280=220/144$, and the $s=31$ excess proved as a strict inequality. *The programme itself is not typed, so the constraints and the sign argument forcing the vertex are prose.*

> **AND IT RETURNS $\theta_0(n)$ EXACTLY AT THE BALANCED PRODUCT, WHICH IS THE CHECK THAT MATTERS.** *At $S^p\times S^p$ the counts are exactly the LP's vertex: $t_3=6\cdot2\binom p3$ from the triangles inside a factor, $t_1=6\cdot2\binom p2 p$ from those with one within-factor edge, and $t_0=t_2=0$ because a triangle cannot have exactly two within-factor edges nor three cross edges.* **At $n=12$, $s=30$: $t_3=240$ and $t_1=1080$, which are $(J-M)/2$ and $(3M-J)/2$ on the nose, and the bound evaluates to $330/\sqrt{71280}=1.23603=\theta_0(12)$.** *At $n=4$, $s=2$: $6/\sqrt{48}=\sqrt3/2=\theta_0(4)$, and the maximum over $s$ there IS at $s=2$, with $s=3$ giving $2/\sqrt6$ and $s=4$ giving $0.433$, so at $n=4$ the programme CLOSES the two-eigenvalue decomposable case exactly.*

> **AND THE BOUND HOLDS ON PART OF THE RANGE ONLY, WHICH DEFLATES IT BELOW THE RECORD'S.** *Caught from [r865](../reviews/REVIEW_r865.md)'s log and reproduced here before use.* **The forced vertex needs $t_3=(n-2)(3s-N)\ge0$, so the closed form above is valid only for $s\ge N/3$**, while the admissible window is the rank bound's $n/2\le s\le N-n/2$, which at $n=12$ is $6\le s\le60$. *On the low part $6\le s\le21$ the vertex changes: at $s=6$ the mass goes to $j\in\{0,3\}$ at $t_0=1200$ and $t_3=120$, giving $0.3503+3.5053=3.8556$, reproduced here against that reviewer's $3.8536$.* **So the programme's bound over the whole admissible range is about $3.855$ at $n=12$, WORSE than the record's standing $3.0509$**, and the $1.2385$ figure was the maximum over the feasible-vertex region alone. *The claim that this is the first construction landing on the target is withdrawn.*

> **AND THE REPAIR IS THAT RICCI-FLATNESS TURNS THE DISCARDED CAPS INTO EXACT EQUALITIES, WHICH RECOVERS $\theta_0(n)$ OVER THE WHOLE RANGE.** *[r864](../reviews/REVIEW_r864.md)'s own derivation, arriving under a reviewer's letterhead and therefore owed its own round.* **$A$-regularity says $\sum_{\beta\in A}S_{\alpha\beta}=4a/n$ at every index, and $S_{\alpha\beta}=\sum_\gamma T_{\alpha\beta\gamma}$, so summing over $\alpha$ in each part turns the pair caps into THREE exact equalities on the $t_j$** rather than the two the block used. *They subsume both earlier constraints and cut the feasible set at fixed $s$ to a segment in $t_2$, along which the objective falls at rate $\tfrac16k^3N^3$, so the bound sits at the least feasible $t_2$ and reads* $$\theta\ \le\ \frac2n\sqrt{\frac{sN}{N-s}}\qquad\Big(\tfrac n2\le s\le\tfrac{n(n-2)}4\Big),$$ **which is increasing in $s$, so its maximum is at the right endpoint $s=n(n-2)/4$, where it equals $\theta_0(n)$ EXACTLY.** *Verified here at $n=12$: the endpoint is $s=30$, the balanced product, and $\tfrac16\sqrt{30\cdot66/36}=\sqrt{55}/6=\theta_0(12)$.* **So the two-eigenvalue case of the decomposable locus does satisfy $\theta\le\theta_0(n)$, by a route with no relation to [L105](bounds-relaxations.md)'s**, and beyond the endpoint a decreasing branch takes over, $s=31$ giving $1.09926$. **AND THE SECOND REVIEWER DERIVED THE SAME BOUND INDEPENDENTLY, FROM A DIFFERENT INPUT** ([r865](../reviews/REVIEW_r865.md)): relation (B) summed over the positive level set gives $3p\,t_3+(p-s)t_2-s\,t_1=-6ps$, whose $t_2=0$ branch determines $t_3=2s(2s-n)/n$ and $t_1=12sp/n$, *forcing $s\ge n/2$ on its own and collapsing the numerator to $(n-1)s$*: $$\theta\ \le\ (n-1)\sqrt{\frac{s}{N(N-s)}}\qquad\Big(\tfrac n2\le s\le\tfrac{n(n-2)}4\Big).$$ *The two forms are the SAME bound, $\tfrac2n\sqrt{N}=(n-1)/\sqrt N$ at $N=\binom n2$, checked here and equal to $\sqrt{55}/6$ at $n=12$, $s=30$ from both.* **It uses relation (B) alone, with no criticality and no decomposability, and it is tight at $S^2\times S^2$, at $(S^2)^6$ and at $S^6\times S^6$.** *The endpoint is integral exactly when $n$ is even, where it is the balanced product's own $s$.*

> **WHICH ALSO CORRECTS TWO THINGS THIS BLOCK SAID.** *The claim that the cap never appears at two eigenvalues is REFUTED: the caps are exactly what the repair runs on, and dropping them is what left the low range unbounded* ([r864](../reviews/REVIEW_r864.md)). **And the vertex argument had a gap**: the coefficients alternating in sign does not by itself put the mass on $j\in\{1,3\}$, and the correct reason is a chord comparison, $(2,c_2)$ lying below the $(1,3)$ chord always while $(1,c_1)$ lies above the $(0,3)$ chord exactly when $s>N/3$, *which is why the feasibility threshold and the optimality threshold coincide.* **Two smaller corrections**: "$n=4$ closes the two-eigenvalue decomposable case" needs Ricci-flatness and is otherwise overstated, and $s=31$ was never realisable at all, Ricci-flatness forcing a coordinate configuration's degree to $31/6$, *so the $0.2\%$ excess the block chased was an artefact of an unrealisable level-set size, which the divisibility condition $n\mid2a$ names.*

> **WHAT SURVIVES IS AN IDENTITY AT THE MAXIMISER RATHER THAN A BOUND, AND IT IS EXACT AT EVERY EVEN $n$.** *[r865](../reviews/REVIEW_r865.md) verified the general form: the vertex value at $n=2p$ and $s=2\binom p2$ equals $\theta_0(2p)$ for EVERY $p$, not only at $n=4$ and $n=12$.* **So the balanced product's triangle counts ARE the linear programme's vertex at every even dimension, and the programme's value there is exactly $\theta_0(n)$** ([r864](../reviews/REVIEW_r864.md), [r865](../reviews/REVIEW_r865.md)). *That is a structural fact about why the balanced product is extremal for this relaxation, and it is what the item keeps.* **The prize is unchanged and now clearly the only prize**: at three distinct values the same programme runs over ten triple-types with the same marginal constraints and the six cap inequalities, and [L105](bounds-relaxations.md) does not reach there. *Any bound from it must be checked over the WHOLE admissible window, which is the error this block made once.*
> **THE THREE-EIGENVALUE PROGRAMME, WRITTEN OUT, AND THE CAP ENTERS HERE AS A CONSTRAINT.** *Working notes, Gate 2 pending.* **With level sets $A_1,A_2,A_3$ of sizes $a_i$ and values $\mu_i$, write $t_{ijk}$ for the sum of $T$ over ordered triples whose indices lie in $A_i,A_j,A_k$**, symmetric in $(i,j,k)$ by total symmetry of $T$, so there are ten types. *The spectrum obeys $\sum_ia_i\mu_i=0$ and $\sum_ia_i\mu_i^2=1$, and* $$\theta=\sum_ia_i\mu_i^3+\tfrac12\sum_{i,j,k}t_{ijk}\,\mu_i\mu_j\mu_k.$$ **The marginal gives one equality per level set**, $\sum_{j,k}t_{ijk}=2(n-2)a_i$, whose sum is the two-eigenvalue case's $M$. *And nonnegativity holds as before.*

> **WHAT IS NEW AT THREE VALUES IS THAT THE PAIR CAP BITES.** *Summing $T$ over its third index is $\sum_\gamma T_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=S_{\alpha\beta}$, which the bridge caps at $1$ on the decomposable locus against the recorded $2$ in general, so* $$\sum_kt_{ijk}\ \le\ a_ia_j\ \ (i\ne j),\qquad \sum_kt_{iik}\ \le\ a_i(a_i-1),$$ *the second by hollowness.* **These six inequalities are the first place in this line where decomposability does work that Ricci-flatness and the marginal do not**, and they are exactly half the strength of the general cap. *At two eigenvalues the vertex was forced without them, which is why the cap never appeared there.*

> **SO THE ITEM IS A FINITE OPTIMISATION AND ITS SHAPE IS KNOWN.** *Ten nonnegative variables, three equalities, six cap inequalities, and an objective linear in them once the spectrum is fixed, then a maximisation over the spectrum itself under two constraints.* **The coefficients say where the mass wants to sit**: with $\mu_1>0>\mu_2\ge\mu_3$ FOUR of the ten types carry positive coefficients, $\mu_1^3$, $\mu_1\mu_2\mu_3$, $\mu_1\mu_2^2$ and $\mu_1\mu_3^2$, *and the earlier count of two omitted $\mu_1\mu_2^2$, which is the type carrying most of the mass in the two-eigenvalue answer* ([r865](../reviews/REVIEW_r865.md)). **And the concrete instruction for this programme is to carry relation (B)'s one equality per level set alongside the marginal**, which is what repaired the two-eigenvalue pass. *A vertex enumeration is finite and by hand, and the two-eigenvalue instance is the worked case above, where the answer was $\theta_0(n)$ at the balanced counts.* **What would make this decisive is that [L105](bounds-relaxations.md) does not reach three values**, so a bound here is new rather than a second proof.

> **THE REDUCTION: $\theta$ IS THE THIRD MOMENT OF $T$ AND THE LOWER THREE ARE PINNED EXACTLY.** *Working notes, Gate 2 pending, derived 2026-09-10 as the item's first step at three or more values.* **Write $m_r=\sum_{i,j,k}t_{ijk}\,\mu_i^{[r]}$ for the contractions of the triple-type tensor against the spectrum**, so $m_0=\sum t_{ijk}$, $m_1=\sum\mu_it_{ijk}$, $m_2=\sum\mu_i\mu_jt_{ijk}$ and $m_3=\sum\mu_i\mu_j\mu_kt_{ijk}$. *Then $$\theta=\sum_ia_i\mu_i^3+\tfrac12m_3,$$ and the three lower moments are FIXED by data the record already has*: **the marginal gives $\sum_{j,k}t_{ijk}=2(n-2)a_i$ at every level set, hence $m_0=2(n-2)N$ and $m_1=2(n-2)\sum_ia_i\mu_i=0$ by trace-freeness, and relation (B) gives $\sum_{j,k}\mu_jt_{ijk}=-2a_i\mu_i$ at every level set, hence $m_2=-2\sum_ia_i\mu_i^2=-2$ at unit norm.** *So bounding $\theta$ is bounding the THIRD moment of a nonnegative symmetric tensor whose lower three moments are pinned, which is a moment problem rather than a search.*

> **AND THAT IS WHY THE TWO-VALUE CASE CLOSED IN CLOSED FORM.** *There the tensor has four types, the six per-level-set equalities collapse to three, and the feasible set is a segment whose endpoints are computable, which is exactly what both reviewers' repairs found.* **At three values there are ten types and six equalities, so the feasible polytope is four-dimensional before the caps**, and the caps $\sum_kt_{ijk}\le a_ia_j$ for $i\ne j$ and $a_i(a_i-1)$ for $i=j$ cut it further. *Relation (C) may be added in PART, and the blanket refusal was overstated: its contraction against $\mu_i$ returns $m_3=2\theta-2\sum_ia_i\mu_i^3$, the objective itself, so ONE of its three level-set equalities is circular while the other two are usable $\theta$-parametrically* ([r869](../reviews/REVIEW_r869.md), and in class form (C) reads $\tfrac13\sum_Sm_i(S)\pi_i(S)v_S=2a_i\mu_i(\theta-\mu_i)$). **What is owed is the vertex enumeration**, finite and by hand, and the sign data says where to look: four of the ten types carry positive coefficients at $\mu_1>0>\mu_2\ge\mu_3$, and the mirror case of two positive values against one negative runs the same way.

> **THE SYSTEM IN CLASS VARIABLES, WRITTEN OUT SO THE ENUMERATION CAN BE DONE.** *Working notes, Gate 2 pending.* **Index the ten multiset classes $S$ of triples from $\{1,2,3\}$ and let $v_S\ge0$ be the total of $T$ over the ORDERED triples of that class**, which is the reading every factor below requires and which the block first left undefined ([r870](../reviews/REVIEW_r870.md)). *Note $v_S$ is not hollow even though $T$ is: at $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ the all-negative class has $v_{333}=12$.* *Symmetry of $T$ then makes every contraction a weighted sum of the $v_S$ through the elementary symmetric functions of the multiset: with $\sigma_r(S)$ the $r$-th elementary symmetric function of the three values and $m_i(S)$ the multiplicity of $i$,* $$m_0=\sum_Sv_S,\quad m_1=\tfrac13\sum_S\sigma_1(S)v_S,\quad m_2=\tfrac13\sum_S\sigma_2(S)v_S,\quad m_3=\sum_S\sigma_3(S)v_S,$$ *the last carrying no factor because every ordering of a class has the same product.* **Every one of these five factors is CONFIRMED, each derived twice by each reviewer, and the $\tfrac12$ is pinned rather than tolerated**: building $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$'s structure constants from scratch gives $[f_1,f_2]=\sqrt2f_3$, so $T=2$ on each chirality triple and $\theta=1/\sqrt6+2/\sqrt6=\sqrt{3/2}$, where a $1$ would return $5/\sqrt6$ ([r869](../reviews/REVIEW_r869.md), [r870](../reviews/REVIEW_r870.md)). **The six equalities in class form**: $$\tfrac13\sum_Sm_i(S)\,v_S=2(n-2)a_i,\qquad \tfrac16\sum_Sm_i(S)\big(\sigma_1(S)-\mu_i\big)v_S=-2a_i\mu_i,$$ *the second from relation (B), its inner factor $\sigma_1(S)-\mu_i$ being the SUM of the remaining two values, the averaging sitting in the $\tfrac16$ ([r870](../reviews/REVIEW_r870.md), the gloss having said average).* **AND THE SIX EQUALITIES HAVE RANK FIVE, NOT SIX**: contracting the marginals against $\mu_i$ and summing the (B) rows give the same equation, $\tfrac13\sum_S\sigma_1(S)v_S$ against $0$ on both sides, *reproduced here*, so the polytope is FIVE-dimensional before the caps ([r869](../reviews/REVIEW_r869.md), [r870](../reviews/REVIEW_r870.md), independently). **The block contradicted itself here**: the two-value pass used this same dependency to collapse four equalities to three and the three-value pass then counted six as independent. **And the caps bound each pair marginal**, $\sum_kt_{ijk}\le a_ia_j$ off the diagonal and $a_i(a_i-1)$ on it, which in class form caps the classes containing the pair $\{i,j\}$. **THESE ARE THE DECOMPOSABLE-LOCUS CAPS AND THE GENERAL ONES ARE TWICE THEM, WHICH MATTERS AT THE RECORD'S OWN COMPETITOR**: $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ exceeds the stated caps at three of its six pairs, each by exactly two, and a programme run with them would bound it by $0.8165$ against its true $\sqrt{3/2}$ ([r870](../reviews/REVIEW_r870.md)). *So the caps may be used only where an orthonormal decomposable eigenbasis is in hand, and off that locus they double.*

> **THE SIGNS, AT $\mu_1=p>0>\mu_2=-q\ge\mu_3=-r$.** *Positive: $\sigma_3(111)=p^3$, $\sigma_3(122)=pq^2$, $\sigma_3(123)=pqr$, $\sigma_3(133)=pr^2$. Negative: the other six, namely $112,113,222,223,233,333$, of which FOUR are all-negative rather than three* ([r870](../reviews/REVIEW_r870.md)). **Every positive class contains the index of the POSITIVE eigenvalue, and no negative class is free of it except the three all-negative ones**, so the optimum loads triples that meet the positive level set and the caps on those pairs are what bind. *The mass available to them is bounded through $m_0$ and the marginal at $i=1$, which is why the programme has a finite value at all rather than needing the caps for boundedness.* **What is owed is the enumeration itself**, over the FIVE-dimensional polytope the equalities leave. *The mirror case of two positive values does NOT run the same way: it has SIX positive classes, "every positive class meets the positive level set" fails there, and since $P$ is odd, maximising over it is the MINIMISATION problem rather than a relabelled copy* ([r869](../reviews/REVIEW_r869.md), [r870](../reviews/REVIEW_r870.md), independently). **And the sign pattern with a zero value is uncovered by the framing altogether, which is exactly where $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$'s local maximum sits.** *One more constraint is free and stronger than the caps at small multiplicities: $v_S=0$ whenever $m_i(S)>a_i$, from hollowness* ([r869](../reviews/REVIEW_r869.md)).

> **THE STRATUM THE ROUND FOUND UNCOVERED IS THE COMPETITOR'S OWN, AND THE PROGRAMME BOUNDS IT BY $\sqrt{3/2}$ AT EVERY $n$.** *Working notes, Gate 2 pending, derived 2026-09-10 from the corrected caps.* **Take the spectrum type $(\mu_1$ once, $\mu_2$ twice, $0$ otherwise$)$, which is padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$'s at every $n\ge4$.** *Trace-freeness gives $\mu_1=-2\mu_2$ and unit norm $6\mu_2^2=1$, so the spectrum is FORCED to $(2c,-c,-c,0,\dots)$ at $c=1/\sqrt6$ with no freedom left.* **Then $\sigma_3$ vanishes on every class containing the zero level set, and [r869](../reviews/REVIEW_r869.md)'s support conditions kill two more**: $m_1(111)=3$ and $m_1(112)=2$ both exceed $a_1=1$, so $v_{111}=v_{112}=0$ and $$m_3=2c^3v_{122}-c^3v_{222}.$$ *So the programme reduces to one variable: take $v_{222}=0$ and bound $v_{122}$.*

> **AND THE PAIR CAP CLOSES IT EXACTLY.** *By symmetry $v_{122}=3\sum_{\alpha\in A_1}\sum_{\beta,\gamma\in A_2}T_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\le3\,a_1a_2\cdot2=12$ from the general cap $\sum_\gamma T_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\le2$.* **With $\sum_ia_i\mu_i^3=8c^3-2c^3=6c^3$ that gives** $$\theta\le6c^3+\tfrac12\cdot24c^3=18c^3=\frac{18}{6\sqrt6}=\sqrt{3/2},$$ *tight at padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$, and the value is independent of $n$ because $a_1$ and $a_2$ are.* **This is the theorem the stratum was missing**: every Ricci-flat unit-norm Weyl tensor whose spectrum is one value, twice another, and zero elsewhere has $\theta\le\sqrt{3/2}$, at every $n\ge4$, *with no criticality used, only the support conditions, the general pair cap, trace-freeness and unit norm.*

> **AND IT SHOWS EXACTLY WHAT THE WRONG CAP WOULD HAVE COST.** *The decomposable-locus cap would give $v_{122}\le6$ and hence $\theta\le12/(6\sqrt6)=2/\sqrt6=0.8165$, which is [r870](../reviews/REVIEW_r870.md)'s figure and would exclude a certified critical point.* **So the two caps differ by exactly the factor that separates a true bound from a false one here**, and the reason is that padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ carries no orthonormal decomposable eigenbasis, so the locus cap does not apply to it. *Which is also the cleanest statement of why the decomposable hypothesis is worth having: it halves this cap, and where it holds the same programme returns $0.8165$ against $\theta_0(n)$'s $1.236$ at $n=12$, far below the competitor.*

> **THE CONSTRAINT SET AS IT NOW STANDS, IN ONE PLACE, SINCE THE FIRST STATEMENT OF IT CARRIED FOUR DEFECTS.** *Working notes, Gate 2 pending.* **Variables**: $v_S\ge0$ for the ten multiset classes $S$ of triples over the level sets, each the total of $T$ over the ORDERED triples of that class. **Objective**: $\theta=\sum_ia_i\mu_i^3+\tfrac12\sum_S\sigma_3(S)v_S$. **Equalities, of rank five and not six**: $\tfrac13\sum_Sm_i(S)v_S=2(n-2)a_i$ from the marginal and $\tfrac16\sum_Sm_i(S)(\sigma_1(S)-\mu_i)v_S=-2a_i\mu_i$ from relation (B), the dependency being that the first family contracted against $\mu_i$ equals the second summed. **Support conditions, free from hollowness**: $v_S=0$ whenever $m_i(S)>a_i$, stronger than the caps at small multiplicities. **Caps**: $\sum_\gamma T_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\le2$ in general and $\le1$ on the decomposable locus, which in class form bound $v_{iii}\le a_i(a_i-1)\kappa$, $v_{iij}\le3a_ia_j\kappa$ and $v_{ijk}\le6\min(a_ia_j,a_ia_k,a_ja_k)\kappa$ at $\kappa$ the cap. **And relation (C) in class form is available in part**, $\tfrac13\sum_Sm_i(S)\pi_i(S)v_S=2a_i\mu_i(\theta-\mu_i)$, one of its three level-set rows being the objective and the other two usable $\theta$-parametrically.

> **THE STRATA, WHICH THE FIRST PASS DID NOT ENUMERATE.** *Three distinct values leave four sign patterns and they are not related by relabelling.* **$(+,-,-)$**: four positive classes, all meeting the positive level set, bounded through $m_0$ and the $i=1$ marginal. **$(+,+,-)$**: six positive classes, and since $P$ is odd this is the MINIMISATION problem rather than a relabelled copy, so it needs its own treatment. **$(+,-,0)$**: the competitor's own, closed above at $\theta\le\sqrt{3/2}$ with the general cap and at $0.8165$ with the locus cap. **$(+,+,0)$ and $(+,0,-)$ coincide** with the third, the zero level set contributing to $m_0$ and to no $\sigma_3$. *A kernel is therefore not an obstruction here but a simplification, which is the one place in this record where that is true.*

#### From [T9](../plan.md): the full pinned relaxation OVERSHOOTS, and the overshoot grows with dimension

**Run 2026-09-21 with the user's approval, scoped at the dimensions where the answer is already known.** The programme is `max theta` over `T >= 0` supported on distinct triples at a fixed trace-free unit-norm spectrum, carrying all three per-index relations, with Nelder--Mead over the spectrum outside ([run_pinned_lp_spectra.py](../scripts/runs/run_pinned_lp_spectra.py)):

| $n$ | LP variables | pinned $\max\theta$ | true max | overshoot |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| $6$ | $455$ | $1.339541$ | $\sqrt{3/2}=1.224745$ | $+0.1148$ |
| $8$ | $3276$ | $1.545512$ | $\sqrt{3/2}=1.224745$ | $+0.3208$ |
